## I Carson Napier / I 卡森·内皮尔

===Original===
“If a female figure in a white shroud enters your bedchamber at midnight on the thirteenth day of this month, answer this letter; otherwise, do not.”
Having read this far in the letter, I was about to consign it to the wastebasket, where all my crank letters go; but for some reason I read on, “If she speaks to you, please remember her words and repeat them to me when you write.” I might have read on to the end; but at this juncture the telephone bell rang, and I dropped the letter into one of the baskets on my desk. It chanced to be the “out” basket; and had events followed their ordinary course, this would have been the last of the letter and the incident in so far as I was concerned, for from the “out” basket the letter went to the files.
It was Jason Gridley on the telephone. He seemed excited and asked me to come to his laboratory at once. As Jason is seldom excited about anything, I hastened to accede to his request and satisfy my curiosity. Jumping into my roadster, I soon covered the few blocks that separate us, to learn that Jason had good grounds for excitement. He had just received a radio message from the inner world, from Pellucidar.
On the eve of the departure of the great dirigible, O-220, from the earth’s core, following the successful termination of that historic expedition, Jason had determined to remain and search for von Horst, the only missing member of the party; but Tarzan, David Innes, and Captain Zuppner had persuaded him of the folly of such an undertaking, inasmuch as David had promised to dispatch an expedition of his own native Pellucidarian warriors to locate the young German lieutenant if he still lived and it were possible to discover any clue to his whereabouts.
Notwithstanding this, and though he had returned to the outer world with the ship, Jason had always been harassed by a sense of responsibility for the fate of von Horst, a young man who had been most popular with all the members of the expedition; and had insisted time and time again that he regretted having left Pellucidar until he had exhausted every means within his power of rescuing von Horst or learned definitely that he was dead.

===Chinese===
「本月十三日午夜，若有一名身着白色裹尸布的女性身影进入你的卧室，请回复此信；否则，请勿回复。」
信读到这里，我正打算把它丢进废纸篓——我所有怪人的来信都去那儿；可出于某种原因，我又接着读了下去：「如果她对你说话，请记住她的原话，待你回信时转告于我。」我本可能一口气读到结尾；可就在这时，电话铃响了，我把信随手丢进办公桌上的一个篮子里。那恰好是「发出」篮；倘若事情照常理发展，这封信和这桩事本该就此与我无关——因为从「发出」篮出发，信便进了档案。
电话那头是杰森·格里德利（Jason Gridley）。他似乎很激动，要我立刻去他实验室一趟。杰森难得为任何事动容，于是我不敢怠慢，赶紧应允，也好满足自己的好奇心。我跳上敞篷跑车，转眼便驶过我们之间相隔的几个街区，这才知道杰森确有激动的理由——他刚收到一份来自地心世界、来自佩鲁西达（Pellucidar）的无线电讯。
在那次具有历史意义的探险圆满结束之后，就在巨型飞艇 O-220 即将驶离地心之际，杰森本决定留下来搜寻冯·霍斯特（von Horst）——探险队唯一失踪的队员；可人猿泰山、戴维·英尼斯（David Innes）和祖普纳上尉（Captain Zuppner）一道劝他打消这念头，理由是戴维已答应派遣一支由他本族佩鲁西达武士组成的搜救队，去寻找这位年轻的德国中尉——只要他还活着，只要还能找到关于他下落的任何线索。
尽管如此，尽管他已随飞艇回到了地表世界，杰森始终为冯·霍斯特的命运牵肠挂肚——这位年轻人极受全队上下的爱戴；他一次次坚称，后悔自己离开了佩鲁西达，因为他尚未穷尽一切手段去营救冯·霍斯特，也未确知他已身亡。

===Original===
Jason waved me to a chair and offered me a cigarette. “I’ve just had a message from Abner Perry,” he announced, “the first for months.”
“It must have been interesting,” I commented, “to excite *you*.”
“It was,” he admitted. “A rumor has reached Sari that von Horst has been found.”
Now as this pertains to a subject entirely foreign to the present volume, I might mention that I have alluded to it only for the purpose of explaining two facts which, while not vital, have some slight bearing on the remarkable sequence of events which followed. First, it caused me to forget the letter I just mentioned, and, second, it fixed the date in my mind﻿—the tenth.
My principal reason for mentioning the first fact is to stress the thought that the matter of the letter, so quickly and absolutely forgotten, had no opportunity to impress itself upon my mind and therefore could not, at least objectively, influence my consideration of ensuing events. The letter was gone from my mind within five minutes of its reading as completely as though it had never been received.
The next three days were exceedingly busy ones for me, and when I retired on the night of the thirteenth my mind was so filled with the annoying details of a real estate transaction that was going wrong, that it was some time before I could sleep. I can truthfully affirm that my last thoughts were of trust deeds, receivers in equity, and deficiency judgments.

===Chinese===
杰森挥手示意我坐下，递给我一支烟。「我刚收到阿布纳·佩里（Abner Perry）的讯息，」他宣布，「几个月来的头一遭。」
「那一定挺有意思，」我说，「居然能让*你*激动。」
「确实，」他承认，「萨里（Sari）那边传出消息，说冯·霍斯特找到了。」
这事跟本书毫无干系，我提它，只是想借此交代两桩虽然无关紧要、却对后来那一连串离奇事件多少有些影响的事实：其一，它让我把方才提到的那封信忘得一干二净；其二，它把一个日子钉在了我的记忆里——十号。
我之所以要强调第一点，主要是想说明：那封信被我忘得如此之快、如此之彻底，根本没机会在我脑子里留下印象，因而也就不可能——至少客观上不可能——影响我对后续事件的判断。读完不到五分钟，那封信便从我心头消失得无影无踪，仿佛从未收到过。
接下来三天我忙得不可开交，十三号夜里躺下时，脑子里塞满了一桩正闹纠纷的房地产交易的恼人琐事，许久都睡不着。我可以如实说，我睡前最后想的，是信托契约、衡平法上的接管人、还有不足额判决。

===Original===
What awoke me, I do not know. I sat up with a start just in time to see a female figure, swathed in what appeared to be a white winding sheet, enter my room through the door. You will note that I say door rather than doorway, for such was the fact; the door was closed. It was a clear, moonlit night; the various homely objects in my room were plainly discernible, especially the ghostly figure now hovering near the foot of my bed.
I am not subject to hallucinations, I had never seen a ghost, I had never wished to, and I was totally ignorant of the ethics governing such a situation. Even had the lady not been so obviously supernatural, I should yet have been at a loss as to how to receive her at this hour in the intimacy of my bedchamber, for no strange lady had ever before invaded its privacy, and I am of Puritan stock.
“It is midnight of the thirteenth,” she said, in a low, musical voice.
“So it is,” I agreed, and then I recalled the letter that I had received on the tenth.
“He left Guadalupe today,” she continued; “he will wait in Guaymas for your letter.”

===Chinese===
是什么把我惊醒的，我不知道。我一个激灵坐起身，恰好看见一个女性身影——身上裹着看似白色殓衣的东西——正从门里走进我的房间。请留意，我说的是「门」而非「门口」，因为事实就是如此：那扇门是关着的。那是个清朗的月夜；屋里种种家常物件都看得一清二楚，尤其是此刻在我床脚徘徊的那个鬼魅般的身影。
我不是那种会产生幻觉的人，我从没见过鬼，也从未想见过，对处理这种场合的规矩更是一窍不通。即便这位女士看起来不是这般明显地超自然，面对她在此时闯入我私密卧房，我也照样会手足无措——因为从来没有陌生的女士这样冒犯过它的清静，况且我出身清教徒家庭。
「现在是十三号午夜。」她说道，嗓音低柔，宛如乐音。
「可不是嘛。」我应道，随即想起了十号收到的那封信。
「他今天离开瓜达卢佩（Guadalupe）了，」她接着说，「他会在瓜伊马斯（Guaymas）等你的回信。」

===Original===
That was all. She crossed the room and passed out of it, not through the window which was quite convenient, but through the solid wall. I sat there for a full minute, staring at the spot where I had last seen her and endeavoring to convince myself that I was dreaming, but I was not dreaming; I was wide awake. In fact I was so wide awake that it was fully an hour before I had successfully wooed Morpheus, as the Victorian writers so neatly expressed it, ignoring the fact that his sex must have made it rather embarrassing for gentlemen writers.
I reached my office a little earlier than usual the following morning, and it is needless to say that the first thing that I did was to search for that letter which I had received on the tenth. I could recall neither the name of the writer nor the point of origin of the letter, but my secretary recalled the latter, the letter having been sufficiently out of the ordinary to attract his attention.
“It was from somewhere in Mexico,” he said, and as letters of this nature are filed by states and countries, there was now no difficulty in locating it.
You may rest assured that this time I read the letter carefully. It was dated the third and postmarked Guaymas. Guaymas is a seaport in Sonora, on the Gulf of California.
Here is the letter:

===Chinese===
就这些。她穿过房间，从屋里走了出去——不是从那扇挺方便的窗子，而是穿过厚实的墙壁。我在原地呆坐了足有一分钟，盯着她最后消失的那块地方，拼命说服自己我是在做梦，可我并非在做梦；我清醒得很。事实上我清醒到这般地步，竟足足过了一个钟头才把摩耳甫斯（Morpheus）成功求来——维多利亚时代的作家们曾如此精妙地形容入睡——全然不顾这位睡神的性别，恐怕会让诸位男作家颇为难堪。
第二天早上我比平时稍早到了办公室，不用说，我做的第一件事便是去翻十号收到的那封信。我既想不起写信人的名字，也想不起信的寄出地，可我的秘书却记得后者——那封信足够离奇，引起了他的注意。
「是从墨西哥某个地方寄来的。」他说，而这类信件按州、按国别归档，这下要找它便不难了。
各位尽可放心，这一回我把信读得仔仔细细。它的落款日期是三号，邮戳是瓜伊马斯。瓜伊马斯是索诺拉（Sonora）的一座港口，位于加利福尼亚湾畔。
信的原文如下：

===Original===
> My dear Sir:
> Being engaged in a venture of great scientific importance, I find it necessary to solicit the assistance (not financial) of someone psychologically harmonious, who is at the same time of sufficient intelligence and culture to appreciate the vast possibilities of my project.
> Why I have addressed you I shall be glad to explain in the happy event that a personal interview seems desirable. This can only be ascertained by a test which I shall now explain.
> If a female figure in a white shroud enters your bedchamber at midnight on the thirteenth day of this month, answer this letter; otherwise, do not. If she speaks to you, please remember her words and repeat them to me when you write.
> Assuring you of my appreciation of your earnest consideration of this letter, which I realize is rather unusual, and begging that you hold its contents in strictest confidence until future events shall have warranted its publication, I am, Sir,
> Very respectfully yours,
> **Carson Napier.**

===Chinese===
> 亲爱的先生：
> 在一项关乎重大科学价值的事业中，我亟需寻得一位与我心理契合之人的襄助（非资金方面的）；此人须兼具相当的智识与教养，方能领会我所筹划之事的深远前景。
> 至于我为何将此信寄予您，倘若有幸获蒙面晤，我自当欣然说明。而是否需作此面晤，唯有借一项测试方可断定，现将该测试说明如下。
> 本月十三日午夜，若有一名身着白色裹尸布的女性身影进入您的卧室，请回复此信；否则，请勿回复。若她开口对您说话，请务必记住她的原话，待您回信时一并转告于我。
> 深感此信颇为唐突，谨此对您拨冗认真披阅致以谢忱，并恳请您对信中所述严守秘密，直至日后事态发展足以佐证其可公之于众之时。先生，我是您忠诚的
> 谨上，
> **卡森·内皮尔（Carson Napier）。**

===Original===
“It looks to me like another nut,” commented Rothmund.
“So it did to me on the tenth,” I agreed; “but today is the fourteenth, and now it looks like another story.”
“What has the fourteenth got to do with it?” he demanded.
“Yesterday was the thirteenth,” I reminded him.
“You don’t mean to tell me﻿—” he started, skeptically.
“That is just what I do mean to tell you,” I interrupted. “The lady came, I saw, she conquered.”
Ralph looked worried. “Don’t forget what your nurse told you after your last operation,” he reminded me.
“Which nurse? I had nine, and no two of them told me the same things.”
“Jerry. She said that narcotics often affected a patient’s mind for months afterward.” His tone was solicitous.
“Well, at least Jerry admitted that I had a mind, which some of the others didn’t. Anyway, it didn’t affect my eyesight; I saw what I saw. Please take a letter to Mr. Napier.”

===Chinese===
「我看这又是个疯子。」罗斯蒙德（Rothmund）评论道。
「十号那天我也这么想，」我表示赞同，「可今天是十四号了，如今看来这另当别论。」
「十四号又跟这事有什么关系？」他追问。
「昨天是十三号。」我提醒他。
「你该不会是想告诉我——」他满脸狐疑地开口。
「我恰恰就是要告诉你这个，」我打断他，「那位女士来了，我看见了，她征服了我。」
拉尔夫（Ralph）面露忧色。「别忘了你上次手术之后护士跟你说过的话。」他提醒我。
「哪位护士？我一共有九个，没有哪两个说法是一样的。」
「杰瑞。她说麻醉药往往会影响病人的心智，长达数月之久。」他的语气颇为关切。
「嗯，至少杰瑞承认我还有个心智，其他几位连这都不承认。再说了，它没影响我的视力；我看见了就是看见了。请给内皮尔先生去一封信。」

===Original===
A few days later I received a telegram from Napier dated Guaymas.
“Letter received Stop Thanks Stop Shall call on you tomorrow,” it read.
“He must be flying,” I commented.
“Or coming in a white shroud,” suggested Ralph. “I think I’ll phone Captain Hodson to send a squad car around here; sometimes these nuts are dangerous.” He was still skeptical.
I must admit that we both awaited the arrival of Carson Napier with equal interest. I think Ralph expected to see a wild-eyed maniac. I could not visualize the man at all.

===Chinese===
几天后，我收到内皮尔从瓜伊马斯发来的电报。
「信已收悉 顿首 致谢 明日登门拜访。」电文如此。
「他一定是飞过来的。」我说。
「要么是穿着白裹尸布飘过来的。」拉尔夫接口道，「我想给霍德森上尉打个电话，让他派辆警车过来；有时候这些疯子挺危险。」他仍将信将疑。
我必须承认，我和拉尔夫以同样的心情盼着卡森·内皮尔的到来。我想拉尔夫等着看的是一个两眼发直的狂人。至于这个人长什么样，我脑海里一片空白。

===Original===
About eleven o’clock the following morning Ralph came into my study. “Mr. Napier is here,” he said.
“Does his hair grow straight out from his scalp, and do the whites of his eyes show all around the irises?” I inquired, smiling.
“No,” replied Ralph, returning the smile; “he is a very fine looking man, but,” he added, “I still think he’s a nut.”
“Ask him to come in,” and a moment later Ralph ushered in an exceptionally handsome man whom I judged to be somewhere between twenty-five and thirty years old, though he might have been even younger.
He came forward with extended hand as I rose to greet him, a smile lighting his face; and after the usual exchange of banalities he came directly to the point of his visit.

===Chinese===
第二天早上十一点光景，拉尔夫走进我的书房。「内皮尔先生到了。」他说。
「他头发是不是直挺挺地从头皮上竖起来？眼白是不是把虹膜围了一圈全露在外面？」我笑着问。
「不是，」拉尔夫也笑了，「他长得一表人才，不过，」他补充道，「我还是觉得他是个疯子。」
「请他进来吧。」不一会儿拉尔夫便领进来一位格外英俊的男子，我估摸他二十五到三十岁之间，也可能更年轻些。
我起身相迎，他伸出手走上前来，脸上挂着笑意；照例寒暄几句之后，他便径直道明了来意。

===Original===
“To get the whole picture clearly before you,” he commenced, “I shall have to tell you something about myself. My father was a British army officer, my mother an American girl from Virginia. I was born in India while my father was stationed there, and brought up under the tutorage of an old Hindu who was much attached to my father and mother. This Chand Kabi was something of a mystic, and he taught me many things that are not in the curriculums of schools for boys under ten. Among them was telepathy, which he had cultivated to such a degree that he could converse with one in psychological harmony with himself quite as easily at great distances as when face to face. Not only that, but he could project mental images to great distances, so that the recipient of his thought waves could see what Chand Kabi was seeing, or whatever else Chand Kabi wished him to see. These things he taught me.”
“And it was thus you caused me to see my midnight visitor on the thirteenth?” I inquired.

===Chinese===
「为了让您把事情的全貌看得一清二楚，」他开口道，「我得先谈谈我自己。家父是英国陆军军官，家母是来自弗吉尼亚（Virginia）的美国姑娘。我出生在印度，那时家父正驻防那里，我由一位老印度人启蒙教养，他与家父家母感情极深。这位钱德·卡比（Chand Kabi）多少算个神秘主义者，他教了我许多十岁以下男童学校课程里没有的东西，其中之一便是心灵感应。他把这门功夫练到了这般地步：与一个和他心理契合的人交谈，不论相隔多远，都和当面一样轻松。不仅如此，他还能将心智影像投射到遥远的地方，让接收他脑波的人看到钱德·卡比正在看的东西，或是任何钱德·卡比希望对方看到的东西。这些，他都传授给了我。」
「那么，十三号午夜让我看见那位不速之客的，也正是您了？」我问。

===Original===
He nodded. “That test was necessary in order to ascertain if we were in psychological harmony. Your letter, quoting the exact words that I had caused the apparition to appear to speak, convinced me that I had at last found the person for whom I have been searching for some time.
“But to get on with my story. I hope I am not boring you, but I feel that it is absolutely necessary that you should have full knowledge of my antecedents and background in order that you may decide whether I am worthy of your confidence and assistance, or not.” I assured him that I was far from being bored, and he proceeded.
“I was not quite eleven when my father died and my mother brought me to America. We went to Virginia first and lived there for three years with my mother’s grandfather, Judge John Carson, with whose name and reputation you are doubtless familiar, as who is not?

===Chinese===
他点点头。「这项测试是必须的，为的是查明你我是否心理契合。您那封信一字不差地引述了我让那幻影说出的话，这让我确信，自己终于找到了寻觅已久的人。」
「言归正传。希望我没让您厌烦，但我感到您必须充分了解我的出身和经历，这样您才能判断我是否值得您的信任与襄助。」我向他保证我绝无厌烦之意，他便继续说道。
「家父去世时我还不到十一岁，母亲带着我来到了美国。我们先去了弗吉尼亚，与母亲的祖父约翰·卡森法官（Judge John Carson）一起生活了三年——他的名望与声誉您想必如雷贯耳，又有谁不是呢？

===Original===
“After the grand old man died, mother and I came to California, where I attended public schools and later entered a small college at Claremont, which is noted for its high scholastic standing and the superior personnel of both its faculty and student body.
“Shortly after my graduation the third and greatest tragedy of my life occurred﻿—my mother died. I was absolutely stunned by this blow. Life seemed to hold no further interest for me. I did not care to live, yet I would not take my own life. As an alternative I embarked upon a life of recklessness. With a certain goal in mind, I learned to fly. I changed my name and became a stunt man in pictures.

===Chinese===
「那位可敬的老人辞世之后，母亲和我来到加利福尼亚（California）。我在那里上了公立学校，后来进了克莱蒙特（Claremont）的一所小学院，那所学校以优异的学术水准和出色的师生素质著称。
「毕业后不久，我生命中第三桩、也是最沉重的一桩悲剧发生了——母亲去世了。这一击令我彻底怔住。生活似乎再无意义可言。我不想活了，却又不愿亲手了结自己。作为另一条出路，我过起了一种玩命的生活。我心里有个既定的目标，于是我学会了飞行。我改了名字，去做了电影里的特技替身演员。

===Original===
“I did not have to work. Through my mother I had inherited a considerable fortune from my great-grandfather, John Carson; so great a fortune that only a spendthrift could squander the income. I mention this only because the venture I am undertaking requires considerable capital, and I wish you to know that I am amply able to finance it without help.
“Not only did life in Hollywood bore me, but here in Southern California were too many reminders of the loved one I had lost. I determined to travel, and I did. I flew all over the world. In Germany I became interested in rocket cars and financed several. Here my idea was born. There was nothing original about it except that I intended to carry it to a definite conclusion. I would travel by rocket to another planet.

===Chinese===
「我本不必工作。通过母亲，我从外曾祖父约翰·卡森那里继承了一笔可观的财产；数目之大，除非是个败家子，否则光靠利息也挥霍不尽。我提这一笔，只是因为我所筹划的事业需要相当的本钱，我希望您知道，无需旁人援手，我完全出资得起。
「好莱坞的生活不仅令我厌倦，南加利福尼亚更处处是与那位逝去挚爱相关的旧物。我决定去旅行，于是便上路了。我飞遍了世界各地。在德国，我对火箭汽车产生了兴趣，资助了好几辆。我的构想便在此萌生——它谈不上有何独创，只不过我决心把它贯彻到底：我要乘火箭去往另一颗星球。

===Original===
“My studies had convinced me that of all the planets Mars alone offered presumptive evidence of habitability for creatures similar to ourselves. I was at the same time convinced that if I succeeded in reaching Mars the probability of my being able to return to earth was remote. Feeling that I must have some reason for embarking upon such a venture, other than selfishness, I determined to seek out someone with whom I could communicate in the event that I succeeded. Subsequently it occurred to me that this might also afford the means for launching a second expedition, equipped to make the return journey, for I had no doubt but that there would be many adventurous spirits ready to undertake such an excursion once I had proved it feasible.
“For over a year I have been engaged in the construction of a gigantic rocket on Guadalupe Island, off the west coast of Lower California. The Mexican government has given me every assistance, and today everything is complete to the last detail. I am ready to start at any moment.”

===Chinese===
「我的研究使我确信，在所有行星中，唯有火星（Mars）有迹象表明可能适宜与人类相仿的生物生存。与此同时我也清楚，纵使我成功抵达火星，能重返地球的希望也微乎其微。我感到自己投身这样一桩事业，除了私心之外，总还要有些别的缘由，于是我决心寻得一人，待我成功之后能与之中通消息。后来我又想到，这或许也为日后派遣第二支、配备了返航设备的探险队提供了可能——因为我毫不怀疑，一旦我证明了此事可行，必会有许多胆大冒险之人争相前来。
「一年多来，我一直在下加利福尼亚西海岸外的瓜达卢佩岛上建造一枚巨型火箭。墨西哥政府给了我一切便利，如今万事俱备，只欠东风。我随时可以启程。」

===Original===
As he ceased speaking, he suddenly faded from view. The chair in which he had been sitting was empty. There was no one in the room but myself. I was stunned, almost terrified. I recalled what Rothmund had said about the effect of the narcotics upon my mentality. I also recalled that insane people seldom realize that they are insane. Was *I* insane! Cold sweat broke out upon my forehead and the backs of my hands. I reached toward the buzzer to summon Ralph. There is no question but that Ralph is sane. If he had seen Carson Napier and shown him into my study﻿—what a relief that would be!
But before my finger touched the button Ralph entered the room. There was a puzzled expression on his face. “Mr. Napier is back again,” he said, and then he added, “I didn’t know he had left. I just heard him talking to you.”

===Chinese===
他话音刚落，整个人竟忽地从我眼前淡去。他方才坐过的椅子空空如也。屋里除了我再无旁人。我惊呆了，几乎要吓坏了。我想起罗斯蒙德说过的、麻醉药对我神志的影响。我又想起，精神失常的人极少意识到自己失常。莫非*我*疯了！冷汗从我额头和手背沁了出来。我伸手去按蜂鸣器唤拉尔夫。拉尔夫的神志毫无疑问是清醒的。要是他真见过卡森·内皮尔、把他领进过我的书房——那该是多大的安慰啊！
可我的手指还没够到按钮，拉尔夫就进了屋。他一脸困惑。「内皮尔先生又回来了。」他说，随即又补了一句，「我都不知道他出去了。我刚才还听见他在跟您说话。」

===Original===
I breathed a sigh of relief as I wiped the perspiration from my face and hands; if I was crazy, so was Ralph. “Bring him in,” I said, “and this time you stay here.”
When Napier entered there was a questioning look in his eyes. “Do you fully grasp the situation as far as I have explained it?” he asked, as though he had not been out of the room at all.
“Yes, but﻿—” I started.
“Wait, please,” he requested. “I know what you are going to say, but let me apologize first and explain. I have not been here before. That was my final test. If you are confident that you saw me and talked to me and can recall what I said to you as I sat outside in my car, then you and I can communicate just as freely and easily when I am on Mars.”

===Chinese===
我抹去脸上和手上的汗，长舒了一口气；要是我疯了，那拉尔夫也疯了。「带他进来，」我说，「这回你留下来。」
内皮尔进来时，眼中带着探询的神色。「我讲到这儿为止的情况，您都完全领会了吗？」他问，仿佛他压根儿没离开过这间屋子。
「是的，可是——」我开口。
「请等一下，」他请求道，「我知道您要说什么，但请容我先致歉并解释。我之前并不在这里。那是我最后一项测试。只要您确信自己看见过我、与我说过话、还记得我坐在外面车里对您说的那些话，那么等我到了火星，您我之间照样可以这样自如顺畅地通信。」

===Original===
“But,” interjected Rothmund, “you *were* here. Didn’t I shake hands with you when you came in, and talk to you?”
“You thought you did,” replied Napier.
“Who’s loony now?” I inquired inelegantly, but to this day Rothmund insists that we played a trick on him.
“How do you know he’s here now, then?” he asked.
“I don’t,” I admitted.
“I am, this time,” laughed Napier. “Let’s see; how far had I gotten?”
“You were saying that you were all ready to start, had your rocket set up on Guadalupe Island,” I reminded him.

===Chinese===
「可是，」罗斯蒙德插嘴道，「你*刚才*就在这儿。你进来时我不是跟你握过手、跟你说过话吗？」
「您只是以为自己这么做了。」内皮尔回答。
「这会儿究竟是谁疯了？」我这话问得不大文雅，可罗斯蒙德至今仍坚持是我们合伙捉弄了他。
「那你怎么知道他这会儿就在这儿呢？」他问。
「我不知道。」我承认。
「这回我真在了。」内皮尔笑道，「让我想想；我讲到哪儿了？」
「你说你万事俱备，随时可以启程，火箭已经在瓜达卢佩岛上架好了。」我提醒他。

===Original===
“Right! I see you got it all. Now, as briefly as possible, I’ll outline what I hope you will find it possible to do for me. I have come to you for several reasons, the more important of which are your interest in Mars, your profession (the results of my experiment must be recorded by an experienced writer), and your reputation for integrity﻿—I have taken the liberty of investigating you most thoroughly. I wish you to record and publish the messages you receive from me and to administer my estate during my absence.”
“I shall be glad to do the former, but I hesitate to accept the responsibility of the latter assignment,” I demurred.
“I have already arranged a trust that will give you ample protection,” he replied in a manner that precluded further argument. I saw that he was a young man who brooked no obstacles; in fact I think he never admitted the existence of an obstacle. “As for your remuneration,” he continued, “you may name your own figure.”

===Chinese===
「对！看得出来您全听进去了。那么现在，我尽可能简短地把希望您能为我做的事讲一讲。我找您有几个原因，其中较为要紧的是：您对火星感兴趣、您的职业（我的实验结果必须由一位有经验的作家记录下来）、以及您为人正直的声名——我已冒昧地将您彻彻底底地调查了一番。我希望您记录并发表从我这里收到的讯息，并在我离开期间代为打理我的财产。」
「前者我乐意从命，但后一项责任，我怕是不敢应承。」我婉拒道。
「我已经安排好了一项信托，足以为您提供周全的保障。」他的语气不容再辩。我看出这是个不承认任何障碍的年轻人；事实上，我想他压根儿不承认障碍的存在。「至于您的报酬，」他接着说，「您自己开个价便是。」
我摆了摆手，表示不必在意。「这是件乐事。」我向他保证。

===Original===
“It may take a great deal of your time,” interjected Ralph, “and your time is valuable.”
“Precisely,” agreed Napier. “Mr. Rothmund and I will, with your permission, arrange the financial details later.”
“That suits me perfectly,” I said, for I detest business and everything connected with it.
“Now, to get back to the more important and far more interesting phases of our discussion; what is your reaction to the plan as a whole?”
“Mars is a long way from earth,” I suggested; “Venus is nine or ten million miles closer, and a million miles are a million miles.”

===Chinese===
「这也许会占去您大量时间，」拉尔夫插话，「而您的时间是宝贵的。」
「一点不错。」内皮尔附和，「若蒙许可，罗斯蒙德先生和我稍后会把报酬的细节谈妥。」
「这样正合我意。」我说，因为我厌恶生意经以及与之相关的一切。
「好吧，咱们回到讨论中更紧要、也更有趣的那些部分；您对整个计划作何感想？」
「火星离地球可不近，」我提议道，「金星（Venus）近了九百万到一千万英里，而一百万英里就是一百万英里。」

===Original===
“Yes, and I would prefer going to Venus,” he replied. “Enveloped in clouds, its surface forever invisible to man, it presents a mystery that intrigues the imagination; but recent astronomical research suggests conditions there inimical to the support of any such life as we know on earth. It has been thought by some that, held in the grip of the Sun since the era of her pristine fluidity, she always presents the same face to him, as does the Moon to earth. If such is the case, the extreme heat of one hemisphere and the extreme cold of the other would preclude life.
“Even if the suggestion of Sir James Jeans is borne out by fact, each of her days and nights is several times as long as ours on earth, these long nights having a temperature of thirteen degrees below zero, Fahrenheit, and the long days a correspondingly high temperature.”
“Yet even so, life might have adapted itself to such conditions,” I contended; “man exists in equatorial heat and arctic cold.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，我也更愿意去金星，」他回答，「它裹在云层里，表面永远不为人类所见，呈现出一桩撩人想象的秘辛；可最近的天文研究表明，那里的条件不利于我们所知的任何生命存续。有人认为，由于金星自远古熔融时期起便被太阳的引力锁定，永远以同一面对着太阳，一如月球之对地球。若真如此，一个半球的酷热与另一半球的严寒便将扼杀一切生机。
「即便詹姆斯·金斯爵士（Sir James Jeans）的设想属实，金星上的每一个昼夜都比地球长上数倍，那漫长的黑夜温度低至华氏零下十三度，漫长的白昼则相应地酷热。」
「纵然如此，生命也可能已适应了这般环境，」我争辩道，「人类不也在赤道的酷热和北极的严寒中生存吗？」

===Original===
“But not without oxygen,” said Napier. “St. John has estimated that the amount of oxygen above the cloud envelope that surrounds Venus is less than one tenth of one percent of the terrestrial amount. After all, we have to bow to the superior judgment of such men as Sir James Jeans, who says, ‘The evidence, for what it is worth, goes to suggest that Venus, the only planet in the solar system outside Mars and the earth on which life could possibly exist, possesses no vegetation and no oxygen for higher forms of life to breathe,’ which definitely limits my planetary exploration to Mars.”

===Chinese===
「可不能没有氧啊。」内皮尔说，「圣约翰（St. John）估算过，金星云层之上所含的氧，不到地球含氧量的千分之一。说到底，我们总得听从詹姆斯·金斯爵士这些权威的判断。他说：『就现有证据而言——不论其价值几何——足以表明：金星，这太阳系中除火星与地球外唯一可能存在生命的行星，既无植被，也无供养高等生物呼吸的氧气。』这话便把我星际探险的目标，死死限定在了火星之上。」

===Original===
We discussed his plans during the remainder of the day and well into the night, and early the following morning he left for Guadalupe Island in his Sikorsky amphibian. I have not seen him since, at least in person, yet, through the marvellous medium of telepathy, I have communicated with him continually and seen him amid strange, unearthly surroundings that have been graphically photographed upon the retina of my mind’s eye. Thus I am the medium through which the remarkable adventures of Carson Napier are being recorded on earth; but I am only that, like a typewriter or a dictaphone﻿—the story that follows is his.

===Chinese===
那一整天直到深夜，我们都在讨论他的计划。第二天一早，他便驾着那架西科斯基水陆两栖机飞往瓜达卢佩岛去了。自那以后我再没见过他——至少没见过他本人；然而，借由心灵感应这一奇妙的媒介，我始终与他保持联络，看见他置身于种种陌生而离奇的境地之中，那些景象如照片般清晰地烙印在我心眼（mind's eye）的视网膜上。于是，我便成了卡森·内皮尔那些非凡冒险在地球上得以记录的媒介；可我也仅仅是媒介而已，一如一台打字机、一架口述记录仪——接下来要讲的，是他的故事。

## II Off for Mars / II 飞向火星

===Original===
As I set my ship down in the sheltered cove along the shore of desolate Guadalupe a trifle over four hours after I left Tarzana, the little Mexican steamer I had chartered to transport my men, materials, and supplies from the mainland rode peacefully at anchor in the tiny harbor, while on the shore, waiting to welcome me, were grouped the laborers, mechanics, and assistants who had worked with such wholehearted loyalty for long months in preparation for this day. Towering head and shoulders above the others loomed Jimmy Welsh, the only American among them.
I taxied in close to shore and moored the ship to a buoy, while the men launched a dory and rowed out to get me. I had been absent less than a week, most of which had been spent in Guaymas awaiting the expected letter from Tarzana, but so exuberantly did they greet me, one might have thought me a long-lost brother returned from the dead, so dreary and desolate and isolated is Guadalupe to those who must remain upon her lonely shores for even a brief interval between contacts with the mainland.
Perhaps the warmth of their greeting may have been enhanced by a desire to conceal their true feelings. We had been together constantly for months, warm friendships had sprung up between us, and tonight we were to separate with little likelihood that they and I should ever meet again. This was to be my last day on earth; after today I should be as dead to them as though three feet of earth covered my inanimate corpse.

===Chinese===
离开塔扎纳（Tarzana）不过四个多小时，我便把飞船降落在荒凉的瓜达卢佩沿岸一处避风的小海湾里。我雇来运送人员、器材和补给的那艘墨西哥小轮船，正静静地停泊在小小的港湾中；岸上，等着我的一群劳工、机械师和助手们聚在一处，他们已怀着一腔赤诚，为这一天埋头苦干了数月之久。在他们中间，比众人高出一头的，是吉米·韦尔什（Jimmy Welsh）——他们当中唯一的美国人。
我把飞船滑行到近岸，系泊在浮标上，工人们则放下一只小划艇，划过来接我。我离开还不到一个星期，其中大部分时间都耗在瓜伊马斯等塔扎纳那封预期中的信，可他们迎接我时那股热乎劲儿，倒像是在迎接一个死里逃生、失散多年的兄弟——只因瓜达卢佩对那些不得不驻守在她孤寂海岸上、哪怕只是短暂等候与大陆联络的人来说，是如此凄凉、荒僻、与世隔绝。
或许他们问候的热情里，也掺着些想掩饰真实心境的成分。我们朝夕相处数月，彼此间结下了深厚的情谊，而今晚一别，他们与我很可能再无相见之日。这是我在地球上的最后一天；过了今天，对他们而言我便如同已死之人，仿佛三尺黄土已掩埋了我冰冷的尸骸。

===Original===
It is possible that my own sentiments colored my interpretation of theirs, for I am frank to confess that I had been apprehending this last moment as the most difficult of the whole adventure. I have come in contact with the peoples of many countries, but I recall none with more lovable qualities than Mexicans who have not been contaminated by too close contact with the intolerance and commercialism of Americans. And then there was Jimmy Welsh! It was going to be like parting with a brother when I said goodbye to him. For months he had been begging to go with me; and I knew that he would continue to beg up to the last minute, but I could not risk a single life unnecessarily.
We all piled into the trucks that we had used to transport supplies and materials from the shore to the camp, which lay inland a few miles, and bumped over our makeshift road to the little tableland where the giant torpedo lay upon its mile long track.

===Chinese===
或许是我自己的情绪染了我对他们情绪的体味，因为我坦白承认，我一直把临别这一刻视作整趟冒险中最难捱的关头。我与许多国家的人都打过交道，可在我的记忆里，没有哪个民族比那些尚未被美国人的偏狭与市侩习气污染的墨西哥人，更惹人喜爱了。更何况还有吉米·韦尔什！与他道别，将如同与亲兄弟分别。几个月来他一直求着要跟我同行；我知道他会一直恳求到最后一刻，可我不能无谓地拿任何一条命去冒险。
我们一伙人挤上几辆卡车——那原是用来从岸边往营地运送物资器材的——在临时辟出的土路上颠簸着，驶向内陆几英里外的那片小台地。巨型鱼雷状的火箭，便横卧在那里一条长达一英里的轨道上。

===Original===
“Everything is ready,” said Jimmy. “We polished off the last details this morning. Every roller on the track has been inspected by at least a dozen men, we towed the old crate back and forth over the full length of the track three times with the truck, and then repacked all the rollers with grease. Three of us have checked over every item of equipment and supplies individually; we’ve done about everything but fire the rockets; and now we’re ready to go﻿—you *are* going to take me along, aren’t you, Car?”
I shook my head. “Please don’t, Jimmy,” I begged; “I have a perfect right to gamble with my own life, but not with yours; so forget it. But I am going to do something for you,” I added, “just as a token of my appreciation of the help you’ve given me and all that sort of rot. I’m going to give you my ship to remember me by.”
He was grateful, of course, but still he could not hide his disappointment in not being allowed to accompany me, which was evidenced by an invidious comparison he drew between the ceiling of the Sikorsky and that of the old crate, as he had affectionately dubbed the great torpedolike rocket that was to bear me out into space in a few hours.
“A thirty-five million mile ceiling,” he mourned dolefully; “think of it! Mars for a ceiling!”
“And may I hit the ceiling!” I exclaimed, fervently.

===Chinese===
「万事俱备。」吉米说，「今早我们把最后一些细节收尾了。轨道上每一个滚轮都至少有十几个人检查过，我们用卡车拖着这老伙计在整条轨道上来来回回走了三趟，又给所有滚轮重新上了油。我们三个人把每一件设备、每一样补给都单独核对过；除了点火发射，该做的全做完了；现在可以出发了——你*会*带上我的，对吧，卡（Car）？」
我摇了摇头。「别这样，吉米，」我恳求道，「我有十足的权利拿自己的命去赌，却没权拿你的命去赌；所以死了这条心吧。不过我要送你点东西，」我补充道，「就当是谢你帮我的忙，聊表心意，如此而已。我要把我的飞机送给你，权当留个念想。」
他当然感激，可仍旧掩饰不住不能随我同行的失望——他这一点，从他把西科斯基的「升限」和「老伙计」的「升限」所作的那番酸溜溜的对比里便看得出来。「老伙计」是他给那枚巨型鱼雷火箭起的昵称，几个钟头后，它就要载着我冲入太空了。
「三千五百万英里的升限啊，」他凄然哀叹，「你想想！以火星为升限！」
「愿我一头撞上那个升限！」我热切地叫道。

===Original===
The laying of the track upon which the torpedo was to take off had been the subject of a year of calculation and consultation. The day of departure had been planned far ahead and the exact point at which Mars would rise above the eastern horizon on that night calculated, as well as the time; then it was necessary to make allowances for the rotation of the earth and the attraction of the nearer heavenly bodies. The track was then laid in accordance with these calculations. It was constructed with a very slight drop in the first three quarters of a mile and then rose gradually at an angle of two and one half degrees from horizontal.
A speed of four and one half miles per second at the takeoff would be sufficient to neutralize gravity; to overcome it, I must attain a speed of 6.93 miles per second. To allow a sufficient factor of safety I had powered the torpedo to attain a speed of seven miles per second at the end of the runway, which I purposed stepping up to ten miles per second while passing through the earth’s atmosphere. What my speed would be through space was problematical, but I based all my calculations on the theory that it would not deviate much from the speed at which I left the earth’s atmosphere, until I came within the influence of the gravitational pull of Mars.

===Chinese===
火箭起飞所用的那条轨道该如何铺设，曾是我整整一年计算与磋商的课题。启程之日早已提前定下，那天夜里火星从东方地平线升起的精确方位、以及升起的时刻，也都已算妥；然后还要扣除地球自转和较近天体引力的影响。轨道便是按这些计算结果铺设的。它的头四分之三英里有极轻微的下坡，随后以与水平面成二点五度的角度缓缓上升。
起飞时达到每秒四点五英里的速度，便足以抵消引力；要彻底克服引力，我必须达到每秒六点九三英里。为留出足够的安全余量，我把火箭的动力调到能在跑道尽头达到每秒七英里，打算在穿过地球大气层时再把它提到每秒十英里。至于在太空中我的速度会如何，则难以预料，但我所有的计算都基于这样一个假设：在进入火星引力范围之前，我的速度不会偏离离开地球大气层时的速度太多。

===Original===
The exact instant at which to make the start had also caused me considerable anxiety. I had calculated it again and again, but there were so many factors to be taken into consideration that I had found it expedient to have my figures checked and rechecked by a well-known physicist and an equally prominent astronomer. Their deductions tallied perfectly with mine﻿—the torpedo must start upon its journey toward Mars some time before the red planet rose above the eastern horizon. The trajectory would be along a constantly flattening arc, influenced considerably at first by the earth’s gravitational pull, which would decrease inversely as the square of the distance attained. As the torpedo left the earth’s surface on a curved tangent, its departure must be so nicely timed that when it eventually escaped the pull of the earth its nose would be directed toward Mars.
On paper, these figures appeared most convincing; but, as the moment approached for my departure, I must confess to a sudden realization that they were based wholly upon theory, and I was struck with the utter folly of my mad venture.

===Chinese===
至于动身的精确时刻，也曾令我大伤脑筋。我一再计算，可要考虑的因素实在太多，于是我索性请了一位著名物理学家和一位同样有声望的天文学家，把我的数字反复核验。他们推导的结果与我完全吻合——火箭必须在红色行星升起于东方地平线之前的一刻，踏上飞往火星的旅程。飞行轨迹将沿一条不断趋于平缓的弧线，起初受地球引力影响颇大，而该引力会随距离平方成反比递减。由于火箭是沿一条弯曲切线离开地球表面的，启程的时刻必须卡得恰到好处，使得它最终摆脱地球引力时，箭头正好指向火星。
在纸上，这些数字看起来颇有说服力；可随着启程时刻临近，我必须承认，我忽然意识到它们全建立在理论之上，我一时被自己这桩疯狂事业的十足愚蠢击中了。

===Original===
For a moment I was aghast. The enormous torpedo, with its sixty tons, lying there at the end of its mile long track, loomed above me, the semblance of a gargantuan coffin﻿—my coffin, in which I was presently to be dashed to earth, or to the bottom of the Pacific, or cast out into space to wander there to the end of time. I was afraid. I admit it, but it was not so much the fear of death as the effect of the sudden realization of the stupendousness of the cosmic forces against which I had pitted my puny powers that temporarily unnerved me.
Then Jimmy spoke to me. “Let’s have a last look at things inside the old crate before you shove off,” he suggested, and my nervousness and my apprehensions vanished beneath the spell of his quiet tones and his matter-of-fact manner. I was myself again.

===Chinese===
刹那间我惊骇万分。那枚六十吨重的巨型火箭，横卧在一英里长的轨道尽头，在我头顶投下巨影，形同一口硕大无朋的棺材——我的棺材，过不多时我便要在里头被摔成肉泥，砸向地面，或栽进太平洋底，要么就被抛入太空，在那里游荡到时间的尽头。我害怕了。这一点我承认，但与其说是怕死，不如说是骤然意识到自己所对抗的宇宙之力何等浩瀚，而自己的微末之力又何等渺小——正是这一念头，暂时击垮了我的神经。
接着，吉米开口了。「在你出发前，咱们再最后瞧瞧『老伙计』里头吧。」他提议。他那沉静的嗓音、那不慌不忙的做派，顿时让我心中的紧张与忧虑消散殆尽。我又恢复了常态。

===Original===
Together we inspected the cabin where are located the controls, a wide and comfortable berth, a table, a chair, writing materials, and a well-stocked bookshelf. Behind the cabin is a small galley and just behind the galley a storeroom containing canned and dehydrated foods sufficient to last me a year. Back of this is a small battery room containing storage batteries for lighting, heating, and cooking, a dynamo, and a gas engine. The extreme stern compartment is filled with rockets and the intricate mechanical device by which they are fed to the firing chambers by means of the controls in the cabin. Forward of the main cabin is a large compartment in which are located the water and oxygen tanks, as well as a quantity of odds and ends necessary either to my safety or comfort.
Everything, it is needless to say, is fastened securely against the sudden and terrific stress that must accompany the takeoff. Once out in space, I anticipate no sense of motion, but the start is going to be rather jarring. To absorb, as much as possible, the shock of the takeoff, the rocket consists of two torpedoes, a smaller torpedo within a larger one, the former considerably shorter than the latter and consisting of several sections, each one comprising one of the compartments I have described. Between the inner and outer shells and between each two compartments is installed a system of ingenious hydraulic shock absorbers designed to more or less gradually overcome the inertia of the inner torpedo during the takeoff. I trust that it functions properly.

===Chinese===
我们一道检查了驾驶舱——控制装置、一张宽大舒适的卧铺、一张桌子、一把椅子、书写用品，以及一只藏书颇丰的书架，都在这里。舱后是一间小厨房，厨房再往后是储藏室，里头的罐头和脱水食品足够我吃上一年。储藏室后面是一间小小的电池室，装着用于照明、取暖和烹饪的蓄电池，还有一台发电机和一台煤气机。船体最尾端的舱室里塞满了火箭，以及一具精密的机械装置，靠驾驶舱里的控制钮把火箭一一送入燃烧室。主舱前方是一个大舱，里面安置着水舱和氧气罐，还有不少为我的安全或舒适所不可或缺的零七杂八的物件。
不用说，每一样东西都牢牢固定住了，以抵御起飞时必然袭来的那股突如其来、猛烈无比的力量。一旦进入太空，我估计不会再有什么运动感，可起步那一瞬势必相当颠簸。为了尽可能吸收起飞的冲击，火箭做成内外两层鱼雷相套的结构——小的套在大的里面，前者比后者短得多，分为数节，我方才描述的每个舱室便各占一节。在内外两层壳体之间、以及每两节舱室之间，都装有一套精巧的液压减震系统，旨在起飞时多少能渐进地克服内层鱼雷的惯性。但愿它管用。

===Original===
In addition to these precautions against disaster at the start, the chair in which I shall sit before the controls is not only heavily overstuffed but is secured to a track or framework that is equipped with shock absorbers. Furthermore, there are means whereby I may strap myself securely into the chair before taking off.
I have neglected nothing essential to my safety, upon which depends the success of my project.
Following our final inspection of the interior, Jimmy and I clambered to the top of the torpedo for a last inspection of the parachutes, which I hope will sufficiently retard the speed of the rocket after it enters the atmosphere of Mars to permit me to bail out with my own parachute in time to make a safe landing. The main parachutes are in a series of compartments running the full length of the top of the torpedo. To explain them more clearly, I may say that they are a continuous series of batteries of parachutes, each battery consisting of a number of parachutes of increasing diameter from the uppermost, which is the smallest. Each battery is in an individual compartment, and each compartment is covered at the will of the operator by controls in the cabin. Each parachute is anchored to the torpedo by a separate cable. I expect about one half of them to be torn loose while checking the speed of the torpedo sufficiently to permit the others to hold and further retard it to a point where I may safely open the doors and jump with my own parachute and oxygen tank.

===Chinese===
除了这些防止起飞灾难的预防措施之外，我在控制台前要坐的那把椅子，不仅厚厚地垫了软垫，还固定在一副带减震器的滑轨或框架上。况且，我还能在起飞前用带子把自己牢牢绑在椅子里。
凡关乎我安全的要紧事，我一桩也没有疏漏——而安全，正是整桩事业成败之所系。
内部检查完毕，吉米和我又爬上火箭顶部，最后检查一遍降落伞——我希望它们能在火箭进入火星大气层后充分减速，让我来得及背上自己的降落伞跳出来，安全着陆。主降落伞装在火箭顶部一排贯通全长的小舱里。说得更清楚些：它们是一连串的降落伞组，每组由数顶降落伞组成，直径从最上面、也就是最小的一顶开始逐级增大。每组各占一个独立小舱，每个小舱的舱盖都可由驾驶舱里的控制钮随意启闭。每顶降落伞各用一根缆绳单独锚定在火箭上。我预计大约一半的伞会在为火箭减速时被冲脱，剩下的伞得以撑住，进一步把速度降到我可以安全开门、带着自己的降落伞和氧气罐跳出的程度。

===Original===
The moment for departure was approaching. Jimmy and I had descended to the ground and the most difficult ordeal now faced me﻿—that of saying goodbye to these loyal friends and co-workers. We did not say much, we were too filled with emotion, and there was not a dry eye among us. Without exception none of the Mexican laborers could understand why the nose of the torpedo was not pointed straight up in the air if my intended destination were *Marte*. Nothing could convince them that I would not shoot out a short distance and make a graceful nose dive into the Pacific﻿—that is, if I started at all, which many of them doubted.
There was a handclasp all around, and then I mounted the ladder leaning against the side of the torpedo and entered it. As I closed the door of the outer shell, I saw my friends piling into the trucks and pulling away, for I had given orders that no one should be within a mile of the rocket when I took off, fearing, as I did, the effect upon them of the terrific explosions that must accompany the takeoff. Securing the outer door with its great vaultlike bolts, I closed the inner door and fastened it; then I took my seat before the controls and buckled the straps that held me to the chair.

===Chinese===
启程的时刻逼近了。吉米和我下到地面，此刻最艰难的考验摆在我面前——向这些忠诚的友人和同僚道别。我们没说多少话，情绪都满得快要溢出来，个个泪眼婆娑。无一例外，所有墨西哥劳工都想不明白：既然我要去的是火星（Marte），那火箭的箭头为什么不直直地朝天竖着？怎么也无法说服他们相信我不会蹿出一小段距离，然后优雅地一头栽进太平洋——前提是我真能飞起来，而这一点他们当中许多人都深表怀疑。
我和众人一一握手，然后登上靠在火箭边上的梯子，钻了进去。我关上外壳的舱门时，看见朋友们正纷纷爬上卡车驶离——因为我下过命令，起飞时任何人不得待在火箭一英里之内，我担心起飞时必然发出的猛烈爆燃会伤及他们。我用那几道宛如金库门栓般的粗大螺栓锁好外门，又关上内门拴牢；随后我在控制台前坐定，系紧了把我和椅子绑在一起的带子。

===Original===
I glanced at my watch. It lacked nine minutes of the zero hour. In nine minutes I should be on my way out into the great void, or in nine minutes I should be dead. If all did not go well, the disaster would follow within a fraction of a split second after I touched the first firing control.
Seven minutes! My throat felt dry and parched; I wanted a drink of water, but there was no time.
Four minutes! Thirty-five million miles are a lot of miles, yet I planned on spanning them in between forty and forty-five days.
Two minutes! I inspected the oxygen gauge and opened the valve a trifle wider.
One minute! I thought of my mother and wondered if she were way out there somewhere waiting for me.
Thirty seconds! My hand was on the control. Fifteen seconds! Ten, five, four, three, two﻿—one!

===Chinese===
我瞥了一眼表。离零时刻还差九分钟。九分钟后，我也许正在飞向那无垠的太虚；九分钟后，我也许已是一具尸骸。倘若一切不顺，灾难将在我触动第一个点火控制钮的须臾之间降临。
七分钟！我觉得喉咙又干又燥；我想喝口水，可已没了时间。
四分钟！三千五百万英里是相当漫长的一段路，可我打算用四十到四十五天跨越它。
两分钟！我检查了氧气表，把阀门又拧大了一点。
一分钟！我想起了母亲，不知她是否就在那遥远某处，等着我。
三十秒！我的手已搭在控制钮上。十五秒！十、五、四、三、二——一！

===Original===
I turned the pointer! There was a muffled roar. The torpedo leaped forward. I was off!
I knew that the takeoff was a success. I glanced through the port at my side at the instant that the torpedo started, but so terrific was its initial speed that I saw only a confused blur as the landscape rushed past. I was thrilled and delighted by the ease and perfection with which the takeoff had been accomplished, and I must admit that I was not a little surprised by the almost negligible effects that were noticeable in the cabin. I had had the sensation as of a giant hand pressing me suddenly back against the upholstery of my chair, but that had passed almost at once, and now there was no sensation different from that which one might experience sitting in an easy chair in a comfortable drawing-room on terra firma.

===Chinese===
我把指针一拧！一声沉闷的轰鸣。火箭向前一跃。我出发了！
我知道起飞成功了。火箭启动的刹那，我从身侧的舷窗向外瞥了一眼，可它的初速快得惊人，我只看到景物飞掠而过化作一片模糊。起飞完成得如此轻松、如此完美，令我激动不已、欣喜若狂；我也得承认，舱内几乎感觉不到什么影响，这叫我相当意外。我曾有那么一种感觉，仿佛一只巨手猛地把我按回椅子的软垫里，但这几乎转瞬即逝，此刻我再无异样，与一个坐在陆地上一间舒适客厅的安乐椅里的人并无二致。

===Original===
There was no sensation of motion after the first few seconds that were required to pass through the earth’s atmosphere, and now that I had done all that lay within my power to do, I could only leave the rest to momentum, gravitation, and fate. Releasing the straps that held me to the chair, I moved about the cabin to look through the various ports, of which there were several in the sides, keel, and top of the torpedo. Space was a black void dotted with countless points of light. The earth I could not see, for it lay directly astern; far ahead was Mars. All seemed well. I switched on the electric lights, and seating myself at the table, made the first entries in the log; then I checked over various computations of time and distances.
My calculations suggested that in about three hours from the takeoff the torpedo would be moving almost directly toward Mars; and from time to time I took observations through the wide-angle telescopic periscope that is mounted flush with the upper surface of the torpedo’s shell, but the results were not entirely reassuring. In two hours Mars was dead ahead﻿—the arc of the trajectory was not flattening as it should. I became apprehensive. What was wrong? Where had our careful computations erred?

===Chinese===
穿过地球大气层所需的最初几秒一过，便再无半点运动感了。既然我已竭尽人事，剩下的只能交给惯性、引力和命运。我解开绑住自己的带子，在舱里走动着，从各个舷窗往外看——火箭的两侧、底部、顶部都有舷窗。太空是一片漆黑的虚空，缀着无数光点。地球我看不到，因为它正正当当地在船尾方向；遥远的正前方，是火星。一切似乎都很正常。我打开电灯，在桌前坐下，写下日志的头几条记录；接着又核对了几处关于时间和距离的计算。
按我的推算，起飞约三小时后，火箭便将几乎笔直地朝火星飞去；我不时透过那具与火箭外壳上表面齐平安装的广角望远镜式潜望镜观测一番，可结果并不十分令人安心。两小时后，火星正正当当地在正前方——弹道的弧线并没有按它应有的样子趋于平缓。我焦虑起来。哪儿出了问题？我们那些缜密的计算又错在了哪里？

===Original===
I left the periscope and gazed down through the main keel port. Below and ahead was the Moon, a gorgeous spectacle as viewed through the clear void of space from a distance some seventy-two thousand miles less than I had ever seen it before and with no earthly atmosphere to reduce visibility. Tycho, Plato, and Copernicus stood out in bold relief upon the brazen disc of the great satellite, deepening by contrast the shadows of Mare Serenitatis and Mare Tranquilitatis. The rugged peaks of the Apennine and the Altai lay revealed as distinctly as I had ever seen them through the largest telescope. I was thrilled, but I was distinctly worried, too.

===Chinese===
我离开潜望镜，俯身从底部的中央龙骨舷窗望下去。前下方是月亮，透过清朗的太空望去，那景象瑰丽无比——比我从前所见近了约七万两千英里，又没有地球大气遮挡视线。第谷、柏拉图、哥白尼三座环形山，在这颗巨大卫星那张青铜色的圆盘上赫然凸起，相衬之下，把澄海与静海的阴影衬得愈发深邃。亚平宁山脉和阿尔泰山脉那嶙峋的群峰，一览无余，清晰得如同我曾透过最大的望远镜所见。我心潮澎湃，却也分明忧心忡忡。

===Original===
Three hours later I was less than fifty-nine thousand miles from the Moon; where its aspect had been gorgeous before, it now beggared description, but my apprehension had cause to increase in proportion; I might say, as the square of its increasing gorgeousness. Through the periscope I had watched the arc of my trajectory pass through the plane of Mars and drop below it. I knew quite definitely then that I could never reach my goal. I tried not to think of the fate that lay ahead of me; but, instead, sought to discover the error that had wrought this disaster.
For an hour I checked over various calculations, but could discover nothing that might shed light on the cause of my predicament; then I switched off the lights and looked down through the keel port to have a closer view of the Moon. It was not there! Stepping to the port side of the cabin, I looked through one of the heavy circular glasses out into the void of space. For an instant I was horror stricken; apparently just off the port bow loomed an enormous world. It was the Moon, less than twenty-three thousand miles away, and I was hurtling toward it at the rate of thirty-six thousand miles an hour!

===Chinese===
三小时后，我距月亮已不足五万九千英里；先前它那令人叹为观止的景象，如今已难以用言语形容，而我的忧虑也按比例水涨船高——不妨说，是随着它那日益壮丽的平方数在递增。透过潜望镜，我眼看着自己弹道的弧线穿过了火星所在的平面，又掉到了它之下。那时我便相当确凿地知道，我再也到不了目的地了。我竭力不去想前方等待我的命运，转而设法找出酿成这场灾难的那个错误。
足足一个钟头我都在核对各种计算，却找不到任何能解释我这般困境的线索；于是我关上灯，从龙骨舷窗俯身去看月亮。它不在那儿！我走到舱的左舷，透过一扇厚重的圆形玻璃向太空的虚空望去。刹那间我惊恐万分——就在左舷正前方不远处，赫然逼近一个巨大的世界。那是月亮，离我已不足两万三千英里，而我正以每小时三万六千英里的速度朝它直冲过去！

===Original===
I leaped to the periscope, and in the next few seconds I accomplished some lightning mental calculating that must constitute an all-time record. I watched the deflection of our course in the direction of the Moon, following it across the lens of the periscope, I computed the distance to the Moon and the speed of the torpedo, and I came to the conclusion that I had better than a fighting chance of missing the great orb. I had little fear of anything but a direct hit, since our speed was so great that the attraction of the Moon could not hold us if we missed her even by a matter of feet; but it was quite evident that it had affected our flight, and with this realization came the answer to the question that had been puzzling me.

===Chinese===
我一跃扑到潜望镜前，在随后的几秒钟里完成了一连串神速的心算，那速度想必破了有史以来的纪录。我注视着航线朝月亮方向的偏移，循着它在潜望镜的镜头上移动，我估算着到月亮的距离和火箭的速度，最后得出结论：躲开这个巨大星体的把握，倒还略占上风。我只担心正面撞上，因为我们的速度实在太快，哪怕只差几英尺擦肩而过，月亮的引力也留不住我们；可它的引力显然已影响了我们的飞行——随着这一层认识，那个一直困扰我的问题的答案，也随之浮现。

===Original===
To my mind flashed the printer’s story of the first perfect book. It had been said that no book had ever before been published containing not a single error. A great publishing house undertook to publish such a book. The galley proofs were read and reread by a dozen different experts, the page proofs received the same careful scrutiny. At last the masterpiece was ready for the press﻿—errorless! It was printed and bound and sent out to the public, and then it was discovered that the title had been misspelled on the title page. With all our careful calculation, with all our checking and rechecking, we had overlooked the obvious; we had not taken the Moon into consideration at all.

===Chinese===
我脑海里倏地闪起那个印刷匠讲的「第一本完美之书」的故事。据说，从来没有任何一本书能做到没有一个错。一家大出版社决心要出这样一本书。长条校样由十几位不同的专家反复校阅，页样也受到同样仔细的审读。终于，这部杰作可以付印了——零差错！书印出来、装订好、发往公众之后，才发现扉页上书名就拼错了。我们做了那么缜密的计算，一遍又一遍地核对，却忽略了那个最显眼的东西——我们压根儿没把月亮算进去。

===Original===
Explain it if you can; I cannot. It was just one of those things, as people say when a good team loses to a poor one; it was a *break*, and a bad one. How bad it was I did not even try to conjecture at the time; I just sat at the periscope watching the Moon racing toward us. As we neared it, it presented the most gorgeous spectacle that I have ever witnessed. Each mountain peak and crater stood out in vivid detail. Even the great height of summits over twenty-five thousand feet appeared distinguishable to me, though imagination must have played a major part in the illusion, since I was looking down upon them from above.
Suddenly I realized that the great sphere was passing rapidly from the field of the periscope, and I breathed a sigh of relief﻿—we were not going to score a clean hit, we were going to pass by.

===Chinese===
你若能解释，便解释吧；我可解释不了。这不过是那种「事情就是如此」的事，正如人们看到强队输给弱队时爱说的那样；这是一次*意外*，一次糟糕的意外。究竟有多糟，我当时甚至懒得去推测；我只是坐在潜望镜前，眼看着月亮朝我们飞速逼近。我们越是接近，它呈现出的景象便越发瑰丽，胜过我平生所见。每一座山峰、每一个环形山都纤毫毕现。就连两万五千英尺以上的高峰那巍峨的高度，我似乎也分辨得出——尽管这幻象里想必掺了不少想象，因为我是从高处俯瞰它们的。
忽然我意识到，这颗巨大的球体正迅速从潜望镜的视野里滑过，我长舒了一口气——我们不会正面撞上，我们要从它旁边掠过了。

===Original===
I returned then to the porthole. The Moon lay just ahead and a little to the left. It was no longer a great sphere; it was a world that filled my whole range of vision. Against its black horizon I saw titanic peaks; below me huge craters yawned. I stood with God on high and looked down upon a dead world.
Our transit of the Moon required a little less than four minutes; I timed it carefully that I might check our speed. How close we came I may only guess; perhaps five thousand feet above the tallest peaks, but it was close enough. The pull of the Moon’s gravitation had definitely altered our course, but owing to our speed we had eluded her clutches. Now we were racing away from her, but to what?

===Chinese===
于是我回到舷窗前。月亮就在正前方稍稍偏左之处。它已不再是一颗巨大的球体；它是一个充斥着我整个视野的世界。在它那漆黑的地平线映衬下，我看见一座座巨峰；我脚下，一个个巨大的环形山张着大口。我伫立高处，与上帝比肩，俯瞰着一个死寂的世界。
我们掠过月亮用了不到四分钟；我仔细计了时，以便核对我们的速度。我们靠得多近，只能靠猜——大约在最高峰之上五千英尺吧，但已经够近了。月亮的引力确凿无疑地改变了我们的航向，但凭借我们的速度，我们从它的魔爪下溜了出来。此刻我们正飞驰着远离它——可去往何方呢？

===Original===
The nearest star, Alpha Centauri, is twenty-five and a half million million miles from earth. Write that on your typewriter﻿—25,500,000,000,000 miles. But why trifle with short distances like this? There was little likelihood that I should visit Alpha Centauri with all the wide range of space at my command and many more interesting places to go. I knew that I had ample room in which to wander, since science has calculated the diameter of space to be eighty-four thousand million light years, which, when one reflects that light travels at the rate of one hundred eighty-six thousand miles a second, should satisfy the wanderlust of the most inveterate roamer.

===Chinese===
离地球最近的恒星，半人马座α星（Alpha Centauri），距地球二百五十五亿亿英里。这个数字打在打字机上便是——25,500,000,000,000 英里。可何必拿这样「短」的距离来凑趣呢？太空如此辽阔，可去之处如此之多，我跑到半人马座α星去的可能性微乎其微。我知道自己有充裕的空间可供游荡——科学已算出宇宙的直径为八百四十亿光年，而光每秒可行十八万六千英里，细想之下，这点空间应当足以满足最嗜漂泊的浪子的远游之欲了。

===Original===
However, I was not greatly concerned with any of these distances, as I had food and water for only a year, during which time the torpedo might travel slightly more than three hundred fifteen million miles. Even if it reached our near neighbor, Alpha Centauri, I should not then be greatly interested in the event, as I should have been dead for over eighty thousand years. Such is the immensity of the universe!
During the next twenty-four hours the course of the torpedo nearly paralleled the Moon’s orbit around the earth. Not only had the pull of the Moon deflected its course, but now it seemed evident that the earth had seized us and that we were doomed to race through eternity around her, a tiny, second satellite. But I did not wish to be a moon, certainly not an insignificant moon that in all probability might not be picked up by even the largest telescope.

===Chinese===
然而，我对这些距离都不怎么上心，因为我的饮食只够支撑一年，而一年里火箭充其量也只能飞三亿一千五百万英里出头。纵然它当真抵达了我们的近邻半人马座α星，那时我也不会有多大兴趣——因为我早已死了八万多年了。宇宙之大，一至于斯！
其后二十四小时，火箭的航线几乎与月亮绕地球的轨道平行。不仅月亮的引力使航线偏转，如今看来，地球显然也已攫住了我们，我们注定要绕着它永世飞驰，做它一颗小小的第二卫星。可我不想做月亮，更不想做一颗连最大的望远镜都未必能发现的无名小卫星。

===Original===
The next month was the most trying of my life. It seems the height of egotism even to mention my life in the face of the stupendous cosmic forces that engulfed it; but it was the only life I had and I was fond of it, and the more imminent seemed the moment when it should be snuffed out, the better I liked it.
At the end of the second day it was quite apparent that we had eluded the grip of the earth. I cannot say that I was elated at the discovery. My plan to visit Mars was ruined. I should have been glad to return to earth. If I could have landed safely on Mars, I certainly could have landed safely on earth. But there was another reason why I should have been glad to have returned to earth, a reason that loomed, large and terrible, ahead﻿—the Sun. We were heading straight for the Sun now. Once in the grip of that mighty power, nothing could affect our destiny; we were doomed. For three months I must await the inevitable end, before plunging into that fiery furnace. Furnace is an inadequate word by which to suggest the Sun’s heat, which is reputedly from thirty to sixty million degrees at the center, a fact which should not have concerned me greatly, since I did not anticipate reaching the center.

===Chinese===
接下来的一个月，是我有生以来最难熬的日子。在吞噬我生命的浩瀚宇宙伟力面前，竟然还提起「我的生命」，似乎自大到了极点；可这是我仅有的一条命，而我爱惜它，越是它行将被掐灭的那一刻迫近，我反而越觉得它可爱。
到第二天结束时，事情已相当清楚：我们逃出了地球的掌控。我不能说这一发现让我欣喜若狂。我飞往火星的计划已然泡汤。我本该乐意重返地球的。既然我能安全降落在火星上，自然也能安全降落在地球上。可我之所以愿意回到地球，还有另一层缘故，一个在前方赫然耸立、庞大而可怖的缘故——太阳。此刻我们正径直朝着太阳飞去。一旦落入那股伟力的掌控，再没什么能左右我们的命运了；我们完了。在坠入那座熔炉之前，我还要等上三个月，等候那不可避免的结局。用「熔炉」来形容太阳的酷热是远远不够的——据说太阳中心的温度高达三千万到六千万度，不过这一点倒也不该让我太操心，因为我并不指望能到达它的中心。

===Original===
The days dragged on, or, I should say, the long night﻿—there were no days, other than the record that I kept of the passing hours. I read a great deal. I made no entries in the log. Why write something that was presently to be plunged into the Sun and consumed? I experimented in the galley, attempting fancy cooking. I ate a great deal; it helped to pass the time away, and I enjoyed my meals.
On the thirtieth day I was scanning space ahead when I saw a gorgeous, shimmering crescent far to the right of our course; but I must confess that I was not greatly interested in sights of any sort. In sixty days I should be in the Sun. Long before that, however, the increasing heat would have destroyed me. The end was approaching rapidly.

===Chinese===
日子一天天拖过去——或者说，是那漫长的黑夜拖着，因为除了我记录流逝时辰的账本之外，再没有白昼可言。我读了许多书。我没有在日志上写任何东西。何必写下一些转眼就要被投进太阳烧成灰的东西呢？我在厨房里搞起试验，试着做点花样的菜。我吃了许多东西；这能帮着打发时间，而且我吃得津津有味。
到了第三十天，我扫视前方的太空，看见我们航线右侧很远的地方有一弯瑰丽、闪烁的月牙；但我必须承认，我对任何景象都已提不起兴致。再过六十天，我就要葬身太阳了。不过在那之前很久，日益炽烈的高温便已把我毁掉了。终点正在飞快逼近。

## III Rushing Toward Venus / III 飞向金星

===Original===
The psychological effects of an experience such as that through which I had been passing must be considerable, and even though they could be neither weighed nor measured, I was yet conscious of changes that had taken place in me because of them. For thirty days I had been racing alone through space toward absolute annihilation, toward an end that would probably not leave a single nucleus of the atoms that compose me an electron to carry on with, I had experienced the ultimate in solitude, and the result had been to deaden my sensibilities; doubtless a wise provision of nature.
Even the realization that the splendid crescent, looming enormously off the starboard bow of the torpedo, was Venus failed to excite me greatly. What if I were to approach Venus more closely than any other human being of all time! It meant nothing. Were I to see God, himself, even that would mean nothing. It became apparent that the value of what we see is measurable only by the size of our prospective audience. Whatever I saw, who might never have an audience, was without value.

===Chinese===
我所经历的这番体验，其心理影响想必相当可观；纵使无法称量、无从测度，我仍觉察到它在我身上引发的种种变化。整整三十天，我独自一人在太空中疾驰，奔向彻底的湮灭——奔向一个很可能连构成我躯体的原子核都不剩一个电子去延续的结局。我尝到了孤独的极致，其结果便是我的感官变得迟钝；这无疑是大自然一项明智的安排。
就连那轮壮丽的新月——它巨大无比地悬在鱼雷右舷前方——我意识到那是金星，也未能令我有多大兴奋。纵使我即将比有史以来任何人都更贴近金星，又当如何！那毫无意义。就算我亲眼见到上帝他老人家，那也毫无意义。事情变得很明显：我们所见之物的价值，唯有以我们预期听众的规模来衡量。无论我看见什么，而我这个人也许永远不会有一个听众，那都是没有价值的。

===Original===
Nevertheless, more to pass away the time than because I was particularly interested in the subject, I began to make some rough calculations. These indicated that I was about eight hundred sixty-five thousand miles from the orbit of Venus and that I should cross it in about twenty-four hours. I could not, however, compute my present distance from the planet accurately. I only knew that it appeared very close. When I say close, I mean relatively. The earth was some twenty-five million miles away, the Sun about sixty-eight million, so that an object as large as Venus, at a distance of one or two million miles, appeared close.
As Venus travels in her orbit at the rate of nearly twenty-two miles per second, or over one million six hundred thousand miles in a terrestrial day, it appeared evident to me that she would cross my path some time within the next twenty-four hours.
It occurred to me that, passing closely, as was unavoidable, she might deflect the course of the torpedo and save me from the Sun; but I knew this to be a vain hope. Undoubtedly, the path of the torpedo would be bent, but the Sun would not relinquish his prey. With these thoughts, my apathy returned, and I lost interest in Venus.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，与其说是对这题目特别上心，不如说是为了打发时间，我动手做了些粗略的计算。结果表明：我距金星轨道约八十六万五千英里，大约二十四小时后便会越过它。然而，我无法精确算出此刻与行星本体的距离。我只知道它看上去很近。我说近，是相对而言。地球远在大约两千五百万英里之外，太阳约六千八百万英里，因而在一两百万英里之外一个金星那般大小的天体，便显得近了。
金星沿其轨道运行的速度将近每秒二十二英里，也就是地球一昼夜要走过一百六十多万英里；由此看来，她显然会在未来二十四小时之内的某一刻横越我的航线。
我转念一想：既然不可避免地要从她近旁掠过，她或许能使鱼雷偏转航向，把我从太阳手里救下；可我明白这只是徒然之望。鱼雷的路径无疑会被拽弯，但太阳绝不会松开他的猎物。想到这里，我的漠然重又涌上心头，对金星也失了兴致。

===Original===
Selecting a book, I lay down on my bed to read. The interior of the cabin was brightly illuminated. I am extravagant with electricity. I have the means of generating it for eleven more months; but I shall not need it after a few weeks, so why should I be parsimonious?
I read for a few hours, but as reading in bed always makes me sleepy, I eventually succumbed. When I awoke, I lay for a few minutes in luxurious ease. I might be racing toward extinction at the rate of thirty-six thousand miles an hour, but I, myself, was unhurried. I recalled the beautiful spectacle that Venus had presented when I had last observed her and decided to have another look at her. Stretching languorously, I arose and stepped to one of the starboard portholes.

===Chinese===
我挑了一本书，躺到床上翻看。舱内灯火通明。我在用电上头是挥霍无度的。我还有办法继续发电十一个月；可再过几个星期我就用不着它了，又何必小气？
我读了几小时，可卧床读书总让我犯困，我终究还是败给了睡意。醒来时，我慵懒惬意地躺了几分钟。我也许正以每小时三万六千英里的速度奔向灭亡，可我本人却从容不迫。我回想起上一次观测时金星呈现的那番美景，决意再去看她一眼。我懒洋洋地伸了个懒腰，起身走到右舷的一扇舷窗前。

===Original===
The picture framed by the casing of that circular opening was gorgeous beyond description. Apparently less than half as far away as before, and twice as large, loomed the mass of Venus outlined by an aureole of light where the Sun, behind her, illuminated her cloudy envelope and lighted to burning brilliance a thin crescent along the edge nearest me.
I looked at my watch. Twelve hours had passed since I first discovered the planet, and now, at last, I became excited. Venus was apparently half as far away as it had been twelve hours ago, and I knew that the torpedo had covered half the distance that had separated us from her orbit at that time. A collision was possible, it even seemed within the range of probability that I should be dashed to the surface of this inhospitable, lifeless world.
Well, what of it? Am I not already doomed? What difference can it make to me if the end comes a few weeks sooner than I had anticipated? Yet I was excited. I cannot say that I felt fear. I have no fear of death﻿—that left me when my mother died; but now that the great adventure loomed so close I was overwhelmed by contemplation of it and the great wonder that it induced. What would follow?

===Chinese===
那圆形窗框所框住的一幅图景，华美得难以言表。金星那巨大的星体赫然可见——显然不到原先一半的距离，个头却大了一倍——其轮廓镶着一圈光环，那是身后的太阳照亮了她云雾似的外衣，又把她朝我这一侧边缘上一弯薄薄的新月点燃成灼目的辉煌。
我看了看表。自我初次发现这颗行星起，已过去了十二个小时，如今，我终于兴奋起来。金星显然只及十二小时前一半的距离，我由此可知鱼雷已驶完那时我与她轨道之间相隔路程的一半。碰撞是可能的，甚至看来很可能——我将被摔上这个不宜居、无生机的世界的表面。
那么，又当如何？我岂非早已在劫难逃？结局比我所预期的早来几个星期，于我又有何分别？然而我确实兴奋。我不能说自己感到了恐惧。我不怕死——那份恐惧在母亲去世时便离我而去了；可如今这伟大的冒险已迫在眉睫，我竟被对它的冥想、被它所唤起的巨大惊奇压倒了。接下来会发生什么？

===Original===
The long hours dragged on. It seemed incredible to me, accustomed though I am to thinking in units of terrific speed, that the torpedo and Venus were racing toward the same point in her orbit at such inconceivable velocities, the one at the rate of thirty-six thousand miles per hour, the other at over sixty-seven thousand.
It was now becoming difficult to view the planet through the side port, as she moved steadily closer and closer to our path. I went to the periscope﻿—she was gliding majestically within its range. I knew that at that moment the torpedo was less than thirty-six thousand miles, less than an hour, from the path of the planet’s orbit, and there could be no doubt now but that she had already seized us in her grasp. We were destined to make a clean hit. Even under the circumstances I could not restrain a smile at the thought of the marksmanship that this fact revealed. I had aimed at Mars and was about to hit Venus; unquestionably the all-time cosmic record for poor shots.

===Chinese===
漫长的时辰缓缓流逝。尽管我惯于以惊人的速度为单位来思考，鱼雷与金星正以如此不可思议的速度朝着她轨道上的同一点竞速飞驰——前者时速三万六千英里，后者逾六万七千英里——这于我仍像是难以置信的事。
如今要从侧舷窗观察这颗行星已越来越难，因为她正稳步地越来越贴近我们的航线。我走向潜望镜——她正庄严地滑入镜中视野。我明白此刻鱼雷距行星轨道已不足三万六千英里，不足一小时之程，且毫无疑问，她已把我们攥在了掌中。我们注定要正中目标。即便在此情形下，一想到这桩事实所揭示的枪法，我仍不禁莞尔。我瞄准的是火星，却即将命中金星；这无疑是亘古宇宙以来最糟糕的一枪。

===Original===
Even though I did not shrink from death, even though the world’s best astronomers have assured us that Venus must be unfitted to support human life, that where her surface is not unutterably hot it is unutterably cold, even though she be oxygenless, as they aver, yet the urge to live that is born with each of us compelled me to make the same preparations to land that I should have had I successfully reached my original goal, Mars.
Slipping into a fleece-lined suit of coveralls, I donned goggles and a fleece-lined helmet; then I adjusted the oxygen tank that was designed to hang in front of me, lest it foul the parachute, and which can be automatically jettisoned in the event that I reach an atmosphere that will support life, for it would be an awkward and dangerous appendage to be cumbered with while landing. Finally, I adjusted my chute.
I glanced at my watch. If my calculations have been correct, we should strike in about fifteen minutes. Once more I returned to the periscope.

===Chinese===
纵使我不畏惧死亡，纵使世上最优秀的天文学家们都断言金星必不适宜人类生存，她的表面非极度炎热即极度苦寒，纵使如他们所言她全无氧气，可那种与生俱来的求生本能仍驱使我做出与当初顺利抵达原定目标火星时同样的着陆准备。
我套上一身内有绒衬的连体工装，戴上护目镜和绒衬头盔；接着调好那只氧气罐——它设计成挂在我身前的样式，免得缠住降落伞，一旦我进入可供呼吸的大气层，它便能被自动抛掉，因为带着这么个累赘着陆既笨拙又危险。最后，我整理好降落伞。
我瞥了一眼表。倘若我的计算无误，我们大约十五分钟后撞击。我再度回到潜望镜前。

===Original===
The sight that met my eyes was awe inspiring. We were plunging toward a billowing mass of black clouds. It was like chaos on the dawn of creation. The gravitation of the planet had seized us. The floor of the cabin was no longer beneath me﻿—I was standing on the forward bulkhead now; but this condition I had anticipated when I designed the torpedo. We were diving nose on toward the planet. In space there had been neither up nor down, but now there was a very definite down.
From where I stood I could reach the controls, and beside me was the door in the side of the torpedo. I released three batteries of parachutes and opened the door in the wall of the inner torpedo. There was a noticeable jar, as though the parachutes had opened and temporarily checked the speed of the torpedo. This must mean that I had entered an atmosphere of some description and that there was not a second to waste.

===Chinese===
映入眼帘的景象令人敬畏。我们正朝着一团翻涌的黑色云海俯冲而下。那宛如创世之初的混沌。行星的引力已攫住了我们。舱底不再在我脚下——此刻我站在前舱壁上；但这种情形，我在设计鱼雷时便已预料到了。我们正箭头朝下向行星俯冲。在太空中本无上下之分，可如今却有了非常明确的「下」。
从我站着的位置能够得着操纵装置，身旁便是鱼雷侧面的舱门。我放开三组降落伞，又打开了内层鱼雷壁上的门。传来一阵明显的震动，仿佛降落伞已经张开，暂时拽住了鱼雷的速度。这必然意味着我已进入某种大气层，一刻也耽搁不得。

===Original===
With a single movement of a lever I loosed the remaining parachutes; then I turned to the outer door. Its bolts were controlled by a large wheel set in the center of the door and were geared to open quickly and with ease. I adjusted the mouthpiece of the oxygen line across my lips and quickly spun the wheel.
Simultaneously the door flew open and the air pressure within the torpedo shot me out into space. My right hand grasped the rip cord of my chute; but I waited. I looked about for the torpedo. It was racing almost parallel with me, all its parachutes distended above it. Just an instant’s glimpse I had of it, and then it dove into the cloud mass and was lost to view; but what a weirdly magnificent spectacle it had presented in that brief instant!

===Chinese===
我把操纵杆一推，放出了剩余的降落伞；随即转向外层舱门。门上的插销由门中央一个大轮盘控制，传动机构使其能迅速而省力地开启。我把输氧管衔在唇间，迅速旋动轮盘。
门应声弹开，鱼雷内的气压把我喷射到了太空之中。我右手紧攥着降落伞的开伞索；可我等着。我四下张望寻找鱼雷。它几乎与我平行疾驰，所有的降落伞在它上方撑得满满的。我只瞥见了它一瞬，它便一头扎进云团，消失不见；可就在那一刹那，它呈现了一幅何等奇诡壮丽的景象！

===Original===
Safe now from any danger of fouling with the torpedo, I jerked the rip cord of my parachute just as the clouds swallowed me. Through my fleece-lined suit I felt the bitter cold; like a dash of ice water the cold clouds slapped me in the face; then, to my relief, the chute opened, and I fell more slowly.
Down, down, down I dropped. I could not even guess the duration, nor the distance. It was very dark and very wet, like sinking into the depths of the ocean without feeling the pressure of the water. My thoughts during those long moments were such as to baffle description. Perhaps the oxygen made me a little drunk; I do not know. I felt exhilarated and intensely eager to solve the great mystery beneath me. The thought that I was about to die did not concern me so much as what I might see before I died. I was about to land on Venus﻿—the first human being in all the world to see the face of the veiled planet.

===Chinese===
如今已无与鱼雷缠绕之虞，我赶在被云团吞没的一瞬猛拉开伞索。隔着绒衬的工装，我感到刺骨的严寒；冰冷的云雾像一泼冰水般抽在我脸上；继而，令我如释重负的是，降落伞张开了，我下落得慢了些。
我坠落、坠落、坠落。我连时间多久、距离多远都无从揣测。四周漆黑而潮湿，宛如沉入海洋深处却感受不到水的压力。在那漫长的时刻里，我心头的思绪难以言表。也许是氧气让我有些微醺；我不晓得。我感到一阵振奋，急切地想揭开身下那个巨大奥秘。我即将死去这一念头，倒不如我临死前可能看见的景象更叫我挂心。我即将踏上金星——全世界有史以来第一个得见这颗蒙面行星真容的人。

===Original===
Suddenly I emerged into a cloudless space; but far below me were what appeared in the darkness to be more clouds, recalling to my mind the often advanced theory of the two cloud envelopes of Venus. As I descended, the temperature rose gradually, but it was still cold.
As I entered the second cloud bank, there was a very noticeable rise in temperature the farther I fell. I shut off the oxygen supply and tried breathing through my nose. By inhaling deeply I discovered that I could take in sufficient oxygen to support life, and an astronomical theory was shattered. Hope flared within me like a beacon on a fog-hid landing field.

===Chinese===
忽然我钻入一片无云的空间；可在我身下远处，黑暗中显出的仿佛又是云层，这让我想起常被人提起的「金星双云层」理论。我继续下降，气温缓缓上升，可仍带着寒意。
进入第二层云带之后，我落得越深，气温的上升就越发明显。我关掉氧气，试着用鼻子呼吸。我发觉只要深深吸气，便能吸入足以维持生命的氧气——一项天文学理论就此被打破。希望在我心中燃起，宛如浓雾笼罩的机场上一盏灯塔。

===Original===
As I floated gently downward, I presently became aware of a faint luminosity far below. What could it be? There were many obvious reasons why it could not be sunlight; sunlight would not come from below, and, furthermore, it was night on this hemisphere of the planet. Naturally many weird conjectures raced through my mind. I wondered if this could be the light from an incandescent world, but immediately discarded that explanation as erroneous, knowing that the heat from an incandescent world would long since have consumed me. Then it occurred to me that it might be refracted light from that portion of the cloud envelope illuminated by the Sun, yet if such were the case, it seemed obvious that the clouds about me should be luminous, which they were not.

===Chinese===
我悠悠地往下飘，不多时便察觉身下远处有一抹微光。那会是什么？有许多明显的理由说明它不可能是阳光：阳光不会来自下方；况且在这颗行星的此半球此刻正是黑夜。自然，种种离奇的猜测掠过我心头。我猜想这会不会是一个白热世界所发的光，可旋即否定了这个解释——因为一个白热世界的热度早就把我烤成灰了。继而我想到，它或许是云层外衣中被太阳照亮的那部分折射下来的光；可倘若如此，我身边的云团理应也是明亮的，然而它们并不。

===Original===
There seemed only one practical solution. It was the solution that an earth man would naturally arrive at. Being what I am, a highly civilized creature from a world already far advanced by science and invention, I attributed the source of this light to these twin forces of superior intelligence. I could only account for that faint glow by attributing it to the reflection upon the under side of the cloud mass of artificial light produced by intelligent creatures upon the surface of this world toward which I was slowly settling.
I wondered what these beings would be like, and if my excitement grew as I anticipated the wonders that were soon to be revealed to my eyes, I believe that it was a pardonable excitement, under the circumstances. Upon the threshold of such an adventure who would not have been moved to excitement by contemplation of the experiences awaiting him?

===Chinese===
看来只有一个说得通的解释。那是一个地球人自然而然会得出的解释。身为我自己这样一个人——一个来自已被科学与发明推向高度文明的世界的、高度文明的生物——我把这光源归于优越智力的这两股孪生之力。那抹微光，我只能这样来解释：在我缓缓降落下去的这个世界表面上，有智慧的生灵制造了人造光，那光反射到云团的底面。
我暗自揣测这些生灵会是何模样，而当我预期着即将展现在眼前的种种奇景时，我的兴奋若是有增无减，我相信在此情形下那是情有可原的。站在这样一场冒险的门槛上，谁不会因思量着等着自己的种种经历而心潮澎湃呢？

===Original===
Now I removed the mouthpiece of the oxygen tube entirely and found that I could breathe easily. The light beneath me was increasing gradually. About me I thought I saw vague, dark shapes among the cloud masses. Shadows, perhaps, but of what? I detached the oxygen tank and let it fall. I distinctly heard it strike something an instant after I had released it. Then a shadow loomed darkly beneath me, and an instant later my feet struck something that gave beneath them.
I dropped into a mass of foliage and grasped wildly for support. A moment later I began to fall more rapidly and guessed what had happened; the parachute had been up-tilted by contact with the foliage. I clutched at leaves and branches, fruitlessly, and then I was brought to a sudden stop; evidently the chute had fouled something. I hoped that it would hold until I found a secure resting place.

===Chinese===
这时我索性把输氧管衔嘴整个拿开，发觉自己能够自如呼吸。身下的光一点一点亮起来。我似乎看见云团之间有模糊的黑色形体。大概是影子吧，可是什么的影子？我解下氧气罐，任它坠落。我清楚地听见它在脱手后一瞬撞上了什么东西。接着一团黑影在我身下赫然现出，转眼之间我的双脚便踩上了某个在脚下让了一让的东西。
我一头扎进一团枝叶之中，拼命伸手抓握支撑。片刻之后我开始坠落得更快，猜到了是怎么回事：降落伞被枝叶顶得翘了起来。我胡乱抓扯叶子和枝桠，徒劳无功，接着我骤然被拉住；显然是伞缠住了什么东西。我盼望它能撑住，直到我寻得一个安稳的落脚处。

===Original===
As I groped about in the dark, my hand finally located a sturdy branch, and a moment later I was astride it, my back to the bole of a large tree﻿—another theory gone the ignoble path of countless predecessors; it was evident that there was vegetation on Venus. At least there was one tree; I could vouch for that, as I was sitting in it, and doubtless the black shadows I had passed were other, taller trees.
Having found secure lodgment, I divested myself of my parachute after salvaging some of its ropes and the straps from the harness, which I thought I might find helpful in descending the tree. Starting at the top of a tree, in darkness and among clouds, one may not be positive what the tree is like nearer the ground. I also removed my goggles. Then I commenced to descend. The girth of the tree was enormous, but the branches grew sufficiently close together to permit me to find safe footing.

===Chinese===
我在黑暗中摸索，手终于摸到一根粗壮的枝桠，转眼我便跨坐其上，背靠着大树的树干——又一项理论步上了无数前驱者的不体面之路：金星上显然有植被。至少有一棵树；这一点我可以担保，因为我正坐在它身上，而方才我掠过的那些黑影，无疑是一棵棵更高的树。
既已寻得稳固的栖身之处，我便卸下了降落伞，不过事先留了几根伞绳和背带上的带子，心想它们或许有助于我下树。从树顶开始，身处黑暗与云雾之中，谁也不能断定这棵树越往下是什么模样。我也摘掉了护目镜。然后我开始往下攀。这棵树粗壮异常，但枝桠长得足够紧密，让我能找到稳当的落脚处。

===Original===
I did not know how far I had fallen through the second cloud stratum before I lodged in the tree nor how far I had descended the tree, but all together it must have been close to two thousand feet; yet I was still in the clouds. Could the entire atmosphere of Venus be forever fog laden? I hoped not, for it was a dreary prospect.
The light from below had increased a little as I descended, but not much; it was still dark about me. I continued to descend. It was tiresome work and not without danger, this climbing down an unfamiliar tree in a fog, at night, toward an unknown world. But I could not remain where I was, and there was nothing above to entice me upward; so I continued to descend.

===Chinese===
我不知道自己在落入树中之前穿过第二层云掉了多远，也不知道自己沿树下了多远，但两者相加想必已近两千英尺；然而我仍在云雾之中。难道金星的整个大气层永远笼罩在雾里？我但愿不是，因为那前景实在凄凉。
我往下时身下的光略微亮了些，但不多；我四周依旧昏暗。我继续往下攀。在雾中、在夜里、沿一棵陌生的树攀向一个未知的世界——这活儿既乏味又不无危险。可我不能待在原地，上头也并无诱我攀回的东西；于是我继续往下。

===Original===
What a strange trick fate had played me. I had wanted to visit Venus, but had discarded the idea when assured by my astronomer friends that the planet could not support either animal or vegetable life. I had started for Mars, and now, fully ten days before I had hoped to reach the red planet, I was on Venus, breathing perfectly good air among the branches of a tree that evidently dwarfed the giant Sequoias.
The illumination was increasing rapidly now, the clouds were thinning; through breaks I caught glimpses far below, glimpses of what appeared to be an endless vista of foliage, softly moonlit﻿—but Venus had no moon. In that, insofar as the seeming moonlight was concerned, I could fully concur with the astronomers. This illumination came from no moon, unless Venus’s satellite lay beneath her inner envelope of clouds, which was preposterous.

===Chinese===
命运跟我开了一个多么离奇的玩笑。我曾想造访金星，可当天文学界的朋友们向我保证这颗行星无法承载任何动植物生命时，我便打消了念头。我本是奔着火星去的，而如今，比我所盼望抵达那颗红色行星的时间足足早了十天，我却在金星上，在一棵显然使地球巨杉相形见绌的大树的枝桠间，呼吸着再好不过的空气。
此刻光照迅速增强，云层渐薄；透过云隙我瞥见身下极远处，瞥见了仿佛无边无际的连绵树冠，沐浴在柔和的月光中——可是金星没有月亮。在这一点上，就那似有若无的月光而言，我完全赞同天文学家们。这光并非来自任何月亮，除非金星的卫星藏在她内层云幕之下，而那是荒谬的。

===Original===
A moment later I emerged entirely from the cloud bank, but though I searched in all directions, I saw nothing but foliage, above, around, below me, yet I could see far down into that abyss of leaves. In the soft light I could not determine the color of the foliage, but I was sure that it was not green; it was some light, delicate shade of another color.
I had descended another thousand feet since I had emerged from the clouds, and I was pretty well exhausted (the month of inactivity and overeating had softened me), when I saw just below me what appeared to be a causeway leading from the tree I was descending to another adjacent. I also discovered that from just below where I clung the limbs had been cut away from the tree to a point below the causeway. Here were two startling and unequivocal evidences of the presence of intelligent beings. Venus was inhabited! But by what? What strange, arboreal creatures built causeways high among these giant trees? Were they a species of monkey-man? Were they of a high or low order of intelligence? How would they receive me?

===Chinese===
不多时我整个钻出了云层，可纵使我四处张望，所见唯有枝叶——上下左右全是我，然而我能望见那片叶子深渊下头很远的地方。在柔光之中我辨不出枝叶的颜色，但我确信它不是绿的；它是另一种浅淡、轻柔的色调。
自钻出云层以来我又下了约莫一千英尺，已相当疲惫（一个月的无所事事和暴饮暴食让我身子发软），就在这时，我看见身下正下方仿佛有一条栈道，从我攀着的那棵树通往相邻的另一棵。我还发觉，就在我紧贴之处往下，这棵树的枝桠被人砍掉，一直砍到栈道之下。这是智慧生灵存在的两项惊人而确凿的证据。金星上有人居住！可是些什么样的人？是些什么样奇异的、营树栖的生灵，在这些巨树高处搭起栈道？他们是某种猿人吗？他们的智慧是高是低？他们会怎样对待我？

===Original===
At this juncture in my vain speculations I was startled by a noise above me. Something was moving in the branches overhead. The sound was coming nearer, and it seemed to me that it was being made by something of considerable size and weight, but perhaps, I realized, that conjecture was the child of my imagination. However, I felt most uncomfortable. I was unarmed. I have never carried weapons. My friends had urged a perfect arsenal upon me before I embarked upon my adventure, but I had argued that if I arrived on Mars unarmed it would be prima facie evidence of my friendly intentions, and even if my reception were warlike, I should be no worse off, since I could not hope, single-handed, to conquer a world, no matter how well armed I were.
Suddenly, above me, to the crashing of some heavy body through the foliage were added hideous screams and snarls; and in the terrifying dissonance I recognized the presence of more than a single creature. Was I being pursued by all the fearsome denizens of this Venusan forest!

===Chinese===
正当我徒然胡乱揣测之际，头顶一阵声响惊得我一哆嗦。有东西正在上方的枝桠间移动。那声音越来越近，我觉得像是个颇大颇重的东西弄出来的，不过我随即意识到，这也许只是我想象的产物。然而我感到极不自在。我手无寸铁。我从不携带武器。朋友们在我启程冒险前曾硬塞给我整整一座军火库，我却辩称：若我手无寸铁地抵达火星，那便是我善意相向的明证；即便对方以兵戎相迎，我的处境也不会更糟，因为单枪匹马，无论武装多么精良，也别想征服一整个世界。
忽然，头顶之上，重型身躯冲撞枝叶的哗啦声之外，又添上了可怖的尖叫与狺狺；在那骇人的嘈杂之中，我辨出不止一只野兽的声息。莫非这金星森林里所有令人胆寒的居民都在追捕我！

===Original===
Perhaps my nerves were slightly unstrung; and who may blame them if they were, after what I had passed through so recently and during the long, preceding month? They were not entirely shattered, however, and I could still appreciate the fact that night noises often multiply themselves in a most disconcerting way. I have heard coyotes yapping and screaming around my camp on Arizona nights when, but for the actual knowledge that there were but one or two of them, I could have sworn that there were a hundred, had I trusted only to my sense of hearing.
But in this instance I was quite positive that the voices of more than a single beast were mingling to produce the horrid din that, together with the sound of their passage, was definitely and unquestionably drawing rapidly nearer me. Of course I did not know that the owners of those awesome voices were pursuing me, though a still, small voice within seemed to be assuring me that such was the fact.
I wished that I might reach the causeway below me (I should feel better standing squarely on two feet), but it was too far to drop and there were no more friendly branches to give me support; then I thought of the ropes I had salvaged from the abandoned parachute. Quickly uncoiling them from about my waist, I looped one of them over the branch upon which I sat, grasped both strands firmly in my hands, and prepared to swing from my porch. Suddenly the screams and snarling growls ceased; and then, close above me now, I heard the noise of something descending toward me and saw the branches shaking to its weight.

===Chinese===
也许我的神经稍有些失常；可经历了最近这些事、又熬过之前漫长的整整一个月，谁又能怪它呢？不过它们并未全然崩溃，我仍能体认到这样一个事实：夜里的声响常常以一种最叫人不安的方式放大自身。我曾在亚利桑那的夜里听过郊狼围着营地又吠又叫，若非确切知道只有一两只，单凭听觉我定会发誓有一百只之多。
但这一次我相当肯定，不止一只野兽的嗓音混在一起，制造出那片可怖的喧嚣，而那喧嚣连同它们行动的声响，正明确无疑地迅速逼近我。当然我并不确知那些骇人嗓音的主人在追我，尽管内心深处一个细微的声音似乎在向我保证：事实正是如此。
