我巴不得能落到身下那条栈道上（双脚稳稳地踩着，我会安心些），可那距离跳下去太远，又再无友善的枝桠给我支撑；这时我想起了从弃用的降落伞上留下的绳索。我赶紧把它们从腰间解开，把其中一根绕过我坐着的枝桠甩过去，双手紧紧攥住两股绳，准备从我这「门廊」上荡下去。骤然间，尖叫和狺狺的嗥叫停了；紧接着，就在我头顶近处，我听见有东西朝我降下来的动静，并看见枝桠被它的重量压得直晃。

===Original===
Lowering my body from the branch, I swung downward and slid the fifteen or more feet to the causeway, and as I alighted the silence of the great forest was again shattered by a hideous scream just above my head. Looking up quickly, I saw a creature launching itself toward me and just beyond it a snarling face of utter hideousness. I caught but the briefest glimpse of it﻿—just enough to see that it was a face, with eyes and a mouth﻿—then it was withdrawn amidst the foliage.
Perhaps I only sensed that hideous vision subconsciously at the time, for the whole scene was but a flash upon the retina of my eye, and the other beast was in midair above me at the instant; but it remained indelibly impressed upon my memory, and I was to recall it upon a later day under circumstances so harrowing that the mind of mortal earth man may scarce conceive them.
As I leaped back to avoid the creature springing upon me, I still clung to one strand of the rope down which I had lowered myself to the causeway. My grasp upon the rope was unconscious and purely mechanical; it was in my hand, and my fist was clenched; and as I leaped away, I dragged the rope with me. A fortuitous circumstance, no doubt, but a most fortunate one.

===Chinese===
我把身子从枝桠上放下去，往下一荡，滑了十五英尺有余落到栈道上；我落地的当口，巨林的寂静再度被头顶正上方一声可怖的尖叫击碎。我赶紧抬头，只见一只野兽正朝我扑来，它身后是一张狺狺的、丑陋至极的脸。我不过瞥了它极短的一瞬——只够看出那是一张脸，有眼睛有嘴——它便缩回了枝叶之中。
或许当时我只是下意识地感应到了那副可怖的模样，因为整个场景不过是落在视网膜上的一闪，而另一只野兽此刻正凌空在我头顶；可它却不可磨灭地烙在了我的记忆里，日后我将在一种凡人难以想象的惊心动魄的情形下重新记起它。
我向后一跃以躲开扑向我的野兽，手里仍攥着我荡下栈道时用的那根绳的一股。我攥着绳子是无意识的、纯属机械的动作；它就在我手里，我的拳头攥得紧紧的；我跃开时，便把绳也一并拖了过去。这无疑是个偶然，却是个极其幸运的偶然。

===Original===
The creature missed me, alighting on all fours a few feet from me, and there it crouched, apparently slightly bewildered, and, fortunately for me, it did not immediately charge, giving me the opportunity to collect my wits and back slowly away, at the same time mechanically coiling the rope in my right hand. The little, simple things one does in moments of stress or excitement often seem entirely beyond reason and incapable of explanation; but I have thought that they may be dictated by a subconscious mind reacting to the urge of self-preservation. Possibly they are not always well directed and may as often fail to be of service as not, but then it may be possible that subconscious minds are no less fallible than the objective mind, which is wrong far more often than it is right. I cannot but seek for some explanation of the urge that caused me to retain that rope, since, all unknown to me, it was to be the slender thread upon which my life was to hang.
Silence had again descended upon the weird scene. Since the final scream of the hideous creature that had retreated into the foliage after this thing had leaped for me, there had been no sound. The creature that crouched facing me seemed slightly bewildered. I am positive now that it had not been pursuing me, but that it itself had been the object of pursuit by the other beast that had retreated.

===Chinese===
那野兽扑了个空，四脚着地落在我几英尺外，蹲伏下来，似乎有些发懵；而于我有幸的是，它并未立刻发起攻击，给了我机会定下神来，缓缓后退，同时右手机械地把绳子绕成一卷。人在紧张或激动的时刻所做的那些细小、简单的举动，往往全然不合情理、无从解释；可我总觉得，它们或许是潜意识在求生本能的驱使下作出的反应。这些反应未必总是得当，也许往往成事不足；不过潜意识未必不比显意识更易犯错——后者错的时候远比对的时候多。我不由得想为促使我留下那根绳子的冲动找些解释，因为彼时全然不知情的我并不晓得，它将是悬系我性命的一根细线。
诡异的场景再度归于寂静。自从那只丑陋的野兽——在我扑来的这只之后缩回枝叶中的那一只——发出最后一声尖叫以来，再无声息。蹲伏着与我对峙的那只野兽似乎有些发懵。如今我确信，它并非一直在追我，相反，它自己才是那只退去的野兽所追捕的对象。

===Original===
In the dim half-light of the Venusan night I saw confronting me a creature that might be conjured only in the half-delirium of some horrid nightmare. It was about as large as a full-grown puma, and stood upon four handlike feet that suggested that it might be almost wholly arboreal. The front legs were much longer than the hind, suggesting, in this respect, the hyena; but here the similarity ceased, for the creature’s furry pelt was striped longitudinally with alternate bands of red and yellow, and its hideous head bore no resemblance to any earthly animal. No external ears were visible, and in the low forehead was a single large, round eye at the end of a thick antenna about four inches long. The jaws were powerful and armed with long, sharp fangs, while from either side of the neck projected a powerful chela. Never have I seen a creature so fearsomely armed for offense as was this nameless beast of another world. With those powerful crablike pincers it could easily have held an opponent far stronger than a man and dragged it to those terrible jaws.

===Chinese===
在金星之夜昏暗的微光里，我看见与我相对的那只野兽，唯有在某种可怖噩梦的半谵妄中方能被构想出来。它约莫一头成年美洲狮大小，站在四只像手一样的脚上，看样子几乎完全是树栖的。它的前腿比后腿长得多，这一点倒叫人想起鬣狗；但相似之处到此为止，因为这畜生毛茸茸的皮是纵纹的，红黄相间一道一道，而它那张丑陋的头与任何地球动物都毫无相似之处。它没有外耳可见，低低的额头上长着一只又大又圆的眼睛，安在一根约四英寸长的粗触角的顶端。它的颚强劲有力，武装着又长又利的獠牙，而脖子两侧各伸出一只有力的螯钳。我这辈子从未见过哪只野兽像这只异世界的无名畜生这样，为进攻而生得如此可怖的武装。凭着那两只蟹钳般有力的钳子，它能轻而易举地钳住一个比人强壮得多的对手，把它拖向那可怕的颚。

===Original===
For a time it eyed me with that single, terrifying eye that moved to and fro at the end of its antenna, and all the time its chelae were waving slowly, opening and closing. In that brief moment of delay I looked about me, and the first thing that I discovered was that I stood directly in front of an opening cut in the bole of the tree; an opening about three feet wide and over six feet high. But the most remarkable thing about it was that it was closed by a door; not a solid door, but one suggesting a massive wooden grill.
As I stood contemplating it and wondering what to do, I thought that I saw something moving behind it. Then a voice spoke to me out of the darkness beyond the door. It sounded like a human voice, though it spoke in a language that I could not understand. The tones were peremptory. I could almost imagine that it said, “Who are you, and what do you want here in the middle of the night?”
“I am a stranger,” I said. “I come in peace and friendship.”

===Chinese===
一时间它用那只可怖的独眼盯着我，那眼睛在触角顶端来回摆动，与此同时它的两只螯钳缓缓挥舞，一张一合。就在那短暂的僵持之中，我环顾四周，头一样发现的是：我正站在树干上一个开口的正前方；那开口约三英尺宽、六英尺多高。可最不寻常的是，它被一扇门关着；不是实心门，倒像是一面厚重的木栅。
我站着打量它、不知如何是好时，觉得自己看见门后有东西在动。接着一个声音从门后的黑暗中朝我说话。那听上去像人的嗓音，虽然说的是我听不懂的语言。语调是命令式的。我几乎可以想象它在说：「你是谁，半夜三更在这儿要干什么？」
「我是个外乡人，」我说，「我为和平与友谊而来。」

===Original===
Of course I knew that whatever it was behind that door, it could not understand me; but I hoped that my tone would assure it of my peaceful designs. There was a moment’s silence and then I heard other voices. Evidently the situation was being discussed; then I saw that the creature facing me upon the causeway was creeping toward me, and turned my attention from the doorway to the beast.
I had no weapons, nothing but a length of futile rope; but I knew that I must do something. I could not stand there supinely and let the creature seize and devour me without striking a blow in my own defense. I uncoiled a portion of the rope and, more in despair than with any hope that I could accomplish anything of a defensive nature, nicked the end of it in the face of the advancing beast. You have seen a boy snap a wet towel at a companion; perhaps you have been flicked in that way, and if you have, you know that it hurts.

===Chinese===
我当然知道，门后无论是什么，都听不懂我的话；但我希望我的语气能使它确信我怀着和平的用意。沉默了片刻，我听见了别的声音。显然他们正在商量眼下的局面；这时我看见栈道上与我相对的那只野兽正朝我悄悄逼近，便把注意力从门口转回到野兽身上。
我手无寸铁，只有一截无用的绳子；可我知道自己总得有所动作。我不能木然地站在那儿，由着这畜生把我抓住吞掉而不为自卫还一下手。我解开一段绳，与其说抱着能有何防御之效的希望，不如说出于绝望，朝逼近的野兽脸上甩了一下绳头。你见过男孩子用湿毛巾去抽同伴吧；也许你也这么被人抽过，要是抽过，你便知道那有多疼。

===Original===
Of course I did not expect to overcome my adversary by any such means as this; to be truthful, I did not know what I did expect to accomplish. Perhaps I just felt that I must do something, and this was the only thing that occurred to me. The result merely demonstrated the efficiency of that single eye and the quickness of the chelae. I snapped that rope as a ringmaster snaps a whip; but though the rope end travelled with great speed and the act must have been unexpected, the creature caught the rope in one of its chelae before it reached its face. Then it hung on and sought to drag me toward those frightful jaws.
I learned many a trick of roping from a cowboy friend of my motion picture days, and one of these I now put into use in an endeavor to entangle the crablike chelae. Suddenly giving the rope sufficient slack, I threw a half hitch around the chela that gripped it, immediately following it with a second, whereupon the creature commenced to pull desperately away. I think it was motivated solely by an instinctive urge to pull toward its jaws anything that was held in its chelae; but for how long it would continue to pull away before it decided to change its tactics and charge me, I could not even guess; and so I acted upon a sudden inspiration and hurriedly made fast the end of the rope that I held to one of the stout posts that supported the handrail of the causeway; then, of a sudden, the thing charged me, roaring furiously.

===Chinese===
我当然不指望用这种法子制服对手；说句实话，我自己也不晓得指望达成什么。也许我只是觉得非做点什么不可，而这便是唯一想到的办法。其结果不过是证明了那只独眼的高效和螯钳的迅捷。我像马戏团领班甩鞭子那样甩出那根绳；可尽管绳头速度极快、这一招必是出于对方意料，那畜生还是在绳头够到脸之前，用一只螯钳把它钳住了。接着它死死钳住不放，想把我拽向那可怖的颚。
我在电影圈时跟一位牛仔朋友学过不少套绳的诀窍，此刻我便用上其中一招，设法缠住那蟹钳般的螯钳。我突然把绳子放得松松的，在钳住绳的那只螯钳上绕了个半结，紧接着又来一个，于是那畜生开始拼命地往外拽。我想它纯粹是出于一种本能冲动，要把钳在螯钳里的东西往自己嘴边拽；可它还会这样拽多久才决定改换策略、向我冲来，我连猜都没法猜；于是我灵机一动，赶忙把手里的绳头拴在支撑栈道扶手的一根粗柱上；随即，那畜生咆哮着朝我扑来。

===Original===
I turned and ran, hoping that I could get out of the reach of those terrible chelae before the creature was stopped by the rope; and this I but barely managed to do. I breathed a sigh of relief as I saw the great body flipped completely over on its back as the rope tautened, but the hideous scream of rage that followed left me cold. Nor was my relief of any great duration, for as soon as the creature had scrambled to its feet, it seized the rope in its other chela and severed it as neatly as one might with a pair of monstrous tinner’s snips; and then it was after me again, but this time it did not creep.
It seemed evident that my stay upon Venus was to be brief, when suddenly the door in the tree swung open and three men leaped to the causeway just behind the charging terror that was swiftly driving down upon me. The leading man hurled a short, heavy spear that sank deep into the back of my infuriated pursuer. Instantly the creature stopped in its tracks and wheeled about to face these new and more dangerous tormentors; and as he did so two more spears, hurled by the companions of the first man, drove into his chest, and with a last frightful scream, the thing dropped in its tracks, dead.

===Chinese===
我转身就跑，指望在那畜生被绳子拽住之前跑出那些可怖螯钳的够得着的范围；我勉强做到了。我看见绳子绷紧时那庞大的身躯被整个翻了个背朝天，不禁松了一口气；可紧随其后那声愤怒的可怖尖叫，又令我凉了半截。而我的轻松也没持续多久，因为那畜生一翻身爬起来，便用另一只螯钳钳住绳子，像用一把硕大的剪锡剪一般利落地把它剪断了；接着它又朝我扑来，这一次它可不是悄悄逼近了。
看来我在金星的日子注定短暂，就在这时，树上的门忽然洞开，三个男人跃上栈道，恰在那头疾扑而来的可怖野兽身后。为首的那人掷出一杆又短又重的矛，深深扎进了我那暴怒追兵的背。那畜生当即收住脚步，转身面对这些新的、更危险的折磨者；就在它转身之际，又两杆矛——为首那人两个同伴掷出的——扎进了它的胸口，那畜生发出最后一声可怖的尖叫，倒地身亡。

===Original===
Then the leading man came toward me. In the subdued light of the forest he appeared no different from an earth man. He held the point of a straight, sharp sword pointed at my vitals. Close behind him were the other two men, each with a drawn sword.
The first man spoke to me in a stern, commanding voice, but I shook my head to indicate that I could not understand; then he pressed the point of his weapon against my coveralls, opposite the pit of my stomach, and jabbed. I backed away. He advanced and jabbed at me again, and again I backed along the causeway. Now the other two men advanced and the three of them fell to examining me, meanwhile talking among themselves.

===Chinese===
接着为首那人朝我走来。在林中柔和的光线里，他看起来与地球人并无二致。他用一杆又直又利的剑，剑尖指着我的要害。紧随其后的是另外两人，各人手中都握着出鞘的剑。
第一个人用严厉、命令式的口吻对我说话，我摇头示意听不懂；他便把剑尖抵住我工装胸口正对心窝处，捅了一下。我退后。他上前又捅，我又沿栈道退后。这时另外两人也上前，三人一齐打量起我来，一边彼此交谈。

===Original===
I could see them better now. They were about my own height and in every detail of their visible anatomy they appeared identical with terrestrial human beings, nor was a great deal left to my imagination﻿—the men were almost naked. They wore loincloths and little else other than the belts that supported the scabbards of their swords. Their skins appeared to be much darker than mine, but not so dark as a negro’s, and their faces were smooth and handsome.
Several times one or another of them addressed me and I always replied, but neither understood what the other said. Finally, after a lengthy discussion, one of them reentered the opening in the tree and a moment later I saw the interior of a chamber, just within the doorway, illuminated; then one of the two remaining men motioned me forward and pointed toward the doorway.
Understanding that he wished me to enter, I stepped forward, and, as I passed them, they kept their sword points against my body﻿—they were taking no chances with me. The other man awaited me in the center of a large room hewn from the interior of the great tree. Beyond him were other doorways leading from this room, doubtless into other apartments. There were chairs and a table in the room; the walls were carved and painted; there was a large rug upon the floor; from a small vessel depending from the center of the ceiling a soft light illuminated the interior as brightly as might sunlight flooding through an open window, but there was no glare.

===Chinese===
此刻我能看清他们了。他们与我身高相仿，可见的体貌特征每一处都与地球人毫无二致，而且也用不着我多加想象——这几人几乎是赤身露体的。他们只系着缠腰布，此外无非是系剑鞘的腰带而已。他们的肤色看上去比我深得多，却又不似黑人那般黑，面庞光洁而英俊。
他们中有好几次这个或那个对我说话，我总是作答，可彼此谁也听不懂对方。末了，经过一番冗长的商议，其中一人重新走进树上的开口，不多时我便看见门内一间屋子的内部亮了起来；随后剩下的两人中有一人示意我上前，朝门口指了指。
我明白他要我进去，便迈步上前，而在我经过他们身旁时，他们始终把剑尖抵在我身上——他们对我毫不掉以轻心。另一人在一间从巨树内部凿出的大屋子中央等着我。他身后还有几道门通向别处，无疑是通往别的房间。屋里有椅子和一张桌子；四壁雕花彩绘；地板上铺着一大块毯子；从天花板正中垂下的一只小器皿发出柔和的光，把屋内照得如同阳光从敞开的窗户倾泻进来一般明亮，却不刺眼。

===Original===
The other men had entered and closed the door, which they fastened by a device that was not apparent to me at the time; then one of them pointed to a chair and motioned me to be seated. Under the bright light they examined me intently, and I them. My clothing appeared to puzzle them most; they examined and discussed its material, texture, and weave, if I could judge correctly by their gestures and inflections.
Finding the heat unendurable in my fleece-lined coveralls, I removed them and my leather coat and polo shirt. Each newly revealed article aroused their curiosity and comment. My light skin and blond hair also received their speculative attention.
Presently one of them left the chamber, and while he was absent another removed the various articles that had lain upon the table. These consisted of what I took to be books bound in wooden and in leather covers, several ornaments, and a dagger in a beautifully wrought sheath.

===Chinese===
其余的人也进了屋，关上门，用一种我当时看不出来的机关把它扣好；接着其中一人指了指一把椅子，示意我坐下。在明亮的光线下他们细细打量我，我也打量他们。我的衣服似乎最让他们困惑；据他们的手势和语调判断，他们正仔细察看并讨论它的料子、质地和织法。
穿着绒衬工装实在热得难耐，我便把它连同皮外衣和马球衫一并脱了。每露出一件新东西，都引来他们的好奇和议论。我浅色的皮肤和金色的头发也受到了他们探究性的关注。
不多时其中一人离开了屋子，他不在时，另一人把桌上摆着的各样物件收走。那些东西包括几本我以为的书——木封面和皮封面的都有、几件饰物，还有一把匕首，插在一只打造得极精美的鞘里。

===Original===
When the man who had left the room returned, he brought food and drink which he placed upon the table; and by signs the three indicated that I might eat. There were fruits and nuts in highly polished, carved wooden bowls; there was something I took to be bread, on a golden platter; and there was honey in a silver jug. A tall, slender goblet contained a whitish liquid that resembled milk. This last receptacle was a delicate, translucent ceramic of an exquisite blue shade. These things and the appointments of the room bespoke culture, refinement, and good taste, making the savage apparel of their owners appear incongruous.
The fruits and nuts were unlike any with which I was familiar, both in appearance and flavor; the bread was coarse but delicious; and the honey, if such it were, suggested candied violets to the taste. The milk (I can find no other earthly word to describe it) was strong and almost pungent, yet far from unpleasant. I imagined at the time that one might grow to be quite fond of it.
The table utensils were similar to those with which we are familiar in civilized portions of the earth; there were hollowed instruments with which to dip or scoop, sharp ones with which to cut, and others with tines with which to impale. There was also a handled pusher, which I recommend to earthly hostesses. All these were of metal.

===Chinese===
离开的那人回来时，带来了饮食，摆到桌上；三人打着手势示意我可以吃。有水果和坚果，装在擦得锃亮的雕花木碗里；有一样我以为的面包，盛在一只金盘上；还有蜂蜜，装在银壶里。一只又高又细的高脚杯里盛着一种乳白色的液体，像是奶。最后这件器皿是一只精致的半透明白瓷，呈一种极美的蓝色。这些东西连同屋内的陈设，无一不透着文化、教养与好品位，反衬出主人们那身蛮荒装束是何等的不协调。
那些水果和坚果无论外貌还是滋味都与我熟悉的截然不同；面包粗粝却可口；那蜂蜜——倘若确是蜂蜜的话——入口却像糖渍紫罗兰。那奶（我找不到别的地球词汇来形容）味浓，几乎带点辛辣，却绝不令人不快。我当时暗想，日子久了人是会爱上它的。
餐具与我们在地球文明之地所熟悉的相仿：有凹形的器皿用以舀或勺，有尖利的用以切，还有带齿的用以叉。此外还有一把带柄的推食器，我愿向地球的主妇们推荐。这些器具无一不是金属所制。

===Original===
While I ate, the three men conversed earnestly, one or another of them occasionally offering me more food. They seemed hospitable and courteous, and I felt that if they were typical of the inhabitants of Venus I should find my life here a pleasant one. That it would not be a bed of roses, however, was attested by the weapons that the men constantly wore; one does not carry a sword and a dagger about with him unless he expects to have occasion to use them, except on dress parade.
When I had finished my meal, two of the men escorted me from the room by a rear doorway, up a flight of circular stairs, and ushered me into a small chamber. The stairway and corridor were illuminated by a small lamp similar to that which hung in the room where I had eaten, and light from this lamp shone through the heavy wooden grating of the door, into the room where I was now locked and where my captors left me to my own devices.
Upon the floor was a soft mattress over which were spread coverings of a silky texture. It being very warm, I removed all of my clothing except my undershorts and lay down to sleep. I was tired after my arduous descent of the giant tree and dozed almost immediately. I should have been asleep at once had I not been suddenly startled to wakefulness by a repetition of that hideous scream with which the beast that had pursued me through the tree had announced its rage and chagrin when I had eluded it.
However, it was not long before I fell asleep, my dozing mind filled with a chaos of fragmentary recollections of my stupendous adventure.

===Chinese===
我吃饭时，三人热切地交谈着，时而这个、时而那个偶尔再添些食物给我。他们显得殷勤有礼，我觉得倘若金星上的居民都像他们这般，我此处的日子倒会过得舒坦。然而此地的日子未必尽是安乐——他们随身佩戴的武器便印证了这一点：一个人随身带着剑和匕刀，除非是阅兵典礼，否则便是料到会有用着它们的时候。
我吃完饭后，其中两人领我从后门出了屋，沿一道螺旋楼梯上去，把我带进一间小室。楼梯和走廊由一盏小灯照明，与方才吃饭那间屋里挂着的那盏相仿，这盏灯的光透过门上厚重的木栅照进来，照进我此刻被锁在其中、被俘获者把我独自丢下的这间屋子。
地板上铺着一张软垫，上头铺着丝滑质地的盖被。屋里很热，我把除内裤外的衣物尽数脱去，躺下睡觉。攀下巨树的一番折腾让我疲惫不堪，几乎立刻就打起盹来。要不是那声可怖的尖叫——正是追我的那只野兽在树上被我甩脱时用以宣告它的愤怒与懊恼的那一声——忽然又响了一次把我惊醒，我本该立刻就睡着的。
然而没过多久我还是睡着了，那昏沉的脑海里满是这桩惊天冒险零零碎碎的混乱回忆。

## IV To the House of the King / IV 前往国王的居所

===Original===
When I awoke, it was quite light in the room, and through a window I saw the foliage of trees, lavender and heliotrope and violet in the light of a new day. I arose and went to the window. I saw no sign of sunlight, yet a brightness equivalent to sunlight pervaded everything. The air was warm and sultry. Below me I could see sections of various causeways extending from tree to tree. On some of these I caught glimpses of people. All the men were naked, except for loincloths, nor did I wonder at their scant apparel, in the light of my experience of the temperatures on Venus. There were both men and women; and all the men were armed with swords and daggers, while the women carried daggers only. All those whom I saw seemed to be of the same age; there were neither children nor old people among them. All appeared comely.
From my barred window I sought a glimpse of the ground, but as far down as I could see there was only the amazing foliage of the trees, lavender, heliotrope, and violet. And what trees! From my window I could see several enormous boles fully two hundred feet in diameter. I had thought the tree I descended a giant, but compared with these, it was only a sapling.

===Chinese===
我醒来时，屋里相当明亮，透过一扇窗户，我看见树木的枝叶在新一天的光线里呈淡紫、紫红与堇色。我起身走到窗前。我看不见太阳的踪影，可一种与日光相当的明亮却弥漫在万物之上。空气温暖而闷热。在我脚下，可以望见一段段栈道从一棵树延伸到另一棵树。在其中一些上面，我瞥见了人影。男人们除了缠腰布外赤身露体——鉴于我对金星气温的体验，我对他们这身打扮一点也不觉得奇怪。男女都有；男人们个个佩剑带匕，女人们则只带匕首。我所见到的所有人似乎都是同一年纪；其中既无孩童，也无老人。一个个都眉清目秀。
我从装有栅栏的窗户往下试图窥见地面，可我能望见的极深处，仍是那些惊人的枝叶——淡紫、紫红、堇色。多惊人的树啊！从我的窗户望出去，能看见好几根足有两百英尺粗的巨树树干。我本以为我攀下来的那棵已是巨树，可与这些一比，它不过是棵小苗。

===Original===
As I stood contemplating the scene before me, there was a noise at the door behind me. Turning, I saw one of my captors entering the room. He greeted me with a few words, which I could not understand, and a pleasant smile, that I could. I returned his smile and said, “Good morning!”
He beckoned me to follow him from the room, but I made signs indicating that I wished to don my clothes first. I knew I should be hot and uncomfortable in them; I was aware that no one I had seen here wore any clothing, yet so powerful are the inhibitions of custom and habit that I shrank from doing the sensible thing and wearing only my undershorts.
At first, when he realized what I wished to do, he motioned me to leave my clothes where they were and come with him as I was; but eventually he gave in with another of his pleasant smiles. He was a man of fine physique, a little shorter than I; by daylight, I could see that his skin was about that shade of brown that a heavy sun tan imparts to people of my own race; his eyes were dark brown, his hair black. His appearance formed a marked contrast to my light skin, blue eyes, and blond hair.

===Chinese===
正当我凝视眼前的景象时，身后那扇门响了起来。我转身一看，俘获我的人当中有一个正走进屋来。他用几句我听不懂的话、和一个我看得懂的友善微笑向我致意。我也回他一个微笑，说道：「早安！」
他招手让我随他出屋，可比划着示意我想先把衣裳穿上。我明知穿着它会又热又不舒服；我也清楚在这里所见的人没有一个穿衣服，可习俗与习惯的禁锢之力竟如此强大，使我对做那件明智之事——只穿条内裤——也踟蹰不前。
起初，他弄明白我的意图后，示意我把衣服留在原处、就这么跟他走；可终究他还是带着又一个友善的微笑让了步。他体格健美，比我略矮；在日光下，我看清他的肤色大约就是我本族人晒得黝黑时那种棕色；他的眼睛是深棕色的，头发乌黑。他的模样与我浅色的皮肤、蓝色的眼睛、金色的头发形成了鲜明的对照。

===Original===
When I had dressed, I followed him downstairs to a room adjoining the one I had first entered the previous night. Here the man’s two companions and two women were seated at a table on which were a number of vessels containing food. As I entered the room the women’s eyes were turned upon me curiously; the men smiled and greeted me as had their fellow, and one of them motioned me to a chair. The women appraised me frankly but without boldness, and it was evident that they were discussing me freely between themselves and with the men. They were both uncommonly good-looking, their skins being a shade lighter than those of the men, while their eyes and hair were of about the same color as those of their male companions. Each wore a single garment of a silken material similar to that of which my bed cover had been made and in the form of a long sash, which was wrapped tightly around the body below the armpits, confining the breasts. From this point it was carried halfway around the body downward to the waist, where it circled the body again, the loose end then passing between the legs from behind and up through the sash in front, after the manner of a G string, the remainder falling in front to the knees.

===Chinese===
穿戴停当后，我随他下楼，来到一间与我昨夜初次进入的那间相邻的屋子。他那两个同伴和两个女人正坐在一张桌前，桌上摆着几只盛食物的器皿。我进屋时，两个女人好奇地把目光投向我；男人们像他们的同伴那样微笑着向我致意，其中一人示意我入座。两个女人坦率地打量我，却并无轻浮之态，看得出她们彼此之间、以及与男人们之间正在毫无顾忌地议论我。两人都长得格外好看，肤色比男人们略浅一线，眼睛和头发的颜色则与男伴们大致相同。各人身上只有一件衣裳，料子是丝质的，与我床上的盖被相仿，形如一条长长的带子，紧紧地缠在腋下的身上，把胸部束住。自此往下，带子绕身半周至腰间，在腰上又绕一圈，松着的一头从身后穿过两腿、再从前方穿过腰带——形如兜布——余下的部分垂在身前，直垂到膝。

===Original===
In addition to these garments, which were beautifully embroidered in colors, the women wore girdles from which depended pocket pouches and sheathed daggers, and both were plentifully adorned with ornaments such as rings, bracelets, and hair ornaments. I could recognize gold and silver among the various materials of which these things were fabricated, and there were others that might have been ivory and coral; but what impressed me most was the exquisite workmanship they displayed, and I imagined that they were valued more for this than for the intrinsic worth of the materials that composed them. That this conjecture might be in accordance with fact was borne out by the presence among their ornaments of several of the finest workmanship, obviously carved from ordinary bone.
On the table was bread different from that which I had had the night before, a dish that I thought might be eggs and meat baked together, several which I could not recognize either by appearance or taste, and the familiar milk and honey that I had encountered before. The foods varied widely in range of flavor, so that it would have been a difficult palate indeed that would not have found something to its liking.

===Chinese===
这些衣裳用彩线绣得十分精美，除此之外，女人们腰间还系着腰带，上面垂着小荷包和带鞘的匕首，两人都大量佩戴着戒指、手镯、发饰之类的饰物。在这些饰物的种种材质里，我能认出金银，还有一些也许是象牙和珊瑚；可最令我印象深刻的，是它们所展现的精湛手艺，我揣想它们之所以被人珍视，与其说是因为用料本身的价值，不如说是因为这份手艺。这一猜测也许与事实相符——他们的饰物里有几件工艺最为精巧的，显然是用寻常骨头雕成的。
桌上摆着与我昨夜吃的不同的面包，一道我以为是蛋和肉烤在一起的菜，几样凭外观和滋味我都辨不出的东西，还有我先前已领教过的熟悉的奶与蜜。食物的滋味跨度极大，挑剔的舌头也难以找不到一两样合胃口的。

===Original===
During the meal they engaged in serious discussion, and I was certain from their glances and gestures that I was the subject of their debate. The two girls enlivened the meal by attempting to carry on a conversation with me, which appeared to afford them a great deal of merriment, nor could I help joining in their laughter, so infectious was it. Finally one of them hit upon the happy idea of teaching me their language. She pointed to herself and said, “Zuro,” and to the other girl and said, “Alzo”; then the men became interested, and I soon learned that the name of him who seemed to be the head of the house, the man who had first challenged me the preceding night, was Duran, the other two Olthar and Kamlot.
But before I had mastered more than these few words and the names of some of the foods on the table, breakfast was over and the three men had conducted me from the house. As we proceeded along the causeway that passed in front of the house of Duran, the interest and curiosity of those we passed were instantly challenged as their eyes fell upon me; and it was at once evident to me that I was a type either entirely unknown on Venus or at least rare, for my blue eyes and blond hair caused quite as much comment as my clothing, as I could tell by their gestures and the direction of their gaze.

===Chinese===
吃饭时他们认真地商议着，我从他们的目光与手势断定，我正是他们争论的题目。两位姑娘试着和我攀谈，给这顿饭添了不少兴致，这似乎让她们乐不可支，那笑声极富感染力，我也禁不住跟着一起笑。末了，其中一位想出了个好主意——教我他们的语言。她指着自己说「祖罗（Zuro）」，又指着另一位姑娘说「阿尔佐（Alzo）」；男人们也来了兴致，我很快便得知：那看似一家之主、昨夜头一个喝问我的人，名叫杜兰（Duran），另外两位一个叫奥尔塔（Olthar），一个叫卡姆洛特（Kamlot）。
可我还没学会比这几句话和桌上几样食物的名字更多的东西，早餐便已结束，三个男人领着我出了门。我们沿着杜兰家门前的栈道前行，所过之人一看见我，无不立刻被勾起兴趣与好奇；我当即明白，我这种人在金星要么闻所未闻，要么至少极为罕见，因为我的蓝眼睛和金头发所引起的议论，与我的衣裳所引起的几乎一样多——这是我从他们的手势和目光所投的方向看出来的。

===Original===
We were often stopped by curious friends of my captors, or hosts (I was not sure yet in which category they fell); but none offered me either harm or insult, and if I were the object of their curious scrutiny, so were they of mine. While no two of them were identical in appearance, they were all handsome and all apparently of about the same age. I saw no old people and no children.
Presently we approached a tree of such enormous diameter that I could scarcely believe the testimony of my eyes when I saw it. It was fully five hundred feet in diameter. Stripped of branches for a hundred feet above and below the causeway, its surface was dotted with windows and doors and encircled by wide balconies or verandas. Before a large and elaborately carved doorway was a group of armed men before whom we halted while Duran addressed one of their number.
I thought at the time that he called this man Tofar, and such I learned later was his name. He wore a necklace from which depended a metal disc bearing a hieroglyphic in relief; otherwise he was not accoutered differently from his companions. As he and Duran conversed, he appraised me carefully from head to feet. Presently he and Duran passed through the doorway into the interior of the tree, while the others continued to examine me and question Kamlot and Olthar.

===Chinese===
我们不时被我的俘获者——或者主人（究竟是哪一类我一时还拿不准）——那些好奇的朋友们拦下；可谁也没有加害我或侮辱我，如果说我是他们好奇打量的对象，他们也是我打量的对象。他们当中没有两个人长得一模一样，却个个英俊，年纪显然也都相仿。我没看见老人，也没看见小孩。
不多时我们走近一棵直径大得惊人的树，我看见它时几乎不敢相信自己的眼睛。它足有五百英尺粗。在栈道上下各一百英尺的范围内，它的树身不见一枝一桠，表面布满门窗，又被一道道宽阔的阳台或回廊环绕。一扇宽阔而雕工繁复的大门前站着一群武装的人，我们在他们面前停下，杜兰与其中一人搭话。
我当时听他唤那人「托法尔」，后来得知这确是他的名字。他脖子上挂着一串项链，下面垂着一块金属圆牌，上面浮雕着一个象形文字；除此之外，他的装束与同伴并无不同。他与杜兰交谈时，把我从头到脚仔细打量了一番。不多时他和杜兰穿过大门进入树内，其余人则继续打量我，向卡姆洛特和奥尔塔询问。

===Original===
While I waited there, I embraced the opportunity to study the elaborate carvings that surrounded the portal, forming a frame fully five feet wide. The motif appeared historical, and I could easily imagine that the various scenes depicted important events in the life of a dynasty or a nation. The workmanship was exquisite, and it required no stretch of the imagination to believe that each delicately carved face was the portrait of some dead or living celebrity. There was nothing grotesque in the delineation of the various figures, as is so often the case in work of a similar character on earth, and only the borders that framed the whole and separated contiguous plaques were conventional.
I was still engrossed by these beautiful examples of the wood carver’s art when Duran and Tofar returned and motioned Olthar and Kamlot and me to follow them into the interior of the great tree. We passed through several large chambers and along wide corridors, all carved from the wood of the living tree, to the head of a splendid stairway, which we descended to another level. The chambers near the periphery of the tree received their light through windows, while the interior chambers and corridors were illuminated by lamps similar to those I had already seen in the house of Duran.

===Chinese===
等候之际，我趁机端详起环绕门洞、足有五英尺宽的那一圈繁复雕花边框。其题材似乎是历史性的，我不难想象其中的种种场景描绘的是一个王朝或一个民族的重大事件。雕工精湛，要相信每一张精雕细琢的面孔都是某位已故或尚在的名人肖像，无需多大想象。各种人物的刻画毫无怪诞之感——地球同类作品常常如此——唯有整体外框和分隔相邻嵌板之间的边饰是程式化的。
我正出神地欣赏着这些木雕艺术的精美范例，杜兰和托法尔回来了，招手让奥尔塔、卡姆洛特和我随他们进入巨树内部。我们穿过几间大屋和宽阔的走廊——全都凿自这棵活树的木质——来到一道气派的楼梯顶端，沿梯下到另一层。靠近树身外缘的屋子靠窗户采光，里头的屋子和走廊则由与我在杜兰家所见相仿的灯照明。

===Original===
Near the foot of the stairway we had descended we entered a spacious chamber, before the doorway to which stood two men armed with spears and swords, and before us, across the chamber, we saw a man seated at a table near a large window. Just inside the doorway we halted, my companions standing in respectful silence until the man at the table looked up and spoke to them; then they crossed the room, taking me with them, and halted before the table, upon the opposite side of which the man sat facing us.
He spoke pleasantly to my companions, calling each by name, and when they replied they addressed him as *Jong*. He was a fine-looking man with a strong face and a commanding presence. His attire was similar to that worn by all the other male Venusans I had seen, differing only in that he wore about his head a fillet that supported a circular metal disc in the center of his forehead. He appeared much interested in me and watched me intently while listening to Duran, who, I had no doubt, was narrating the story of my strange and sudden appearance the night before.

===Chinese===
在我们下来的楼梯近底处，我们走进一间宽敞的屋子，门前站着两个手持矛与剑的人，而在屋子的那一头，我们看见一人坐在一扇大窗旁的桌前。我们在门内停下，我的同伴们恭敬地静立着，直到桌前那人抬眼与他们说话；他们才带我穿过屋子，停在他坐着的桌子对面。
他愉快地与我的同伴们交谈，一一唤他们的名字；他们回话时则称他为「君（Jong）」。他相貌堂堂，面庞坚毅，气度威严。他的装束与我所见的其他金星男子相仿，唯独不同处在于：他额上束着一条额带，正中托着一块圆形金属牌。他对我颇为关注，一边听杜兰讲话——杜兰无疑正在讲述我昨夜那般奇异而突然地出现的经过——一边目不转睛地打量我。

===Original===
When Duran had concluded, the man called Jong addressed me. His manner was serious, his tones kindly. Out of courtesy, I replied, though I knew that he could understand me no better than I had understood him. He smiled and shook his head; then he fell into a discussion with the others. Finally he struck a metal gong that stood near him on the table; then he arose and came around the table to where I stood. He examined my clothing carefully, feeling its texture and apparently discussing the materials and the weave with the others. Then he examined the skin of my hands and face, felt of my hair, and made me open my mouth that he might examine my teeth. I was reminded of the horse market and the slave block. “Perhaps,” I thought, “the latter is more apropos.”
A man entered now whom I took to be a servant and, receiving instructions from the man called Jong, departed again, while I continued to be the object of minute investigation. My beard, which was now some twenty-four hours old, elicited considerable comment. It is not a beautiful beard at any age, being sparse and reddish, for which reason I am careful to shave daily when I have the necessary utensils.

===Chinese===
杜兰讲完，那位被称为「君」的人对我说话。他神色庄重，语调却很和善。出于礼节，我也答了话，尽管我明知他听不懂我的，一如我听不懂他的。他笑着摇头；接着与众人们商议起来。末了，他敲了敲桌上靠近自己的一面铜锣；随后起身绕过桌朝我走来。他仔细查看我的衣裳，摸它的质地，显然在与人讨论它的料子与织法。接着他查看我手和脸的皮肤，又摸了摸我的头发，还叫我张嘴让他看牙。我联想起马市和奴隶拍卖台。「也许，」我心想，「后者更贴切些。」
这时进来一个我以为是仆人的人，从那位「君」处受了吩咐又出去了，而我继续被这般细细查验。我那已有约莫二十四小时没刮的胡子引来了不少议论。我这胡子无论在什么年纪都不算美观——稀稀拉拉，颜色发红——正因如此，但凡有必要的用具，我都每日仔细刮净。

===Original===
I cannot say that I enjoyed this intimate appraisal, but the manner in which it was conducted was so entirely free from any suggestion of intentional rudeness or discourtesy, and my position here was so delicate that my better judgment prevented me from openly resenting the familiarities of the man called Jong. It is well that I did not.
Presently a man entered through a doorway at my right. I assumed that he had been summoned by the servant recently dispatched. As he came forward, I saw that he was much like the others; a handsome man of about thirty. There are those who declaim against monotony; but for me there can never be any monotony of beauty, not even if the beautiful things were all identical, which the Venusans I had so far seen were not. All were beautiful, but each in his own way.
The man called Jong spoke to the newcomer rapidly for about five minutes, evidently narrating all that they knew about me and giving instructions. When he had finished, the other motioned me to follow him; and a few moments later I found myself in another room on the same level. It had three large windows and was furnished with several desks, tables, and chairs. Most of the available wall space was taken up by shelves on which reposed what I could only assume to be books﻿—thousands of them.

===Chinese===
我不能说享受这般近乎贴身的查验，可查验者的做派毫无蓄意粗鲁或失礼之嫌，加之我此处的处境微妙，我的理智劝住了我，没让我对这位「君」的诸般亲昵举动公然表露不快。幸而我没表露。
不多时，一个男子从我右边一道门走进来。我猜想他便是方才派出的仆人召来的。他上前时，我看清他与众人无甚两样；一个约莫三十岁的英俊男子。世上有人喟叹单调之美；可于我而言，美永远不会单调——纵使美的东西千篇一律亦然，何况我所见的金星人并非千篇一律。他们个个都美，却各有各的美。
那位「君」对来人快速说了约五分钟话，显然是在讲述他们所知的关于我的一切，并交代了吩咐。说完，对方便示意我随他走；不多时我已身处另一间同层的屋子。它有三扇大窗，屋里摆着几张书桌、桌子和椅子。可用的墙面大半被书架占据，架上陈列着我只能认作书的东西——成千上万册。

===Original===
The ensuing three weeks were as delightful and interesting as any that I have ever experienced. During this time, Danus, in whose charge I had been placed, taught me the Venusan language and told me much concerning the planet, the people among whom I had fallen, and their history. I found the language easy to master, but I shall not at this time attempt to describe it fully. The alphabet consists of twenty-four characters, five of which represent vowel sounds, and these are the only vowel sounds that the Venusan vocal chords seem able to articulate. The characters of the alphabet all have the same value, there being no capital letters. Their system of punctuation differs from ours and is more practical; for example, before you start to read a sentence you know whether it is exclamatory, interrogative, a reply to an interrogation, or a simple statement. Characters having values similar to the comma and semicolon are used much as we use these two; they have no colon; their character that functions as does our period follows each sentence, their question mark and exclamation point preceding the sentences the nature of which they determine.
A peculiarity of their language that renders it easy to master is the absence of irregular verbs; the verb root is never altered for voice, mode, tense, number, or person, distinctions that are achieved by the use of several simple, auxiliary words.

===Chinese===
接下来的三个星期，过得与我平生任何一段日子一样愉悦而有趣。这段时间里，受托照管我的达努斯（Danus）教我金星语，又给我讲了许多关于这颗行星、我所落入的人群、以及他们历史的事。我觉得这门语言易于掌握，但眼下我不打算细述。它的字母共二十四个字符，其中五个代表元音，而这似乎是金星人声带所能发出的全部元音。字母表里所有字符大小同等，没有大写字母。他们的标点体系与我们不同，却更为实用；比方说，你尚未开读一个句子，便已知它是感叹、是疑问、是对疑问的回答、还是一句简单的陈述。功能近似逗号和分号的字符，用法与我们的逗号分号相仿；他们没有冒号；功能如同我们句号的字符跟在每个句子之后，问号和感叹号则放在它们所判定的那种句子的前面。
这门语言一个便于掌握的特点是没有不规则动词；动词词根绝不因语态、语气、时态、数或人称而改变，这些区别是靠几个简单的助词来实现的。

===Original===
While I was learning to speak the language of my hosts, I also learned to read and write it, and I spent many enjoyable hours delving into the large library of which Danus is the curator while my tutor was absent attending to his other duties, which are numerous. He is chief physician and surgeon of his country, physician and surgeon to the king, and head of a college of medicine and surgery.
One of the first questions that Danus had asked me when I had acquired a working knowledge of his language was where I came from, but when I told him I had come from another world more than twenty-six million miles from his familiar Amtor, which is the name by which the Venusans know their world, he shook his head skeptically.
“There is no life beyond Amtor,” he said. “How can there be life where all is fire?”

===Chinese===
在我学说我主人语言的同时，我也学着读写它。导师因其他公务繁多而外出时，我便在达努斯掌管的那座大图书馆里消磨了许多愉快的时光，埋头翻阅馆藏。他是本国首席内外科医师、国王的御医，也是一所内外科医学院的院长。
待我初步掌握了达努斯的语言，他最早问我的问题之一便是：我从何处来。可当我告诉他，我来自另一个距他们熟知的阿姆托（Amtor）——这是金星人对他们这个世界的称呼——两千六百多万英里之外的世界时，他怀疑地摇了摇头。
「阿姆托之外没有生命，」他说，「到处都是烈火的地方，怎么会有生命？」

===Original===
“What is your theory of the﻿—” I started, but I had to stop. There is no Amtorian word for universe, neither is there any for sun, moon, star, or planet. The gorgeous heavens that we see are never seen by the inhabitants of Venus, obscured as they perpetually are by the two great cloud envelopes that surround the planet. I started over again. “What do you believe surrounds Amtor?” I asked.
He stepped to a shelf and returned with a large volume, which he opened at a beautifully executed map of Amtor. It showed three concentric circles. Between the two inner circles lay a circular belt designated as Trabol, which means warm country. Here the boundaries of seas, continents, and islands were traced to the edges of the two circles that bounded it, in some places crossing these boundaries as though marking the spots at which venturesome explorers had dared the perils of an unknown and inhospitable land.

===Chinese===
「你对——的看法是？」我开口，却不得不说下去。阿姆托语里没有「宇宙」一词，也没有「太阳」「月亮」「星星」「行星」。我们所见那壮丽的苍穹，金星上的居民从未见过——它们被环绕行星的两重巨大云幕永远遮蔽。我重新起头：「你认为阿姆托之外是什么？」我问。
他走到一个书架前，拿来一卷大开本，翻到一幅绘制精美的阿姆托地图。图上是三个同心圆。两个内圆之间是一片环形地带，标注为特拉博尔（Trabol），意为「温暖的国度」。这里海洋、大陆和岛屿的疆界都画到界定它的两个圆的边上，有些地方还越过了这些疆界，仿佛在标记胆大的探险家冒险闯入那片未知而不宜居之地的所在。

===Original===
“This is Trabol,” explained Danus, placing a finger upon that portion of the map I have briefly described. “It entirely surrounds Strabol, which lies in the center of Amtor. Strabol is extremely hot, its land is covered with enormous forests and dense undergrowth, and is peopled by huge land animals, reptiles, and birds, its warm seas swarm with monsters of the deep. No man has ventured far into Strabol and lived to return.
“Beyond Trabol,” he continued, placing his finger on the outer band designated as Karbol (Cold Country), “lies Karbol. Here it is as cold as Strabol is hot. There are strange animals there too, and adventurers have returned with tales of fierce human beings clothed in fur. But it is an inhospitable land into which there is no occasion to venture and which few dare penetrate far for fear of being precipitated over the rim into the molten sea.”

===Chinese===
「这是特拉博尔，」达努斯指着地图上我方才简述的那一带解释道，「它整个地环绕着斯特拉博尔（Strabol），后者位于阿姆托正中。斯特拉博尔酷热难当，陆地上覆着巨大的森林和茂密的林下灌丛，栖息着硕大的走兽、爬虫和飞禽，温暖的海洋里成群游弋着深渊巨怪。没有人深入斯特拉博尔还能活着回来。
「特拉博尔之外，」他接着说，手指移到外圈标注为卡博尔（Karbol，寒冷国度）的地带，「便是卡博尔。这里有多冷，斯特拉博尔就有多热。这里也有奇异的动物，探险者们带回了身着皮毛的凶猛人类的传闻。但这是一片不宜居的土地，没有理由冒险前往，敢深入的人也寥寥无几——唯恐被翻过边缘、跌入熔岩之海。」

===Original===
“Over what rim?” I asked.
He looked at me in astonishment. “I can well believe that you come from another world when you ask me such questions as you do,” he remarked. “Do you mean to tell me that you know nothing of the physical structure of Amtor?”
“I know nothing of your theory concerning it,” I replied.
“It is not a theory; it is a fact,” he corrected me gently. “In no other way may the various phenomena of nature be explained. Amtor is a huge disc with an upturned rim, like a great saucer; it floats upon a sea of molten metal and rock, a fact that is incontrovertably proved by the gushing forth of this liquid mass occasionally from the summits of mountains, when a hole has been burned in the bottom of Amtor. Karbol, the cold country, is a wise provision of nature that tempers the terrific heat that must constantly surge about the outer rim of Amtor.

===Chinese===
「什么边缘？」我问。
他愕然地看着我。「你问我这样的问题，我倒真信你是从另一个世界来的了，」他说，「你莫不是要告诉我，你对阿姆托的构造一无所知？」
「我对你们关于它的理论一无所知。」我答道。
「这不是理论，是事实。」他温和地纠正我，「否则大自然的种种现象便无从解释。阿姆托是一只边缘上翘的巨盘，形如一只大碟子；它漂浮在熔融金属与岩石的海洋之上——这一点无可辩驳，每当阿姆托底部被烧穿了洞，这种液体便会从山顶喷涌而出。卡博尔这片寒冷国度，是大自然一项明智的安排，用来调和那必定在阿姆托外缘不断涌动的酷热。

===Original===
“Above Amtor, and entirely surrounding her above the molten sea, is a chaos of fire and flame. From this our clouds protect us. Occasionally there have occurred rifts in the clouds, and at such times the heat from the fires above, when the rifts occurred in the daytime, has been so intense as to wither vegetation and destroy life, while the light that shone through was of blinding intensity. When these rifts occurred at night there was no heat, but we saw the sparks from the fire shining above us.”
I tried to explain the spherical shape of the planets and that Karbol was only the colder country surrounding one of Amtor’s poles, while Strabol, the hot country, lay in the equatorial region; that Trabol was merely one of two temperate zones, the other one being beyond the equatorial region, which was a band around the middle of a globe and not, as he supposed, a circular area in the center of a disc. He listened to me politely, but only smiled and shook his head when I had finished.

===Chinese===
「阿姆托之上、整个地环绕着它的，在熔岩之海上方，是一片烈火与火焰的混沌。我们的云层护着我们免受其害。云层偶尔会出现裂缝，白天裂缝出现时，上方烈火传来的热力极为炽烈，足以使草木枯萎、生灵毙命，而透下来的光亮则令人目眩。夜里出现裂缝时没有热，但我们能看见上方烈火迸出的火花。」
我试图向他解释行星的球体形状，说明卡博尔不过是环绕阿姆托某一极的寒冷地带，而炎热的斯特拉博尔位于赤道地区；特拉博尔只是两个温带之一，另一个温带在赤道地区之外，赤道地区是环绕球体中段的一条带，而不是他所认为的、圆盘中央的一片圆形区域。他礼貌地听着，听我讲完却只是笑了笑，摇了摇头。

===Original===
At first I could not comprehend that a man of such evident intelligence, education, and culture should cling to such a belief as his, but when I stopped to consider the fact that neither he nor any of his progenitors had ever seen the heavens, I began to realize that there could not be much foundation for any other theory, and even theories must have foundations. I also realized, even more than I had before, something of what astronomy has meant to the human race of earth in the advancement of science and civilization. Could there have been such advancement had the heavens been perpetually hidden from our view? I wonder.
But I did not give up. I drew his attention to the fact that if his theory were correct, the boundary between Trabol and Strabol (the temperate and the equatorial zones) should be much shorter than that separating Trabol from Karbol, the polar region, as was shown on the map, but could not have been proved by actual survey; while my theory would require that the exact opposite be true, which was easily demonstrable and must have been demonstrated if surveys had ever been made, which I judged from the markings on the map to be the case.
He admitted that surveys had been made and that they had shown the apparent discrepancy that I had pointed out, but he explained this ingeniously by a purely Amtorian theory of the relativity of distance, which he proceeded to elucidate.

===Chinese===
起初我怎么也想不明白，一个如此聪明、有教养、有文化的人竟会抱着他那种信念不放；可当我停下来想想，他和他的祖辈们从未见过头顶的苍穹，我才开始意识到，别的任何理论恐怕都没有多少根基——而即便理论，也得有根基才行。同时我也比以往更深切地体会到，天文学对地球人类的科学与文明之进步意味着什么。倘若头顶的苍穹永远被遮蔽，我们还能有那样的进步吗？我不禁这样想。
可我没有放弃。我提醒他注意：倘若他的理论成立，那么特拉博尔与斯特拉博尔（温带与赤道带）之间的边界，理应远短于特拉博尔与极地的卡博尔之间的边界——这一点地图上有所显示，却无法用实测证明；而我的理论则要求截然相反的情形，这一点易于验证，倘若实测做过便必已验证过——我从地图上的标记判断，实测是做过的。
他承认实测是做过的，也确曾显示出我所指出的那种表面上的出入，但他巧妙地用一套纯阿姆托式的「距离相对论」加以解释，并着手向我阐明。

===Original===
“A degree is one thousandth part of the circumference of a circle,” he commenced. (This is the Amtorian degree, her savants not having had the advantage of a visible sun to suggest another division of the circumference of a circle as did the Babylonians, who hit upon three hundred sixty as being close enough.) “And no matter what the length of the circumference, it measures just one thousand degrees. The circle which separates Strabol from Trabol is necessarily one thousand degrees in length. You will admit that?”
“Certainly,” I replied.
“Very good! Then, will you admit that the circle which separates Trabol from Karbol measures exactly one thousand degrees?”
I nodded my assent.

===Chinese===
「一度，是一个圆周的一千分之一，」他开讲。（这是阿姆托的度，他们的学者没有可见的太阳这一便利——可见的太阳曾启发巴比伦人把圆周分为三百六十度，他们觉得三百六十已足够近似——从而想到别的分法。）「而无论圆周多长，它都恰为一千度。分隔斯特拉博尔与特拉博尔的圆，其长度必然是一千度。这一点你承认吧？」
「当然。」我答。
「很好！那么，你是否承认，分隔特拉博尔与卡博尔的圆，恰为一千度？」
我点头表示同意。

===Original===
“Things which equal the same thing equal each other, do they not? Therefore, the inner and outer boundaries of Trabol are of equal length, and this is true because of the truth of the theory of relativity of distance. The degree is our unit of linear measure. It would be ridiculous to say that the farther one was removed from the center of Amtor the longer the unit of distance became; it only appears to become longer; in relation to the circumference of the circle and in relation to the distance from the center of Amtor it is precisely the same.
“I know,” he admitted, “that on the map it does not appear to be the same, nor do actual surveys indicate that it is the same; but it must be the same, for if it were not, it is obvious that Amtor would be larger around the closer one approached the center and smallest of all at the perimeter, which is so obviously ridiculous as to require no refutation.
“This seeming discrepancy caused the ancients considerable perturbation until about three thousand years ago, when Klufar, the great scientist, expounded the theory of relativity of distance and demonstrated that the real and apparent measurements of distance could be reconciled by multiplying each by the square root of minus one.”
I saw that argument was useless and said no more; there is no use arguing with a man who can multiply anything by the square root of minus one.

===Chinese===
「等量加等量，其和相等，对吧？因此，特拉博尔的内外两条边界长度相等，而这一点之所以成立，是因为距离相对论是真理。度，是我们的线性计量单位。倘若说离阿姆托中心越远、单位距离便越长，那便是荒谬的；它只是看上去变长了；相对于圆周、相对于离阿姆托中心的距离而言，它恰恰是一样的。
「我知道，」他承认，「在地图上它看上去并不一样，实测也显示不一样；但它必然是一样的，因为倘若不然，那么显然，越接近阿姆托中心，圆周便越大，而在最外缘处最小——这显然荒谬得无须反驳。
「这一看似矛盾之处曾令古人相当困惑，直到约三千年前的伟大科学家克卢法尔（Klufar）阐明了距离相对论，并证明真实的与表观的距离测量可以通过各自乘以负一的平方根来调和。」
我明白再争下去也是徒劳，便不再多言；跟一个能把任何东西乘以负一的平方根的人争论，是毫无意义的。

## V The Girl in the Garden / V 花园里的姑娘

===Original===
For some time I had been aware that I was in the house of Mintep, the king, and that the country was called Vepaja. *Jong*, which I had originally thought to be his name, was his title; it is Amtorian for king. I learned that Duran was of the house of Zar and that Olthar and Kamlot were his sons; Zuro, one of the women I had met there, was attached to Duran; the other, Alzo, was attached to Olthar; Kamlot had no woman. I use the word attached partially because it is a reasonably close translation of the Amtorian word for the connection and partially because no other word seems exactly to explain the relationship between these men and women.
They were not married, because the institution of marriage is unknown here. One could not say that they belonged to the men, because they were in no sense slaves or servants, nor had they been acquired by purchase or feat of arms. They had come willingly, following a courtship, and they were free to depart whenever they chose, just as the men were free to depart and seek other connections; but, as I was to learn later, these connections are seldom broken, while infidelity is as rare here as it is prevalent on earth.

===Chinese===
早些时候起，我便知道我身在国王明特普（Mintep）的居所，而这个国度叫韦帕贾（Vepaja）。「君（Jong）」——我最初以为是他的名字——其实是他的头衔；它是阿姆托语里「国王」的意思。我得知杜兰出自扎尔（Zar）家族，奥尔塔和卡姆洛特是他的儿子；我在那里见过的女子之一祖罗，与杜兰相伴；另一位阿尔佐，与奥尔塔相伴；卡姆洛特没有女子相伴。我用「相伴」一词，一半是因为它相当贴切地译出了表达这种结合的阿姆托词，一半是因为再没有别的词能恰如其分地说明这些男女之间的关系。
他们并非结发夫妻，因为此地不知婚姻这一制度。也不能说他们从属于那些男人，因为他们绝不是奴仆，也并非凭买卖或武力抢来。她们是经求爱之后自愿来的，也随时可自由离去，一如那些男人也可自由离去、另寻良缘；然而，我后来才知道，这种结合很少破裂，而忠贞不贰在这里的少见程度，一如它在地球上的泛滥程度。

===Original===
Each day I took exercise on the broad veranda that encircled the tree at the level upon which my apartment was located; at least, I assumed that it encircled the tree, but I did not know, as that portion assigned to me was but a hundred feet long, a fifteenth part of the circumference of the great tree. At each end of my little segment was a fence. The section adjoining mine on the right appeared to be a garden, as it was a mass of flowers and shrubbery growing in soil that must have been brought up from that distant surface of the planet that I had as yet neither set foot upon nor seen. The section on my left extended in front of the quarters of several young officers attached to the household of the king. I call them young because Danus told me they were young, but they appear to be about the same age as all the other Amtorians I have seen. They were pleasant fellows, and after I learned to speak their language we occasionally had friendly chats together.
But in the section at my right I had never seen a human being; and then one day, when Danus was absent and I was walking alone, I saw a girl among the flowers there. She did not see me; and I only caught the briefest glimpse of her, but there was something about her that made me want to see her again, and thereafter I rather neglected the young officers on my left.

===Chinese===
每天，我都在那条环绕巨树、与我住所同层的宽阔回廊上活动筋骨；至少我假定它是环树的，但我并不清楚，因为分给我的那一段不过一百英尺长，是这棵巨树周长的十五分之一。我这小段的两端各有一道栅栏。右边与我相邻的那一段似乎是一座花园，因为那里满是花木，长在一种想必是从行星遥远的地面运来的土壤里——那地面我至今既未涉足，也未曾看见。左边那一段则从几名隶属国王府邸的年轻军官的住处前经过。我称他们「年轻」，是因为达努斯告诉我他们年轻，可他们看上去与我见过的其他阿姆托人年纪相仿。他们都和蔼可亲，待我学会了他们的语言，我们偶尔也闲聊几句，甚是融洽。
但右边那一段，我从未见过人影；直到有一天，达努斯不在，我独自踱步时，看见那里的花丛中有一位姑娘。她没瞧见我；我也只瞥了她极短的一瞬，可她身上有某种东西，使我想再见她一面，此后我便颇有些冷落了左边那些年轻军官。

===Original===
Though I haunted the end of my veranda next the garden for several days, I did not again see the girl during all that time. The place seemed utterly deserted until one day I saw the figure of a man among the shrubbery. He was moving with great caution, creeping stealthily; and presently, behind him, I saw another and another, until I had counted five of them all together.
They were similar to the Vepajans, yet there was a difference. They appeared coarser, more brutal, than any of the men I had as yet seen; and in other ways they were dissimilar to Danus, Duran, Kamlot, and my other Venusan acquaintances. There was something menacing and sinister, too, in their silent, stealthy movements.
I wondered what they were doing there; and then I thought of the girl, and for some reason the conclusion was forced upon me that the presence of these men here had something to do with her, and that it boded her harm. Just in what way I could not even surmise, knowing so little of the people among whom fate had thrown me; but the impression was quite definite, and it excited me. Perhaps it rather overcame my better judgment, too, if my next act is an index to the matter.

===Chinese===
虽说我接连几天都在紧挨花园的回廊那一头徘徊，可那段时间里再没见过那姑娘。那地方似乎完全荒着，直到有一天，我看见灌木丛中有一条人影。他异常小心地移动着，蹑手蹑脚；不多时，在他身后我又看见一个、又一个，直到我数出总共五个人。
他们与韦帕贾人相像，却又有所不同。他们比我迄今所见任何男子都显得粗鲁、残暴；在别的一些方面，他们与达努斯、杜兰、卡姆洛特以及我结识的其他金星熟人也截然不同。他们那悄然无声、蹑手蹑脚的行动中，更透着一种危险而阴险的味道。
我心下纳罕他们在此做什么；继而我想到了那姑娘，不知为何，一个念头油然而生：这些人出现在此必与姑娘有关，且对她不怀好意。具体是怎样，我无从揣测——命运将我抛到这些人中间，我对他们所知尚浅；但这印象却十分笃定，让我心绪难平。倘若我下一步的举动可作为印证，那么这念头恐怕也压过了我的理智。

===Original===
Without thought of the consequences and in total ignorance of the identity of the men or the purpose for which they were in the garden, I vaulted the low fence and followed them. I made no noise. They had not seen me originally because I had been hidden from their view by a larger shrub that grew close to the fence that separated the garden from my veranda. It was through the foliage of this shrub that I had observed them, myself unobserved.
Moving cautiously but swiftly, I soon overtook the hindmost man and saw that the five were moving toward an open doorway beyond which, in a richly furnished apartment, I saw the girl who had aroused my curiosity and whose beautiful face had led me into this mad adventure. Almost simultaneously, the girl glanced up and saw the leading man at the doorway. She screamed, and then I knew that I had not come in vain.

===Chinese===
我顾不上想后果，对这些人的身份、他们来花园的目的一无所知，便一跃翻过那道低矮的栅栏，跟了上去。我没弄出半点声响。起初他们没看见我，是因为紧挨花园与我的回廊之间那道栅栏旁，长着一丛较大的灌木，把我遮住了。我便是透过这丛灌木的枝叶观察他们的，而他们并未察觉。
我小心翼翼却迅捷地移动着，很快便追上了殿后那人，只见这五人正朝一扇敞开的门移动，门内一间陈设华美的屋里，我看见了那位勾起我好奇、又因那一张美丽脸庞把我卷入这场鲁莽冒险的姑娘。几乎与此同时，姑娘抬眼，看见了门口打头的那人。她尖叫起来，这一刻我便知道：我没白来。

===Original===
Instantly I leaped upon the man in front of me, and as I did so I gave a great shout, hoping by that means to distract the attention of the other four from the girl to me, and in that I was wholly successful. The other four turned instantly. I had taken my man so completely by surprise that I was able to snatch his sword from its scabbard before he could recover his wits; and as he drew his dagger and struck at me, I ran his own blade through his heart; then the others were upon me.
Their faces were contorted by rage, and I could see that they would give me no quarter.

===Chinese===
我当即扑向我身前那人，扑过去的同时大喝一声，指望借此把另外四人的注意力从姑娘身上引到我身上——这一点我完全做到了。另外四人立时回身。我把眼前这人攻了个猝不及防，趁他还没回过神，便从他鞘里抽出了他的剑；他拔出匕首朝我刺来，我则用他自己的剑刺穿了他的心；随即其他人便一齐扑上。
他们脸上因狂怒而扭曲，我看得出他们不会饶我。

===Original===
The narrow spaces between the shrubbery reduced the advantage which four men would ordinarily have had over a single antagonist, for they could attack me only singly; but I knew what the outcome must eventually be if help did not reach me, and as my only goal was to keep the men from the girl, I backed slowly toward the fence and my own veranda as I saw that all four of the men were following me.
My shout and the girl’s scream had attracted attention; and presently I heard men running in the apartment in which I had seen the girl, and her voice directing them toward the garden. I hoped they would come before the fellows had backed me against the wall, where I was confident that I must go down in defeat beneath four swords wielded by men more accustomed to them than I. I thanked the good fortune, however, that had led me to take up fencing seriously in Germany, for it was helping me now, though I could not long hold out against these men with the Venusan sword which was a new weapon to me.

===Chinese===
灌木丛之间狭窄的空隙，削弱了四人对付一人通常享有的优势，因为他们只能鱼贯而上、一个一个地攻我；可我也明白，倘若没有援手，结局迟早会怎样；而我唯一的目标是不让这些人接近那姑娘，于是在看见四人一齐逼上来时，我便缓缓向栅栏和我自己的回廊退去。
我那一声大喝和姑娘的尖叫惊动了旁人；不多时我便听见姑娘那间屋里有奔跑声，还有她的声音在指挥众人朝花园来。我指望他们在我被逼到墙根之前赶到——一旦被逼到墙根，我笃定会败在四柄剑下，更何况使剑的人比我更惯于此道。然而我庆幸自己的好运气，庆幸当年在德国认真练过击剑，此刻它正帮着我，尽管用一柄对我来说全然陌生的金星剑，我抵挡不了这些人多久。

===Original===
I had reached the fence at last and was fighting with my back toward it. The fellow facing me was cutting viciously at me. I could hear the men coming from the apartment Could I hold out? Then my opponent swung a terrific cut at my head, and, instead of parrying it, I leaped to one side and simultaneously stepped in and cut at him. His own swing had carried him off balance, and, of course, his guard was down. My blade cut deep into his neck, severing his jugular. From behind him another man was rushing upon me.
Relief was coming. The girl was safe. I could accomplish no more by remaining there and being cut to pieces, a fate I had only narrowly averted in the past few seconds. I hurled my sword, point first, at the oncoming Venusan; and as it tore into his breast I turned and vaulted the fence into my own veranda.

===Chinese===
我终于退到了栅栏边，背靠着它应战。与我相对的那人正凶狠地朝我劈砍。我听得见屋里的那些人正赶来——我能撑住吗？这时我对手朝我头顶狠狠一剑劈来，我不去招架，反而向旁一跃，同时贴身上前一剑。他自己的那一劈已使他失了重心，自然门户大开。我的剑深深切入他的脖颈，斩断了他的颈静脉。他身后又有一人朝我扑来。
援兵已到。姑娘安全了。再留在此地被砍成肉酱已无益——过去几秒里这一命运只差一线便落在我头上。我把剑尖朝前一掷，掷向那扑来的金星人；剑扎进他胸膛之际，我转身翻过栅栏，回到自己的回廊。

===Original===
Then, as I looked back, I saw a dozen Vepajan warriors overwhelm the two remaining intruders, butchering them like cattle. There was no shouting and no sound other than the brief clash of swords as the two sought desperately but futilely to defend themselves. The Vepajans spoke no word. They seemed shocked and terrified, though their terror had most certainly not been the result of any fear of their late antagonists. There was something else which I did not understand, something mysterious in their manner, their silence, and their actions immediately following the encounter.
Quickly they seized the bodies of the five strange warriors that had been killed and carrying them to the outer garden wall, hurled them over into that bottomless abyss of the forest the terrific depths of which my eyes had never been able to plumb. Then, in equal silence, they departed from the garden by the same path by which they had entered it.
I realized that they had not seen me, and I knew that the girl had not. I wondered a little how they accounted for the deaths of the three men I had disposed of, but I never learned. The whole affair was a mystery to me and was only explained long after in the light of ensuing events.

===Chinese===
这时我回头一望，只见十几个韦帕贾武士一拥而上，压倒了剩下的两个闯入者，像宰牲口一样把他们宰了。没有呼喝，也没有别的声响，唯有两人拼死却徒劳地自卫时，剑与剑短暂的相击。韦帕贾人一言不发。他们似乎既震惊又恐惧，可那恐惧显然不是因为害怕方才那些对手。他们的神情、他们的沉默、以及交锋之后他们紧接着的举动里，还有别的什么我不懂的东西，透着神秘。
他们麻利地扛起那五个被杀的陌生武士的尸首，抬到花园外墙边，扔进了下面那片深不见底的森林深渊——那骇人的深度，我的眼睛从未能望穿。接着，他们又同样沉默地，沿进来时走的路离开了花园。
我意识到他们没有看见我，也知道那姑娘没看见我。我多少有点好奇：他们会怎样解释被我解决掉的那三人之死呢？可我始终不得而知。这整件事对我是个谜，直到很久以后，借着后续发生的事才得以解开。

===Original===
I thought that Danus might mention it and thus give me an opportunity to question him; but he never did, and something kept me from broaching the subject to him, modesty perhaps. In other respects, however, my curiosity concerning these people was insatiable; and I fear that I bored Danus to the verge of distraction with my incessant questioning, but I excused myself on the plea that I could only learn the language by speaking it and hearing it spoken; and Danus, that most delightful of men, insisted that it was not only a pleasure to inform me but his duty as well, the *jong* having requested him to inform me fully concerning the life, customs, and history of the Vepajans.
One of the many things that puzzled me was why such an intelligent and cultured people should be living in trees, apparently without servants or slaves and with no intercourse, as far as I had been able to discover, with other peoples; so one evening I asked him.

===Chinese===
我原以为达努斯或许会提起这事，从而给我个机会盘问他；可他从未提起，而某种东西——也许是羞怯——也让我迟迟未向他开口。然而在别的方面，我对这些人的好奇简直贪得无厌；我怕我连珠炮似的发问已把达努斯烦得几近抓狂，但我为自己开脱说，唯有开口说、听见人说，我才能学会这门语言；而最可爱的达努斯则坚持说，告知我不仅是他的乐事，更是他的职责——因为「君」曾嘱他向我详尽介绍韦帕贾人的生活、风俗和历史。
我许多想不通的事中，有一桩是：如此聪明而又有教养的民族，为何要住在树上，似乎既无仆从也无奴隶，而且就我所知，也不与其他民族往来；于是一天傍晚，我问他。

===Original===
“It is a long story,” replied Danus; “much of it you will find in the histories here upon my shelves, but I can give you a brief outline that will at least answer your question.
“Hundreds of years ago the kings of Vepaja ruled a great country. It was not this forest island where you now find us, but a broad empire that embraced a thousand islands and extended from Strabol to Karbol; it included broad land masses and great oceans; it was graced by mighty cities and boasted a wealth and commerce unsurpassed through all the centuries before or since.
“The people of Vepaja in those days were numbered in the millions; there were millions of merchants and millions of wage earners and millions of slaves, and there was a smaller class of brain workers. This class included the learned professions of science, medicine, and law, of letters and the creative arts. The military leaders were selected from all classes. Over all was the hereditary *jong*.

===Chinese===
「说来话长，」达努斯答道，「其中很多你可以在我的书架上那些史书里找到，不过我可以给你简略地勾勒一下，至少能解答你的疑问。
「数百年前，韦帕贾的国王们统治着一个大国。它并非你如今见到我们的这座森林岛屿，而是一个广袤的帝国，辖一千座岛屿，从斯特拉博尔一直延伸到卡博尔；它包括辽阔的陆地和浩瀚的大洋；它以雄伟的城池为荣，其富庶与商贸之盛，为古往今来所无出其右。
「那时候的韦帕贾人民数以百万计；商人成百万，靠工钱过活的成百万，奴隶也成百万，此外还有一个较小的脑力劳动者阶层。这个阶层包括科学、医学、法律、文学和创作艺术这些学问行当。军事领袖则从各阶层选拔。凌驾于一切之上的，是世袭的「君」。

===Original===
“The lines between the classes were neither definitely nor strictly drawn; a slave might become a free man, a free man might become anything he chose within the limits of his ability, short of *jong*. In social intercourse the four principal classes did not intermingle with each other, due to the fact that members of one class had little in common with members of the other classes and not through any feeling of superiority or inferiority. When a member of a lower class had won by virtue of culture, learning, or genius to a position in a higher class, he was received upon an equal footing, and no thought was given to his antecedents.
“Vepaja was prosperous and happy, yet there were malcontents. These were the lazy and incompetent. Many of them were of the criminal class. They were envious of those who had won to positions which they were not mentally equipped to attain. Over a long period of time they were responsible for minor discord and dissension, but the people either paid no attention to them or laughed them down. Then they found a leader. He was a laborer named Thor, a man with a criminal record.

===Chinese===
「各阶层之间的界限并不那么明确、那么严格；奴隶可以成为自由人，自由人只要能力所及，可以成为任何他想成为的人——除了「君」。在社交往来中，四个主要阶层彼此并不混迹一处，这是因为一阶层的人与另一阶层的人本就没什么共同语言，而并非出于什么高低贵贱之感。当较低阶层的一员凭教养、学识或才华攀升到较高阶层的位置时，他便以平等之姿被接纳，其出身无人置喙。
「韦帕贾富庶而安乐，然而也有心怀不满之人。他们便是那些懒惰而无能之辈。其中不少属于犯罪阶层。他们嫉妒那些凭本事赢得了他们智力所不及之高位的人。在漫长的岁月里，他们制造些小风波、小龃龉，可人们要么对他们置之不理，要么一笑置之把他们笑下去。后来他们找到了一个领袖。他是个名叫索尔（Thor）的劳工，一个有犯罪前科的人。

===Original===
“This man founded a secret order known as Thorists and preached a gospel of class hatred called Thorism. By means of lying propaganda he gained a large following, and as all his energies were directed against a single class, he had all the vast millions of the other three classes to draw from, though naturally he found few converts among the merchants and employers which also included the agrarian class.
“The sole end of the Thorist leaders was personal power and aggrandizement; their aims were wholly selfish, yet, because they worked solely among the ignorant masses, they had little difficulty in deceiving their dupes, who finally rose under their false leaders in a bloody revolution that sounded the doom of the civilization and advancement of a world.
“Their purpose was the absolute destruction of the cultured class. Those of the other classes who opposed them were to be subjugated or destroyed; the *jong* and his family were to be killed. These things accomplished, the people would enjoy absolute freedom; there would be no masters, no taxes, no laws.

===Chinese===
「此人创立了一个名为索尔党（Thorists）的秘密组织，宣扬一套称为索尔主义（Thorism）的阶级仇恨福音。凭着一整套谎言式的宣传，他赢得了大批追随者；由于他的全部火力都指向单一阶层，他便可从其他三个阶层那成百上千万人中招兵买马——虽说在商人、雇主（也包括务农的阶层）中他自然发展不到几个信徒。
「索尔党领袖们的唯一目的，是个人权势与扩张；他们的目标全然自私，然而正因为他们只在无知大众中间活动，要蒙骗那些上当的人并不难——这些人终于在那些伪领袖的带领下揭竿而起，发动了一场血腥革命，为一个世界的文明与进步敲响了丧钟。
「他们的目的是彻底摧毁文化阶层。其他阶层中反对他们的人，要被征服或消灭；「君」及其家人要被处死。这些事一旦做成，人民便可享受绝对的自由；不再有主人，不再有赋税，不再有法律。

===Original===
“They succeeded in killing most of us and a large proportion of the merchant class; then the people discovered what the agitators already knew, that someone must rule, and the leaders of Thorism were ready to take over the reins of government. The people had exchanged the beneficent rule of an experienced and cultured class for that of greedy incompetents and theorists.
“Now they are all reduced to virtual slavery. An army of spies watches over them, and an army of warriors keeps them from turning against their masters; they are miserable, helpless, and hopeless.
“Those of us who escaped with our *jong* sought out this distant, uninhabited island. Here we constructed tree cities, such as this, far above the ground, from which they cannot be seen. We brought our culture with us and little else; but our wants are few, and we are happy. We would not return to the old system if we might. We have learned our lesson, that a people divided amongst themselves cannot be happy. Where there are even slight class distinctions there are envy and jealousy. Here there are none; we are all of the same class. We have no servants; whatever there is to do we do better than servants ever did it. Even those who serve the *jong* are not servants in the sense that they are menials, for their positions are considered posts of honor, and the greatest among us take turns in filling them.”

===Chinese===
「他们杀了我们中的大多数，也杀了商人阶层中的很大一部分；这时人民才发现，煽动者们早就知道的那件事：总得有人来统治。而索尔主义的领袖们早已蓄势待发，要接管政权。人民用一个有经验、有文化的阶层的仁政，换来了贪婪的无能之辈和空想家的统治。
「如今他们全都沦为事实上的奴隶。一支密探大军监视着他们，一支武士大军阻止他们反咬主人一口；他们悲惨、无助、绝望。
「我们这些随「君」逃出来的人，寻到了这座遥远而无人居住的岛屿。在这里，我们在远离地面的高处建造了像这样的树城，使他们看不见。我们把文化随身带来，除此之外所带无多；可我们所需甚少，且我们快乐。纵使我们能回去，我们也不愿再回到旧制度。我们吸取了教训：一个内部分裂的民族不可能幸福。哪里有哪怕些微的阶级之分，哪里就有嫉妒与眼红。这里没有；我们都是同一阶层。我们没有仆人；凡是要做的事，我们都做得比仆人更好。即便是侍奉「君」的人，也不是奴仆意义上的仆人——他们的职位被视为荣誉之职，我们中最了不起的人也轮流担任。」

===Original===
“But I still do not understand why you choose to live in trees, far above the ground,” I said.
“For years the Thorists hunted us down to kill us,” he explained, “and we were forced to live in hidden, inaccessible places; this type of city was the solution of our problem. The Thorists still hunt us; and there are still occasional raids, but now they are for a very different purpose. Instead of wishing to kill us, they now wish to capture as many of us as they can.
“Having killed or driven away the brains of the nation, their civilization has deteriorated, disease is making frightful inroads upon them which they are unable to check, old age has reappeared and is taking its toll; so they seek to capture the brains and the skill and the knowledge which they have been unable to produce and which we alone possess.”

===Chinese===
「可我还是不明白，你们为什么偏要住在树上、住在远离地面的高处。」我说。
「多年来，索尔党一直在追捕我们、要杀我们，」他解释道，「我们被迫栖身在隐蔽难至之处；这种类型的城市便是我们问题的解决之道。索尔党至今仍在追捕我们；偶有袭击，可如今目的已大不相同。他们不再想杀我们，而是想尽量多地活捉我们。
「他们杀掉了、或者赶走了国家的头脑，他们的文明便衰落了，疾病正可怕地侵蚀着他们，他们束手无策；衰老重新出现，开始收取它的代价；于是他们企图掠取那些他们无法自行产出、而我们独有的头脑、技艺与知识。」

===Original===
“Old age is reappearing! What do you mean?” I asked.
“Have you not noticed that there are no signs of old age among us?” he inquired.
“Yes, of course,” I replied, “nor any children. I have often meant to ask you for an explanation.”
“These are not natural phenomena,” he assured me; “they are the crowning achievements of medical science. A thousand years ago the serum of longevity was perfected. It is injected every two years and not only provides immunity from all diseases but insures the complete restoration of all wasted tissue.
“But even in good there is evil. As none grew old and none died, except those who met with violent death, we were faced with the grave dangers of overpopulation. To combat this, birth control became obligatory. Children are permitted now only in sufficient numbers to replace actual losses in population. If a member of a house is killed, a woman of that house is permitted to bear a child, if she can; but after generations of childlessness there is a constantly decreasing number of women who are capable of bearing children. This situation we have met by anticipating it.

===Chinese===
「衰老重新出现！你这话怎讲？」我问。
「你难道没注意到，我们中间没有任何衰老的迹象吗？」他反问。
「是，当然注意到了，」我答，「也没看见小孩。我常想问你个中究竟。」
「这些可不是自然现象，」他向我保证，「这是医学的最高成就。一千年前，长寿血清研制成功。每两年注射一次，不仅能免疫一切疾病，还能确保一切损耗的组织完全复原。
「然而福中亦有祸。既然无人衰老、无人死亡——除非横死——我们便面临着人口过剩的严重危险。为应对这一局面，节制生育成了硬性规定。如今只允许生育足够补充人口实际损失数目的孩子。一户中若有人被杀，这户的一名女子便获准生育一个孩子，倘若她能的话；可经过一代又一代的少生少育，能生育的女子数目不断减少。这一局面，我们靠未雨绸缪来应对。

===Original===
“Statistics compiled over a period of a thousand years indicate the average death rate expectancy per thousand people; they have also demonstrated that only fifty percent of our women are capable of bearing children; therefore, fifty percent of the required children are permitted yearly to those who wish them, in the order in which their applications are filed.”
“I have not seen a child since I arrived in Amtor,” I told him.
“There are children here,” he replied, “but, of course, not many.”
“And no old people,” I mused. “Could you administer that serum to me, Danus?”
He smiled. “With Mintep’s permission, which I imagine will not be difficult to obtain. Come,” he added, “I’ll take some blood tests now to determine the type and attenuation of serum best adapted to your requirements.” He motioned me into his laboratory.

===Chinese===
「根据一千年间积累的统计，可推知每千人的平均预期死亡率；这些统计也表明，我们的女子中只有百分之五十能生育；因此，每年所需的孩童名额中，百分之五十按提出申请的先后顺序，分给那些想要孩子的人。」
「我来到阿姆托后，还从未见过一个孩子。」我对他说。
「这里是有孩子的，」他答，「不过，当然，不多。」
「也没有老人。」我沉吟道，「达努斯，你能把那种血清也给我注射吗？」
他笑了。「只要明特普点头，我想这不难办到。来，」他补充道，「我现在就给你验验血，定下最适合你的血清种类和稀释度。」他招我进了他的实验室。

===Original===
When he had completed the tests, which he accomplished with ease and rapidity, he was shocked by the variety and nature of malignant bacteria they revealed.
“You are a menace to the continued existence of human life on Amtor,” he exclaimed with a laugh.
“I am considered a very healthy man in my own world,” I assured him.
“How old are you?” he asked.
“Twenty-seven.”
“You would not be so healthy two hundred years from now if all those bacteria were permitted to have their way with you.”
“How old might I live to be if they were eradicated?” I asked.
He shrugged. “We do not know. The serum was perfected a thousand years ago. There are people among us today who were of the first to receive injections. I am over five hundred years old; Mintep is seven hundred. We believe that, barring accidents, we shall live forever; but, of course, we do not know. Theoretically, we should.”

===Chinese===
他做完了化验——动作熟练而迅捷——却被化验所揭示的恶性细菌的种类和性质吓了一跳。
「你对阿姆托上人类生命的延续是个威胁。」他笑着叫道。
「在我的世界里，我可是被认为十分健康的。」我向他保证。
「你多大年纪？」他问。
「二十七。」
「要是任由这些细菌为所欲为，两百年后你可就不会这么健康了。」
「要是把它们清除掉，我能活到多大岁数？」我问。
他耸耸肩。「我们不晓得。血清是一千年前研制出来的。今天我们之中还有人，是当初第一批接受注射的。我五百多岁；明特普七百岁。我们相信，不出意外，我们将永生不死；可当然，我们并不确知。理论上应该如此。」

===Original===
He was called away at this juncture; and I went out on the veranda to take my exercise, of which I have found that I require a great deal, having always been athletically inclined. Swimming, boxing, and wrestling had strengthened and developed my muscles since I had returned to America with my mother when I was eleven, and I became interested in fencing while I was travelling in Europe after she died. During my college days I was amateur middleweight boxer of California, and I captured several medals for distance swimming; so the inforced inactivity of the past two months had galled me considerably. Toward the end of my college days I had grown into the heavyweight class, but that had been due to an increase of healthy bone and sinew; now I was at least twenty pounds heavier and that twenty pounds was all fat.
On my one hundred feet of veranda I did the best I could to reduce. I ran miles, I shadow boxed, I skipped rope, and I spent hours with the old seventeen setting-up exercises of drill regulations. Today I was shadow boxing near the right end of my veranda when I suddenly discovered the girl in the garden observing me. As our eyes met I halted in my tracks and smiled at her. A frightened look came into her eyes, and she turned and fled. I wondered why.

===Chinese===
这时有人把他叫走了；我出到回廊上锻炼身体——我发现我极需运动，因为我一向好动。自从十一岁随母亲回到美国，游泳、拳击、摔跤便锻炼、塑造了我的肌肉；母亲去世后我在欧洲旅行时又对击剑起了兴致。大学时代我是加州业余中量级拳击冠军，还得过几枚长距离游泳的奖牌；所以过去两个月的强制清闲，让我憋得够呛。大学后期我已长成重量级，但那是因为筋骨的健全增长；而此刻我至少重了二十磅，这二十磅全是肥肉。
在我那一百英尺的回廊上，我尽力减肥。我跑了一英里又一英里，我挥空拳，我跳绳，我还花不少时间做操典里那十七节老式的徒手操。今天我正在回廊右端挥空拳，猛然发现花园里那位姑娘正在打量我。我们四目相对时，我收住步子，朝她一笑。她眼中掠过一丝惊惶，转身便逃。我纳闷为什么。

===Original===
Puzzled, I walked slowly back toward my apartment, my exercises forgotten. This time I had seen the girl’s full face, looked her squarely in the eyes, and I had been absolutely dumbfounded by her beauty. Every man and woman I had seen since I had come to Venus had been beautiful; I had come to expect that. But I had not expected to see in this or any other world such indescribable perfection of coloring and features, combined with character and intelligence, as that which I had just seen in the garden beyond my little fence. But why had she run away when I smiled?
Possibly she had run away merely because she had been discovered watching me for, after all, human nature is about the same everywhere. Even twenty-six million miles from earth there are human beings like ourselves and a girl, with quite human curiosity, who runs away when she is discovered. I wondered if she resembled earthly girls in other respects, but she seemed too beautiful to be just like anything on earth or in heaven. Was she young or old? Suppose she were seven hundred years old!

===Chinese===
我满腹狐疑，把锻炼忘在脑后，慢慢踱回住所。这一回我看清了姑娘的整张脸，与她正正地对视，被她的美彻底惊得说不出话。自我来到金星，所见男女无不貌美；我已习以为常。但我万万没想到，会在这个世界或任何别的世界，看见这样难以言喻的、肤色与五官的完美，并伴之以品性与聪慧——我刚刚在小栅栏那头的花园里所见到的，正是如此。可我一笑，她为何要逃？
也许她逃开，不过是因为偷看我时被发现了——毕竟天下人性，到处都差不多。即便离地球两千六百万英里之遥，也有和我们一样的人类，也有一个满怀人之常情的好奇心的姑娘，被发现时便逃开。我暗想她在别的方面是否也像地球姑娘，可她美得太甚，似乎不该与地上或天上的任何东西一样。她是年轻还是年迈？万一她已经七百岁了呢！

===Original===
I went to my apartment and prepared to bathe and change my loincloth; I had long since adopted the apparel of Amtor. As I glanced in a mirror that hangs in my bathroom I suddenly understood why the girl may have looked frightened and run away﻿—my beard! It was nearly a month old now and might easily have frightened anyone who had never before seen a beard.
When Danus returned I asked him what I could do about it. He stepped into another room and returned with a bottle of salve.
“Rub this into the roots of the hair on your face,” he directed, “but be careful not to get it on your eyebrows, lashes, or the hair on your head. Leave it there a minute and then wash your face.”
I stepped into my bathroom and opened the jar; its contents looked like vaseline and smelled like the devil, but I rubbed it into the roots of my beard as Danus had directed. When I washed my face a moment later my beard came off, leaving my face smooth and hairless. I hurried back to the room where I had left Danus.

===Chinese===
我回到住所，准备沐浴、换一条缠腰布；我早改穿阿姆托的装束了。当我瞥了一眼浴室里挂着的镜子，我忽然明白了那姑娘为何会显得惊惶而逃——我的胡子！它已将近一个月没刮，足可吓着任何从未见过胡子的人。
达努斯回来后，我问他可有办法。他走进另一间屋，拿来一瓶药膏。
「把这揉进脸上毛发的根部，」他嘱咐道，「但小心别沾到眉毛、睫毛，也别沾到头上的头发。抹上一会儿，再把脸洗净。」
我走进浴室，揭开罐子；里头的东西看上去像凡士林，闻起来却恶臭难当，但我还是按达努斯所嘱，把它揉进了胡子的根部。过一会儿我洗脸时，胡子竟整片脱落，脸变得光洁无毛。我赶忙回到方才与达努斯同在的那间屋。

===Original===
“You are quite handsome after all,” he remarked. “Do all the people of this fabulous world of which you have told me have hair growing on their faces?”
“Nearly all,” I replied, “but in my country the majority of men keep it shaved off.”
“I should think the women would be the ones to shave,” he commented. “A woman with hair on her face would be quite repulsive to an Amtorian.”
“But our women do not have hair on their faces,” I assured him.
“And the men do! A fabulous world indeed.”
“But if Amtorians do not grow beards, what was the need of this salve that you gave me?” I asked.
“It was perfected as an aid to surgery,” he explained. “In treating scalp wounds and in craniectomies it is necessary to remove the hair from about the wound. This unguent serves the purpose better than shaving and also retards the growth of new hair for a longer time.”
“But the hair will grow out again?” I asked.
“Yes, if you do not apply the unguent too frequently,” he replied.
“How frequently?” I demanded.
“Use it every day for six days and the hair will never again grow on your face. We used to use it on the heads of confirmed criminals. Whenever one saw a bald-headed man or a man wearing a wig he watched his valuables.”

===Chinese===
「原来你还挺英俊。」他说道，「你对我讲起的那个神奇世界里的人，脸上都长毛吗？」
「差不多都长，」我答，「不过在我国，大多数男人把它刮掉。」
「照我看，该剃的倒是女人，」他评论道，「一个女人脸上长毛，对阿姆托人来说简直令人作呕。」
「可我们的女人脸上不长毛。」我向他保证。
「男人倒长！真是个神奇的世界。」
「可既然阿姆托人不长胡子，你给我这药膏又有什么用呢？」我问。
「它是作为一种外科辅助手段研制出来的，」他解释，「在处理头皮伤口和开颅手术时，必须把伤口周围的毛发去掉。这种药膏比剃刀更顶事，还能更久地延缓新发长出。」
「可头发还会再长出来吧？」我问。
「会，只要你不把药膏抹得太勤。」他答。
「多勤？」我追问。
「连用六天、天天用，你脸上的毛就再也长不出来。我们从前把它用在惯犯头上。谁一看见个秃头或戴假发的，就得看好自己的财物。」

===Original===
“In my country when one sees a bald-headed man,” I said, “he watches his girls. And that reminds me; I have seen a beautiful girl in a garden just to the right of us here. Who is she?”
“She is one whom you are not supposed to see,” he replied. “Were I you, I should not again mention the fact that you have seen her. Did she see you?”
“She saw me,” I replied.
“What did she do?” His tone was serious.
“She appeared frightened and ran.”
“Perhaps you had best keep away from that end of the veranda,” he suggested.

===Chinese===
「在我国，人一看见个秃头，」我说，「就得看好自己的姑娘。这倒提醒我——就在我们右边的那座花园里，我见过一位美丽的姑娘。她是谁？」
「她不是你该见的人。」他答，「若我是你，便不再提见过她这回事。她看见你了吗？」
「看见我了。」我答。
「她怎么做的？」他语气凝重。
「她显得害怕，跑了。」
「也许你最好离回廊那一头远点。」他建议。

===Original===
There was that in his manner which precluded questions, and I did not pursue the subject further. Here was a mystery, the first suggestion of mystery that I had encountered in the life of Vepaja, and naturally it piqued my curiosity. Why should I not look at the girl? I had looked at other women without incurring displeasure. Was it only this particular girl upon whom I must not look, or were there other girls equally sacrosanct? It occurred to me that she might be a priestess of some holy order, but I was forced to discard that theory because of my belief that these people had no religion, at least none that I could discover in my talks with Danus. I had attempted to describe some of our earthly religious beliefs to him, but he simply could not perceive either their purpose or meaning any more than he could visualize the solar system of the universe.
Having once seen the girl, I was anxious to see her again; and now that the thing was proscribed, I was infinitely more desirous than ever to look upon her divine loveliness and to speak with her. I had not promised Danus that I would heed his suggestions, for I was determined to ignore them should the opportunity arise.

===Chinese===
他神情中有一种不容追问的东西，我便不再深究。这便是一个谜——我在韦帕贾的生活中所遇到的第一个谜团的影子——自然激起了我的好奇。为什么我不能看那位姑娘？我看其他女子时并未招致不快。是单单这一位姑娘我看不得，还是另有别的姑娘同样碰不得？我想到她也许是某个神圣教门的祭司，可我不得不放弃这一猜测，因为我相信这些人没有宗教——至少在我与达努斯的交谈中，我未能发现任何宗教的踪影。我曾试着向他描述地球上某些宗教信仰，可他完全无法领会其目的或含义，正如他无法想象宇宙的太阳系。
既已见过这位姑娘，我便急切地想再见她一面；如今这件事又被明令禁止，我反倒比从前更加千百倍地渴望再睹她那天仙般的容颜，与她交谈。我可没向达努斯允诺会听从他的劝告，因为我已下定决心，机会一旦出现，我便要将这劝告抛诸脑后。

===Original===
I was commencing to tire of the virtual imprisonment that had been my lot ever since my advent upon Amtor, for even a kindly jailer and a benign prison regime are not satisfactory substitutes for freedom. I had asked Danus what my status was and what they planned for me in the future, but he had evaded a more direct answer by saying that I was the guest of Mintep, the *jong*, and that my future would be a matter of discussion when Mintep granted me an audience.
Suddenly now I felt more than before the restrictions of my situation, and they galled me. I had committed no crime. I was a peaceful visitor to Vepaja. I had neither the desire nor the power to harm anyone. These considerations decided me. I determined to force the issue.
A few minutes ago I had been contented with my lot, willing to wait the pleasure of my hosts; now I was discontented. What had induced this sudden change? Could it be the mysterious alchemy of personality that had transmuted the lead of lethargy to the gold of ambitious desire? Had the aura of a vision of feminine loveliness thus instantly reversed my outlook upon life?

===Chinese===
