我渐渐对自来到阿姆托便如影随形、近乎软禁的处境感到厌倦，因为即便一个和善的狱卒、一个仁厚的牢狱，也无法令人满意地替代自由。我曾问达努斯我究竟是什么身份、他们对我将来有何打算，可他避而不答，只说我乃「君」明特普的客人，将来如何待我，待明特普召见我时再议。
此刻我比以往更强烈地感到自身处境的种种限制，它们让我憋闷。我并未犯罪。我是韦帕贾一个和平的访客。我既无加害任何人的念头，也无加害任何人的能力。这些考虑使我下了决心。我决定把事情挑明。
几分钟前我还安于现状，情愿等候主人的安排；此刻我却满心不满。是什么引发了这一骤变？莫非是那神秘的性格点金术，把怠惰之铅点成了雄心之金？莫非是一个女性之美幻影的光晕，便这样瞬间颠覆了我对人生的看法？

===Original===
I turned toward Danus. “You have been very kind to me,” I said, “and my days here have been happy, but I am of a race of people who desire freedom above all things. As I have explained to you, I am here through no intentional fault of my own; but I am here, and being here I expect the same treatment that would be accorded you were you to visit my country under similar circumstances.”
“And what treatment would that be?” he asked.
“The right to life, liberty, and the pursuit of happiness﻿—freedom,” I explained. I did not think it necessary to mention chambers of commerce dinners, Rotary and Kiwanis luncheons, triumphal parades and ticker tape, keys to cities, press representatives and photographers, nor news reel cameramen, the price that he would undoubtedly have had to pay for life, liberty, and the pursuit of happiness.
“But, my dear friend, one would think from your words that you are a prisoner here!” he exclaimed.
“I am, Danus,” I replied, “and none knows it better than you.”

===Chinese===
我转向达努斯。「你对我一向很好，」我说，「我在这里的日子也过得快活，可我是属于一个把自由看得高于一切的种族的人。我已向你解释过，我落到此地并非出于我有意犯下的过错；可我既然在此，便指望得到——倘若你在类似情形下造访我国，也会得到的——同等待遇。」
「那会是什么样的待遇呢？」他问。
「生命权、自由权，以及追求幸福的权利——自由。」我解释道。我觉得没必要提商会晚宴、扶轮社和同济会的午餐、凯旋游行和漫天彩带、城市钥匙、新闻代表和摄影师，以及新闻片摄影机——这些，他无疑要为生命、自由和追求幸福而付出的代价。
「可是，我亲爱的朋友，听你的话，倒像你在此地是个囚徒呢！」他叫道。
「我正是，达努斯，」我答，「这一点无人比你更清楚。」

===Original===
He shrugged. “I am sorry that you feel that way about it, Carson.”
“How much longer is it going to last?” I demanded.
“The *jong* is the *jong*,” he replied. “He will send for you in his own time; until then, let us continue the friendly relations that have marked our association up to now.”
“I hope they will never be changed, Danus,” I told him, “but you may tell Mintep, if you will, that I cannot accept his hospitality much longer; if he does not send for me soon, I shall leave on my own accord.”
“Do not attempt that, my friend,” he warned me.
“And why not?”
“You would not live to take a dozen steps from the apartments that have been assigned you,” he assured me seriously.
“Who would stop me?”
“There are warriors posted in the corridors,” he explained; “they have their orders from the *jong*.”
“And yet I am not a prisoner!” I exclaimed with a bitter laugh.
“I am sorry that you raised the question,” he said, “as otherwise you might never have known.”
Here indeed was the iron hand in the velvet glove. I hoped it was not wielded by a wolf in sheep’s clothing. My position was not an enviable one. Even had I the means to escape, there was no place that I could go. But I did not want to leave Vepaja﻿—I had seen the girl in the garden.

===Chinese===
他耸耸肩。「你这么想，我很遗憾，卡森。」
「这还要持续多久？」我追问。
「『君』便是『君』，」他答，「他自会在他认为合适的时候召见你；在那之前，让我们继续这一向以来的友好往来吧。」
「我希望永远不变，达努斯，」我对他说，「但若你愿意，不妨转告明特普：他的款待我不能再消受多久了；他若不早日召见我，我便自行离去。」
「别打这个主意，我的朋友。」他警告我。
「为什么不？」
「你从分配给你的住所走出去，迈不出十来步便会没命。」他郑重地向我保证。
「谁会拦我？」
「走廊里有武士把守，」他解释，「他们有『君』的命令。」
「可我还不是个囚徒！」我苦笑着叫道。
「可惜你提起了这事，」他说，「否则你也许永远都不会知道。」
这便是天鹅绒手套里的铁拳了。但愿它不是握在一只披着羊皮的狼手里。我的处境并不令人羡慕。纵使我有脱身之法，也无路可去。可我并不想离开韦帕贾——我已看见了花园里的那位姑娘。

## VI Gathering Tarel / VI 采集塔雷尔

===Original===
A week passed, a week during which I permanently discarded my reddish whiskers and received an injection of the longevity serum. The latter event suggested that possibly Mintep would eventually liberate me, for why bestow immortality upon a potential enemy who is one’s prisoner; but then I knew that the serum did not confer absolute immortality﻿—Mintep could have me destroyed if he wished, by which thought was suggested the possibility that the serum had been administered for the purpose of lulling me into a sense of security which I did not, in reality, enjoy. I was becoming suspicious.
While Danus was injecting the serum, I asked him if there were many doctors in Vepaja. “Not so many in proportion to the population as there were a thousand years ago,” he replied. “All the people are now trained in the care of their bodies and taught the essentials of health and longevity. Even without the serums we use to maintain resistance to disease constantly in the human body, our people would live to great ages. Sanitation, diet, and exercise can accomplish wonders by themselves.

===Chinese===
一个星期过去了。这一周里，我永久地舍弃了那部微红的络腮胡，并注射了长寿血清。后一件事，似乎暗示明特普终有一天会放我自由——何必把不朽之身赐给一个被囚的潜在敌人呢？可转念我又明白，血清所赐的并非绝对的不朽——明特普若想毁我，仍可毁我；由此我又想到一种可能：给我注射血清，也许是为了哄我生出一种安全感，而实际上我并无这种安全。我渐渐多疑起来。
达努斯为我注射血清时，我问他韦帕贾的医生多不多。「按人口比例算，不像一千年前那样多了，」他答道，「如今所有人都受训练，懂得照料自己的身体，并被传授健康与长寿的要点。即便不用我们用以在人体内持续维持抗病力的那些血清，我国人民也能活到很大岁数。卫生、饮食和运动，单凭自身便能创造奇迹。

===Original===
“But we must have some doctors. Their numbers are limited now to about one to each five thousand citizens, and in addition to administering the serum, the doctors attend those who are injured by the accidents of daily life, in the hunt, and in duels and war.
“Formerly there were many more doctors than could eke out an honest living, but now there are various agencies that restrict their numbers. Not only is there a law restricting these, but the ten years of study required, the long apprenticeship thereafter, and the difficult examinations that must be passed have all tended to reduce the numbers who seek to follow this profession; but another factor probably achieved more than all else to rapidly reduce the great number of doctors that threatened the continuance of human life on Amtor in the past.
“This was a regulation that compelled every physician and surgeon to file a complete history of each of his cases with the chief medical officer of his district. From diagnosis to complete recovery or death, each detail of the handling of each case had to be recorded and placed on record for the public to consult. When a citizen requires the services of a physician or surgeon now, he may easily determine those who have been successful and those who have not. Fortunately, today there are few of the latter. The law has proved a good one.”
This was interesting, for I had had experience with physicians and surgeons on earth. “How many doctors survived the operation of this new law?” I asked.
“About two percent,” he replied.
“There must have been a larger proportion of good doctors on Amtor than on earth,” I commented.

===Chinese===
「可我们总得有些医生。如今医生的人数被限制在大约每五千公民一名，除了施打血清之外，医生还要诊治那些在日常意外、狩猎、决斗和战争中受伤的人。
「从前医生多得多，多到没法靠诚实的营生糊口，可如今有种种办法限制他们的人数。不但有法律加以限制，而且必须完成的十年学业、此后漫长的学徒期、必须通过的艰难考试，无不使投身此业的人减少；然而，比起这一切更能迅速削减昔日众多医生人数的，大概还是另一项因素——那些医生一度威胁到阿姆托上人类生命的延续。
「这便是一项规定，它强制每位内外科医师把自己经手的每一个病例的完整经过，呈报给本区的首席医官。从诊断到完全康复或死亡，每个病例处理的每一细节都必须记录在案，供公众查阅。如今一个公民需要内外科医师的服务时，便不难分辨谁是良医、谁是庸医。所幸如今后者已经不多。这条法律证明是一条好法。」
这话颇有意思，因为我在地球上和内外科医师打过交道。「这条新法施行后，活下来的医生有多少？」我问。
「大约百分之二。」他答。
「阿姆托的好医生比例，想必比地球上的高。」我评论道。

===Original===
Time hung heavily upon my hands. I read a great deal, but an active young man cannot satisfy all his varied life interests with books alone. And then there was the garden at my right. I had been advised to avoid that end of my veranda, but I did not, at least not when Danus was absent. When he was away I haunted that end of the veranda, but it seemed deserted. And then one day I caught a glimpse of her; she was watching me from behind a flowering shrub.
I was close to the fence that separated my runway from her garden; it was not a high fence, perhaps slightly under five feet. She did not run this time, but stood looking straight at me, possibly thinking that I could not see her because of the intervening foliage. I could not see her plainly enough, that is true; and, God, how I wanted to see her!
What is that inexplicable, subtle attraction that some woman holds for every man? For some men there is only one woman in the world who exercises this influence upon him, or perhaps if there are more, the others do not cross his path; for other men there are several; for some none. For me there was this girl of an alien race, upon an alien planet. Perhaps there were others, but if there were, I had never met them. In all my life before I had never been moved by such an irresistible urge. What I did, I did upon the strength of an impulse as uncontrollable as a law of nature; perhaps it was a law of nature that motivated me. I vaulted the fence.

===Chinese===
时间在我手里沉甸甸地难以打发。我读了很多书，可一个活跃的年轻人，岂能仅凭书本满足他多姿多彩的人生兴致。何况右边还有那座花园。我曾被劝告躲着回廊那一头，可我没有——至少达努斯不在时没有。他外出时，我便在回廊那一头徘徊，可那里似乎荒着。直到有一天，我瞥见了她；她正躲在一丛花木后打量我。
我贴近那道分隔我的走道与她花园的栅栏；栅栏不高，也许略低于五英尺。这一回她没有逃，而是站直了直直地看着我，也许是以为隔着当中那些枝叶我瞧不见她。我确实瞧不够真切，这是实情；天哪，我是多么想看清她！
究竟是种怎样的、难以言喻又微妙的吸引力，使某一个女人对每一个男人施展着魔力？对某些男人而言，世上只有一个女人对他有这种影响力——也许还有别的，只是那些别的并未闯入他的路途；对另一些男人则有数人；对某些人则一个也没有。于我而言，则是这个异族女子，身处一颗异乡的星球。也许还有别的，可倘若真有，我从未遇见。我有生以来从未被这样一股不可抗拒的冲动所左右。我所做的，是凭着一种如同自然法则般不可抑制的冲动去做的；也许推动我的，正是一道自然法则。我翻过了栅栏。

===Original===
Before the girl could escape me, I stood before her. There were consternation and horror in her eyes. I thought that she was afraid of me.
“Do not be afraid,” I said; “I have not come to harm you, only to speak to you.”
She drew herself up proudly. “I am not afraid of you,” she said; “I﻿—,” she hesitated and then started over. “If you are seen here you will be destroyed. Go back to your quarters at once and never dare such a rash act again.”
I thrilled to the thought that the fear that I had seen so clearly reflected in her eyes was for my safety. “How may I see you?” I asked.
“You may never see me,” she replied.
“But I have seen you, and I intend seeing you again. I am going to see a lot of you, or die in the attempt.”
“Either you do not know what you are doing or you are mad,” she said and turned her back on me as she started to walk away.
I seized her arm. “Wait,” I begged.
She wheeled on me like a tigress and slapped my face, and then she whipped the dagger from the scabbard at her girdle. “How dared you,” she cried, “lay a hand upon me! I should kill you.”
“Why don’t you?” I asked.
“I loathe you,” she said, and it sounded as though she meant it.
“I love you,” I replied, and I knew that I spoke the truth.
At that declaration her eyes did indeed reflect horror. She wheeled then so quickly that I could not stop her and was gone. I stood for a moment, debating whether I should follow her or not, and then a modicum of reason intervened to save me from such an asininity. An instant later I had vaulted the fence again. I did not know whether anyone had seen me or not, and I did not care.

===Chinese===
在姑娘躲开之前，我便站到了她跟前。她眼中满是惊愕与骇然。我以为她怕我。
「别怕，」我说，「我不是来害你的，只是想同你说说话。」
她傲然地挺直了身。「我并不怕你，」她说，「我——」她迟疑了一下，重新开口，「你若被人看见在此，便会没命。立刻回你的住处去，再不敢这样胆大妄为。」
我想到，方才那么清楚地映在她眼里的那份恐惧，原来是为我的安危——这一念头令我心中一颤。「我怎样才能再见你？」我问。
「你再也见不到我。」她答。
「可我已见到了你，也打算再见你。我要常常看见你，否则便死在这上面。」
「你不是不知道自己在做什么，便是疯了。」她说着，转过身背对我，要往回走。
我抓住她的手臂。「等等。」我恳求。
她像雌虎般回身掴了我一掌，又从腰间鞘里刷地抽出匕首。「你怎敢，」她叫道，「碰我一下！我该杀了你。」
「那为何不杀？」我问。
「我厌恶你。」她说，听上去像是当真。
「我爱你。」我答，而我知道我说的是真话。
听了这番表白，她的眼中确然流露出惊恐。她倏地一转身，我来不及拦，她已走了。我站了片刻，犹豫着要不要追上去，这时些许理智插了进来，把我从这种蠢行里救了出来。转眼间我又翻回了栅栏。有没有人瞧见我，我不知道，也不在乎。

===Original===
When Danus returned a short time later, he told me that Mintep had sent him for me. I wondered if the summons was in any way related to my adventure in the garden at the right, but I did not inquire. If it were, I should know in due time. The attitude of Danus was unchanged, but that no longer reassured me. I was beginning to suspect that the Amtorians were masters of dissimulation.
Two young officers from the quarters adjoining mine accompanied us to the chamber where the *jong* was to question me. Whether or not they were acting as an escort to prevent my escape I could not tell. They chatted pleasantly with me during the short walk along the corridor and up the staircase to the level above; but then the guards usually chat pleasantly with the condemned man, if he feels like chatting. They accompanied me into the room where the *jong* sat. This time he was not alone; there were a number of men gathered about him, and among these I recognized Duran, Olthar, and Kamlot. For some reason the assemblage reminded me of a grand jury, and I could not help but wonder if they were going to return a true bill.

===Chinese===
不久达努斯回来，告诉我明特普派他来叫我。我心下琢磨这次传唤是否与我在右边花园的那番历险有何关联，但未发问。若是有关，我迟早会知道。达努斯的态度一如既往，可这已不能令我安心。我开始怀疑阿姆托人都是深藏不露的高手。
两个与我住所相邻的军官，陪同我们前往「君」将要问话的那间屋子。他们是否充当着押解、以防我脱逃的角色，我无从判断。沿走廊、上楼梯到上一层这段短短的路上，他们与我愉快地闲聊；可话又说回来，狱卒通常也愉快地与死囚闲聊，只要死囚有兴致。他们陪我走进「君」所坐的那间屋。这一次他并非独自一人；他身侧围着一群人，其中我认出了杜兰、奥尔塔和卡姆洛特。这阵仗不知怎的让我想起大陪审团来，我不禁猜想他们是不是要提出正式起诉。

===Original===
I bowed to the *jong*, who greeted me quite pleasantly enough, and smiled and nodded to the three men in whose home I had spent my first night on Venus. Mintep looked me over in silence for a moment or two; when he had seen me before I had been dressed in my earthly clothes, now I was garbed (or ungarbed) like a Vepajan.
“Your skin is not as light in color as I thought it,” he commented.
“Exposure to light on the veranda has darkened it,” I replied. I could not say sunlight, because they have no word for sun, of the existence of which they do not dream. However, such was the case, the ultra violet rays of sunlight having penetrated the cloud envelopes surrounding the planet and tanned my body quite as effectively as would exposure to the direct rays of the sun have done.

===Chinese===
我向「君」一鞠躬，他相当和气地回礼，又对我在金星头一夜下榻的那家的三个月含笑点头。明特普默默打量了我一兩眼；上一次他见我时，我穿的是地球的衣裳，此刻我已穿戴（或未穿戴）得像个韦帕贾人了。
「你的肤色没有我以为的那么浅。」他评论道。
「在回廊上晒……被光晒着，便晒黑了些。」我答。我没法说「阳光」，因为他们没有「太阳」一词，做梦也想不到太阳的存在。然而事实确乎如此——阳光中的紫外线穿透了环绕行星的云幕，把我晒得与直晒阳光几乎一般黑。

===Original===
“You have been quite happy here, I trust,” he said.
“I have been treated with kindness and consideration,” I replied, “and have been quite as happy as any prisoner could reasonably be expected to be.”
The shadow of a smile touched his lips. “You are candid,” he commented.
“Candor is a characteristic of the country from which I come,” I replied.
“However, I do not like the word prisoner,” he said.
“Neither do I, *jong*, but I like the truth. I have been a prisoner, and I have been awaiting this opportunity to ask you why I am a prisoner and to demand my freedom.”
He raised his eyebrows; then he smiled quite openly. “I think that I am going to like you,” he said; “you are honest and you are courageous, or I am no judge of men.”
I inclined my head in acknowledgment of the compliment. I had not expected that he would receive my blunt demand in a spirit of such generous understanding; but I was not entirely relieved, for experience had taught me that these people could be very suave while being most uncompromising.

===Chinese===
「我想，你在这里过得还挺快活吧。」他说。
「我受了厚待与体谅，」我答，「也像任何囚徒所能合理期望的那样快活过。」
一丝笑意的影子掠过他的唇角。「你倒是坦率。」他评论。
「坦率是我所来自的那个国度的特质。」我答。
「不过，我不喜欢『囚徒』这个词。」他说。
「我也不喜欢，『君』，可我喜欢真相。我一直是囚徒，也一直在等候这个机会，问你为何囚我，并要求我的自由。」
他扬了扬眉；继而相当坦然地笑了。「我想我会喜欢你，」他说，「你诚实，又有胆识，否则我便不识人了。」
我欠身以领受这番夸奖。我没料到他会以这般宽宏的气度接受我直率的质问；可我也未全然放下心来，因为经验告诉我，这些人能一面极为圆滑、一面又毫不让步。

===Original===
“There are some things that I wish to tell you and some questions that I wish to ask you,” he continued. “We are still beset by our enemies, who yet send occasional raiding parties against us, who upon numerous occasions have sought to introduce their spies among us. We have three things that they require if they are not to suffer extinction: scientific knowledge, and the brains and experience to apply it. Therefore they go to any lengths to abduct our men, whom they purpose holding in slavery and forcing to apply the knowledge that they themselves do not have. They also abduct our women in the hope of breeding children of greater mentality than those which are now born to them.
“The story that you told of crossing millions of miles of space from another world is, of course, preposterous and naturally aroused our suspicions. We saw in you another Thorist spy, cleverly disguised. For this reason you have been under the careful and intelligent observation of Danus for many days. He reports that there is no doubt but that you were totally ignorant of the Amtorian language when you came among us, and as this is the only language spoken by any of the known races of the world, we have come to the conclusion that your story may be, in part, true. The fact that your skin, hair, and eyes differ in color from those of any known race is further substantiation of this conclusion. Therefore, we are willing to admit that you are not a Thorist, but the questions remain: who are you, and from whence came you?”
“I have told only the truth,” I replied; “I have nothing to add other than to suggest that you carefully consider the fact that the cloud masses surrounding Amtor completely obscure your view and therefore your knowledge of what lies beyond.”

===Chinese===
「有几件事我想告诉你，也有几个问题想问你，」他接着说，「我们仍受敌人围困，他们仍不时派袭击队来犯，又曾多次设法把他们的密探混入我们中间。他们若不想灭绝，便有三样东西是必须向我们索取的：科学知识，以及运用这些知识的头脑与经验。因此他们不择手段地掳掠我们的男子，意欲将他们奴役，迫使他们运用那些他们自己不具备的知识。他们还掳掠我们的女子，指望 thereby 培育出比他们如今所生的孩子智力更高的后代。
「你讲的那个从另一个世界跨越数百万英里太空而来的故事，当然荒诞不经，自然激起我们的疑心。我们把你视为又一个巧妙伪装的索尔党密探。正因如此，多日来你一直在达努斯仔细而得法的观察之下。他报告说，你来我们中间时，对阿姆托语一窍不通，这一点毫无疑问；而既然这是世界上一切已知种族所操的唯一语言，我们便得出结论：你的故事可能部分属实。你的肤色、发色、眼色与任何已知种族都不同，这一事实进一步印证了这一结论。因此，我们愿意承认你不是索尔党人。可问题仍在：你是谁？从何而来？」
「我只说了实话，」我答，「此外无可奉陈，只建议你仔细想想这样一个事实：环绕阿姆托的云层完全遮蔽了你们的视线，因而也遮蔽了你们对云层之外事物的认识。」

===Original===
He shook his head. “Let us not discuss it; it is useless to attempt to overthrow the accumulated scientific research and knowledge of thousands of years. We are willing to accept you as of another race, perhaps, as was suggested by the clothing you wore upon your arrival, from cold and dreary Karbol. You are free to come and go as you please. If you remain, you must abide by the laws and customs of Vepaja, and you must become self-supporting. What can you do?”
“I doubt that I can compete with Vepajans at their own trades or professions,” I admitted, “but I can learn something if I am given time.”
“Perhaps we can find someone who will undertake your training,” said the *jong*, “and in the meantime you may remain in my house, assisting Danus.”
“We will take him into our house and train him,” spoke up Duran, “if he cares to help us collect tarel and hunt.”

===Chinese===
他摇了摇头。「此事不必再论；妄图推翻数千年积累的科学研究与知识，徒劳无益。我们愿意承认你属于另一种族——也许正如你抵达时所穿衣裳所提示的那样，来自苦寒荒凉的卡博尔。你来自由来去。你若留下，便须遵守韦帕贾的法律与风俗，并须自食其力。你会做什么？」
「在韦帕贾人本行的手艺或行当里，我恐怕与他们争不过，」我承认，「可只要给我时间，我能学。」
「也许我们能找到愿意承担你训练的人，」「君」说，「在此期间，你可以留在我的府上，给达努斯打打下手。」
「我们愿把他接到我们家里来训练，」杜兰插话道，「只要他愿意帮我们采集塔雷尔（tarel）和打猎。」

===Original===
Tarel is the strong, silky fiber from which their cloth and cordage are made. I imagined that collecting it would be tame and monotonous work, but the idea of hunting appealed to me. In no event, however, could I ignore Duran’s well-meant invitation, as I did not wish to offend him, and, furthermore, anything would be acceptable that would provide the means whereby I might become self-supporting. I therefore accepted his offer, and, the audience being concluded, I bid goodbye to Danus, who invited me to visit him often, and withdrew with Duran, Olthar, and Kamlot.
As no mention had been made of the subject, I concluded that no one had witnessed my encounter with the girl in the garden, who was still uppermost in my thoughts and the principal cause of my regret that I was to leave the house of the *jong*.

===Chinese===
塔雷尔是他们用来织布、搓绳的那种坚韧而丝滑的纤维。我想采集它想必是平淡乏味的活计，但打猎这一项倒合我心意。可无论如何，杜兰这一番善意相邀我都不能拒却——我不愿得罪他；何况任何能让我借此自食其力的差事，我皆可接受。于是我便应下他的提议。觐见既毕，我向达努斯道别——他邀我常去拜访——便随杜兰、奥尔塔和卡姆洛特退了出来。
由于无人提及此事，我便断定没有人目击我在花园里与姑娘的那场相遇；那姑娘仍在我心头挥之不去，也是我遗憾将要离开「君」府的主要原因。

===Original===
Once more I was established in the house of Duran, but this time in a larger and more comfortable room. Kamlot took charge of me. He was the younger of the brothers, a quiet, reserved man with the muscular development of a trained athlete. After he had shown me my room, he took me to another apartment, a miniature armory, in which were many spears, swords, daggers, bows, shields, and almost countless arrows. Before a window was a long bench with racks in which were tools of various descriptions; above the bench were shelves upon which were stacked the raw materials for the manufacture of bows, arrows, and spear shafts. Near the bench were a forge and anvil, and there were sheets and rods and ingots of metal stored near by.
“Have you ever used a sword?” he asked as he selected one for me.
“Yes, but for exercise only,” I replied; “in my country we have perfected weapons that render a sword useless in combat.”
He asked me about these weapons and was much interested in my description of earthly firearms. “We have a similar weapon on Amtor,” he said. “We of Vepaja do not possess them, because the sole supply of the material with which they are charged lies in the heart of the Thorist country. When the weapons are made they are charged with an element that emits a ray of extremely short wave length that is destructive of animal tissue, but the element only emits these rays when exposed to the radiation of another rare element. There are several metals that are impervious to these rays. Those shields that you see hanging on the walls, the ones that are metal covered, are ample protection from them. A small shutter of similar metal is used in the weapon to separate the two elements; when this shutter is raised and one element is exposed to the emanations of the other, the destructive R-ray is released and passes along the bore of the weapon toward the target at which the latter has been aimed.

===Chinese===
我又一次在杜兰家安顿下来，这一回住的房间更宽敞舒适。卡姆洛特负责照管我。他是兄弟中较年轻的一个，是个沉静、内敛的男子，有着训练有素的运动员般的肌肉线条。他领我看过房间后，又带我去了另一间屋——一间小型军械库，里面有许多矛、剑、匕首、弓、盾，以及几乎数不清的箭。一扇窗前是一条长凳，凳上的架子里放着各式各样的工具；长凳上方是架子，摞着制造弓、箭和矛杆的原料。长凳旁是一座锻炉和一只铁砧，旁边还堆着金属的板、杆和锭。
「你使过剑吗？」他一面问，一面为我挑了一柄。
「使过，但只作为锻炼，」我答，「在我国，我们已造出了使剑在战斗中毫无用处的武器。」
他问起这些武器，听我描述地球上的枪械，十分感兴趣。「阿姆托上也有类似的武器，」他说，「我们韦帕贾人不拥有它们，因为它们所装填的材料的唯一来源，就在索尔党国家的腹地。这些武器造好时，装填了一种元素，它能发出一种波长极短、足以破坏动物组织的射线，但只有当这种元素受到另一种稀有元素的辐射时，才会发出这种射线。有几种金属能挡住这些射线。你看见墙上挂着的那些盾，那些包了金属的盾，便是足以抵御它们的防护。武器里有一片同种金属的小快门，用以分隔这两种元素；快门一抬起、一种元素暴露在另一种元素的辐射下时，那道具破坏性的 R 射线便释放出来，沿着武器的内膛射向所瞄的目标。

===Original===
“My people invented and perfected this weapon,” he added ruefully, “and now it has been turned against us; but we get along very well with what we have, as long as we remain in our trees.
“In addition to a sword and dagger, you will need a bow, arrows, and a spear,” and as he enumerated them he selected the various articles for me, the last of which was really a short, heavy javelin. A swivelled ring was attached to the end of the shaft of this weapon, and attached to the ring was a long, slender cord with a hand loop at its extremity. This cord, which was no heavier than ordinary wrapping twine, Kamlot coiled in a peculiar way and tucked into a small opening in the side of the shaft.
“What is the purpose of that cord?” I asked, examining the weapon.
“We hunt high in the trees,” he replied, “and if it were not for the cord we should lose many spears.”
“But that cord is not heavy enough for that, is it?” I asked.
“It is of tarel,” he replied, “and could support the weight of ten men. You will learn much of the properties and value of tarel before you have been with us long. Tomorrow we shall go out together and gather some. It has been rather scarce of late.”

===Chinese===
「这武器是我族发明并完善的，」他懊然补了一句，「如今却被用来对付我们；不过只要我们待在树上，凭现有的东西也过得去。
「除了一柄剑和一把匕首，你还需要一张弓、一些箭和一杆矛。」他一边列举，一边为我挑选这几样物件，最后一样其实是一杆短而重的标枪。这武器的杆尾装着一只转环，环上系着一根又长又细的绳，绳头是个手环。这绳子不过和寻常的捆绳一般重，卡姆洛特用一种独特的方式把它盘起来，塞进矛杆侧面一个小开口里。
「这绳子是做什么用的？」我打量着武器问。
「我们在高处树上打猎，」他答，「若没有这绳子，我们会丢掉很多矛。」
「可那绳子未必够结实吧？」我问。
「它是塔雷尔做的，」他答，「能承受十个人的重量。你跟我们待久了，便会深知塔雷尔的种种性质与价值。明天我们就一同出去采些。近来它颇为稀少。」

===Original===
At the evening meal that day I met Zuro and Alzo again, and they were most gracious to me. In the evening they all joined in teaching me the favorite Vepajan game, tork, which is played with pieces that are much like those used in mah-jongg and bears a startling resemblance to poker.
I slept well that night in my new quarters and when daylight broke I arose, for Kamlot had warned me that we should start early upon our expedition. I cannot say that I looked forward with any considerable degree of enthusiasm to spending the day gathering tarel. The climate of Vepaja is warm and sultry, and I pictured the adventure as being about as monotonous and disagreeable as picking cotton in Imperial Valley.
After a light breakfast, which I helped Kamlot to prepare, he told me to get my weapons. “You should always wear your sword and dagger,” he added.
“Even in the house?” I asked.
“Always, wherever you are,” he replied. “It is not only a custom, but it is the law. We never know when we may be called upon to defend ourselves, our houses, or our *jong*.”
“Those are all that I need bring, I suppose,” I remarked as I was leaving the room.
“Bring your spear, of course; we are going to gather tarel,” he replied.

===Chinese===
那天晚饭时我又见到了祖罗和阿尔佐，她们对我十分殷勤。晚上众人一起教我下韦帕贾人最喜爱的游戏——托尔克（tork），它用的牌与麻将的牌颇为相似，又与扑克有着惊人的相像。
当夜我在新居睡得很好，天一亮便起身，因为卡姆洛特提醒过我，这趟出门要趁早。我不能说我有多大的兴致，盼着花一整天去采集塔雷尔。韦帕贾的气候温暖而闷热，我把这趟冒险想象得与在帝王谷摘棉花一般，又单调又难受。
草草吃过早饭——这早饭是我帮卡姆洛特做的——他叫我去拿武器。「你应该一直佩着剑和匕首。」他补了一句。
「在屋里也要？」我问。
「总是要，无论你在哪儿，」他答，「这不仅是风俗，也是法律。我们永远不知道何时会被唤去保卫自己、保卫家园、或保卫『君』。」
「我需要带的，就这些了吧。」我离开房间时说道。
「当然还有你的矛；我们是去采集塔雷尔的。」他答。

===Original===
Why I should need a spear to gather tarel I could not imagine; but I brought all the weapons that he had mentioned, and when I returned he handed me a bag with a strap that went around my neck to support it at my back.
“Is this for the tarel?” I asked.
He replied that it was.
“You do not expect to gather much,” I remarked.
“We may not get any,” he replied. “If we get a bagful between us we may do some tall boasting when we return.”
I said no more, thinking it best to learn by experience rather than to be continually revealing my lamentable ignorance. If tarel were as scarce as his statement suggested, I should not have much picking to do, and that suited me perfectly. I am not lazy, but I like work that keeps my mind on the alert.

===Chinese===
采集塔雷尔为何要用矛，我百思不得其解；可他提到的武器我都带了，等我回来时他递给我一只袋子，袋子上有一根带子绕过脖子，把袋子托在背后。
「这是装塔雷尔的？」我问。
他说是。
「你指望采的不多。」我说。
「也许一无所获，」他答，「我们俩若能采满一袋，回去便可大大地吹嘘一番了。」
我不再多说，心想与其不断暴露我可悲的无知，倒不如亲身经历学得快。倘若塔雷尔真如他所说那般稀少，我也用不着采多少，正中我下怀。我并不懒，但我喜欢让脑子时刻警醒的活儿。

===Original===
When we were both ready, Kamlot led the way upstairs, a procedure which mystified me, but did not tempt me into asking any more questions. We passed the two upper levels of the house and entered a dark, spiral staircase that led still farther upward into the tree. We ascended this for about fifteen feet, when Kamlot halted and I heard him fumbling with something above me.
Presently the shaft was bathed with light, which I saw came through a small circular opening that had been closed with a stout door. Through this opening Kamlot crawled, and I followed him, to find myself on a limb of the tree. My companion closed and locked the door, using a small key. I now saw that the door was covered on the outside with bark, so that when it was closed it would have been difficult for anyone to have detected it.
With almost monkeylike agility, Kamlot ascended, while I, resembling anything but a monkey in this respect, followed, thankful for the lesser gravitational pull of Venus, however little less than that of earth it might be, for I am not naturally arboreal.

===Chinese===
我们都备齐后，卡姆洛特领我上楼——这一举动叫我摸不着头脑，却也没诱我再发问。我们穿过房子上面两层，进入一道漆黑的螺旋楼梯，它仍向上通入树中。我们爬了约莫十五英尺，卡姆洛特停下，我听见他在我头顶摸索什么。
不多时，竖井里洒进一片光，我看见光是从一个不大的圆口里透进来的，那口子原用一扇厚实的门关着。卡姆洛特从这口子钻出去，我跟着钻出去，发觉自己到了这棵树的一根枝桠上。我的同伴用一把小钥匙把门关好锁上。我这才看见，门的外侧覆着树皮，因此关上后旁人很难发现。
卡姆洛特以近乎猿猴般的敏捷向上攀，而我在这方面半点不像猿猴，也跟着攀，倒要感谢金星的引力比地球小——无论小得多么有限——因为我天生不是个会爬树的。

===Original===
After ascending about a hundred feet, Kamlot crossed to an adjacent tree, the branches of which interlocked with those of the one we had been ascending, and again the upward climb commenced. Occasionally the Vepajan stopped to listen as we passed from tree to tree or clambered to higher levels. After we had travelled for an hour or more, he stopped again and waited until I had overtaken him. A finger on his lips enjoined me to silence.
“Tarel,” he whispered, pointing through the foliage in the direction of an adjacent tree.
I wondered why he had to whisper it, as my eyes followed the direction of his index finger. Twenty feet away I saw what appeared to be a huge spider web, partially concealed by the intervening foliage.
“Be ready with your spear,” whispered Kamlot. “Put your hand through the loop. Follow me, but not too closely; you may need room to cast your spear. Do you see him?”
“No,” I admitted. I saw nothing but the suggestion of a spider web; what else I was supposed to see I did not know.

===Chinese===
攀了约一百英尺，卡姆洛特横到相邻一棵树上——两树的枝桠彼此交错——又开始向上攀。从这棵树到那棵树、或攀向更高处时，这位韦帕贾人不时停下倾听。我们走了一个多钟头，他又停下，等我追上来。一根手指竖在唇前，命我噤声。
「塔雷尔。」他悄声说，透过枝叶指向相邻一棵树的方向。
我心下纳罕他何必悄声，一边顺着他的食指方向望去。二十英尺开外，我看见一个像是巨大蛛网的东西，被当中的枝叶半遮半掩。
「准备好你的矛，」卡姆洛特悄声道，「把手穿过手环。跟着我，但别跟太近；你可能需要空间投矛。你看见它了吗？」
「没有。」我承认。我除了那一团疑似蛛网的东西外什么也没看见；我还该看见什么，我并不知道。

===Original===
“Neither do I, but he may be hiding. Look up occasionally so that he can’t take you by surprise from above.”
This was more exciting than picking cotton in Imperial Valley, though as yet I did not know just what there was to be excited about. Kamlot did not appear excited; he was very cool, but he was cautious. Slowly he crept toward the great web, his javelin ready in his hand; and I followed. When we were in full sight of it we saw that it was empty. Kamlot drew his dagger.
“Start cutting it away,” he said. “Cut close to the branches and follow the web around; I will cut in the other direction until we meet. Be careful that you do not get enmeshed in it, especially if he happens to return.”
“Can’t we go around it?” I asked.
Kamlot looked puzzled. “Why should we go around it?” he demanded, a little shortly I thought.
“To get the tarel,” I replied.
“What do you suppose this is?” he demanded.
“A spider’s web.”
“It is tarel.”

===Chinese===
「我也没看见，但它也许藏着。不时抬头看看，免得它从上方给你来个出其不意。」
这可比在帝王谷摘棉花刺激多了，尽管我还不知道究竟有什么可激动的。卡姆洛特似乎并不激动；他十分冷静，但很谨慎。他握着标枪，缓缓朝那张大网爬去；我跟在后面。等我们把它整个看在眼里时，才发现它是空的。卡姆洛特抽出匕首。
「动手把它割下来吧，」他说，「贴着枝桠割，沿着网一圈割过去；我从另一头割，直到我们碰头。小心别把自己缠进去，尤其它万一回来。」
「我们不能绕过它吗？」我问。
卡姆洛特一脸困惑。「为何要绕过它？」他追问，我觉得语气略有些生硬。
「去取塔雷尔啊。」我答。
「你以为这是什么？」他追问。
「一张蛛网。」
「这就是塔雷尔。」

===Original===
I subsided. I had thought that the tarel he pointed at was beyond the web, although I had seen nothing; but then of course I had not known what tarel was or what it looked like. We had been cutting away for a few minutes when I heard a noise in a tree near us. Kamlot heard it at the same time.
“He is coming,” he said. “Be ready!” He slipped his dagger into his sheath and grasped his spear. I followed his example.
The sound stopped, but I could see nothing through the foliage. Presently there was a rustling among the foliage, and a face appeared some fifteen yards from us. It was a hideous face﻿—the face of a spider tremendously enlarged. When the thing saw that we had discovered it, it emitted the most frightful scream I had ever heard save once before. Then I recognized them﻿—the voice and the face. It had been a creature such as this that had pursued my pursuer the night that I had dropped to the causeway in front of the house of Duran.
“Be ready,” cautioned Kamlot; “he will charge.”

===Chinese===
我不作声了。我原以为他所指的塔雷尔是在蛛网那一边——尽管我什么也没看见；可话说回来，我本就不知道塔雷尔是什么、长什么样。我们割了几分钟，我听见附近一棵树上有动静。卡姆洛特同时听见了。
「它来了。」他说，「准备好！」他把匕首插回鞘中，握住矛。我也依样画葫芦。
声响停了，可我隔着枝叶什么也看不见。不多时枝叶一阵窸窣，一张脸在离我们约莫十五码处露了出来。那是一张可怖的脸——一张放大了无数倍的蜘蛛脸。那东西见自己已被我们发现，便发出一声我所听过最骇人的尖叫——只有另一次除外。这一刻我认出了它们——那嗓音、那张脸。那夜我落到杜兰家门前栈道上时，追捕我那追捕者的，正是这样一只怪兽。
「准备好，」卡姆洛特提醒，「它要扑过来了。」

===Original===
The words had scarcely crossed the lips of the Vepajan when the hideous creature rushed toward us. Its body and legs were covered with long, black hair, and there was a yellow spot the size of a saucer above each eye. It screamed horribly as it came, as though to paralyze us with terror.
Kamlot’s spear hand flew back and forward, and the heavy javelin, rushing to meet the maddened creature, buried itself deeply in the repulsive carcass; but it did not stop the charge. The creature was making straight for Kamlot as I hurled my javelin, which struck it in the side; but even this did not stop it, and to my horror I saw it seize my companion as he fell back upon the great limb upon which he had stood, with the spider on top of him.
The footing was secure enough for Kamlot and the spider, for they were both accustomed to it, but to me it seemed very precarious. Of course the tree limbs were enormous and often the branches were laced together, yet I felt anything but secure. However, I had no time to think of that now. If not already dead, Kamlot was being killed. Drawing my sword, I leaped to the side of the huge arachnid and struck viciously at its head, whereupon it abandoned Kamlot and turned upon me; but it was badly wounded now and moved with difficulty.

===Chinese===
话音未落，那可怖的怪兽便朝我们扑来。它的身子和腿上覆着长长的黑毛，每只眼睛上方各有一块碟子大小的黄斑。它边扑边发出骇人的尖叫，仿佛要用恐怖把我们吓瘫。
卡姆洛特投矛的手一扬一送，那杆沉重的标枪迎着发狂的怪兽飞去，深深扎进它那令人作呕的身躯；可这并未挡住它的扑击。那东西直扑卡姆洛特，我赶紧投出我的标枪，扎在它肋上；可即便这样也没拦住它，我惊骇地看见它扑倒我的同伴——他仰面倒在他方才所立的那根大枝上，那蜘蛛压在他身上。
这种立足之处，对卡姆洛特和蜘蛛来说是够稳的，因为他们都习惯了；可于我而言，它似乎岌岌可危。诚然，树枝粗大得很，枝桠又常常交错相缠，可我仍觉一点也不稳当。不过此刻我无暇顾及这些。卡姆洛特若非已死，便正在被弄死。我拔出剑，跃到这只巨型蜘蛛兽身侧，狠狠朝它头上劈去；它于是丢下卡姆洛特，转身向我；可此刻它已受了重伤，行动艰难。

===Original===
As I struck at that hideous face, I was horrified to see that Kamlot lay as though dead. He did not move. But I had only time for that single brief glance. If I were not careful I, too, should soon be dead. The thing confronting me seemed endowed with unsapable vitality. It was oozing sticky blood from several wounds, at least two of which I thought should have been almost instantly lethal; yet still it struggled to reach me with the powerful claws that terminated its forelegs, that it might draw me to those hideous jaws.
The Vepajan blade is a keen, two-edged affair, a little wider and thicker near the point than at the haft, and, while not well balanced to my way of thinking, is a deadly cutting weapon. I found it so in this my first experience with it, for as a great claw reached out to seize me I severed it with a single blow. At this the creature screamed more horribly than ever, and with its last remaining vitality sprang upon me as you have seen spiders spring upon their prey. I cut at it again as I stepped back; and then thrust my point directly into that hideous visage, as the weight of the creature overbore me and I went down beneath it.

===Chinese===
我朝那张可怖的脸劈去时，惊恐地发现卡姆洛特一动不动地躺着，像死了一样。他毫无动静。可我只来得及瞥这一眼。若不当心，我也快死了。眼前这东西似乎有着耗之不尽的生命力。它身上好几处伤口往外渗着黏稠的血，其中至少两处我以为本应几乎能立时致命；可它仍挣扎着，想用前肢末端那有力的钳子够到我，把我拖向那可怖的颚。
韦帕贾的剑是一种锋利的双刃家伙，靠剑尖处略宽略厚，虽依我看配重并不算好，却是一件致命的劈砍兵刃。我在头一回使它的实战中便领教了这一点：一只大钳子伸来抓我，我一剑便把它斩断。那怪兽的尖叫比刚才更可怖，凭着最后一点生命力，像你曾见过的蜘蛛扑向猎物那样朝我扑来。我退后一步又劈了一剑；接着把剑尖直直刺入那张可怖的脸——这时那怪兽的重量压倒了我，我被它压在了身下。

===Original===
As it crashed upon me, my body toppled from the great branch upon which I had been standing, and I felt myself falling. Fortunately, the interlacing, smaller branches gave me some support; I caught at them and checked my fall, bringing up upon a broad, flat limb ten or fifteen feet below. I had clung to my sword, and being unhurt, clambered back as quickly as I could to save Kamlot from further attack, but he needed no protection﻿—the great targo, as the creature is called, was dead.
Dead also was Kamlot; I could find no pulse nor detect any beating of the heart. My own sank within my breast. I had lost a friend, I who had so few here, and I was as utterly lost as one may be. I knew that I could not retrace our steps to the Vepajan city, even though my life depended on my ability to do so, as it doubtless did. I could descend, but whether I was still over the city or not I did not know; I doubted it.
So this was gathering tarel; this was the occupation that I had feared would bore me with its monotony!

===Chinese===
它砸下来时，我的身子从我方才所立的那根大枝上倾倒下去，我感觉自己在坠落。幸而，那些交错的小枝给了我一些支撑；我抓住它们，止住了坠落，落在了下方十到十五英尺处一根又宽又扁的枝上。我一直没松开剑，并且毫发无伤，便尽快攀回去，想救卡姆洛特免受进一步的攻击——可他已无需保护了——那只巨大的塔戈（targo）——这怪兽就是叫这个名字——已经死了。
卡姆洛特也死了；我摸不到他的脉搏，也听不见他的心跳。我的心一下子沉到了胸底。我失去了一位朋友——在这里我本就朋友寥寥——而我自己也彻底陷入了走投无路的境地。我知道我无法循来路返回韦帕贾的树城，纵然我的性命就系于我能否做到这一点——无疑正是如此。我可以下落，可我是否仍在树城正上方，我不知道；我深表怀疑。
原来这就是采集塔雷尔；原来这就是我曾担心会因其单调而让我乏味的那项营生！

## VII By Kamlot's Grave / VII 在卡姆洛特的墓畔

===Original===
Having set out to gather tarel, I finished the work that Kamlot and I had nearly completed when the targo attacked us; if I succeeded in finding the city, I should at least bring something to show for our efforts. But what about Kamlot? The idea of leaving the body here was repugnant to me. Even in the brief association I had had with the man I had come to like him and to look upon him as my friend. His people had befriended me; the least that I could do would be to take his body back to them. I realized, of course, that that was going to be something of a job, but it must be done. Fortunately, I am extraordinarily muscular, and then, too, the gravitational pull of Venus favored me more than would that of earth, giving me an advantage of over twenty pounds in the dead weight I should have to carry and even a little better than that in the amount of my own live weight, for I am heavier than Kamlot.
With less difficulty than I had anticipated I succeeded in getting Kamlot’s body onto my back and trussed there with the cord attached to his javelin. I had previously strapped his weapons to him with strands of the tarel that half filled my bag, for, being unfamiliar with all the customs of the country, I did not know precisely what would be expected of me in an emergency of this nature, and preferred to be on the safe side.

===Chinese===
既已出来采集塔雷尔，我便把卡姆洛特和我几乎做完的活儿做完——我们被塔戈袭击时，活儿正接近尾声；倘若我能寻见那座树城，至少也该带点东西回去，好有个交代。可卡姆洛特怎么办？把他遗体丢在这里，我实在于心不忍。虽说我与此人相处时日不长，却已喜欢上他，把他看作朋友。他的族人待我以诚；我至少能做的，便是把他的遗体送回他们身边。我当然明白这绝非易事，可这事必须做。所幸我臂力过人，加之金星的引力比地球更成全我——让我在要扛的死重上占了二十多磅的便宜，连我自己的活重量也少上那么一点，因为我比卡姆洛特更重。
出乎我意料，我相当顺利地把卡姆洛特的遗体驮上了背，用他标枪上的绳把他捆牢。我先前已用半袋的塔雷尔纤维把他的兵器绑在他身上——我对本国风俗所知不深，不晓得这般变故之下究竟该我怎么做，宁求稳妥。

===Original===
The experiences of the next ten or twelve hours are a nightmare that I should like to forget. Contact with the dead and naked body of my companion was sufficiently gruesome, but the sense of utter bewilderment and futility in this strange world was even more depressing. As the hours passed, during which I constantly descended, except for brief rests, the weight of the corpse seemed to increase. In life Kamlot would have weighed about one hundred eighty pounds on earth, nearly one hundred sixty on Venus, but by the time darkness enveloped the gloomy forest I could have sworn that he weighed a ton.
So fatigued was I that I had to move very slowly, testing each new hand- and foothold before trusting my tired muscles to support the burden they were carrying, for a weak hold or a misstep would have plunged me into eternity. Death was ever at my elbow.

===Chinese===
接下来十来个钟头的经历，是一场我巴不得忘掉的噩梦。与同伴那赤裸的尸身相贴已是够骇人，然而在这陌生世界里那种全然不知所措、徒然无望的感觉，更叫人沮丧。时间一个钟头一个钟头过去，我一路向下——除去短暂歇息——尸首的分量竟似越来越沉。卡姆洛特活着时，在地球上该有一百八十磅上下，在金星上近一百六十磅；可当黑暗笼罩这片阴森的森林时，我敢发誓他重达一吨。
我疲惫到了极点，不得不放得很慢很慢地移动，每抓一个新抓手、每踩一个新落脚处之前，都要先试一试，才敢让我疲倦的肌肉托起它们正扛着的担子——因为一抓不牢、一脚踏空，便会把我送进永恒。死亡一直就在我肘边。

===Original===
It seemed to me that I descended thousands of feet and yet I had seen no sign of the city. Several times I heard creatures moving through the trees at a distance, and twice I heard the hideous scream of a targo. Should one of these monstrous spiders attack me﻿—well, I tried not to think about that. Instead I tried to occupy my mind with recollections of my earthly friends; I visualized my childhood days in India as I studied under old Chand Kabi, I thought of dear old Jimmy Welsh, and I recalled a bevy of girls I had liked and with some of whom I had almost been serious. These recalled the gorgeous girl in the garden of the jong, and the visions of the others faded into oblivion. Who was she? What strange interdiction had forbidden her to see or to speak with me? She had said that she loathed me, but she had heard me tell her that I loved her. That sounded rather silly now that I gave it thought. How could I love a girl the first instant that I laid eyes upon her, a girl concerning whom I knew absolutely nothing, neither her age nor her name? It was preposterous, yet I knew that it was true. I loved the nameless beauty of the little garden.

===Chinese===
我觉得自己下了好几千英尺，可仍不见那座树城的踪影。好几次我听见远处的树间有活物移动的动静，还有两次我听见了塔戈那可怖的尖叫。万一这些巨蛛当中有一只来攻我——唉，我尽量不去想它。我转而尽力用对地球朋友的回忆来填满心神；我回想起在印度随老昌德·卡比（Chand Kabi）读书的童年时光，想起亲爱的老吉米·韦尔什，又忆起一群我曾中意的姑娘——其中几位我差点动了真心。这些姑娘又让我想起了「君」府花园里那位绝代佳人，旁人的影子便都隐入了遗忘。她是谁？是怎样的奇禁，不许她见我、不许她与我说话？她说过厌恶我，可她听我对她说我爱她了。如今细想，这话听着颇有些傻气。我怎能在初见一个姑娘的一刹那便爱上她——一个我全无所知的姑娘，连她的年纪、名字都不晓得？这荒谬绝伦，可我知道这是真的。我爱上了那座小花园里那位无名的美人。

===Original===
Perhaps my preoccupation with these thoughts made me careless; I do not know, but my mind was filled with them when my foot slipped a little after night had fallen. I grasped for support, but the combined weights of myself and the corpse tore my hands loose, and with my dead companion I plunged downward into the darkness. I felt Death’s cold breath upon my cheek.
We did not fall far, being brought up suddenly by something soft that gave to our combined weights, then bounced up again, vibrating like a safety net such as we have all seen used by aerial performers. In the faint but all pervading light of the Amtorian night I could see what I had already guessed﻿—I had fallen into the web of one of Amtor’s ferocious spiders!
I tried to crawl to an edge where I might seize hold of a branch and drag myself free, but each move but entangled me the more. The situation was horrible enough, but a moment later it became infinitely worse, as, glancing about me, I saw at the far edge of the web the huge, repulsive body of a targo.

===Chinese===
也许正是心神被这些念头占满，才使我失了小心；我不知道，但天黑之后，我心头正塞满这些念头时，脚下打了个滑。我伸手抓支撑，可我和尸首的合计重量把我的手硬生生拽脱，我与死去同伴一道，朝黑暗中坠下去。我感到死亡冰冷的气息拂上面颊。
我们没落多远，被一种软绵绵的东西骤然接住——那东西被我们俩的合计重量压得下沉，又弹了上来，像我们都见过的空中杂技演员所用的安全网那样颤动。在阿姆托之夜那微弱却无处不在的光里，我看清了我早已猜到的事——我落进了一张阿姆托凶蛛的网！
我试图爬到网的边沿，好抓住一根枝桠把自己拽出来，可每动一下，便把自己缠得更紧。这处境已够骇人，可片刻之后更变得无限糟糕——我四下一瞥，看见网的远端伏着塔戈那巨大、令人作呕的身躯。

===Original===
I drew my sword and hacked at the entangling meshes of the web as the fierce arachnid crept slowly toward me. I recall wondering if a fly entangled in a spider’s web suffered the hopelessness and the mental anguish that seized me as I realized the futility of my puny efforts to escape this lethal trap and the ferocious monster advancing to devour me. But at least I had some advantages that no fly enjoys. I had my sword and a reasoning brain; I was not so entirely helpless as the poor fly.
The targo crept closer and closer. It uttered no sound. I presume that it was satisfied that I could not escape and saw no reason why it should seek to paralyze me with fright. From a distance of about ten feet it charged, moving with incredible swiftness upon its eight hairy legs. I met it with the point of my sword.
There was no skill in my thrust; it was just pure luck that my point penetrated the creature’s tiny brain. When it collapsed lifeless beside me, I could scarcely believe the testimony of my eyes. I was saved!

===Chinese===
我拔出剑，在那凶蛛缓缓向我爬来时砍那缠住我的网丝。我记得我曾想：一只被困蛛网的苍蝇，是否也承受着我此刻所感到的那种绝望与心灵煎熬——我意识到自己逃出这致命陷阱、躲开那逼近要吞我的凶兽的微末挣扎纯属徒劳。可至少我享有几样苍蝇所没有的好处。我有剑，还有一个会思考的脑子；我并不像那只可怜的苍蝇那般全然无助。
塔戈越爬越近。它一声不响。我猜它认定我逃不掉，便懒得再用恐怖来吓瘫我。它在约莫十英尺开外发动了扑击，八条毛腿以令人难以置信的迅捷飞驰而来。我用剑尖迎了它。
我这一刺毫无技巧，纯粹是运气使剑尖刺穿了它那丁点大的脑子。它在我身旁颓然倒下、断了气时，我几乎不敢相信自己的眼睛。我获救了！

===Original===
Instantly I fell to work severing the strands of tarel that enmeshed me, and in four or five minutes I was free and had lowered myself to a branch below. My heart was still pounding rapidly and I was weak from exhaustion. For a quarter of an hour I remained resting; then I continued the seemingly endless descent out of this hideous forest.
What other dangers confronted me I could not guess. I knew that there were other creatures in this gigantic wood; those powerful webs, capable of sustaining the weight of an ox, had not been built for man alone. During the preceding day I had caught occasional glimpses of huge birds, which might themselves, if carnivorous, prove as deadly menaces as the targo; but it was not them that I feared now, but the nocturnal prowlers that haunt every forest by night.
Down and down I descended, feeling that each next moment must witness the final collapse of my endurance. The encounter with the targo had taken terrific toll of my great strength, already sapped by the arduous experiences of the day, yet I could not stop, I dared not. Yet how much longer could I drive exhausted nature on toward the brink of utter collapse?

===Chinese===
我立时动手割断缠住我的塔雷尔丝，四五分钟便脱了身，下到下方一根枝上。我心仍怦怦急跳，因虚脱而软弱无力。我歇了一刻钟；接着继续这仿佛无尽头、要走出这片可怖森林的下落。
还有什么别的危险横在我面前，我无从揣测。我知道这片巨林里另有生灵；那些能承住一头牛重量的强劲蛛网，本不是专为人而设的。前一天里我偶尔瞥见过几只巨鸟，倘若它们是食肉的，其致命威胁也许不亚于塔戈；可此刻我怕的不是它们，而是夜夜出没于每一片森林的夜行者。
我下啊，下啊，下啊，觉得下一刻便要目睹自己耐力的彻底崩溃。与塔戈那一战，把我原本就被一日艰辛耗去大半的巨大体力又狠狠抽走一截，可我不能停，也不敢停。然而我还能把精疲力竭的身子再往彻底崩溃的悬崖边上驱多久呢？

===Original===
I had about reached the end of my endurance when my feet struck solid ground. At first I could not believe the truth, but glancing down and about me I saw that I had indeed reached the floor of the forest; after a month on Venus I had at last placed foot upon her surface. I could see little or nothing﻿—just the enormous boles of great trees in whatever direction I looked. Beneath my feet lay a thick matting of fallen leaves, turned white in death.
I cut the cords that bound the corpse of Kamlot to my back and lowered my poor comrade to the ground; then I threw myself down beside him and was asleep almost immediately.
When I awoke, it was daylight again. I looked about me, but could see nothing but the counterpane of whitened leaves spread between the boles of trees of such gargantuan girth that I almost hesitate to suggest the size of some of them, lest I discredit the veracity of this entire story of my experiences on Venus. But indeed they must needs be huge to support their extraordinary height, for many of them towered over six thousand feet above the surface of the ground, their lofty pinnacles enshrouded forever in the eternal fog of the inner cloud envelope.

===Chinese===
就在我耐力将尽之时，双脚踩到了坚实的地面。起初我不敢相信，可低头四下一望，便知我的确到了森林的地面；来到金星一个月，我终于踏上了她的表面。我几乎什么也看不见——只见四面八方都是巨树那庞大无比的树干。脚下铺着厚厚一层落叶，叶子枯死后变成了白色。
我割断把卡姆洛特遗体绑在我背上的绳索，把我可怜的同伴放到地上；接着我便一头倒在他身旁，几乎立刻睡着了。
我醒来时，又是白天。我四下张望，所见唯有枯白落叶铺成的一床「被」，铺在那些粗到难以想象的树干之间——有些树的粗壮，我几乎不敢说出其尺寸，唯恐别人据此怀疑我整部金星见闻的真实性。然而它们也必须如此粗壮，方能支撑其惊人的高度，因为许多树都拔地六千余英尺，它们高耸的顶端永远裹在内层云幕那永恒的雾霭里。

===Original===
To suggest an idea of the size of some of these monsters of the forest, I may say that I walked around the bole of one, counting over a thousand paces in the circuit, which gives, roughly, a diameter of a thousand feet, and there were many such. A tree ten feet in diameter appeared a frail and slender sapling﻿—and there can be no vegetation upon Venus!
What little knowledge of physics I had and a very slight acquaintance with botany argued that trees of such height could not exist, but there must be some special, adaptive forces operating on Venus that permit the seemingly impossible. I have attempted to figure it out in terms of earthly conditions, and I have arrived at some conclusions that suggest possible explanations for the phenomenon. If vertical osmosis is affected by gravity, then the lesser gravity of Venus would favor the growth of taller trees, and the fact that their tops are forever in the clouds would permit them to build up an ample supply of carbohydrates from the abundant water vapor, provided there was the requisite amount of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere of Venus to promote this photosynthetic process.

===Chinese===
为给这些森林巨怪的大小一个概念，我可以说：我曾绕着其中一棵的树干走了一圈，数下来超过一千步，这粗略给出约一千英尺的直径——这样的树还很多。十英尺粗的树在它们中间，看上去就像一根纤细的小苗——可金星上偏偏「不可能有植被」！
我那点微薄的物理学知识和对植物学的一知半解，都说明这般高度的树是不可能存在的；可金星上必有某种特殊的、适应性的力量在起作用，使这看似不可能的事成为可能。我曾试图以地球条件推算，得出了一些结论，可为这一现象提供可能的解释。倘若垂直渗透受引力影响，那么金星较小的引力便有利于更高的树生长；而它们的顶端永远处于云中，便得以从丰沛的水汽中积累充足的碳水化合物——前提是金星大气中含有足量的二氧化碳，以促成这一光合作用过程。

===Original===
I must admit, however, that at the time I was not greatly interested in these intriguing speculations; I had to think about myself and poor Kamlot. What was I to do with the corpse of my friend? I had done my best to return him to his people, and failed. I doubted now that I could ever find his people. There remained but a single alternative; I must bury him.
This decided, I started to scrape away the leaves beside him, that I might reach the ground beneath and dig a grave. There were about a foot of leaves and leaf mold and below that a soft, rich soil which I loosened easily with the point of my spear and scooped out with my hands. It did not take me long to excavate a nice grave; it was six feet long, two feet wide, and three feet deep. I gathered some freshly fallen leaves and carpeted its bottom with them, and then I gathered some more to place around and over Kamlot after I had lowered him to his final resting place.

===Chinese===
然而我得承认，当时我对这些引人入胜的揣测并不大上心；我得想着自己，想着可怜的卡姆洛特。我朋友的遗体该怎么办？我已尽了最大努力要送他回他族人那里，却失败了。如今我怀疑自己还能否找得到他的族人。剩下的只有一条路：我得把他安葬。
既已决定，我便动手把他身旁的落叶拨开，好够到底下的地面，掘一个墓穴。落叶和腐殖质约有一英尺厚，其下是一种松软肥沃的泥土，我用矛尖松土、用手扒土，并不费力。没多久我便挖出一个像样的墓穴：六英尺长，二英尺宽，三英尺深。我又拾了些新落的叶子铺在穴底，再多拾一些，预备等把卡姆洛特放入他最后的安息之处后，围在他四周、盖在他身上。

===Original===
While I worked I tried to recall the service for the dead; I wanted Kamlot to have as decent and orderly a burial as I could contrive. I wondered what God would think about it, but I had no doubt but that he would receive this first Amtorian soul to be launched into the unknown with a Christian burial and welcome him with open arms.
As I stooped and put my arms about the corpse to lower it into the grave, I was astounded to discover that it was quite warm. This put an entirely new aspect on the matter. A man dead for eighteen hours should be cold. Could it be that Kamlot was not dead? I pressed an ear to his chest; faintly I heard the beating of his heart. Never before had I experienced such an access of relief and joy. I felt as one reborn to new youth, to new hopes, to new aspirations. I had not realized until that instant the depth of my loneliness.

===Chinese===
我一边干活，一边努力回忆安息祭文；我要尽我所能，给卡姆洛特一个体面而周全的葬礼。我不晓得上帝对此会怎么看，但我毫不怀疑，他会接纳这第一个以基督教葬礼送入未知的阿姆托灵魂，张开双臂欢迎他。
我弯下腰、双臂环住遗体要把它放进墓穴，却惊愕地发觉它竟然温热。这一来事情便全然变了样。一个死了十八个小时的人应当是冷的。难道卡姆洛特并没有死？我把耳朵贴上他胸口；隐约听见了他的心跳。我从未体验过那样一阵如释重负、欢喜若狂。我觉得自己仿佛重生，又得了新的青春、新的希望、新的抱负。直到那一刻我才意识到自己孤独之深。

===Original===
But why was Kamlot not dead? and how was I to resuscitate him? I felt that I should understand the former before I attempted the latter. I examined the wound again. There were two deep gashes on his chest just below the presternum. They had bled but little, and they were discolored, as I now noticed, by a greenish tint. It was this, meaningless though it may be, that suggested an explanation of Kamlot’s condition. Something about that greenish tint suggested poison to my mind, and at once I recalled that there were varieties of spiders that paralyzed their victims by injecting a poison into them that preserved them in a state of suspended animation until they were ready to devour them. The targo had paralyzed Kamlot!
My first thought was to stimulate circulation and respiration, and to this end I alternately massaged his body and applied the first aid measures adapted to the resuscitation of the drowned. Which of these accomplished the result I do not know (perhaps each helped a little), but at any rate I was rewarded after a long period of effort with evidences of returning animation. Kamlot sighed and his eyelids fluttered. After another considerable period, during which I nearly exhausted myself, he opened his eyes and looked at me.

===Chinese===
可卡姆洛特为何没死？我又当如何使他苏醒？我觉得在尝试后者之前，先得弄懂前者。我再次检查伤口。他胸上、胸骨柄正下方有两道深深的口子。出血不多，而我此时才注意到，它们染着一层带绿的颜色。正是这一点——也许无甚意义——点醒了我对卡姆洛特处境的解释。那抹绿在我心里勾起了「毒」的联想，我旋即想起，有些种类的蜘蛛会给猎物注入一种毒液，使之陷入假死状态，留待日后享用。塔戈把卡姆洛特麻痹了！
我头一个念头是促进他的循环与呼吸，为此我轮番为他按摩身子、施行用于溺水者复苏的急救措施。究竟是哪一样奏了效，我不知道（也许两样都各尽其力），但无论如何，长久一番努力之后，我得到了回报——他身上出现了恢复生机的迹象。卡姆洛特叹了口气，眼皮颤动。又过了好一阵——这阵子里我几乎把自己累垮——他睁开了眼，看着我。

===Original===
At first his gaze was expressionless and I thought that perhaps his mind had been affected by the poison; then a puzzled, questioning look entered his eyes and eventually recognition. I was witnessing a resurrection.
“What happened?” he asked in a whisper, and then, “Oh, yes, I recall; the targo got me.” He sat up, with my assistance, and looked around. “Where are we?” he demanded.
“On the ground,” I replied, “but where on the ground I do not know.”
“You saved me from the targo,” he said. “Did you kill it? But you must have, or you never could have gotten me away from it. Tell me about it.”
Briefly, I told him. “I tried to get you back to the city, but I became lost and missed it. I have no idea where it lies.”
“What is this?” he asked, glancing at the excavation beside him.
“Your grave,” I replied. “I thought that you were dead.”
“And you carried a corpse half a day and half a night! But why?”
“I do not know all the customs of your people,” I replied; “but your family has been kind to me, and the least that I could do was to bring your body back to them, nor could I leave a friend up there to be devoured by birds and beasts.”

===Chinese===
起初他的目光毫无表情，我担心毒液伤了他的神智；继而一种困惑、疑问的神色进入他眼中，最终化作了认出。我正目睹一场复活。
「出了什么事？」他低声问，接着又说：「哦，对了，我想起来了；塔戈把我抓住了。」他在我搀扶下坐起身，四下张望。「我们在哪儿？」他问。
「在地面上，」我答，「可地面上何处，我不知道。」
「你从塔戈手里救了我，」他说，「你把它杀了？你一定杀了它，否则绝不可能把我从它那儿带走。跟我说说。」
我简略地告诉了他。「我想把你带回树城，可我迷了路，错过了它。它在哪个方向，我毫无头绪。」
「这是什么？」他瞥了瞥身旁的坑问。
「你的坟。」我答，「我以为你死了。」
「你竟扛着一具尸首走了半天半夜！可为什么？」
「你族人的风俗我并不全懂，」我答，「可你一家待我甚厚，我至少能做的便是把你的遗体送回他们那里；何况我也不能把一个朋友丢在上面，任鸟兽吞食。」

===Original===
“I shall not forget,” he said quietly. He tried to rise then, but I had to assist him. “I shall be all right presently,” he assured me, “after I have exercised a little. The effects of the targo’s poison wear off in about twenty-four hours even without treatment. What you have done for me has helped to dissipate them sooner, and a little exercise will quickly eradicate the last vestiges of them.” He stood looking about as though in an effort to orient himself, and as he did so his eyes fell upon his weapons, which I had intended burying with him and which lay on the ground beside the grave. “You even brought these!” he exclaimed. “You are a *jong* among friends!”
After he had buckled his sword belt about his hips, he picked up his spear, and together we walked through the forest, searching for some sign that would indicate that we had reached a point beneath the city, Kamlot having explained that trees along the important trails leading to the location of the city were marked in an inconspicuous and secret manner, as were certain trees leading upward to the hanging city.
“We come to the surface of Amtor but seldom,” he said, “though occasionally trading parties descend and go to the coast to meet vessels from the few nations with which we carry on a surreptitious commerce. The curse of Thorism has spread far, however, and there are few nations of which we have knowledge that are not subject to its cruel and selfish domination. Once in a while we descend to hunt the basto for its hide and flesh.”

===Chinese===
「我不会忘的。」他平静地说。他试着起身，可仍要我搀扶。「我马上便会好的，」他宽慰我，「等我稍微活动一下。塔戈之毒的效力即便不施治，约二十四小时也会自行消退。你为我做的，已帮它提早散去；再稍稍活动一番，便会很快涤荡其余痕。」他站着四下打量，仿佛在努力辨清方位；就在这时，他的目光落在他的兵器上——我本打算把它们随他一同下葬，此刻它们就放在墓旁的地上。「你连这些也带来了！」他叫道，「在朋友里头，你简直是一位『君』！」
他把剑带系上腰际，抄起他的矛，我们便并肩穿过森林，寻找某种能表明我们已到了树城正下方某处的路标——卡姆洛特先前解释过，通往树城所在的重要路径两旁的树，以一种不易察觉、隐秘的方式做了标记，正如通往那座悬空树城的某些树也有标记一样。
「我们难得下到阿姆托的地面，」他说，「虽说偶尔会有商队下到海岸，与少数几个和我们暗中通商的国家来的船只碰头。然而索尔主义的诅咒已蔓延甚广，在我们所知的国度里，几乎无不屈服于它那残酷自私的统治。我们偶尔也会下来猎捕巴斯托（basto），取它的皮和肉。」

===Original===
“What is a basto?” I inquired.
“It is a large, omnivorous animal with powerful jaws armed with four great fangs in addition to its other teeth. On its head grow two heavy horns. At the shoulder it is as tall as a tall man. I have killed them that weighed thirty-six hundred *tob*.”
A *tob* is the Amtorian unit of weight, and is the equivalent of one third of an English pound; all weights are computed in *tobs* or decimals thereof, as they use the decimal system exclusively in their tables of weights and measures. It seems to me much more practical than the confusing earthly collection of grains, grams, ounces, pounds, tons, and the other designations in common use among the various nations of our planet.
From Kamlot’s description I visualized the basto as an enormous boar with horns, or a buffalo with the jaws and teeth of a carnivore, and judged that its twelve hundred pounds of weight would render it a most formidable beast. I asked him with what weapons they hunted the animal.
“Some prefer arrows, others spears,” he explained, “and it is always handy to have a low branched tree near by,” he added with a grin.
“They are bellicose?” I asked.
“Very. When a basto appears upon the scene, man is as often the hunted as the hunter, but we are not hunting bastos now. What I should most like to find is a sign that would tell me where we are.”

===Chinese===
「什么是巴斯托？」我问。
「是一种大型的杂食兽，颚强有力，除其他牙齿外还生着四颗大獠牙。头上长着两只粗壮的角。肩高与一个高个子男人齐平。我杀过重三千六百托（tob）的。」
一托是阿姆托的重量单位，约相当于一英磅的三分之一；一切重量皆以托或其小数来计，因为他们的度量衡表一律采用十进制。在我看来，这远比地球上那套令人混淆的格令、克、盎司、磅、吨等等通行的杂乱名目来得实用。
据卡姆洛特的描述，我把巴斯托想象成一只长角的巨野猪，或一头长着食肉兽颚与牙的水牛，并断定它一千二百磅的体重足令它成为一头可怕的猛兽。我问他用什么武器猎这兽。
「有的偏爱箭，有的偏爱矛，」他解释，「而附近总得有一棵低矮有枝的树才方便。」他咧嘴一笑，补了一句。
「它们好斗？」我问。
「极好斗。巴斯托一出现，人之为猎手与猎物，往往各占一半。可眼下我们不是来猎巴斯托的。我最想找到的，是一个能告诉我我们在哪儿的路标。」

===Original===
We moved on through the forest, searching for the tiny road signs of the Vepajans, which Kamlot had described to me as well as explaining the location in which they are always placed. The sign consists of a long, sharp nail with a flat head bearing a number in relief. These nails are driven into trees at a uniform height from the ground. They are difficult to find, but it is necessary to have them so, lest the enemies of the Vepajans find and remove them, or utilize them in their search for the cities of the latter.
The method of the application of these signs to the requirements of the Vepajans is clever. They would really be of little value to any but a Vepajan as guide posts, yet each nail tells a remarkable story to the initiated; briefly it tells him precisely where he is on the island that comprises the kingdom of Mintep, the jong. Each nail is placed in position by a surveying party and its exact location is indicated on a map of the island, together with the number on the head of the nail. Before a Vepajan is permitted to descend to the ground alone, or to lead others there, he must memorize the location of every sign nail in Vepaja. Kamlot had done so. He told me that if we could find but a single nail he would immediately know the direction of and distance to those on either side of it, our exact position upon the island, and the location of the city; but he admitted that we might wander a long time before we discovered a single nail.

===Chinese===
我们继续穿行林中，寻找韦帕贾人那种小小的路标——卡姆洛特既向我描述过它的样子，也说明了它一向所在的位置。这种路标是一枚长而锋利、平头上浮雕着编号的钉子。这些钉子钉在树上，距地面高度一致。它们难以发现，却必须如此，免得韦帕贾人的敌人发现后拔掉，或借之寻找他们的树城。
这些路标用于韦帕贾人之所需，其法甚巧。它们对韦帕贾人以外的人而言，作路标实在价值不大；可每一枚钉子对懂行的人却讲述着一个了不起的故事——简言之，便是精确地告诉他在明特普「君」所辖的这座岛国上的何处。每一枚钉子都由一支测量队钉下，其精确位置连同钉头上的编号，一并标注在岛的地图上。一名韦帕贾人在获准独自下到地面、或领他人下地之前，必须把韦帕贾境内每一枚路标钉的位置都背下来。卡姆洛特早已背熟。他告诉我，只要我们能找到一枚钉子，他便能立即知道它两侧钉子的方向与距离、我们在岛上的精确位置，以及树城的所在；可他也承认，我们在找到一枚钉子之前，也许要徜徉很久。

===Original===
The forest was monotonously changeless. There were trees of several species, some with branches that trailed the ground, others bare of branches for hundreds of feet from their bases. There were boles as smooth as glass and as straight as a ship’s mast, without a single branch as far up as the eye could see. Kamlot told me that the foliage of these grew in a single enormous tuft far up among the clouds.
I asked him if he had ever been up there, and he said he had climbed, he believed, to the top of the tallest tree, but that he had nearly frozen to death in the attempt. “We get our water supply from these trees,” he remarked. “They drink in the water vapor among the clouds and carry it down to their roots. They are unlike any other tree. A central, porous core carries the water from the clouds to the roots, from whence it rises again in the form of sap that carries the tree’s food upward from the ground. By tapping one of these trees anywhere you may obtain a copious supply of clear, cool water﻿—a fortunate provision of﻿—”
“Something is coming, Kamlot,” I interrupted. “Do you hear it?”
He listened intently for a moment. “Yes,” he replied. “We had better take to a tree, at least until we see what it is.”

===Chinese===
这片森林千篇一律，单调得很。其中有好几种树，有的枝条垂地，有的从基部往上数百英尺不见一枝一桠。有的树干光洁如玻璃、笔直如船桅，目力所及之处，不见一枝。卡姆洛特告诉我，这些树的枝叶长成大大的一簇，远远地生于云端之上。
我问他是否上去过，他说他相信自己曾爬上过最高那棵树的顶端，却差点冻死。「我们的水源便取自这些树，」他说，「它们在云间吸饱水汽，再送到根部。它们与任何别的树都不同。一根多孔的芯把水从云端送到根部，又化作树液从根部把树的养分送上去。在这些树的任意一处开口，都能得到大量的清冽凉水——这是大自然一项幸运的安排——」
「有什么过来了，卡姆洛特，」我打断他，「你听见了吗？」
他凝神听了一会儿。「听见了，」他答，「我们最好上树去，至少等看清是什么再说。」

===Original===
As he climbed into the branches of a nearby tree, I followed him; and there we waited. Distinctly I could hear something moving through the forest as it approached us. The soft carpet of leaves beneath its feet gave forth but little sound﻿—just a rustling of the dry leaves. Nearer and nearer it came, apparently moving leisurely; then, suddenly, its great head came into view from behind the bole of a tree a short distance from us.
“A basto,” whispered Kamlot, but from his previous description of the beast I had already guessed its identity.
It looked like a basto, only more so. From the eyes up its head resembled that of an American bison, with the same short, powerful horns. Its poll and forehead were covered with thick, curly hair, its eyes were small and red-rimmed. Its hide was blue and of about the same texture as that of an elephant, with sparsely growing hairs except upon the head and at the tip of the tail. It stood highest at the shoulders and sloped rapidly to its rump. Its front legs were short and stocky and ended in broad, three-toed feet; its hind legs were longer and the hind feet smaller, a difference necessitated by the fact that the forelegs and feet carried fully three quarters of the beast’s weight. Its muzzle was similar to that of a boar, except that it was broader, and carried heavy, curved tusks.

===Chinese===
他爬上附近一棵树的枝桠，我跟着爬上去；我们便在那里等着。我清楚地听见有东西穿过森林朝我们走来。它脚下柔软的落叶几乎没有声响——只是干叶一阵窸窣。它越来越近，看样子走得悠闲自在；接着，忽地，它的大脑袋从离我们不远的一棵树干后露了出来。
「一头巴斯托。」卡姆洛特悄声道，可凭他先前对此兽的描述，我早已猜出它的身份。
它看上去像一头巴斯托，而且更是有过之。从眼睛往上，它的头像一头美洲野牛，同样生着粗短有力的角。头顶和前额覆着厚厚的卷毛，眼睛小而眼眶发红。它的皮是蓝色的，质地近似大象，毛发稀疏，唯头部和尾尖例外。它肩部最高，迅速向臀部倾斜。前腿短而粗壮，末端是宽阔的三趾脚；后腿较长，后足较小——这一差异是因前腿前足要承载这兽足足四分之三的体重。它的口鼻部近似野猪，只是更宽，并生着粗重、弯曲的獠牙。

===Original===
“Here comes our next meal,” remarked Kamlot in an ordinary tone of voice. The basto stopped and looked about as he heard my companion’s voice. “They are mighty good eating,” added Kamlot, “and we have not eaten for a long while. There is nothing like a basto steak grilled over a wood fire.”
My mouth commenced to water. “Come on,” I said, and started to climb down from the tree, my spear ready in my hand.
“Come back!” called Kamlot. “You don’t know what you are doing.”
The basto had located us and was advancing, uttering a sound that would have put to shame the best efforts of a full-grown lion. I do not know whether to describe it as a bellow or a roar. It started with a series of grunts and then rose in volume until it shook the ground.
“He seems to be angry,” I remarked; “but if we are going to eat him we must kill him first, and how are we to kill him if we remain in the tree?”
“I am not going to remain in the tree,” replied Kamlot, “but you are. You know nothing about hunting these beasts, and you would probably not only get yourself killed but me into the bargain. You stay where you are. I will attend to the basto.”

===Chinese===
「下一顿饭来了。」卡姆洛特以平常的声调说道。巴斯托听见我同伴的声音，停下脚步四下张望。「它们的肉极好吃，」卡姆洛特补充道，「况且我们已很久没吃东西了。再没有比柴火上烤的巴斯托肉排更美的东西了。」
我的嘴里开始流口水。「走。」我说着便要下树，矛握在手中待命。
「回来！」卡姆洛特叫道，「你不知道自己在做什么。」
那巴斯托已发现了我们，正一边逼近，一边发出一种连一头成年雄狮最好的咆哮也要自愧不如的声响。我不知该称之为吼还是嚎。它先是一连串的低哼，继而音量陡升，直到震得地面发颤。
「它像是发怒了，」我说，「可我们既要吃它，就得先把它杀了；我们若待在树上，怎么杀得了它？」
「我并不打算待在树上，」卡姆洛特答，「可你要待着。你对猎这些兽一无所知，很可能不但送了自己的命，还搭上我一条。你待在原地。巴斯托交给我。」

===Original===
This plan did not suit me at all, but I was forced to admit Kamlot’s superior knowledge of things Amtorian and his greater experience and defer to his wishes; but nevertheless I held myself ready to go to his assistance should occasion require.
To my surprise, he dropped his spear to the ground and carried in its stead a slender leafy branch which he cut from the tree before descending to engage the bellowing basto. He did not come down to the floor of the forest directly in front of the beast, but made his way part way around the tree before descending, after asking me to keep the basto’s attention diverted, which I did by shouting and shaking a branch of the tree.
Presently, to my horror, I saw Kamlot out in the open a dozen paces in rear of the animal, armed only with his sword and the leafy branch which he carried in his left hand. His spear lay on the ground not far from the enraged beast and his position appeared utterly hopeless should the basto discover him before he could reach the safety of another tree. Realizing this, I redoubled my efforts to engage the creature’s attention, until Kamlot shouted to me to desist.

===Chinese===
这一安排我一点也不乐意，却不得不承认卡姆洛特在阿姆托之事上见识胜我、经验更丰，便依了他；但我仍暗自戒备，一旦需要便去相助。
出乎我意料，他把矛扔到地上，反倒从树上砍下一根带叶的细枝，拿着它下了树，去迎战那头咆哮的巴斯托。他并不在那兽正前方下到地面，而是绕着树走了半圈才下去，并嘱咐我引开巴斯托的注意——我便大喊大叫、摇晃树枝来办这事。
不久，我惊恐地看见卡姆洛特立在那兽身后十二步的开阔处，手头只有他的剑和左手那根带叶的枝。他的矛就躺在那头狂怒的兽附近不远的地上，倘若巴斯托在他够到另一棵树的安全处之前发现他，他的处境便显得全然无望。意识到这一点，我加倍使劲去吸引那兽的注意，直到卡姆洛特朝我喊停。

===Original===
I thought that he must have gone crazy and should not have heeded him had not his voice attracted the attention of the basto and frustrated any attempt that I might have made to keep the beast’s eyes upon me. The instant that Kamlot called to me the great head turned ponderously in his direction and the savage eyes discovered him. The creature wheeled and stood for a moment eyeing the rash but puny man-thing; then it trotted toward him.
I waited no longer but dropped to the ground with the intention of attacking the thing from the rear. What happened thereafter happened so quickly that it was over almost in the time it takes to tell it. As I started in pursuit, I saw the mighty basto lower its head and charge straight for my companion, who stood there motionless with his puny sword and the leafy branch grasped one in either hand. Suddenly, at the very instant that I thought the creature was about to impale him on those mighty horns, he waved the leaf covered branch in its face and leaped lightly to one side, simultaneously driving the keen point of his blade downward from a point in front of the left shoulder until the steel was buried to the hilt in the great carcass.
The basto stopped, its four legs spread wide; for an instant it swayed, and then it crashed to the ground at the feet of Kamlot. A shout of admiration was on my lips when I chanced to glance upward. What attracted my attention I do not know, perhaps the warning of that inaudible voice which we sometimes call a sixth sense. What I saw drove the basto and the feat of Kamlot from my thoughts.
“My God!” I cried in English, and then in Amtorian, “Look, Kamlot! What are those?”

===Chinese===
我以为他准是疯了，本不会理他，要不是他这一嗓子把巴斯托的注意引了过去，搅黄了我把兽眼拴在我身上的一切努力。卡姆洛特朝我一喊的瞬间，那颗大脑袋便沉甸甸地转向了他，那双凶眼发现了他。那兽一转身，站了片刻，打量着这个冒失却微末的人形之物；随即小跑着朝他奔去。
我不再等，跳到地上，打算从背后攻那兽。其后发生的事发生得极快，几乎说完即完。我刚要追上去，便见那庞大的巴斯托低下头，直冲我的同伴而去——他一动不动地立在那里，一手那柄微末的剑，一手那根带叶的枝。就在我以为那兽要用那两只大角把他挑起的刹那，他突然把带叶的枝朝它脸上一挥，轻巧地向旁一闪，与此同时把剑尖从左肩前方斜斜刺下，直刺到剑柄没入那庞大的身躯。
巴斯托收住步，四腿叉开；晃了一晃，便轰然倒在卡姆洛特脚边。一声赞叹正欲冲上我唇边，我恰巧抬头一望。是什么引我注意，我不知道——也许是那种我们有时称为第六感的、无声之声的警告。我所见之物，把巴斯托和卡姆洛特的壮举一并从我脑海里赶了出去。
「我的天！」我用英语喊了一声，继而又用阿姆托语道：「看，卡姆洛特！那些是什么？」

## VIII On Board the Sofal / VIII 在「索法尔号」上

===Original===
Hovering just above us, I saw what at first appeared to be five enormous birds; but which I soon recognized, despite my incredulity, as winged men. They were armed with swords and daggers, and each carried a long rope at the end of which dangled a wire noose.
“*Voo klangan!*” shouted Kamlot. (The birdmen!)
Even as he spoke a couple of wire nooses settled around each of us. We struggled to free ourselves, striking at the snares with our swords, but our blades made no impression upon the wires, and the ropes to which they were attached were beyond our reach. As we battled futilely to disengage ourselves, the *klangan* settled to the ground, each pair upon opposite sides of the victim they had snared. Thus they held us so that we were helpless, as two cowboys hold a roped steer, while the fifth angan approached us with drawn sword and disarmed us. (Perhaps I should explain that *angan* is singular, *klangan* plural, plurals of Amtorian words being formed by prefixing *kloo* to words commencing with a consonant and *kl* to those commencing with a vowel.)

===Chinese===
就在我们头顶上方盘旋的，我起初看做五只巨鸟；可我很快便不顾难以置信地认出，那是长着翅膀的人。他们带着剑和匕首，每人还提着一根长绳，绳头垂着一只金属活套。
「乌·克兰甘！（voo klangan！）」卡姆洛特喊道。（鸟人！）
他话音未落，我们每人身上便各落下两只金属活套。我们挣扎着想挣脱，用剑去砍那套索，可剑刃在那金属丝上不留一丝痕迹，系在套上的绳我们又够不着。正当我们徒劳地挣扎着想脱身，那些克兰甘（klangan）落到地面，每两只分立他们套住的猎物两侧。他们就这样夹住我们，使我们动弹不得——像两个牛仔牵住一头套了绳的牛——与此同时第五个安甘（angan）拔剑走上前，缴了我们的械。（也许我该解释一下：安甘是单数，克兰甘是复数；阿姆托语的复数，是在以辅音开头的词前加 kloo、以元音开头的词前加 kl 构成的。）

===Original===
Our capture had been accomplished so quickly and so deftly that it was over, with little or no effort on the part of the birdmen, before I had had time to recover from the astonishment that their weird appearance induced. I now recalled having heard Danus speak of *voo klanagan* upon one or two occasions, but I had thought that he referred to poultry breeders or something of that sort. How little could I have dreamed of the reality!
“I guess we are in for it,” remarked Kamlot gloomily.
“What will they do with us?” I inquired.
“Ask them,” he replied.
“Who are you?” demanded one of our captors.

===Chinese===
我们被擒得如此之快、如此之巧，鸟人们几乎不费吹灰之力便已得手——快得我还来不及从他们那怪异模样所引起的惊愕中回过神来。此刻我才想起，曾听达努斯在一两个场合提过「乌·克兰甘」，可我原以为他指的是养家禽的人或诸如此类。我对真相之离奇，做梦也想不到！
「我想我们在劫难逃了。」卡姆洛特沮丧地说。
「他们会把我们怎么样？」我问。
「你问他们。」他答。
「你们是什么人？」俘获我们的人当中有一个喝问。

===Original===
For some reason I was astonished to hear him speak, although I do not know why anything should have astonished me now. “I am a stranger from another world,” I told him. “My friend and I have no quarrel with you. Let us go.”
“You are wasting your breath,” Kamlot advised me.
“Yes, he is wasting his breath,” agreed the angan. “You are Vepajans, and we have orders to bring Vepajans to the ship. You do not look like a Vepajan,” he added, surveying me from head to feet, “but the other does.”
“Anyway, you are not a Thorist, and therefore you must be an enemy,” interjected another.
They removed the nooses from about us and tied ropes around our necks and other ropes about our bodies beneath our arms; then two *klangan* seized the ropes attached to Kamlot and two more those attached to me, and, spreading their wings, rose into the air, carrying us with them. Our weight was supported by the ropes beneath our arms, but the other ropes were a constant suggestion to us of what might happen if we did not behave ourselves.

===Chinese===
不知为何，听见他开口我竟又吃了一惊，尽管如今还有什么能叫我吃惊的，我自己也不晓得了。「我是从另一个世界来的外乡人，」我告诉他，「我朋友与你等无冤无仇。放我们走吧。」
「你白费口舌。」卡姆洛特劝我。
「是啊，他白费口舌，」那安甘附和，「你们是韦帕贾人，我们奉命把韦帕贾人带回船上。你看着不像韦帕贾人，」他把我从头到脚打量一番，补了一句，「可另一位像。」
「反正你不是索尔党人，那便是敌人。」另一个插嘴。
他们把我们身上的活套解下，在我们脖子上系一根绳，又在腋下身上另系一根；接着两个克兰甘抓住系在卡姆洛特身上的绳，另两个抓住系在我身上的，展开双翼，腾空而起，把我们一同带走。我们的分量由腋下那根绳承着，可脖子上那根却时刻提醒我们：不老实的话会有什么下场。

===Original===
As they flew, winding their way among the trees, our bodies were suspended but a few feet above the ground, for the forest lanes were often low ceiled by overhanging branches. The *klangan* talked a great deal among themselves, shouting to one another and laughing and singing, seemingly well satisfied with themselves and their exploit. Their voices were soft and mellow, and their songs were vaguely reminiscent of Negro spirituals, a similarity which may have been enhanced by the color of their skins, which were very dark.
As Kamlot was carried in front of me, I had an opportunity to observe the physical characteristics of these strange creatures into whose hands we had fallen. They had low, receding foreheads, huge, beaklike noses, and undershot jaws; their eyes were small and close set, their ears flat and slightly pointed. Their chests were large and shaped like those of birds, and their arms were very long, ending in long-fingered, heavy-nailed hands. The lower part of the torso was small, the hips narrow, the legs very short and stocky, ending in three-toed feet equipped with long, curved talons. Feathers grew upon their heads instead of hair. When they were excited, as when they attacked us, these feathers stand erect, but ordinarily they lie flat. They are all alike; commencing near the root they are marked with a band of white, next comes a band of black, then another of white, and the tip is red. Similar feathers also grow at the lower extremity of the torso in front, and there is another, quite large bunch just above the buttocks﻿—a gorgeous tail which they open into a huge pompon when they wish to show off.

===Chinese===
他们飞行时在树间穿行，我们悬在离地不过几尺的空中，因为林间通道常常被低垂的枝叶封了顶。克兰甘彼此话极多，相互呼喝、又笑又唱，似乎对自己和自己的这桩战绩颇为得意。他们的嗓音柔和圆润，唱的歌隐隐叫人联想到黑人灵歌——这一相似也许还因他们肤色极黑而更甚。
卡姆洛特被抬在我前面，我便有机会端详这些俘获我们的怪物的形貌。他们额头低而向后斜缩，鼻子大而似喙，下巴前突；眼睛小而靠得近，耳朵扁平而略尖。胸部宽大，形如鸟胸，双臂极长，末端是一双长指、重爪的手。躯干下半部窄小，髋部狭，腿极短而粗壮，末端是三趾的脚，配有长而弯曲的爪。他们头上长的不是头发，而是羽毛。激动时——譬如攻击我们时——这些羽毛会竖起，平常则平伏。它们一根根都一样：靠根处是一道白纹，接着一道黑纹，再一道白纹，尖端是红色。身躯下端前面也长有类似的羽毛，臀部正上方另有一簇颇大的——一条华丽的尾羽，他们想炫耀时便把它张开成一个大绒球。

===Original===
Their wings, which consist of a very thin membrane supported on a light framework, are similar in shape to those of a bat and do not appear adequate to the support of the apparent weight of the creatures’ bodies, but I was to learn later that this apparent weight is deceptive, since their bones, like the bones of true birds, are hollow.
The creatures carried us a considerable distance, though how far I do not know. We were in the air fully eight hours; and, where the forest permitted, they flew quite rapidly. They seemed utterly tireless, though Kamlot and I were all but exhausted long before they reached their destination. The ropes beneath our arms cut into our flesh, and this contributed to our exhaustion as did our efforts to relieve the agony by seizing the ropes above us and supporting the weight of our bodies with our hands.

===Chinese===
他们的翅膀是一层极薄的膜，张在轻巧的骨架上，形状近似蝙蝠的翼，看上去不足以支撑他们那身躯看起来的重量；可后来我才知道，这看起来的重量有欺骗性，因为他们的骨头与真正鸟类的骨头一样，是中空的。
这些怪物把我们运了相当远，究竟多远我不知。我们在空中整整八个小时；森林允许的地方，他们飞得颇快。他们似乎全然不知疲倦，而卡姆洛特和我早在他们抵达目的地之前许久，便已近乎虚脱。腋下的绳勒进肉里，使我们愈发疲惫；我们拼命抓住头顶的绳、用手撑住自己身子的重量以减轻痛苦，这同样耗尽力气。

===Original===
But, as all things must, this hideous journey ended at last Suddenly we broke from the forest and winged out across a magnificent landlocked harbor, and for the first time I looked upon the waters of a Venusan sea. Between two points that formed the harbor’s entrance I could see it stretching away as far as the eye could reach﻿—mysterious, intriguing, provocative. What strange lands and stranger people lay off there beyond the beyond? Would I ever know?
Suddenly now my attention and my thoughts were attracted to something in the left foreground that I had not before noticed; a ship lay at anchor on the quiet waters of the harbor and just beyond it a second ship. Toward one of them our captors were winging. As we approached the nearer and smaller, I saw a craft that differed but little in the lines of its hull from earthly ships. It had a very high bow, its prow was sharp and sloped forward in a scimitarlike curve; the ship was long and narrow of beam. It looked as though it might have been built for speed. But what was its motive power? It had no masts, sails, stacks, nor funnels. Aft were two oval houses﻿—a smaller one resting upon the top of a larger; on top of the upper house was an oval tower surmounted by a small crow’s nest. There were doors and windows in the two houses and the tower. As we came closer, I could see a number of open hatches in the deck and people standing on the walkways that surrounded the tower and the upper house and also upon the main deck. They were watching our approach.

===Chinese===
然而万事皆有尽头，这段可怖的旅程终于也到了头。骤然间我们冲出森林，掠翼飞越一片壮丽的内陆港湾，我头一次看见了金星海的水。在构成港口入口的两个岬角之间，我望见海面一直延伸到目力所及的尽头——神秘、诱人、引人遐思。那遥遥的远方之外，有些怎样奇异的地土、更为奇异的人？我可有知晓的一天？
这时我的注意和心思忽然被左前方一处先前未留意的东西吸引过去：港湾平静的水面上锚着一艘船，紧挨着它还有一艘。俘获我们的人正朝其中一艘飞去。靠近那艘较近、较小的船时，我看见一艘船体线条与地球船只相差无几的船。它船首很高，船头尖削，呈弯刀般的曲线向前斜伸；船身长而窄。看样子是为速度而造。可它的动力是什么？它没有桅、没有帆、没有烟囱、也没有通气筒。船尾有两座椭圆的舱室——小的一座叠在大的之上；上层舱室顶上是一座椭圆的塔，塔顶有一只小小的桅斗。两座舱室和塔上都有门窗。再近些，我看见甲板上开着好几个舱口，塔和上层舱室四周的走道上、以及主甲板上都站着人。他们正看着我们靠近。

===Original===
As our captors deposited us upon the deck, we were immediately surrounded by a horde of jabbering men. A man whom I took to be an officer ordered the ropes removed from us, and while this was being done he questioned the *klangan* who had brought us.
All the men that I saw were similar in color and physique to the Vepajans, but their countenances were heavy and unintelligent; very few of them were good-looking, and only one or two might have been called handsome. I saw evidences of age among them and of disease﻿—the first I had seen on Amtor.
After the ropes had been removed, the officer ordered us to follow him, after detailing four villainous-looking fellows to guard us, and conducted us aft and up to the tower that surmounted the smaller house. Here he left us outside the tower, which he entered.

===Chinese===
俘获我们的人把我们丢到甲板上，我们立刻被一群叽叽喳喳的人围住。一个我认作军官的人下令解开我们身上的绳，一边解一边盘问送我们来的克兰甘。
我所见的这些人，肤色体格都与韦帕贾人相仿，可面容笨重而不聪敏；其中好看的极少，称得上英俊的只有一两个。我在他们中间看到了衰老的痕迹，也看到了疾病——这都是我在阿姆托头一次见到。
绳索解下后，军官命我们跟他走，又点了四个面相凶恶的家伙看管我们，把我们领到船尾、上了那座叠在较小舱室之上的塔。他把我们留在塔外，自己进了塔。

===Original===
The four men guarding us eyed us with surly disfavor. “Vepajans, eh!” sneered one. “Think you’re better than ordinary men, don’t you? But you’ll find out you ain’t, not in The Free Land of Thora; there everybody’s equal. I don’t see no good in bringing your kind into the country anyway. If I had my way you’d get a dose of this,” and he tapped a weapon that hung in a holster at his belt.
The weapon, or the grip of it, suggested a pistol of some kind, and I supposed that it was one of those curious firearms discharging deadly rays, that Kamlot had described to me. I was about to ask the fellow to let me see it when the officer emerged from the tower and ordered the guard to bring us in.
We were escorted into a room in which sat a scowling man with a most unprepossessing countenance. There was a sneer on his face as he appraised us, the sneer of the inferior man for his superior, that tries to hide but only reveals the inferiority complex that prompts it. I knew that I was not going to like him.

===Chinese===
看管我们的四个人阴沉着脸、满怀敌意地打量我们。「韦帕贾人，嘿！」其中一个讥讽道，「自以为比寻常人高贵，是不是？可你们会知道并非如此——在索拉（Thora）自由邦人人平等。反正我看不出把你们这种货色弄到国里来有什么好处。要依着我，就给你来一下这个。」他拍了拍腰间枪套里挂着的一件武器。
那武器，或说它的握柄，像某种手枪；我猜它便是卡姆洛特向我描述过的那种发射致命射线的古怪枪械。我正要叫那家伙给我看看，军官便从塔里出来，命看守把我们带进去。
我们被押进一间屋子，里头坐着一个横眉竖眼、面相极不讨喜的人。他打量我们时脸上挂着冷笑——那是下等人对上等人的冷笑，欲盖弥彰，反倒只暴露了催生它的自卑。我自知不会喜欢他。

===Original===
“Two more *klooganfal*!” he exclaimed. (A *ganfal* is a criminal.) “Two more of the beasts that tried to grind down the workers; but you didn’t succeed, did you? Now we are the masters. You’ll find that out even before we reach Thora. Is either of you a doctor?”
Kamlot shook his head. “Not I,” he said.
The fellow, whom I took to be the captain of the ship, eyed me closely. “You are no Vepajan,” he said. “What are you, anyway? No one ever saw a man with yellow hair and blue eyes before.”
