“As far as you are concerned,” I replied, “I am a Vepajan. I have never been in any other country in Amtor.”
“What do you mean by saying as far as I am concerned?” he demanded.
“Because it doesn’t make any difference what you think about it,” I snapped. I did not like the fellow, and when I do not like people I have difficulty in hiding the fact. In this case I did not try to hide it.

===Chinese===
「又来了两个克罗甘法尔（klooganfal）！」他叫道。（甘法尔即罪犯。）「又来了两只妄图压榨劳工的畜生；可你们没能得逞，是吧？如今我们才是主子。这一点，不等我们到索拉你们便会明白。你们两个谁是医生？」
卡姆洛特摇头。「我不是。」他说。
这家伙——我猜他是船长——紧盯着我打量。「你不是韦帕贾人，」他说，「你到底是什么人？从没有人见过黄头发、蓝眼睛的人。」
「就你而言，」我答，「我是韦帕贾人。阿姆托上别的国家我从没去过。」
「你说『就你而言』，什么意思？」他追问。
「因为你怎么想，无关紧要。」我顶了回去。我不喜欢这家伙，而我不喜欢一个人时，很难掩饰——这一次我也无意掩饰。

===Original===
He flushed and half rose from his chair. “It doesn’t, eh?” he cried.
“Sit down,” I advised him. “You’re here under orders to bring back Vepajans. Nobody cares what you think about them, but you’ll get into trouble if you don’t bring them back.”
Diplomacy would have curbed my tongue, but I am not particularly diplomatic, especially when I am angry, and now I was both angry and disgusted, for there had been something in the attitude of all these people toward us that bespoke ignorant prejudice and bitterness. Furthermore, I surmised from scraps of information I had picked up from Danus, as well as from the remarks of the sailor who had announced that he would like to kill us, that I was not far wrong in my assumption that the officer I had thus addressed would be exceeding his authority if he harmed us. However, I realized that I was taking chances, and awaited with interest the effect of my words.
The fellow took them like a whipped cur and subsided after a single weakly blustering, “We’ll see about that.” He turned to a book that lay open before him. “What is your name?” he asked, nodding in Kamlot’s direction. Even his nod was obnoxious.

===Chinese===
他涨红了脸，半个身子从椅上抬起。「无关紧要，嘿？」他叫。
「坐下，」我劝他，「你来此是奉命带韦帕贾人回去的。你怎么看他们，没人在乎；可你若不带他们回去，便要倒霉。」
若是讲究外交辞令，本该管住舌头；可我并不如何长于外交，尤其怒时更不——此刻我又怒又厌，因为这些人待我们的态度里，有一种透着无知偏见与刻毒的东西。况且，我从达努斯那里听来的片言只语，加上那个水手扬言要杀我们的那番话，让我推断：我这样顶撞的这位军官若加害于我们，便越了权——这一推断料想不远。不过我也明白自己在冒险，便饶有兴味地等着看我这番话的效用。
这家伙像条挨了打的癞狗，虚张声势地咕哝了一句「走着瞧」，便泄了气。他转向面前摊开的本子。「你叫什么名字？」他朝卡姆洛特那边一点头问。连他这点头都叫人厌恶。

===Original===
“Kamlot of Zar,” replied my companion.
“What is your profession?”
“Hunter and wood carver.”
“You are a Vepajan?”
“Yes.”
“From what city of Vepaja?”
“From Kooaad,” replied Kamlot.
“And you?” demanded the officer, addressing me.
“I am Carson of Napier,” I replied, using the Amtorian form; “I am a Vepajan from Kooaad.”
“What is your profession?”
“I am an *aviator*,” I replied, using the English word and English pronunciation.
“A what?” he demanded. “I never heard of such a thing.” He tried to write the word in his book and then he tried to pronounce it, but he could do neither, as the Amtorians have no equivalents for many of our vowel sounds and seem unable even to pronounce them. Had I written the word for him in Amtorian he would have pronounced it ah-vy-ah-tore, as they cannot form the long *a* and short *o* sounds, and their *i* is always long.

===Chinese===
「扎尔的卡姆洛特。」我的同伴答。
「你的营生是什么？」
「猎人和木雕师。」
「你是韦帕贾人？」
「是。」
「韦帕贾哪座城？」
「库阿德（Kooaad）。」卡姆洛特答。
「你呢？」军官转向我。
「我是内皮尔的卡森（Carson of Napier），」我用阿姆托的格式答道，「我是来自库阿德的韦帕贾人。」
「你的营生是什么？」
「我是一名 aviator（飞行员）。」我用英语单词和英语发音答道。
「一个什么？」他追问，「我从没听过这种东西。」他想在本子上写下这个词，又想把它念出来，可两样都办不到——因为阿姆托语没有我们的许多元音，他们似乎连念都念不出来。倘若我用阿姆托语写给他，他便会念成「ah-vy-ah-tore」——因为他们发不出长 a 和短 o 的音，而他们的 i 永远是长音。

===Original===
Finally, to cover his ignorance, he wrote something in his book, but what it was I did not know; then he looked up at me again. “Are you a doctor?”
“Yes,” I replied, and as the officer made the notation in his book, I glanced at Kamlot out of the corner of an eye and winked.
“Take them away,” the man now directed, “and be careful of this one,” he added, indicating me; “he is a doctor.”
We were taken to the main deck and led forward to the accompaniment of jeers and jibes from the sailors congregated on the deck. I saw the *klangan* strutting around, their tail feathers erect. When they saw us, they pointed at Kamlot, and I heard them telling some of the sailors that he was the one who had slain the basto with a single sword thrust, a feat which appeared to force their admiration, as well it might have.

===Chinese===
末了，为了掩饰自己的无知，他在本子上胡乱写了点什么——写了什么我不得而知；接着他又抬头看我。「你是医生吗？」
「是。」我答，趁军官在本子上登记时，我用眼角瞟了卡姆洛特一眼，挤了挤眼。
「把他们带走，」那人下令，又补一句，「这一个小心些，」他指着我，「他是医生。」
我们被带到主甲板，在挤满甲板的水手们的讥笑嘲骂声中被押向船首。我看见那些克兰甘正翘着尾羽大摇大摆地走着。他们一见我们，便朝卡姆洛特指指点点，我听见他们告诉几个水手，说他就是那个一剑刺死巴斯托的人——这一壮举似乎迫使他们心生敬佩，也理当如此。

===Original===
We were escorted to an open hatch and ordered below into a dark, poorly ventilated hole, where we found several other prisoners. Some of them were Thorans undergoing punishment for infractions of discipline; others were Vepajan captives like ourselves, and among the latter was one who recognized Kamlot and hailed him as we descended into their midst.
“*Jodades*, Kamlot!” he cried, voicing the Amtorian greeting “luck-to-you.”
“*Ra jodades*,” replied Kamlot; “what ill fortune brings Honan here?”
“ ‘Ill fortune’ does not describe it,” replied Honan; “catastrophe would be a better word. The *klangan* were seeking women as well as men; they saw Duare” (pronounced Doo-ah-ree) “and pursued her; as I sought to protect her they captured me.”
“Your sacrifice was not in vain,” said Kamlot; “had you died in the performance of such a duty it would not have been in vain.”
“But it was in vain; that is the catastrophe.”
“What do you mean?” demanded Kamlot.
“I mean that they got her,” replied Honan dejectedly.
“They captured Duare!” exclaimed Kamlot in tones of horror. “By the life of the *jong*, it cannot be.”

===Chinese===
我们被押到一个敞开的舱口前，被命令下到一个黑黢黢、闷不通风的窟窿里，里头另有几个囚徒。其中一些是因违反纪律受罚的索拉人；其余是和我们一样的韦帕贾俘虏——后者当中有一个认出了卡姆洛特，我们一进他们中间，他便招呼起来。
「乔达德斯（jodades），卡姆洛特！」他喊出阿姆托的问候——「祝你好运」。
「拉·乔达德斯，」卡姆洛特答，「什么厄运把霍南（Honan）带到了这里？」
「『厄运』二字不足以形容，」霍南答，「『大难临头』还差不多。克兰甘既要抓男人，也要抓女人；他们看见了杜阿雷（Duare，读作「杜—阿—雷」）便追她；我试图护她，就被他们抓住了。」
「你的牺牲没有白费，」卡姆洛特说，「你若因尽此职责而死，也算不得白费。」
「可它就是白费了；这正是大难所在。」
「你这话怎讲？」卡姆洛特追问。
「我是说，他们把她抓走了。」霍南颓然道。
「他们抓住了杜阿雷！」卡姆洛特惊恐地叫道，「以「君」的性命起誓，这不可能。」

===Original===
“I wish it were not,” said Honan.
“Where is she? on this ship?” demanded Kamlot.
“No; they took her to the other, the larger one.”
Kamlot appeared crushed, and I could only attribute his dejection to the hopelessness of a lover who has irretrievably lost his beloved. Our association had not been either sufficiently close nor long to promote confidences, and so I was not surprised that I had never heard him mention the girl, Duare, and, naturally, under the circumstances, I could not question him concerning her. I therefore respected his grief and his silence, and left him to his own sad thoughts.

===Chinese===
「我但愿不是。」霍南说。
「她在哪儿？这艘船上？」卡姆洛特追问。
「不；他们把她带走了，带到那艘——较大的——船上去了。」
卡姆洛特像是被打垮了，我只能把他的颓丧归因于一个永远失去了心上人的恋人的绝望。我们相交的日子既不够长，也不够亲密，不足以促成交心，因此我从未听他提起过杜阿雷这姑娘并不奇怪；自然，在此情形下我也不能就此向他发问。于是我尊重他的悲痛与沉默，让他独自沉浸在他悲伤的念头里。

===Original===
Shortly after dawn the following morning the ship got under way. I wished that I might have been on deck to view the fascinating sights of this strange world, and my precarious situation as a prisoner of the hated Thorists engendered less regret than the fact that I, the first earth man to sail the seas of Venus, was doomed to be cooped up in a stuffy hole below deck where I could see nothing. But if I had feared being kept below for the duration of the voyage, I was soon disillusioned, for shortly after the ship got under way we were all ordered on deck and set to scrubbing and polishing.
As we came up from below, the ship was just passing between the two headlands that formed the entrance to the harbor, in the wake of the larger vessel; and I obtained an excellent view of the adjacent land, the shore that we were leaving, and the wide expanse of ocean stretching away to the horizon.

===Chinese===
次日破晓不久，船便启航了。我但愿自己能在甲板上一瞻这奇异世界的迷人风光，而作为可憎的索尔党人的阶下囚这一岌岌可危的处境，反倒不及另一件事更令我遗憾——我，头一个航行金星之海的地球人，竟注定要被关在甲板下一个什么也看不见的憋闷窟窿里。可我若担心整段航程都要被关在下头，那很快就幻灭了——船启航后不久，我们便全被赶上甲板，擦擦洗洗、揩揩抹抹起来。
我们从下面上来时，船正尾随那艘较大的船，穿过构成港口入口的两座岬角；我把邻近的陆地、我们正要驶离的海岸，以及一直延伸到天边的辽阔大洋，看了个清楚。

===Original===
The headlands were rocky promontories clothed with verdure of delicate hues and supporting comparatively few trees, which were of a smaller variety than the giants upon the mainland. These latter presented a truly awe inspiring spectacle from the open sea to the eyes of an earth man, their mighty boles rearing their weirdly colored foliage straight up for five thousand feet, where they were lost to view among the clouds. But I was not permitted to gaze for long upon the wonders of the scene. I had not been ordered above for the purpose of satisfying the aesthetic longings of my soul.
Kamlot and I were set to cleaning and polishing guns. There were a number of these on either side of the deck, one at the stern, and two on the tower deck. I was surprised when I saw them, for there had been no sign of armament when I came on board the preceding day; but I was not long in discovering the explanation﻿—the guns were mounted on disappearing carriages, and when lowered, a sliding hatch, flush with the deck, concealed them.

===Chinese===
那两座岬角是岩石嶙峋的海角，披着色调柔嫩的草木，树木相对稀少，又比大陆上的那些巨树矮小得多。从外海望到一个地球人眼里，这些大陆巨树呈现出一幅着实令人敬畏的景象：它们粗壮的树干把那颜色诡异的枝叶直直擎起五千英尺，没入云端不见了。可我未被允许长久凝望这奇景。我被赶上甲板，可不是为了满足我灵魂的审美渴望。
卡姆洛特和我被派去擦洗保养炮。甲板两侧各有一些，船尾一门，塔甲板上两门。我看见它们时颇感惊讶，因为前一日我上船时全无武备的迹象；可我很快便弄明白了解释——这些炮装在隐显炮架上，降下时，一块与甲板齐平的滑动舱盖把它们遮了起来。

===Original===
The barrels of these pieces were about eight inches in diameter, while the bore was scarcely larger than my little finger; the sights were ingenious and complicated, but there was no breech block in evidence nor any opening into a breech, unless there was one hidden beneath a hoop that encircled the breech, to which it was heavily bolted. The only thing that I could discover that might have been a firing device projected from the rear of the breech and resembled the rotating crank that is used to revolve the breech block in some types of earthly guns.
The barrels of the guns were about fifteen feet long and of the same diameter from breech to muzzle. When in action they can be extended beyond the rail of the ship about two thirds of their length, thus affording a wider horizontal range and more deck room, which would be of value on a ship such as that on which I was a captive, which was of narrow beam.
“What do these guns fire?” I asked Kamlot, who was working at my side.
“T-rays,” he replied.

===Chinese===
这些炮的炮管直径约八英寸，可炮膛却比我小指粗不了多少；瞄准具精巧而复杂，但既看不见闭锁块，也看不见通向炮膛的开口——除非在紧紧栓住炮膛的一道箍之下藏着这么一个。我唯一能找出来的、可能是击发装置的东西，从炮膛后部伸出来，形似地球上某些火炮用来旋转闭锁块的那种旋转摇柄。
炮的炮管长约十五英尺，从炮膛到炮口直径一致。作战时它们可向舷外伸出约三分之二长度，从而获得更宽的射界和更多的甲板空间——这对于我所被囚的这种窄船尤为难得。
「这些炮发射什么？」我问身旁干活的卡姆洛特。
「T 射线。」他答。

===Original===
“Do those differ materially from the R-rays you described when you were telling me about the small arms used by the Thorans?”
“The R-ray destroys only animal tissue,” he replied, “while there is nothing that the T-ray may not dissipate. It is a most dangerous ray to work with because even the material of the gun barrel itself is not wholly impervious to it, and the only reason that it can be used at all is that its greatest force is expended along the line of least resistance, which in this case naturally is the bore of the gun. But eventually it destroys the gun itself.”
“How is it fired?” I asked.
He touched the crank at the end of the breech. “By turning this, a shutter is raised that permits radiations from element 93 to impinge on the charge, which consists of element 97, thus releasing the deadly T-ray.”
“Why couldn’t we turn this gun about and rake the ship above deck,” I suggested, “thus wiping out the Thorans and giving us our freedom?”
He pointed to a small, irregular hole in the end of the crank shaft. “Because we haven’t the key that fits this,” he replied.
“Who has the key?”
“The officers have keys to the guns they command,” he replied. “In the captain’s cabin are keys to all the guns, and he carries a master key that will unlock any of them. At least that was the system in the ancient Vepajan navy, and it is doubtless the same today in the Thoran navy.”

===Chinese===
「它与你跟我说起索拉人所用小型武器时描述的 R 射线，有本质不同吗？」
「R 射线只破坏动物组织，」他答，「而 T 射线则没有什么不能消融。操作它是一种极危险的射线，因为连炮管本身的材料也不能完全抵御它；它之所以能用，唯一的原因是它最大的力沿最小阻力线耗去——此处的最小阻力线，自然是炮膛。可久而久之，它终会把炮本身也毁了。」
「它怎么击发？」我问。
他碰了碰炮膛末端的摇柄。「转动这个，便抬起一扇快门，让 93 号元素的辐射射到由 97 号元素组成的装药上，从而释放致命的 T 射线。」
「我们何不把这门炮转过来，扫向甲板上的船身，」我提议，「一举把索拉人收拾了，重获自由？」
他指了指摇柄轴末端一个小小的不规则孔洞。「因为我们没有配这孔的钥匙。」他答。
「钥匙在谁手里？」
「军官们各有其所指挥之炮的钥匙，」他答，「船长舱里有所有炮的钥匙，他自己还随身带一把万能钥匙，能开任何一门炮。至少古时韦帕贾海军是这样的体制，如今索拉海军想必也是如此。」

===Original===
“I wish we could get hold of the master key,” I said.
“So do I,” he agreed, “but that is impossible.”
“Nothing is impossible,” I retorted.
He made no answer, and I did not pursue the subject, but I certainly gave it a lot of thought.
As I worked, I noted the easy, noiseless propulsion of the ship and asked Kamlot what drove it. His explanation was long and rather technical; suffice it to say that the very useful element 93 (*vik-ro*) is here again employed upon a substance called *lor*, which contains a considerable proportion of the element *yor-san* (105). The action of *vik-ro* upon *yor-san* results in absolute annihilation of the *lor*, releasing all its energy. When you consider that there is eighteen thousand million times as much energy liberated by the annihilation of a ton of coal than by its combustion you will appreciate the inherent possibilities of this marvellous Venusan scientific discovery. Fuel for the life of the ship could be carried in a pint jar.

===Chinese===
「我们要是能弄到那把万能钥匙就好了。」我说。
「我也这么想，」他赞同，「可那不可能。」
「没有什么不可能。」我反驳。
他没有回答，我也没再往下说，但我心里着实琢磨了很久。
我干活时，留意到船的行进轻松而无声，便问卡姆洛特是什么推动它的。他的解释又长又颇为专业；简而言之，那极为有用的 93 号元素（vik-ro，维克罗）此处又被用来作用于一种叫做 lor（洛尔）的物质，后者含相当比例的 105 号元素 yor-san（约尔桑）。维克罗作用于约尔桑，使洛尔彻底湮灭，释放其全部能量。须知一吨煤湮灭所释放的能量，是其燃烧所释放的一百八十亿倍；想到这一点，你便能体会金星人这项惊人科学发现所蕴含的潜力。够整艘船一辈子用的燃料，一只一品脱的罐子便装得下。

===Original===
I noticed as the day progressed that we cruised parallel to a coast line, after crossing one stretch of ocean where no land was in sight, and thereafter for several days I noted the same fact﻿—land was almost always in sight. This suggested that the land area of Venus might be much greater in proportion to its seas; but I had no opportunity to satisfy my curiosity on that point, and of course I took no stock in the maps that Danus had shown me, since the Amtorians’ conception of the shape of their world precluded the existence of any dependable maps.
Kamlot and I had been separated, he having been detailed to duty in the ship’s galley, which was located in the forward part of the main deck house aft. I struck up a friendship with Honan; but we did not work together, and at night we were usually so tired that we conversed but little before falling asleep on the hard floor of our prison. One night, however, the sorrow of Kamlot having been brought to my mind by my own regretful recollections of the nameless girl of the garden, I asked Honan who Duare was.
“She is the hope of Vepaja,” he replied, “perhaps the hope of a world.”

===Chinese===
这一天下来我留意到，在穿过一段不见陆地的大洋之后，我们便沿着一条海岸线航行；其后几天我注意到同样的情形——几乎总能看见陆地。这暗示金星的陆地面积相对其海洋或许要大得多；可我无从满足这一好奇，自然，达努斯给我看的那些地图我也不信——既然阿姆托人对他们世界的形状尚且抱着那样一种观念，便不可能存在任何靠得住的地图。
卡姆洛特和我被分开了，他被派到船上的厨房干活——厨房位于主甲板室前部靠后处。我与霍南结下了友谊；可我们不在一起干活，夜里又往往累得在那硬邦邦的囚室地板上倒头便睡，很少交谈。然而有一夜，我因自己忆起花园里那位无名姑娘、又念及卡姆洛特的悲伤，便问霍南，杜阿雷是谁。
「她是韦帕贾的希望，」他答，「也许是一个世界的希望。」

## IX Soldiers of Liberty / IX 自由之兵

===Original===
Constant association breeds a certain camaraderie even between enemies. As the days passed, the hatred and contempt which the common sailors appeared to have harbored for us when we first came aboard the ship were replaced by an almost friendly familiarity, as though they had discovered that we were not half bad fellows after all; and, for my part, I found much to like in these simple though ignorant men. That they were the dupes of unscrupulous leaders is about the worst that may be said of them. Most of them were kindly and generous; but their ignorance made them gullible, and their emotions were easily aroused by specious arguments that would have made no impression upon intelligent minds.
Naturally, I became better acquainted with my fellow prisoners than with my guards, and our relations were soon established upon a friendly basis. They were greatly impressed by my blond hair and blue eyes which elicited inquiries as to my genesis. As I answered their questions truthfully, they became deeply interested in my story, and every evening after the day’s work was completed I was besieged for tales of the mysterious, far distant world from which I came. Unlike the highly intelligent Vepajans, they believed all that I told them, with the result that I was soon a hero in their eyes; I should have been a god had they had any conception of deities of any description.

===Chinese===
即便在仇敌之间，朝夕相处也会生出几分同袍之情。随着日子一天天过去，那些普通水手初时待我们那般嫌恶轻蔑，如今竟换作一种近乎友善的熟络，仿佛终于看出我们到底也算不得坏人；而就我而言，在这群头脑简单、见识浅陋的人身上，倒也寻到不少可亲可爱之处。他们至多不过是无耻头领们手里的工具罢了。他们大多心地善良、为人慷慨；只是无知使他们易受哄骗，几句似是而非的说辞便足以煽动他们的情绪——这些话落到明白人耳中，原是激不起半点波澜的。
我自然与同囚的伙伴们比与看守更熟络些，关系很快便建立在友善的基础之上。我的金发碧眼令他们大为惊奇，追问起我的来历。我如实相告，他们对我所说的故事深感兴趣，于是每日收工之后的晚上，我总被团团围住，要我讲我那遥远而神秘的故土的故事。与那些聪慧过人的韦帕贾人不同，他们对我所说的一切照单全收，结果很快我便成了他们眼中的英雄；倘若他们对神祇有半点概念，怕是要把我奉若神明了。

===Original===
In turn, I questioned them; and discovered, with no surprise, that they were not at all contented with their lots. The former free men among them had long since come to the realization that they had exchanged this freedom, and their status of wage earners, for slavery to the state, that could no longer be hidden by a nominal equality.

===Chinese===
我也反过来打听他们的境况；发现他们对自己的命运全然不满，这一点并不出乎我意料。他们当中原本自由的人，早已认清：他们拿这自由、拿雇工的身份，换来了对国家的奴役——而这奴役，再也无法用一句名义上的平等来遮掩了。

===Original===
Among the prisoners were three to whom I was particularly attracted by certain individual characteristics in each. There was Gamfor, for instance, a huge, hulking fellow who had been a farmer in the old days under the jongs. He was unusually intelligent, and although he had taken part in the revolution, he was now bitter in his denunciation of the Thorists, though this he was careful to whisper to me in secrecy.
Another was Kiron, the soldier, a clean-limbed, handsome, athletic fellow who had served in the army of the jong, but mutinied with the others at the time of the revolution. He was being disciplined now for insubordination to an officer who had been a petty government clerk before his promotion.
The third had been a slave. His name was Zog. What he lacked in intelligence he made up in strength and good nature. He had killed an officer who had struck him and was being taken back to Thora for trial and execution. Zog was proud of the fact that he was a free man, though he admitted that the edge was taken off his enthusiasm by the fact that everyone else was free and the realization that he had enjoyed more freedom as a slave than he did now as a freeman.

===Chinese===
囚犯中有三人，各因其独特之处而格外引起我的注意。比如甘福尔，一个身形魁伟、五大三粗的汉子，往昔在「君」治下曾是个农夫。他聪明得出奇，虽说也曾投身革命，如今对索尔党（Thorists）却痛骂不已——只是这话他只敢偷偷对我耳语。
另一个是当兵的基隆，一个体格匀称、相貌英俊、身手矫健的汉子，原在「君」的军队里当差，革命时与众一同哗变。他如今受罚，是因为顶撞了一位军官、不肯听命——而那位军官在升迁之前，不过是政府里一个卑微的小书记。
第三个原本是奴隶。他叫佐格。智力上欠缺之处，他以力气和好脾气来弥补。他杀了一个动手打他的军官，正被押回索拉（Thora）受审处决。佐格以自己是个自由人为荣，却又承认这股得意被人人皆自由这一事实打了折扣——他还意识到，自己当奴隶时反倒比现在当自由人更自在些。

===Original===
“Then,” he explained, “I had one master; now I have as many masters as there are government officials, spies, and soldiers, none of whom cares anything about me, while my old master was kind to me and looked after my welfare.”
“Would you like to be really free?” I asked him, for a plan had been slowly forming in my mind.
But to my surprise he said, “No, I should rather be a slave.”
“But you’d like to choose your own master, wouldn’t you?” I demanded.
“Certainly,” he replied, “if I could find someone who would be kind to me and protect me from the Thorists.”

===Chinese===
「那时候，」他解释道，「我只伺候一个主人；如今政府官、暗探、兵丁有多少，我就有多少主人，没一个把我当人看。可我那旧主待我仁厚，还照管我的死活。」
「你想不想真正地自由一回？」我问他——一个计划已在我心里慢慢成形。
可出乎我意料，他说：「不，我宁可当奴隶。」
「可你想自己挑主人，是吧？」我追问。
「那是自然，」他答，「只要我能找一个待我好、肯护着我免受索尔党欺负的人。」

===Original===
“And if you could escape from them now, you would like to do so?”
“Of course! But what do you mean? I cannot escape from them.”
“Not without help,” I agreed, “but if others would join you, would you make the attempt?”
“Why not? They are taking me back to Thora to kill me. I could be no worse off, no matter what I did. But why do you ask all these questions?”
“If we could get enough to join us, there is no reason why we should not be free,” I told him. “When you are free, you may remain free or choose a master to your liking.” I watched closely for his reaction.

===Chinese===
「那要是现在你能从他们手里逃出去，你想不想？」
「当然想！可你什么意思？我逃不掉的。」
「没人搭把手，自然逃不掉，」我承认，「可要是旁人也肯跟你一起干，你愿不愿意一试？」
「有何不可？他们正押我回索拉去处死我。再坏也坏不过如此，随我做什么都一样。可你问这些做什么？」
「只要我们纠集得起足够的人手，没有理由不自由，」我告诉他，「你一旦自由，可以继续自由下去，也可以挑一个合意的主人。」我紧紧盯着他，看他的反应。

===Original===
“You mean another revolution?” he asked. “It would fail. Others have tried, but they have always failed.”
“Not a revolution,” I assured him, “just a break for liberty.”
“But how could we do it?”
“It would not be difficult for a few men to take this ship,” I suggested. “The discipline is poor, the night watches consist of too few men; they are so sure of themselves that they would be taken completely by surprise.”
Zog’s eyes lighted. “If we were successful, many of the crew would join us,” he said. “Few of them are happy; nearly all of them hate their officers. I think the prisoners would join us almost to a man, but you must be careful of spies—they are everywhere. That is the greatest danger you would have to face. There can be no doubt but there is at least one spy among us prisoners.”

===Chinese===
「你是说再来一场革命？」他问，「成不了的。别人试过，可总是失败。」
「不是革命，」我向他保证，「不过是冲一回自由罢了。」
「可我们怎么做到呢？」
「要几个人夺下这条船，并非难事，」我提议，「军纪松弛，夜里值更的人又太少；他们太自以为是，定会叫我们打个措手不及。」
佐格的眼睛亮了起来。「我们若得手，船上许多水手都会入伙，」他说，「他们里头称心的少，几乎人人都恨自己的长官。我想囚犯们几乎全会跟我们走，只是你得当心暗探——他们无处不在。这便是你要面对的最大危险。囚犯里头有一个是暗探，那是断无疑义的。」

===Original===
“How about Gamfor,” I asked; “is he all right?”
“You can depend upon Gamfor,” Zog assured me. “He does not say much, but in his eyes I can read his hatred of them.”
“And Kiron?”
“Just the man!” exclaimed Zog. “He despises them, and he does not care who knows it; that is the reason he is a prisoner. This is not his first offense, and it is rumored that he will be executed for high treason.”
“But I thought that he only talked back to an officer and refused to obey him,” I said.

===Chinese===
「甘福尔怎么样？」我问，「他靠得住么？」
「甘福尔你尽可放心，」佐格保证，「他话不多，可我从他眼里看得出他对那帮人的恨。」
「基隆呢？」
「正合用！」佐格叫起来，「他瞧他们不起，也不在乎谁晓得——这便是他沦为囚犯的缘故。这已不是他头一回犯事，传闻他要以谋反大罪被处死。」
「可我以为他不过是顶撞了一个军官、不肯听命罢了。」我说。

===Original===
“That is high treason—if they wish to get rid of a man,” explained Zog. “You can depend on Kiron. Do you wish me to speak to him about the matter?”
“No,” I told him. “I will speak to him and to Gamfor; then if anything goes wrong before we are ready to strike, if a spy gets wind of our plot, you will not be implicated.”
“I do not care about that,” he exclaimed. “They can kill me for but one thing, and it makes no difference which thing it is they kill me for.”
“Nevertheless, I shall speak to them, and if they will join us, we can then decide together how to approach others.”

===Chinese===
「那便是谋反大罪——只要他们想除掉一个人，」佐格解释，「基隆你尽可放心。你要不要我去跟他说这事儿？」
「不必，」我告诉他，「我去跟他说、跟甘福尔说；这样万一动手之前出了岔子，万一哪个暗探探到了风声，你也不至于被牵连进去。」
「我才不在乎这个，」他叫道，「他们杀我也不过就那一桩罪名，为哪桩杀我，有何分别。」
「尽管如此，我去跟他们说；他们若肯入伙，我们再一起商量怎么去联络旁人。」

===Original===
Zog and I had been working together scrubbing the deck at the time, and it was not until night that I had an opportunity to speak with Gamfor and Kiron. Both were enthusiastic about the plan, but neither thought that there was much likelihood that it would succeed. However, each assured me of his support; and then we found Zog, and the four of us discussed details throughout half the night. We had withdrawn to a far corner of the room in which we were confined and spoke in low whispers with our heads close together.
The next few days were spent in approaching recruits—a very ticklish business, since they all assured me that it was almost a foregone conclusion that there was a spy among us. Each man had to be sounded out by devious means, and it had been decided that this work should be left to Gamfor and Kiron. I was eliminated because of my lack of knowledge concerning the hopes, ambitions, and the grievances of these people, or their psychology; Zog was eliminated because the work required a much higher standard of intelligence than he possessed.

===Chinese===
佐格和我当时正一起擦洗甲板，直到夜里我才得空去找甘福尔和基隆。两人对这计划都极为热心，可谁都不觉得有多大胜算。然而，两人都向我保证全力襄助；随后我们找到佐格，四人凑在一处，把细节整整商量了半个通宵。我们退到囚室最远的角落里，把头凑在一处低声耳语。
随后几日，都花在联络新同伙上头——这是桩极棘手的活计，因为他们个个都向我断言，我们中间有个暗探，几乎已成定论。每个人都要用迂回的法子试探口风，而这差事已议定交由甘福尔和基隆去办。我被排除在外，因为我全然不知这些人的盼头、抱负、怨气，更不懂他们的脾性；佐格也被排除——因为这差事所需的机灵劲儿，远非他所能及。

===Original===
Gamfor warned Kiron not to divulge our plan to any prisoner who too openly avowed his hatred of the Thorists. “This is a timeworn trick that all spies adopt to lull the suspicions of those they suspect of harboring treasonable thoughts, and to tempt them into avowing their apostasy. Select men whom you know to have a real grievance, and who are moody and silent,” he counselled.
I was a little concerned about our ability to navigate the ship in the event that we succeeded in capturing her, and I discussed this matter with both Gamfor and Kiron. What I learned from them was illuminating, if not particularly helpful.
The Amtorians have developed a compass similar to ours. According to Kiron, it points always toward the center of Amtor—that is, toward the center of the mythical circular area called Strabol, or Hot Country. This statement assured me that I was in the southern hemisphere of the planet, the needle of the compass, of course, pointing north toward the north magnetic pole. Having no sun, moon, nor stars, their navigation is all done by dead reckoning; but they have developed instruments of extreme delicacy that locate land at great distances, accurately indicating this distance and the direction; others that determine speed, mileage, and drift, as well as a depth gauge wherewith they may record soundings anywhere within a radius of a mile from the ship.

===Chinese===
甘福尔叮嘱基隆，切不可把我们的计划透露给任何过分张扬地痛骂索尔党的囚犯。「这是所有暗探惯用的老把戏，专为麻痹他们所疑心怀有谋反念头的人，引这些人自表叛意。你挑那些有真冤情、平日里郁郁寡欢、沉默寡言的人。」他这样劝道。
夺船之后能否驾得动她，我心里不免有些担忧，便把这事拿来同甘福尔和基隆商量。从他们那里听来的，倒颇开眼界，只是并不怎么顶用。
阿姆托（Amtor）人造出了与我们相仿的罗盘。据基隆说，它永远指向阿姆托的中心——也就是那个传说中的圆形地带「斯特拉博尔（Strabol）」，即「炎热国度」的中心。这话倒叫我确信，自己处在这颗星球的南半球，罗盘的针当然是指向北、指向北磁极的。他们没有日、月、星辰，航海全凭航迹推算；可他们造出了精巧至极的仪器，能在极远距离上发现陆地，精确指出距离与方位；另有测定船速、航程、漂移的仪器，还有一具测深仪，可在船周围一英里半径内任何一处测得水深。

===Original===
All of their instruments for measuring distances utilize the radioactivity of the nuclei of various elements to accomplish their ends. The gamma ray, for which they have, of course, another name, being uninfluenced by the most powerful magnetic forces, is naturally the ideal medium for their purposes. It moves in a straight line and at uniform speed until it meets an obstruction, where, even though it may not be deflected, it is retarded, the instrument recording such retardation and the distance at which it occurs. The sounding device utilizes the same principle. The instrument records the distance from the ship at which the ray encounters the resistance of the ocean’s bottom; by constructing a right triangle with this distance representing the hypotenuse it is simple to compute both the depth of the ocean and the distance from the ship at which bottom was found, for they have a triangle of which one side and all three angles are known.

===Chinese===
他们所有测距仪器，都利用各种元素原子核的放射性来达成目的。γ 射线——他们当然另有一套叫法——既不受最强磁力的影响，自然便是他们最理想的介质。它沿直线以匀速行进，直至遇上一处障碍；即便未被偏转，也会受阻而减速，仪器便将这减速及其发生的距离记录下来。测深装置用的也是同一原理。仪器记录射线碰到海底阻力时距船的距离；以此距离为斜边作一直角三角形，便不难算出海深，也不难算出发现海底之处距船多远——因为他们有这样一个三角形：一边与三内角均已知晓。

===Original===
Owing to their extremely faulty maps, however, the value of these instruments has been greatly reduced, for no matter what course they lay, other than due north, if they move in a straight line they are always approaching the antarctic regions. They may know that land is ahead and its distance, but they are never sure what land it is, except where the journey is a short and familiar one. For this reason they cruise within sight of land wherever that is practical, with the result that journeys that might otherwise be short are greatly protracted. Another result is that the radius of Amtorian maritime exploration has been greatly circumscribed; so much so that I believe there are enormous areas in the south temperate zone that have never been discovered by the Vepajans or the Thorists, while the very existence of the northern hemisphere is even unguessed by them. On the maps that Danus showed me considerable areas contained nothing but the single word *joram*, ocean.
However, notwithstanding all this (and possibly because of it), I was confident that we could manage to navigate the ship quite as satisfactorily as her present officers, and in this Kiron agreed.
“At least we know the general direction of Thora,” he argued; “so all we have to do is sail in the other direction.”

===Chinese===
然而，由于他们那些错漏百出的地图，这些仪器的价值大打折扣；因为无论他们定的是什么航向，只要不是正北，沿直线行驶便总在逼近南极地带。他们或许知道前方有陆地、距自己多远，却永远拿不准那是什么陆地——除非那是趟短途而熟络的航行。正因如此，只要办得到，他们便贴着海岸航行，结果原本可短的航程被大大拖长。另一个后果是，阿姆托人航海探险的范围被大大局限；局限到这般地步，我相信南温带有大片大片的区域，韦帕贾人和索尔党人从未发现过，至于北半球的存在，他们更是做梦也想不到。在达努斯（Danus）给我看过的地图上，相当大的区域除了 *joram*（海洋）这一个词之外，别无所有。
然而，尽管如此（或许正因如此），我有把握，我们能把这些船驾驶得与她眼下的军官们一样妥帖；这一点基隆也赞同。
「至少我们知道索拉的大致方向，」他争道，「所以我们要做的，不过是朝相反方向开罢了。」

===Original===
As our plans matured, the feasibility of the undertaking appeared more and more certain. We had recruited twenty prisoners, five of whom were Vepajans, and this little band we organized into a secret order with passwords, which were changed daily, signs, and a grip, the last reminiscent of my fraternity days in college. We also adopted a name. We called ourselves Soldiers of Liberty. I was chosen *vookor*, or captain. Gamfor, Kiron, Zog, and Honan were my principal lieutenants, though I told them that Kamlot would be second in command if we were successful in taking the ship.
Our plan of action was worked out in detail; each man knew exactly what was expected of him. Certain men were to overpower the watch, others were to go to the officers’ quarters and secure their weapons and keys; then we would confront the crew and offer those who chose an opportunity to join us. The others—well, there I was confronted with a problem. Almost to a man the Soldiers of Liberty wanted to destroy all those who would not join us, and really there seemed no alternative; but I still hoped that I could work out a more humane disposition of them.

===Chinese===
随着计划日渐成熟，这桩勾当的可行之处看起来愈发确凿。我们已招募了二十名囚犯，其中五人是韦帕贾人；我们把这支小队伍组织成一个秘密结社，有口令——每日一换——有暗号，还有握手礼，最后一项倒叫我忆起大学兄弟会的日子。我们还立了个名号。我们自称「自由之兵」。我被推举为 *vookor*，也就是船长。甘福尔、基隆、佐格、霍南（Honan）是我手下的主要副手，不过我告诉他们，若我们夺船成功，卡姆洛特（Kamlot）将是副统领。
我们的行动计划已拟定得纤悉无遗；每人都清楚自己该做什么。一些人负责制服值更的，另一些人去军官住处夺取他们的武器与钥匙；随后我们便面对全体船员，给肯入伙者一个机会。至于其余的人——唉，我便撞上一道难题。「自由之兵」几乎众口一词，要铲除所有不肯入伙的人，而旁瞧着似乎也别无他法；可我仍盼能想出个更人道的处置之策。

===Original===
There was one man among the prisoners of whom we were all suspicious. He had an evil face, but that was not his sole claim upon our suspicions—he was too loud in his denunciation of Thorism. We watched him carefully, avoiding him whenever we could, and each member of the band was warned to be careful when talking to him. It was evident to Gamfor first that this fellow, whose name was Anoos, was suspicious. He persisted in seeking out various members of our group and engaging them in conversation which he always led around to the subject of Thorism and his hatred of it, and he constantly questioned each of us about the others, always insinuating that he feared certain ones were spies. But of course we had expected something of this sort, and we felt that we had guarded against it. The fellow might be as suspicious of us as he wished; so long as he had no evidence against us I did not see how he could harm us.

===Chinese===
囚犯中有一个人，我们都疑心。他生了一副恶相，可这并非我们疑心的全部缘由——他骂索尔主义（Thorism）骂得太凶了。我们小心盯着他，能避开便避开，结社里每个人都受到叮嘱，与他交谈时务必当心。最先察觉这厮起了疑心的，是甘福尔。此人名叫阿诺斯（Anoos），他偏要找上我们这帮人里头的这个那个攀谈，话头总往索尔主义、往他对它的痛恨上引，又不停地向我们每个人打听旁人，话里话外暗示他疑心某某是暗探。可这一套我们本就料到，也自觉已有所防备。这厮爱怎么疑我们便由他去疑；只要他拿不住我们的把柄，我看他能奈我们何。

===Original===
One day Kiron came to me evidently laboring under suppressed excitement. It was at the end of the day, and our food had just been issued to us for the evening meal—dried fish and a hard, dark-colored bread made of coarse meal.
“I have news, Carson,” he whispered.
“Let us go off in a corner and eat,” I suggested, and we strolled away together, laughing and talking of the day’s events in our normal voices. As we seated ourselves upon the floor to eat our poor food, Zog joined us.
“Sit close to us, Zog,” directed Kiron; “I have something to say that no one but a Soldier of Liberty may hear.”
He did not say Soldier of Liberty, but “*kung, kung, kung*,” which are the Amtorian initials of the order’s title. *Kung* is the name of the Amtorian character that represents the *k* sound in our language, and when I first translated the initials I was compelled to smile at the similarity they bore to those of a well-known secret order in the United States of America.
“While I am talking,” Kiron admonished us, “you must laugh often, as though I were telling a humorous tale; then, perhaps, no one will suspect that I am not.

===Chinese===
一日基隆来找我，分明是强压着一股兴奋。那时正是一日将尽，晚饭刚发下来——干鱼，还有粗粉做的一种又硬又黑的面包。
「我有消息，卡森。」他悄声道。
「我们到角落里去边吃边说。」我提议。我们便一同踱开，照常谈笑，讲着白天的事。我们在地板上坐下要吃这粗粝的饭食，佐格也凑了过来。
「靠近我们坐，佐格，」基隆吩咐，「我有话要说，这话除『自由之兵』外，谁也不许听。」
他说的并非「自由之兵」，而是「*kung, kung, kung*」——这是本结社名号的阿姆托语首字母缩写。*Kung* 是阿姆托语里代表我们语言中 *k* 音的那个字符的名字。我初次译出这缩写时，不禁莞尔——它与美国某个赫赫有名的秘密结社的首字母竟如此相似。
「我说话的时候，」基隆叮嘱我们，「你们要常常发笑，装作我在讲笑话的样子；如此，或许没人会疑心我并非在讲笑话。

===Original===
“Today I was working in the ship’s armory, cleaning pistols,” he commenced. “The soldier who guarded me is an old friend of mine; we served together in the army of the jong. He is as a brother to me. For either the other would die. We talked of old times under the banners of the jong and compared those days with these, especially we compared the officers of the old regime with those of the present. Like me and like every old soldier, he hates his officers, so we had a pleasant time together.
“Finally he said to me, quite suddenly, ‘What is this I hear of a conspiracy among the prisoners?’
“That almost took me off my feet; but I showed no emotion, for there are times when one must not trust even a brother. ‘What have you heard?’ I asked.
“‘I overheard one of the officers speaking to another,’ he told me. ‘He said that a man named Anoos had reported the matter to the captain and that the captain had told Anoos to get the names of all the prisoners whom he knew to be involved in the conspiracy and to learn their plans if he possibly could.’
“‘And what did Anoos say?’ I asked my friend.
“‘He said that if the captain would give him a bottle of wine he believed that he could get one of the conspirators drunk and worm the story from him. So the captain gave him a bottle of wine. That was today.’

===Chinese===
「今日我在船上的军械库里擦手枪，」他开口道，「看守我的那个兵是我的老友；我们曾在『君』的军队里一同当差。他待我如兄弟一般。我们俩谁肯为谁去死都甘愿。我们谈起『君』麾下的旧日时光，又拿那些日子与如今比，尤其拿旧时的军官与眼下的这帮军官比。他与我、与每一个老兵一样恨他的军官，于是我们一道叙得颇为痛快。
「临了他忽然对我说：『我听说囚犯里头有密谋，是怎么回事？』
「这话险些叫我栽个跟头；可我面上不动声色，因为有些时候，便是兄弟也信不得。『你听说了什么？』我问。
「『我撞见一个军官在对另一个说话，』他告诉我，『他说有个叫阿诺斯的人把这事禀报了船长，船长叫阿诺斯把所知参与密谋的囚犯姓名都报上来，还要他设法探出他们的计划。』
「『阿诺斯怎么说？』我问我的朋友。
「『他说船长若肯给他一瓶酒，他便能灌醉一个同谋者，把内情套出来。于是船长给了他一瓶酒。便是今日的事。』

===Original===
“My friend looked at me very closely, and then he said, ‘Kiron, we are more than brothers. If I can help you, you have but to ask.’
“I knew this, and knowing how close to discovery we already were, I decided to confide in him and enlist his aid; so I told him. I hope you do not feel that I did wrong, Carson.”
“By no means,” I assured him. “We have been forced to tell others of our plans whom we knew and trusted less well than you know and trust your friend. What did he say when you had told him?”
“He said that he would help us, and that when we struck he would join us. He promised, too, that many others of the soldiers would do likewise; but the most important thing he did was to give me a key to the armory.”
“Good!” I exclaimed. “There is no reason now why we should not strike at once.”
“Tonight?” asked Zog eagerly.
“Tonight!” I replied. “Pass the word to Gamfor and Honan, and you four to the other Soldiers of Liberty.”

===Chinese===
「我的朋友定定地看我，然后说：『基隆，我们胜似兄弟。若我能助你，你只管开口。』
「这我知道。而我又知我们离败露已有多近，便决定向他吐实、拉他入伙；于是我照实告诉了他。希望你别怪我做得不对，卡森。」
「绝无此理，」我向他保证，「我们已被迫把我们之中那些你不如了解、不如信任你那朋友的计划，告诉了旁人。你告诉他之后，他怎么说？」
「他说他愿意助我们一臂之力，待我们动手时他也加入。他还答应，许多别的兵丁也会照样行事；可他做的最重要的一桩，是给了我一把军械库的钥匙。」
「好！」我叫起来，「这下我们没理由不立即动手了。」
「今夜？」佐格急切地问。
「今夜！」我答，「把这消息传给甘福尔和霍南，你们四个再传给其余的『自由之兵』。」

===Original===
We all laughed heartily, as though someone had told a most amusing story, and then Kiron and Zog left me, to acquaint Gamfor and Honan with our plan.
But upon Venus as upon earth, the best laid plans of mice and men “gang aft a-gley,” which is slang for haywire. Every night since we had sailed from the harbor of Vepaja the hatch had been left off our ill-smelling prison to afford us ventilation, a single member of the watch patrolling near to see that none of us came out; but tonight the hatch was closed.
“This,” growled Kiron, “is the result of Anoos’s work.”
“We shall have to strike by daylight,” I whispered, “but we cannot pass the word tonight. It is so dark down here that we should certainly be overheard by someone outside our own number if we attempted it.”
“Tomorrow then,” said Kiron.

===Chinese===
我们一齐放声大笑，活像有人讲了个极逗趣的笑话；随后基隆与佐格离了我，去知会甘福尔和霍南我们的计划。
然而金星也罢地球也罢，人鼠之最周密的计划也常「偏了轨」——借用一句俗话，便是全乱了套。自打我们驶离韦帕贾的港口，每夜囚室那腥臭的舱口都敞着好给我们透风，只有一名值更的在附近巡看，不叫我们里头的人出来；可今夜，舱口关上了。
「这，」基隆咬牙道，「便是阿诺斯干的好事。」
「我们只好白天动手了，」我悄声道，「可今夜没法传话。底下黑成这样，我们若一试，定会被自己人之外的谁听见。」
「那就明日。」基隆说。

===Original===
I was a long time getting to sleep that night, for my mind was troubled by fears for our entire plan. It was obvious now that the captain was suspicious, and that while he might not know anything of the details of what we purposed, he did know that something was in the air, and he was taking no chances.
During the night, as I lay awake trying to plan for the morrow, I heard someone prowling around the room, and now and again a whisper. I could only wonder who it was and try to guess what he was about. I recalled the bottle of wine that Anoos was supposed to have, and it occurred to me that he might be giving a party, but the voices were too subdued to bear out that theory. Finally I heard a muffled cry, a noise that sounded like a brief scuffle, and then silence again fell upon the chamber.
“Someone had a bad dream,” I thought and fell asleep.

===Chinese===
那一夜我许久才睡着，因为我们整个计划叫我忧心如焚。如今船长已起了疑心，这是明摆着的事；他或许不知我们图谋的细节，可他确知风头不对，于是半点风险也不肯冒。
夜里我躺着没合眼，盘算着次日，忽听得有人在屋里踅摸，间或一声低语。我只能纳闷那人是谁，揣度他在做什么。我想起阿诺斯理应弄到的那瓶酒，心想许是他请起客来了，可那声音压得太低，对不上这个猜法。临了我听见一声闷喊，一阵像是短暂扭打的声音，随后屋里又归于寂静。
「谁做了个噩梦罢。」我这样想着，便睡着了。

===Original===
Morning came at last, and the hatch was removed, letting a little light in to dissipate the gloom of our prison. A sailor lowered a basket containing the food for our meager breakfast. We gathered about it and each took his share, and moved away to eat it, when suddenly there was a cry from the far side of the room.
“Look what’s here!” the man shouted. “Anoos has been murdered!”

===Chinese===
早晨终于到了，舱口被掀开，透进一点光来，驱散了囚室的幽暗。一个水手放下篮子，里头装着我们那点寒酸的早饭。我们围上去各取一份，走开去吃，忽然屋里另一头传来一声叫喊。
「看这儿！」那人嚷道，「阿诺斯被人杀了！」

## X Mutiny / X 哗变

===Original===
Yes, Anoos had been murdered, and there was a great hue and cry, much more of a hue and cry, it seemed to me, than the death of an ordinary prisoner should have aroused. Officers and soldiers swarmed in our quarters. They found Anoos stretched out on his back, a bottle of wine at his side. His throat was discolored where powerful fingers had crushed it. Anoos had been choked to death.
Soon they herded us on deck, where we were searched for weapons following an order from the captain of the ship, who had come forward to conduct an investigation. He was angry and excited and, I believe, somewhat frightened. One by one, he questioned us. When it was my turn to be questioned, I did not tell him what I had heard during the night; I told him that I had slept all night on the far side of the room from where Anoos’s body was discovered.
“Were you acquainted with the dead man?” he asked.
“No more so than with any of the other prisoners,” I replied.
“But you are very well acquainted with some of them,” he said rather pointedly, I thought. “Have you ever spoken with the man?”
“Yes, he has talked to me on several occasions.”
“About what?” demanded the captain.
“Principally about his grievances against the Thorists.”

===Chinese===
不错，阿诺斯是被谋杀了，于是掀起一场大呼小叫——依我看，这动静比一个寻常囚犯之死所能引起的，大得太多。军官和兵丁一窝蜂涌进我们住处。他们发现阿诺斯仰面摊着，身边一只酒瓶。他喉头变了色，是有力的指头扼出的痕迹。阿诺斯是被人扼死的。
很快他们把我们赶到甲板上，奉船长之命搜身——船长已亲自上前主持调查。他既怒且躁，而且我相信，还有几分惊惶。他一个一个地盘问。轮到我受审时，我并未把夜里听见的告诉他；只说我整夜睡在屋里离发现尸首那一头最远的另一头。
「你认识死者么？」他问。
「不比认识其他囚犯更熟。」我答。
「可你与其中几位相当熟络。」他说得颇为话里有话，我暗想。「你可曾与此人说过话？」
「说过，他在几个场合找我攀谈过。」
「谈些什么？」船长追问。
「主要谈他对索尔党的怨愤。」

===Original===
“But he was a Thorist,” exclaimed the captain.
I knew that he was trying to pump me to discover if I harbored any suspicions concerning the actual status of Anoos, but he was not clever enough to succeed. “I certainly would never had suspected it from his conversation,” I replied. “If he were a Thorist, he must have been a traitor to his country, for he continually sought to enlist my interest in a plan to seize the ship and murder all her officers. I think he approached others, also.” I spoke in a tone loud enough to be heard by all, for I wanted the Soldiers of Liberty to take the cue from me. If enough of us told the same story it might convince the officers that Anoos’s tale of a conspiracy was hatched in his own brain and worked up by his own efforts in an attempt to reap commendation and reward from his superiors, a trick by no means foreign to the ethics of spies.
“Did he succeed in persuading any of the prisoners to join him?” asked the captain.
“I think not; they all laughed at him.”
“Have you any idea who murdered him?”
“Probably some patriot who resented his treason,” I lied glibly.
As he questioned the other men along similar lines, I was pleased to discover that nearly every one of the Soldiers of Liberty had been approached by the perfidious Anoos, whose traitorous overtures they had virtuously repulsed. Zog said that he had never talked with the man, which, to the best of my knowledge, was the truth.

===Chinese===
「可他是个索尔党人。」船长叫道。
我明白他这是要套我的话，看我是否怀疑阿诺斯的真实身份，但他这点聪明还不够得手。「单听他的话，我决计想不到，」我答，「他若是个索尔党人，那必是卖国的国贼，因为他不停地想拉我入伙，图谋夺船、尽杀船上的军官。我想他也找了旁人。」我说话的声气特意放得人人可闻，因为我要「自由之兵」们取我的话为口径。倘若我们有足够多的人都说同一套说辞，或许便能叫那些军官相信：阿诺斯那套密谋之说是他自己脑子里凭空孵出来、又靠他自己煞费苦心编造出来的，为的是从上峰那里讨个夸奖赏赐——这等伎俩，在暗探的行当里原算不得新鲜。
「他说动了哪个囚犯入伙么？」船长问。
「我想没有；众人都笑他。」
「你可知是谁杀了他？」
「许是哪个义愤于他卖国的有心人罢。」我顺口扯谎。
他顺着相似的线追问旁人时，我欣慰地发现，「自由之兵」里几乎人人都曾被那奸诈的阿诺斯搭讪过，而又都义正词严地回绝了他那叛主的引诱。佐格说他从未与那人说一句话——据我所知，此话不假。

===Original===
When the captain finished his investigation, he was farther from the truth than when he commenced it, for I am certain that he went aft convinced that there had been no truth in the tales that Anoos had carried to him.
I had been considerably worried at the time we were being searched, for fear that the key to the armory would be discovered on Kiron, but it had not been, and later he told me that he had hidden it in his hair the night before as a precaution against just such an eventuality as had occurred.

===Chinese===
船长查完，离真相反倒比起头时更远了，因为我确信他走回船尾时已认定，阿诺斯带给他的那套话里没有半点真。
搜身那阵我着实捏了一把汗，生怕军械库的钥匙在基隆身上被搜出来，可到底没被搜到；后来他告诉我，前一夜他已把钥匙藏进了头发里，正是为防我们所料到的这一着。

===Original===
The Amtorian day consists of 26 hours, 56 minutes, 4 seconds of earth time, which the Amtorians divide into twenty equal periods called *te*, which, for clarity, I shall translate into its nearest earthly equivalent, hour, although it contains 80.895 earth minutes. On shipboard, the hours are sounded by a trumpeter, there being a distinguishing bar of music for each hour of the day. The first hour, or one o’clock, corresponds to mean sunrise. It is then that the prisoners are awakened and given food; forty minutes later they start work, which continues until the tenth hour, with a short recess for food in the middle of the day. Occasionally we were allowed to quit work at the ninth and even the eighth hour, according to the caprices of our masters.

===Chinese===
阿姆托的一天合地球时间二十六小时五十六分四秒，阿姆托人将它均分为二十个相等的时段，称 *te*——为明白起见，我权且译作它最相近的地球对应词「时辰」，虽说一个 *te* 实含 80.895 地球分钟。船上由一名号手报时，一日之中每个时辰都有一段各不相同的乐句。头一个时辰，也就是一点钟，约合平均日出之时。囚犯们便在此时被唤醒、领饭食；四十分钟后开始做工，一直做到第十个时辰，正午略歇片刻用饭。偶尔我们也蒙恩准在第九、乃至第八个时辰便收工——全凭主子们一时的高兴。

===Original===
On this day the Soldiers of Liberty congregated during the midday rest period, and, my mind being definitely determined on immediate action, I passed the word around that we would strike during the afternoon at the moment the trumpeter sounded the seventh hour. As many of us as were working aft near the armory were to make a dash for it with Kiron, who would unlock it in the event that it were locked. The remainder were to attack the soldiers nearest them with anything that they could use as weapons, or with their bare hands if they had no weapons, and take the soldiers’ pistols and swords from them. Five of us were to account for the officers. Half of our number was to constantly shout our battle cry, “For liberty!” The other half was instructed to urge the remaining prisoners and the soldiers to join us.
It was a mad scheme and one in which only desperate men could have found hope.
The seventh hour was chosen because at that time the officers were nearly all congregated in the wardroom, where a light meal and wine were served them daily. We should have preferred launching our plan at night, but we feared a continuation of the practice of locking us below deck would prevent, and our experience with Anoos had taught us that we might expect the whole conspiracy to be divulged by another spy at any time; therefore we dared not wait.

===Chinese===
这一日正午歇工，「自由之兵」聚拢来，我心意已决、定要即刻动手，便传话下去：午后号手吹响第七个时辰的那一刻，我们便动手。在军械库附近的船尾做工的人，要随基隆一道扑向军械库，若库门上锁，便由基隆开锁。其余的人则用手边一切可充兵器之物攻击离自己最近的兵丁，赤手空拳也要夺他们的手枪与刀剑。我们中有五人专门去对付军官。半数人要不停地齐呼我们的战号——「为了自由！」另一半则负责怂恿其余囚犯与兵丁入伙。
这是个疯狂的盘算，唯有走投无路的人才在其中看得到希望。
之所以选第七个时辰，是因那时军官们几乎全聚在军官餐厅里——每日彼时都有一顿便餐与酒水。我们本宁可在夜里发动，又怕他们继续把我们锁在底舱而坏事；况且与阿诺斯那一遭也教我们明白，整桩密谋随时可能被又一个暗探抖搂出来，故而我们不敢再等。

===Original===
I must confess to a feeling of increasing excitement as the hour approached. As, from time to time, I glanced at the other members of our little band, I thought that I could note signs of nervousness in some of them, while others worked on as placidly as though nothing unusual was about to occur. Zog was one of these. He was working near me. He never glanced toward the tower deck from which the trumpeter would presently sound the fateful notes, though it was with difficulty that I kept my eyes from it at all. No one would have thought that Zog was planning to attack the soldier lolling near him, nor have imagined that the night before he had murdered a man. He was humming a tune, as he polished the barrel of the big gun on which he was working.
Gamfor and, fortunately, Kiron were working aft, scrubbing the deck, and I saw that Kiron kept scrubbing closer and closer to the door of the armory. How I wished for Kamlot as the crucial moment approached! He could have done so much to insure the success of our coup, and yet he did not even know that such a stroke was contemplated, much less that it was so soon to be launched.

===Chinese===
我必须承认，当时辰将近，我心中的兴奋越发涨满。我不时瞥一眼我们这小队里旁的兄弟，觉得能从其中几位身上瞧出几分紧张的端倪，而另几位却仍自顾自地做工，平静得仿佛什么异乎寻常的事都不会发生。佐格便是后者之一。他在我近旁干活。那号手即刻便要从塔台上吹响那决定生死的乐句，他却一眼也不曾朝那方向望——倒是我，好不容易才忍住不拿眼去看。任谁也不会想到，佐格正盘算着扑向身边那个懒洋洋靠着的兵丁；更想不到前一夜他还杀过一个人。他一边擦着手中那门大炮的炮管，一边还哼着小调。
甘福尔和——幸而——基隆都在船尾擦甲板，我瞧见基隆越擦越挨近军械库的门。成败关头将至，我多么盼着卡姆洛特在身边啊！他本能为这场夺船之举多添几分胜算，可他甚至不知有此一击，更不知这一击来得这样快。

===Original===
As I glanced about, I met Zog’s gaze. Very solemnly he closed his left eye. At last he had given a sign that he was alert and ready. It was a little thing, but it put new heart into me. For some reason, during the past half hour I had felt very much alone.
The time was approaching the zero hour. I moved closer to my guard, so that I stood directly in front of him with my back toward him. I knew precisely what I was going to do, and I knew that it would be successful. Little did the man behind me dream that in a minute, or perhaps a few seconds, he would be lying senseless on the deck, or that the man he guarded would be carrying his sword, his dagger, and his pistol as the last notes of the seventh hour floated sweetly out across the calm waters of this Amtorian sea.

===Chinese===
我四下一顾，正撞上佐格的目光。他郑重其事地闭上了左眼。他终于发出了信号：他警醒着、就绪了。这是桩微不足道的小事，却叫我重新振作。不知怎的，方才这半个钟头我一直觉得自己形单影只。
时辰将到那紧要关头。我朝看守我的兵挨近了些，站到他正前方，背对着他。我清楚自己要做什么，也清楚这一下必能得手。我身后那人做梦也想不到，再过一分钟、也许只几秒钟，他便要人事不省地摊在甲板上；更想不到他看守的人到第七个时辰最后那几记乐音甜美地飘过这阿姆托海平静水面之时，身上已挎着他的剑、他的匕首、他的手枪。

===Original===
My back was now toward the deck houses. I could not see the trumpeter when he emerged from the tower to sound the hour, but I knew that it could not be long now before he stepped out onto the tower deck. Yet when the first note sounded I was as startled as though I had expected it never to sound. I presume it was the reaction after the long period of nervous tension.
My nervousness, however, was all mental; it did not affect my physical reactions to the needs of the moment. As the first note came softly down to my awaiting ears, I pivoted to a heel and swung my right for the chin of my unsuspecting guard. It was one of those blows that is often described as a haymaker, and it made hay. The fellow dropped in his tracks. As I stooped to recover his arms, pandemonium broke loose upon the deck. There were shrieks and groans and curses, and above all rose the war cry of the Soldiers of Liberty—my band had struck, and it had struck hard.

===Chinese===
我此刻背对着那几间舱房。我瞧不见号手何时从塔里出来报时，却知道离他踏上塔台已不在几时。然而头一个音符响起时，我仍如吃了一惊，仿佛原以为它永不会响。我想，这是长久神经紧绷之后的反弹罢。
然而我的紧张全在心头，并不碍着我手脚应付眼下事变的反应。头一记音符软软地落进我等候的耳朵，我便脚跟一转，右手朝那毫无防备的看守下巴抡去。这一记便是常被唤作「一拳定乾坤」的那种重拳，果然一拳定乾坤。那人当场栽倒。我俯身去摘他的兵器，甲板上已炸开了锅。尖叫、呻吟、咒骂齐起，盖过这一切的，是「自由之兵」的战号——我的人动手了，而且下手极狠。

===Original===
For the first time now, I heard the weird staccato hiss of Amtorian firearms. You have heard an X-ray machine in operation? It was like that, but louder and more sinister. I had wrenched the sword and pistol from the scabbard and holster of my fallen guard, not taking the time to remove his belt. Now I faced the scene for which I had so long waited. I saw the powerful Zog wrest the weapons from a soldier, and then lift the man’s body above his head and cast it overboard. Evidently Zog had no time for proselyting.
At the door to the armory a battle was being waged; men were trying to enter, and soldiers were shooting them down. I ran in that direction. A soldier leaped in front of me, and I heard the hiss of the death rays that must have passed close to my body, as he tried to stop me. He must have been either nervous or a very poor shot, for he missed me. I turned my own weapon upon him and pressed the lever. The man slumped to the deck with a hole in his chest, and I ran on.

===Chinese===
此刻我头一回听见阿姆托火器那怪异短促的嘶声。你听过 X 光机开动的声响么？便像那个，只是更响、更阴森。我已从我那倒地看守的鞘里、套里夺下刀剑与手枪，未及解他的皮带。此刻我面对的，正是我久候的那一幕。我瞧见力大无穷的佐格从一个兵丁手里夺下兵器，随即将那人整个身子举过头顶，抛进海里。显然佐格没那闲工夫去劝人入伙。
军械库门口正打得热火朝天；众人要冲进去，兵丁们放枪把他们打倒。我朝那边奔去。一个兵丁跃到我身前，我听得死亡射线那嘶嘶之声紧贴着我身子掠过——他要拦我。他定是太紧张，又或是个蹩脚枪手，竟没打中。我反手举起自己的武器一按扳机，那人胸口被轰出一个洞，瘫倒甲板，我继续向前冲。

===Original===
The fight at the door of the armory was hand to hand with swords, daggers, and fists, for by now the members of the two factions were so intermingled that none dared use a firearm for fear of injuring a comrade. Into this melee I leaped. Tucking the pistol into the band of my G string, I ran my sword through a great brute who was about to knife Honan; then I grabbed another by the hair and dragged him from the door, shouting to Honan to finish him—it took too long to run a sword into a man and then pull it out again. What I wanted was to get into the armory to Kiron’s side and help him.
All the time I could hear my men shouting, “For liberty!” or urging the soldiers to join us—as far as I had been able to judge, all the prisoners had already done so. Now another soldier barred my way. His back was toward me, and I was about to seize him and hurl him back to Honan and the others who were fighting at his side, when I saw him slip his dagger into the heart of a soldier in front of him and, as he did so, cry, “For liberty!” Here was one convert at least. I did not know it then, but at that time there were already many such.

===Chinese===
军械库门口这一仗是白刃相接——刀剑、匕首、拳头齐上，因为此刻两边的人已缠作一团，谁也不敢开火器，唯恐伤了自家兄弟。我一纵身跃入这场混战。把手枪掖进缠腰布的带子里，我一剑捅穿一个正要捅霍南的大汉；又一把抓住另一个的头发，把他从门口拖开，喊霍南来了结他——一剑捅进去再拔出来，实在太费时。我要的是冲进军械库，到基隆身边助他。
这一路我都能听见我的人高喊「为了自由！」或怂恿兵丁入伙——据我判断，所有囚犯早已都倒戈了。此时又一个兵丁拦住我去路。他背对着我，我正要抓起他扔回给霍南和与他并肩厮杀的众人，却见他把匕首捅进了身前一个兵丁的心窝，一边捅一边喊：「为了自由！」至少这里有一个被收服的。我当时尚不知，那时节这样的人已有许多。

===Original===
When I finally got into the armory, I found Kiron issuing arms as fast as he could pass them out. Many of the mutineers were crawling through the windows of the room to get weapons, and to each of these Kiron passed several swords and pistols, directing the men to distribute them on deck.
Seeing that all was right here, I gathered a handful of men and started up the companionway to the upper decks, from which the officers were firing down upon the mutineers and, I may say, upon their own men as well. In fact, it was this heartless and stupid procedure that swung many of the soldiers to our side. Almost the first man I saw as I leaped to the level of the second deck was Kamlot. He had a sword in one hand and a pistol in the other, and he was firing rapidly at a group of officers who were evidently attempting to reach the main deck to take command of the loyal soldiers there.
You may be assured that it did my heart good to see my friend again, and as I ran to his side and opened fire on the officers, he flashed me a quick smile of recognition.

===Chinese===
等我终于冲进军械库，只见基隆正以最快的速度往外分发兵器。许多哗变者正从屋子的窗户爬进来取武器，基隆给每人递上几把刀剑、几把手枪，吩咐他们到甲板上去分发。
见这里诸事妥当，我纠集起一拨人，沿升降口往上层甲板冲去——军官们正从那里朝下向哗变者开火，我不得不说，他们也朝自家的人开火。事实上，正是这等无情而愚蠢的做法，把许多兵丁推到了我们这边。我跃上第二层甲板，头一个瞧见的几乎便是卡姆洛特。他一手持剑、一手握枪，正向一群军官飞快地开火——那群人显然想冲到主甲板上去指挥那里的忠兵。
你尽可相信，再见挚友一面叫我心里何等痛快；我奔到他身边朝军官们开火，他飞快朝我闪过一个认出的笑。

===Original===
Three of the five officers opposing us had fallen, and now the remaining two turned and fled up the companionway to the top deck. Behind us were twenty or more mutineers eager to reach the highest deck, where all the surviving officers had now taken refuge, and I could see more mutineers crowding up the companionway from the main deck to join their fellows. Kamlot and I led the way to the next deck, but at the head of the companionway the surging mob of howling, cursing mutineers brushed past us to hurl themselves upon the officers.
The men were absolutely out of control, and as there were but few of my original little band of Soldiers of Liberty among them, the majority of them knew no leader, with the result that it was every man for himself. I wished to protect the officers, and it had been my intention to do so; but I was helpless to avert the bloody orgy that ensued with a resulting loss of life entirely disproportionate to the needs of the occasion.

===Chinese===
与我们对峙的五名军官已倒下三名，余下两名转身沿升降口逃上顶层甲板。我们身后有二十来个哗变者，巴不得冲上最高一层甲板——所有幸存的军官如今都退守那里——我又见更多哗变者从主甲板涌上升降口，要去与同伴会合。卡姆洛特和我领路冲向上一层甲板，可到了升降口顶端，那群嚎叫咒骂的哗变者如潮水般把我们挤开，直扑军官们而去。
众人已全然失控，而其中我原本那小队「自由之兵」寥寥无几，多数人并不认得首领，结果便是各人顾各人。我本欲护住那些军官，原也作此打算；可随之而来的那场血腥狂欢我无力阻止，其殒命人数与事势之需全然不相称。

===Original===
The officers, fighting for their lives with their backs against a wall, took heavy toll of the mutineers, but they were eventually overwhelmed by superior numbers. Each of the common soldiers and sailors appeared to have a special grudge to settle either with some individual officer or with them all as a class and for the time all were transformed into maniacal furies, as time and again they charged the last fortress of authority, the oval tower on the upper deck.
Each officer that fell, either killed or wounded, was hurled over the rail to the deck below, where willing hands cast the body to the main deck from which, in turn, it was thrown into the sea. And then, at last, the mutineers gained access to the tower, from which they dragged the remaining officers, butchering them on the upper deck or hurling them to their shrieking fellows below.
The captain was the last to be dragged out. They had found him hiding in a cupboard in his cabin. At sight of him arose such a scream of hate and rage as I hope never to hear again. Kamlot and I were standing at one side, helpless witnesses of this holocaust of hate. We saw them literally tear the captain to pieces and cast him into the sea.

===Chinese===
军官们背靠着墙、为性命死战，叫哗变者付出了惨重代价，可到底寡不敌众。每一个寻常兵丁、水手，似乎都有一笔私账要与某个军官清算，或与军官这一类人清算；一时间众人尽化为癫狂的复仇之鬼，一次又一次地朝那权威的最后堡垒——上层甲板上的椭圆塔楼——发起冲击。
每倒下一个军官——无论死、伤——都被抛过栏杆掷到下层甲板，那里自有热心的手把尸体扔到主甲板，主甲板上又有人把尸体抛进大海。终于，哗变者攻进了塔楼，把里头剩下的军官拖出来，在上层甲板上屠杀，或抛给下层嚎叫的同伙。
船长是最后一个被拖出来的。他们在船长室的一个橱柜里找着了藏身的他。一见他，便爆发出一阵恨与怒的尖叫，我只盼再不要听到第二回。卡姆洛特和我立在一旁，无助地目睹这场仇恨的浩劫。我们眼看着他们把船长活活撕成碎块，抛进了大海。

===Original===
With the death of the captain the battle was over, the ship was ours. My plan had succeeded, but the thought suddenly assailed me that I had created a terrible power that it might be beyond me to control. I touched Kamlot on the arm. “Follow me,” I directed and started for the main deck.
“Who is at the bottom of this?” asked Kamlot as we forced our way among the excited mutineers.
“The mutiny was my plan, but not the massacre,” I replied. “Now we must attempt to restore order out of chaos.”
“If we can,” he remarked dubiously.

===Chinese===
船长一死，战事便了，船是我们的了。我的计划成功了，可一个念头猛地袭来：我造出了一股可怕的力量，这力量或许非我所能驾驭。我碰了碰卡姆洛特的手臂。「跟我来。」我吩咐，便朝主甲板走去。
「这事的头儿是谁？」卡姆洛特一面在兴奋的哗变者中间挤着，一面问。
「哗变是我的谋划，屠杀却不是，」我答，「如今我们得设法从大乱中收拾出秩序来。」
「若还办得到的话。」他半信半疑地说。

===Original===
As I made my way toward the main deck, I collected as many of the original band of Soldiers of Liberty as we passed, and when I finally reached my destination, I gathered most of them about me. Among the mutineers I had discovered the trumpeter who had unknowingly sounded the signal for the outbreak, and him I caused to sound the call that should assemble all hands on the main deck. Whether or not the notes of the trumpet would be obeyed, I did not know, but so strong is the habit of discipline among trained men that immediately the call sounded the men began to pour onto the deck from all parts of the vessel.
I mounted the breech of one of the guns, and, surrounded by my faithful band, I announced that the Soldiers of Liberty had taken over the ship, that those who wished to accompany us must obey the *vookor* of the band; the others would be put ashore.

===Chinese===
我朝主甲板走去，一路收拢所遇见的「自由之兵」旧部，待到目的地，已将他们大半聚在身边。我在哗变者中寻到了那个号手——他不知不觉吹响了起事的信号——便命他吹响召集全员到主甲板集合的号音。这号声会不会被人听从，我并不知晓；可训练有素之人纪律的习惯是如此之强，号声一响，众人便立时从船的各处涌上甲板。
我登上一门炮的炮尾，在忠心耿耿的旧部簇拥之下，宣布「自由之兵」已接管此船；愿随我们同行者，须听本帮 *vookor* 之命；其余的人将被送上岸。

===Original===
“Who is *vookor*?” demanded a soldier whom I recognized as one of those who had been most violent in the attack upon the officers.
“I am,” I replied.
“The *vookor* should be one of us,” he growled.
“Carson planned the mutiny and carried it to success,” shouted Kiron. “Carson is *vookor*.”
From the throats of all my original band and from a hundred new recruits rose a cheer of approval, but there were many who remained silent or spoke in grumbling undertones to those nearest them. Among these was Kodj, the soldier who had objected to my leadership, and I saw that already a faction was gathering about him.

===Chinese===
「谁是 *vookor*？」一个兵丁质问——我认出他，他是攻击军官时最凶的一个。
「是我。」我答。
「*vookor* 该是我们自己人。」他咬牙道。
「哗变是卡森谋划、并一手带向成功的，」基隆高喊，「卡森便是 *vookor*。」
从我所有旧部、从百来个新归附者喉咙里爆出一阵叫好，可沉默不语、只低声向身边人嘀咕抱怨的，也大有人在。柯季（Kodj）便是其中之一——就是那个反对我统领的兵丁，我见他身边已在聚拢一伙人。

===Original===
“It is necessary,” I said, “that all men return at once to their duties, for the ship must be handled, no matter who commands. If there is any question about leadership, that can be settled later. In the meantime, I am in command; Kamlot, Gamfor, Kiron, Zog, and Honan are my lieutenants; with me, they will officer the ship. All weapons must be turned over immediately to Kiron at the armory, except those carried by men regularly detailed by him for guard duty.”
“No one is going to disarm me,” blustered Kodj. “I have as much right to carry weapons as anyone. We are all free men now. I take orders from no one.”
Zog, who had edged closer to him as he spoke, seized him by the throat with one of his huge hands and with the other tore the belt from about his hips. “You take orders from the new *vookor* or you go overboard,” he growled, as he released the man and handed his weapons to Kiron.

===Chinese===
「有件事是必须的，」我说，「所有人立即各归其位，因为不管谁指挥，船总得有人驾驭。首领之事若有异议，往后自可再议。眼下，由我指挥；卡姆洛特、甘福尔、基隆、佐格、霍南是我的副手；他们与我一同统辖此船。所有兵器须立即交到军械库的基隆处，唯由他正式派定值更之人方可佩带。」
「谁也别想缴我的械。」柯季咋呼道，「我与任何人一样有资格带家伙。如今我们都是自由人。我不听任何人的号令。」
佐格边说边挨近了他，一只大手掐住他的喉咙，另一只手把他腰里的皮带扯了下来。「你听新 *vookor* 的号令，不然就滚下海去，」他咬牙说完，松开那人，把他的兵器递给了基隆。

===Original===
For a moment there was silence, and there was a tenseness in the situation that boded ill; then someone laughed and cried, “No one is going to disarm me,” mimicking Kodj. That brought a general laugh, and I knew that for the time being the danger was over. Kiron, sensing that the moment was ripe, ordered the men to come to the armory and turn in their weapons, and the remainder of the original band herded them aft in his wake.

===Chinese===
一时众皆默然，局面紧张得不大吉利；随后有人笑了，学着柯季的腔调叫道：「谁也别想缴我的械。」这一下引得众人皆笑，我知眼下这一关算是过去了。基隆察言观色，见火候已到，便命众人到军械库缴械，旧部其余的人在他身后把众人朝船尾赶去。

===Original===
It was an hour before even a semblance of order or routine had been reestablished. Kamlot, Gamfor, and I were gathered in the chart room in the tower. Our consort was hull down below the horizon, and we were discussing the means that should be adopted to capture her without bloodshed and rescue Duare and the other Vepajan prisoners aboard her. The idea had been in my mind from the very inception of the plan to seize our own ship, and it had been the first subject that Kamlot had broached after we had succeeded in quieting the men and restoring order; but Gamfor was frankly dubious concerning the feasibility of the project.
“The men are not interested in the welfare of Vepajans,” he reminded us, “and they may resent the idea of endangering their lives and risking their newfound liberty in a venture that means nothing whatever to them.”
“How do you feel about it, personally?” I asked him.
“I am under your orders,” he replied; “I will do anything that you command, but I am only one—you have two hundred whose wishes you must consult.”
“I shall consult only my officers,” I replied; “to the others, I shall issue orders.”
“That is the only way,” said Kamlot in a tone of relief.

===Chinese===
足足过了一个时辰，才勉强恢复了些秩序与例行事务的模样。卡姆洛特、甘福尔和我聚在塔楼里的海图室。我们的僚船已船身隐没于地平线之下，我们正在商议如何兵不血刃地拿下她、救出杜阿雷（Duare）与她船上其余韦帕贾囚犯。自从夺船之计起念，这想法便在我心里；而卡姆洛特在安顿好众人、恢复秩序之后，头一个提起的也是这事；可甘福尔对此计的可行，直言不讳地存疑。
「众人对韦帕贾人的死活并不上心，」他提醒我们，「他们也许会反感为了一个与他们毫无干系的营生去冒险送命、拿刚到手的自由去赌博。」
「你自己怎么想？」我问他。
「我听你调遣，」他答，「你下令我无不从，可我不过一人——你手下有两百号人，他们的意愿你总得顾着。」
「我只与我的军官们商议，」我答，「对其余的人，我只下命令。」
「只有这样才成。」卡姆洛特松了口气地说。

===Original===
“Inform the other officers that we shall attack the *Sovong* at daybreak,” I instructed them.
“But we dare not fire on her,” protested Kamlot, “lest we endanger the life of Duare.”
“I intend boarding her,” I replied. “There will be no one but the watch on deck at that hour. On two other occasions the ships have been brought close together on a calm sea; so our approach will arouse no suspicion. The boarding party will consist of a hundred men who will remain concealed until the command to board is given when the ships are alongside one another. At that hour in the morning the sea is usually calm; if it is not calm tomorrow morning we shall have to postpone the attack until another morning.
“Issue strict orders that there is to be no slaughter; no one is to be killed who does not resist. We shall remove all of the *Sovong*’s small arms and the bulk of her provisions, as well as the Vepajan prisoners, to the *Sofal*.”

===Chinese===
「通知其他军官，破晓时我们进攻『索翁号』（Sovong）。」我吩咐他们。
「可我们不敢向她开火啊，」卡姆洛特反对，「那会危及杜阿雷的性命。」
「我打算登船，」我答，「那个时辰甲板上只有值更的。此前有过两回，两船在平静的海面上被靠得很近；所以我们的靠近不会引人起疑。登船队由一百人组成，一直隐蔽，待两船并肩之时方下令登船。早晨那个时辰海面通常平静；若明晨不平静，我们便只得推迟到又一个早晨。
「下达严令：不得屠杀；不抵抗者一律不杀。我们要把『索翁号』所有的轻兵器与大半口粮，连同韦帕贾囚犯，一并搬到『索法尔号』上来。」

===Original===
“And then what do you propose doing?” asked Gamfor.
“I am coming to that,” I replied, “but first I wish to ascertain the temper of the men aboard the *Sofal*. You and Kamlot will inform the other officers of my plans insofar as I have explained them; then assemble the original members of the Soldiers of Liberty and explain my intentions to them. When this has been done, instruct them to disseminate the information among the remainder of the ship’s company, reporting to you the names of all those who do not receive the plan with favor. These we shall leave aboard the *Sovong* with any others who may elect to transfer to her. At the eleventh hour muster the men on the main deck. At that time I will explain my plans in detail.”

===Chinese===
「之后你又打算如何？」甘福尔问。
「我正要说这个，」我答，「但先要摸清『索法尔号』众人的心思。你和卡姆洛特把我方才所讲的计划告知其余军官；再把『自由之兵』的旧部召集起来，向他们说明我的意图。做完这些，吩咐他们把这消息在船员其余人等中间散布开去，凡有不乐于接受此计的，把名字报与你们。这些人，我们便连同其他自愿过船的人，一并留在『索翁号』上。到第十一个时辰，把众人集合到主甲板上。届时我再详详细细地把计划说与众人。」

===Original===
After Kamlot and Gamfor had departed to carry out my orders, I returned to the chart room. The *Sofal*, moving ahead at increased speed, was slowly overhauling the *Sovong*, though not at a rate that might suggest pursuit. I was certain that the *Sovong* knew nothing of what had transpired upon her sister ship, for the Amtorians are unacquainted with wireless communication, and there had been no time for the officers of the *Sofal* to signal their fellows aboard the *Sovong*, so suddenly had the mutiny broken and so quickly had it been carried to a conclusion.
As the eleventh hour approached, I noticed little groups of men congregated in different parts of the ship, evidently discussing the information that the Soldiers of Liberty had spread among them. One group, larger than the others, was being violently harangued by a loud-mouthed orator whom I recognized as Kodj. It had been apparent from the first that the fellow was a trouble maker. Just how much influence he had, I did not know; but I felt that whatever it was, it would be used against me. I hoped to be rid of him after we had taken the *Sovong*.

===Chinese===
卡姆洛特与甘福尔去执行我的命令之后，我回到海图室。『索法尔号』加速前行，正慢慢追上『索翁号』，只是这追近的速度不至于引人疑为追击。我确信『索翁号』对其姊妹船上发生的一切一无所知，因为阿姆托人尚不谙无线电通讯，而『索法尔号』的军官们也来不及向『索翁号』上的同僚发信号——哗变起得那样突然，了结得又那样迅速。
第十一时辰将至，我注意到船的各处聚着一小堆一小堆的人，显然在议论「自由之兵」散布开去的消息。其中一群人比别群都大，正被一个大嗓门的演说家煽动着——我认出是柯季。从一开头便看得出，此人是生事的祸根。他有多大影响力，我不知；可我觉得，无论多大，都必将用来与我为难。我盼着拿下『索翁号』之后能甩掉他。

===Original===
The men congregated rapidly as the trumpeter sounded the hour, and I came down the companionway to address them. I stood just above them, on one of the lower steps, where I could overlook them and be seen by all. Most of them were quiet and appeared attentive. There was one small group muttering and whispering—Kodj was its center.
“At daybreak we shall board and take the *Sovong*,” I commenced. “You will receive your orders from your immediate officers, but I wish to emphasize one in particular—there is to be no unnecessary killing. After we have taken the ship we shall transfer to the *Sofal* such provisions, weapons, and prisoners as we wish to take with us. At this time, also, we shall transfer from the *Sofal* to the *Sovong* all of you who do not wish to remain on this ship under my command, as well as those whom I do not care to take with me,” and as I said this, I looked straight at Kodj and the malcontents surrounding him.

===Chinese===
号手一吹响时辰，众人便迅速聚拢，我沿升降口下来对他们讲话。我立在他们上方、较低的一级台阶上，既能俯瞰他们，也人人看得见我。他们大多安静、似乎在留神听。只有一小堆人在嘀嘀咕咕——柯季是那堆人的中心。
「破晓之时，我们要登船拿下『索翁号』，」我开口道，「具体的命令你们会从直属军官处领受，可有一桩我要特别强调——不得滥杀。拿下该船之后，我们要把愿带的口粮、兵器、囚犯搬到『索法尔号』上来。与此同时，也要把不愿留在此船受我指挥的人，以及我不愿带在身边的人，从『索法尔号』转到『索翁号』上去。」我说这话时，直直盯着柯季和他身边那群心怀不满的人。

===Original===
“I shall explain what I have in mind for the future, so that each of you may be able to determine between now and daybreak whether he cares to become a member of my company. Those who do will be required to obey orders; but they will share in the profits of the cruise, if there are profits. The purposes of the expedition are twofold: To prey on Thorist shipping and to explore the unknown portions of Amtor after we have returned the Vepajan prisoners to their own country.
“There will be excitement and adventure; there will be danger, too; and I want no cowards along, nor any trouble makers. There should be profits, for I am assured that richly laden Thorist ships constantly ply the known seas of Amtor; and I am informed that we can always find a ready market for such spoils of war as fall into our hands—and war it shall be, with the Soldiers of Liberty fighting the oppression and tyranny of Thorism.
“Return to your quarters now, and be prepared to give a good account of yourselves at daybreak.”

===Chinese===
「我再把日后的打算说与诸位，好叫各位在破晓之前自行定夺，是否愿入我这一伙。愿入者，须听号令；但若此行有利可图，也自当与众人共分。此行有两个目的：一是劫掠索尔党的船只，二是等我们把韦帕贾囚犯送还故国之后，去探查阿姆托未知的疆域。
「这其中有刺激、有奇遇，也自有危险；我不要懦夫同行，也不要生事之人。应当有利可图——因为有人向我保证，满载货物的索尔党船只在阿姆托已知的海域里川流不息；又有人告知我，凡是落入我们手中的战利品，总找得到现成的买主——而这将是战争，是『自由之兵』与索尔主义的压迫暴政之战。
「现在各回各位，破晓之时，预备好各显身手。」

## XI Duare / XI 杜阿雷

===Original===
I got little sleep that night. My officers were constantly coming to me with reports. From these I learned, what was of the greatest importance to me, the temper of the crew. None was averse to taking the *Sovong*, but there was a divergence of opinion as to what we should do thereafter. A few wanted to be landed on Thoran soil, so that they could make their way back to their homes; the majority was enthusiastic about plundering merchant ships; the idea of exploring the unknown waters of Amtor filled most of them with fear; some were averse to restoring the Vepajan prisoners to their own country; and there was an active and extremely vocal minority that insisted that the command of the vessel should be placed in the hands of Thorans. In this I could see the hand of Kodj even before they told me that the suggestion had come from the coterie that formed his following.
“But there are fully a hundred,” said Gamfor, “upon whose loyalty you may depend. These have accepted you as their leader, and they will follow you and obey your commands.”
“Arm these,” I directed, “and place all others below deck until after we have taken the *Sovong*. How about the *klangan*? They took no part in the mutiny. Are they for us or against us?”

===Chinese===
那一夜我没怎么合眼。手下的军官们不停地来向我禀报。我从这些禀报中得知了船员们的心思——这是我最要紧的事。夺取『索翁号』无人反对，可事成之后该怎么办，众说纷纭。少数人想被送上索拉人的土地，好各自回家；多数人对劫掠商船兴致勃勃；至于探查阿姆托未知水域一事，多半闻之色变；有人不愿把韦帕贾囚犯送还故国；另有一伙活跃而极善鼓噪的少数，坚持船上指挥权应交到索拉人手里。这背后我已看出柯季的手笔，不待他们告诉我此议出自他那一伙小圈子，我已了然。
「可有整整一百人，」甘福尔说，「他们的忠心靠得住。他们已认你为首领，会追随你、听你的号令。」
「给这些人发兵器，」我吩咐，「把其余的人统统关到底舱，待拿下『索翁号』之后再放出来。克兰甘（klangan）怎么说？他们没参与哗变。他们是向着我们，还是向着对方？」

===Original===
Kiron laughed. “They received no orders one way or the other,” he explained. “They have no initiative. Unless they are motivated by such primitive instincts as hunger, love, or hate, they do nothing without orders from a superior.”
“And they don’t care who their master is,” interjected Zog. “They serve loyally enough until their master dies, or sells them, or gives them away, or is overthrown; then they transfer the same loyalty to a new master.”
“They have been told that you are their new master,” said Kamlot, “and they will obey you.”
As there were only five of the birdmen aboard the *Sofal*, I had not been greatly exercised about their stand; but I was glad to learn that they would not be antagonistic.

===Chinese===
基隆笑起来。「他们两边的命令都没接到，」他解释，「他们没半点主见。除非受了饥饿、情爱、仇恨这类原始本能的驱使，否则没有上峰的命令他们便一动不动。」
「而且他们才不在乎主子是谁。」佐格插嘴，「他们伺候得相当忠心——直到主子死了，或卖了他们，或把他们送了人，或被推翻；那时他们便把同样的忠心转给新主子。」
「已有人告诉他们，你是他们的新主子了，」卡姆洛特说，「他们会听你的。」
『索法尔号』上只有五个鸟人，他们的立场我本不甚挂怀；可得知他们不会与我为敌，我仍觉欣慰。

===Original===
At the twentieth hour I ordered the hundred upon whom we could depend assembled and held in the lower deck house, the others having all been confined below earlier in the night, in the accomplishment of which a second mutiny was averted only by the fact that all the men had been previously disarmed except the loyal Soldiers of Liberty.
All during the night we had been gradually gaining upon the unsuspecting *Sovong* until now we were scarcely a hundred yards astern of her, slightly aport. Across our starboard bow I could see her looming darkly in the mysterious nocturnal glow of the moonless Amtorian night, her lanterns white and colored points of light, her watch dimly visible upon her decks.
Closer and closer the *Sofal* crept toward her prey. A Soldier of Liberty, who had once been an officer in the Thoran navy, was at the wheel; no one was on deck but the members of the watch; in the lower deck house a hundred men were huddled waiting for the command to board; I stood beside Honan in the chart room (he was to command the *Sofal* while I led the boarding party), my eyes upon the strange Amtorian chronometer. I spoke a word to him and he moved a lever. The *Sofal* crept a little closer to the *Sovong*. Then Honan whispered an order to the helmsman and we closed in upon our prey.

===Chinese===
到第二十个时辰，我下令将那靠得住的一百人集合、留在下层舱房里；其余的人早在入夜不久便已被关到底下去——办这事时险些又起一场哗变，全靠所有人事先都已被缴了械，只忠心的「自由之兵」例外，这才弭平。
整整一夜，我们一点点追近那毫无觉察的『索翁号』，到如今我们已在她船尾偏左舷、不足百码之处。从我船右舷船首望去，我可见她在阿姆托无月之夜那神秘夜辉中黑黝黝地矗立，她的灯笼是白色与彩色的光点，她甲板上的值更者隐约可见。
『索法尔号』一点一点向她的猎物挨近。掌舵的是一位「自由之兵」——他从前在索拉海军里当过军官；甲板上除了值更的几人之外别无他人；下层舱房里挤着一百号人，等候登船的号令；我站在海图室里霍南身旁（我率队登船时，由他指挥『索法尔号』），眼睛盯着那奇特的阿姆托计时器。我对他说了句话，他推上一根操纵杆。『索法尔号』又向『索翁号』挨近了些。随后霍南向舵手低声发令，我们便朝猎物合围过去。

===Original===
I hastened down the companionway to the main deck and gave the signal to Kamlot standing in the doorway of the deck house. The two ships were close now and almost abreast. The sea was calm; only a gentle swell raised and lowered the softly gliding ships. Now we were so close that a man could step across the intervening space from the deck of one ship to that of the other.
The officer of the watch aboard the *Sovong* hailed us. “What are you about?” he demanded. “Sheer off, there!”
For answer I ran across the deck of the *Sofal* and leaped aboard the other ship, a hundred silent men following in my wake. There was no shouting and little noise—only the shuffling of sandalled feet and the subdued clank of arms.
Behind us the grappling hooks were thrown over the rail of the *Sovong*. Every man had been instructed as to the part he was to play. Leaving Kamlot in command on the main deck, I ran to the tower deck with a dozen men, while Kiron led a score of fighting men to the second deck where most of the officers were quartered.

===Chinese===
我急忙沿升降口下到主甲板，向站在舱房门口的卡姆洛特发出信号。两船此刻已靠得很近，几乎并肩。海面平静，只有轻柔的涌浪把悄然滑行的两船一抬一落。此时我们已近得一个人都能从一船甲板跨过中间的空当踏上另一船了。
『索翁号』上的值更军官朝我们喝问。「你们要做什么？」他喝道，「让开！」
作为回答，我冲过『索法尔号』的甲板，跃上那条船，一百个默不作声的人紧随我身后。无人喊叫，声响也极少——只有草鞋的窸窣与兵器的轻碰。
我们身后，抓钩被抛过了『索翁号』的栏杆。每个人都受过自己所扮角色的叮嘱。我把主甲板交给卡姆洛特指挥，自己带着十几个人冲向塔台甲板，基隆则领着二十名战士扑向第二层甲板——那里住着大部分军官。

===Original===
Before the officer of the watch could gather his scattered wits, I had him covered with a pistol. “Keep quiet,” I whispered, “and you will not be harmed.” My plan was to take as many of them as possible before a general alarm could be sounded and thus minimize the necessity for bloodshed; therefore, the need for silence. I turned him over to one of my men after disarming him; and then I sought the captain, while two of my detachment attended to the helmsman.
I found the officer for whom I sought reaching for his weapons. He had been awakened by the unavoidable noise of the boarding party, and, suspecting that something was amiss, had seized his weapons as he arose and uncovered the lights in his cabin.
I was upon him as he raised his pistol, and struck it from his hand before he could fire; but he stepped back with his sword on guard, and thus we stood facing one another for a moment.
“Surrender,” I told him, “and you will not be harmed.”

===Chinese===
那值更军官还没回过神来，我的手枪已指住了他。「别出声，」我低语，「便不会伤你。」我的盘算是趁全船警报拉响之前，尽可能多地制住他们，把流血的必要降到最低；因此非静默不可。我缴了他的械，把他交给手下一个人看管；随后我去寻船长，我这一队里另两人则去对付舵手。
我找到我要寻的那位军官时，他正伸手去够兵器。他是被登船队那无法避免的声响惊醒的，疑心出了事，便一边起身一边抓起兵器，又点亮了舱中的灯。
他刚举起手枪我便扑了上去，趁他未及开火一掌打飞了他手里的枪；可他退后一步、横剑护住自己，我们就这样对峙了片刻。
「投降，」我对他说，「便不会伤你。」

===Original===
“Who are you?” he demanded, “and where did you come from?”
“I was a prisoner on board the *Sofal*,” I replied, “but now I command her. If you wish to avoid bloodshed, come out on deck with me and give the command to surrender.”
“And then what?” he demanded. “Why have you boarded us if not to kill?”
“To take off provisions, weapons, and the Vepajan prisoners,” I explained.
Suddenly the hissing staccato of pistol fire came up to us from the deck below.
“I thought there was to be no killing!” he snapped.
“If you want to stop it, get out there and give the command to surrender,” I replied.
“I don’t believe you,” he cried. “It’s a trick,” and he came at me with his sword.

===Chinese===
「你是何人？」他喝问，「从哪里来的？」
「我原是『索法尔号』上的囚犯，」我答，「如今指挥她的是我。你若想免却流血，便随我出去，下令投降。」
「然后呢？」他逼问，「你们登船若非为杀人，又是为何？」
「为搬走口粮、兵器，还有韦帕贾囚犯。」我解释。
忽然，楼下甲板传来一阵嘶嘶短促的手枪声。
「不是说好不杀人么！」他厉声道。
「你想叫它停下，便出去下令投降。」我答。
「我不信你，」他叫道，「这是诡计。」说罢挺剑向我刺来。

===Original===
I did not wish to shoot him down in cold blood, and so I met his attack with my own blade. The advantage was on his side in the matter of skill, for I had not yet fully accustomed myself to the use of the Amtorian sword; but I had an advantage in strength and reach and in some tricks of German swordplay that I had learned while I was in Germany.
The Amtorian sword is primarily a cutting weapon, its weight near the tip making it particularly effective for this method of attack, though it lessens its effectiveness in parrying thrusts, rendering it a rather sluggish defensive weapon. I therefore found myself facing a savage cutting attack against which I had difficulty in defending myself. The officer was an active man and skillful with the sword. Being experienced, it did not take him long to discover I was a novice, with the result that he pressed his advantage viciously, so that I soon regretted my magnanimity in not resorting to my pistol before the encounter began; but it was too late now—the fellow kept me so busy that I had no opportunity to draw the weapon.

===Chinese===
我不愿冷血地将他枪杀，便以剑接他的攻势。论剑术，他占了上风，因我尚未完全习惯阿姆托剑的用法；可我有力气、臂长的优势，又有几招在德国时学来的德式剑术巧技。
阿姆托的剑主要是劈砍之器，分量集中在近剑尖处，使它在劈砍时格外凌厉，却也削弱了它格挡刺击的效用，使它作为防御兵器颇为迟钝。于是我发现自己面对的是凌厉的劈砍攻势，应对颇为吃力。那军官身手矫健、剑术娴熟。他经验老到，没多久便瞧出我是个生手，于是穷追猛打，逼得我很快便懊悔起自己交手之前未先用手枪的那股宽宏——可此刻已晚，这厮逼得我手忙脚乱，我竟无暇去拔那兵器。

===Original===
He forced me back and around the room until he stood between me and the doorway, and then, having me where no chance for escape remained, he set to work to finish me with dispatch. The duel, as far as I was concerned, was fought wholly on the defensive. So swift and persistent was his attack that I could only defend myself, and not once in the first two minutes of the encounter did I aim a single blow at him.
