I wondered what had become of the men who had accompanied me; but pride would not permit me to call upon them for help nor did I learn until later that it would have availed me nothing, since they were having all that they could attend to in repelling the attack of several officers who had run up from below immediately behind them.
The teeth of my antagonist were bared in a grim and ferocious smile, as he battered relentlessly at my guard, as though he already sensed victory and was gloating in anticipation. The clanging of steel on steel now drowned all sounds from beyond the four walls of the cabin where we fought; I could not tell if fighting were continuing in other parts of the ship, nor, if it were, whether it were going in our favor or against us. I realized that I *must* know these things, that I was responsible for whatever took place aboard the *Sovong*, and that I must get out of that cabin and lead my men either in victory or defeat.

===Chinese===
他把我逼得节节后退，绕着屋子转，直到他立于我与门之间；如此一来，我再无逃路，他便下手要麻利地了结我。这一场决斗，于我而言尽在防守。他攻势又快又绵密，我只能招架，交手头两分钟里我竟没还过一击。
我纳罕随我同来的那几个人哪里去了；可自尊不许我唤他们相助——况且我后来才知，唤也无益，因为他们正自顾不暇，忙着抵挡几个紧随其后从下层冲上来的军官的攻击。
我对手的牙咬得呲着，一副狰狞的笑，毫不留情地朝我的守势猛劈，仿佛已嗅到胜利的气味，正为将得手而暗自得意。钢与钢相击的铿锵声，此刻已盖过了我们交手这舱房四壁之外的一切声响；我分辨不出船上别处是否还在打，若在打，又是否对我方有利。我意识到自己 *必须* 知道这些事——『索翁号』上所发生的一切都由我负责，我必须冲出这间舱房，无论胜败，去引领我的人。

===Original===
Such thoughts made my position even more impossible than as though only my life were at stake and drove me to attempt heroic measures for releasing myself from my predicament and my peril. I must destroy my adversary, and I must do so at once!
He had me now with my back almost against the wall. Already his point had touched me upon the cheek once and twice upon the body, and though the wounds were but scratches, I was covered with blood. Now he leaped upon me in a frenzy of determination to have done with me instantly, but this time I did not fall back. I parried his cut, so that his sword passed to the right of my body which was now close to his; and then I drew back my point, and, before he could recover himself, drove it through his heart.
As he sagged to the floor, I jerked my sword from his body and ran from his cabin. The entire episode had required but a few minutes, though it had seemed much longer to me, yet in that brief time much had occurred on the decks and in the cabins of the *Sovong*. The upper decks were cleared of living enemies; one of my own men was at the wheel, another at the controls; there was still fighting on the main deck where some of the *Sovong*’s officers were making a desperate last stand with a handful of their men. But by the time I reached the scene of the battle, it was over; the officers, assured by Kamlot that their lives would be spared, had surrendered—the *Sovong* was ours. The *Sofal* had taken her first prize!

===Chinese===
这些念头叫我处境愈发艰难——仿佛不仅是我一条命悬于一线，更逼我使出拼命的法子来摆脱这困局与险境。我必须除掉对手，而且必须立即！
此时他已逼得我背几乎贴到墙上。他的剑尖已在我脸颊上点过一下、身上点过两下，伤口虽不过擦破，我却已浑身是血。此刻他一阵癫狂地朝我扑来，定要立时了结我，可这一回我没退。我格开他的劈砍，使他的剑从我身子右边滑过——此刻我身子已贴近他；随即我收回剑尖，趁他未及回防，一剑捅进他的心窝。
他瘫倒在地，我猛地抽出剑，冲出他的舱房。整桩事不过几分钟，我却觉得漫长得多；可就在这短短时间里，『索翁号』的甲板上与舱房中已发生许多变故。上层甲板的活敌已扫清；我的人有一个在舵轮旁，另一个在操纵器旁；主甲板上仍在打，几个『索翁号』军官带着一伙部下在做殊死的最后抵抗。可等我赶到战场，事已了结；军官们得了卡姆洛特保他们性命的承诺，已投降——『索翁号』是我们的了。『索法尔号』得了她的头一件战利品！

===Original===
As I sprang into the midst of the excited warriors on the main deck, I must have presented a sorry spectacle, bleeding, as I was, from my three wounds; but my men greeted me with loud cheers. I learned later that my absence from the fighting on the main deck had been noticed and had made a poor impression on my men, but when they saw me return bearing the scars of combat, my place in their esteem was secured. Those three little scratches proved of great value to me, but they were as nothing in comparison with the psychological effect produced by the wholly disproportionate amount of blood they had spilled upon my naked hide.
We now quickly rounded up our prisoners and disarmed them. Kamlot took a detachment of men and released the Vepajan captives whom he transferred at once to the *Sofal*. They were nearly all women, but I did not see them as they were taken from the ship, being engaged with other matters. I could imagine, though, the joy in the hearts of Kamlot and Duare at this reunion, which the latter at least had probably never even dared to hope for.

===Chinese===
我带着三处伤口、浑身是血地跃入主甲板上那群激昂的战士中间，模样想必颇为狼狈；可我的人以高声欢呼迎我。后来我才知道，我不在主甲板参战一事被人察觉，在我的人心里留了个不佳的印象，可当他们见我带着交锋的伤痕回来，我在他们心中的地位便稳了。那三道小擦伤对我价值连城，而比起它们溅在我赤裸皮肉上那全然不成比例的血量所产生的心理效用，又算不得什么了。
我们随即把俘虏收拢、缴械。卡姆洛特带了一队人去放出韦帕贾俘虏，立刻把他们转移到『索法尔号』上。他们几乎全是女子，可她们被带下船时我并没看见，因我正忙着别的事。然而，卡姆洛特与杜阿雷重逢时心中的欢喜，我是想得见的——至少后者恐怕做梦也不敢奢望这一日。

===Original===
Rapidly we transferred all of the small arms of the *Sovong* to the *Sofal*, leaving only sufficient to equip the officers of the ill-starred vessel. This work was entrusted to Kiron and was carried out by our own men, while Gamfor, with a contingent of our new-made prisoners, carried all of the *Sovong*’s surplus provisions aboard our own ship. This done, I ordered all the *Sovong*’s guns thrown overboard—by that much at least I would cripple the power of Thora. The last act in this drama of the sea was to march our one hundred imprisoned malcontents from the *Sofal* to the *Sovong* and present them to the latters’ new commander with my compliments. He did not seem greatly pleased, however, nor could I blame him. Neither were the prisoners pleased. Many of them begged me to take them back aboard the *Sofal*; but I already had more men than I felt were needed to navigate and defend the ship; and each of the prisoners had been reported as having expressed disapproval of some part or all of our plan; so that I, who must have absolute loyalty and cooperation, considered them valueless to me.

===Chinese===
我们飞快地把『索翁号』所有轻兵器搬到『索法尔号』上，只留下足够装备这条倒霉船的军官的份。这事交由基隆负责，由我们自己的人执行；甘福尔则带着一队新降的俘虏，把『索翁号』多余的口粮尽数搬到我们船上。办完这事，我下令把『索翁号』所有火炮抛进海里——至少借此削弱索拉的力量一分。这出海上大戏的最后一幕，是把我们囚禁的一百名心怀不满者从『索法尔号』押到『索翁号』上，连同我的问候一起移交给该船的新指挥官。他却似乎并不大高兴，我也怨不得他。囚犯们也不乐意。他们中有许多求我把他们带回『索法尔号』；可我手上的人手已多于驾船与守船所需；况且每个囚犯都曾被报上来过，说他们对我们的计划或部分、或全部地表示过反对；而我须要的是绝对的忠诚与合作，故而视他们于我无益。

===Original===
Kodj, strange to say, was the most persistent. He almost went on his knees as he pleaded with me to permit him to remain with the *Sofal*, and he promised me such loyalty as man had never known before; but I had had enough of Kodj and told him so. Then, when he found that I could not be moved, he turned upon me, swearing by all his ancestors that he would get even with me yet, even though it took a thousand years.
Returning to the deck of the *Sofal*, I ordered the grappling hooks cast off; and presently the two ships were under way again, the *Sovong* proceeding toward the Thoran port that was her destination, the *Sofal* back toward Vepaja. Now, for the first time, I had opportunity to inquire into our losses and found that we had suffered four killed and twenty-one wounded, the casualties among the crew of the *Sovong* having been much higher.

===Chinese===
说来奇怪，柯季最为执拗。他几乎要跪下来求我准他留在『索法尔号』上，并许我以人所未见的忠心；可我已受够了柯季，便照直告诉他。待他见我铁了心，便翻脸咒我，拿他历代祖先起誓，定要与我算这笔账，哪怕耗上一千年。
回到『索法尔号』甲板，我下令解开抓钩；不多时两船又启航了，『索翁号』驶向她原定的索拉港口，『索法尔号』折回韦帕贾。此时我才头一回有空查问损失，得知我方阵亡四人、伤二十一人，而『索翁号』船员的伤亡要惨重得多。

===Original===
For the greater part of the remainder of the day I was busy with my officers organizing the personnel of the *Sofal* and systematizing the activities of this new and unfamiliar venture, in which work Kiron and Gamfor were of inestimable value; and it was not until late in the afternoon that I had an opportunity to inquire into the welfare of the rescued Vepajan captives. When I asked Kamlot about them, he said that they were none the worse for their captivity aboard the *Sovong*.
“You see, these raiding parties have orders to bring the women to Thora unharmed and in good condition,” he explained. “They are destined for more important persons than ships’ officers, and that is their safeguard.

===Chinese===
当日剩下的时间大半都花在与军官们一起整顿『索法尔号』的人手、理顺这桩生疏新营生的事务上——其中基隆与甘福尔帮了大忙，其功难估；直到傍晚时分，我才得空过问被救韦帕贾俘虏的安危。我问起卡姆洛特，他说她们在『索翁号』上囚了一段，倒也并无大碍。
「你要知道，这些劫掠队奉命把女子毫发无损、养得好端端地带到索拉，」他解释，「她们是留给比船上军官更要紧的人物的，这便是她们的护身符。

===Original===
“However, Duare said that notwithstanding this, the captain made advances to her. I wish I might have known it while I was still aboard the *Sovong*, that I might have killed him for his presumption.” Kamlot’s tone was bitter and he showed signs of unusual excitement.
“Let your mind rest at ease,” I begged him; “Duare has been avenged.”
“What do you mean?”
“I killed the captain myself,” I explained.
He clapped a hand upon my shoulder, his eyes alight with pleasure. “Again you have won the undying gratitude of Vepaja,” he cried. “I wish that it might have been my good fortune to have killed the beast and thus wiped out the insult upon Vepaja, but if I could not be the one, then I am glad that it was you, Carson, rather than another.”

===Chinese===
「不过，杜阿雷说尽管如此，那船长仍对她有非分之举。我多盼我在『索翁号』上时便知此事，好为他这放肆一命抵一命。」卡姆洛特语气愤愤，神情异乎寻常地激动。
「你把心安下来，」我劝他，「杜阿雷的仇已报了。」
「这话怎讲？」
「那船长是我亲手杀的。」我解释。
他一掌拍上我肩头，两眼放出欣喜的光。「你又赢得了韦帕贾永远的感念，」他叫道，「我本盼着能有我这般好运，亲手宰了那畜生、洗刷韦帕贾所受的辱；可若那人不能是我，那我倒庆幸那人是你，卡森，而不是旁人。」

===Original===
I thought that he took the matter rather seriously and was placing too much importance upon the action of the *Sovong*’s captain, since it had resulted in no harm to the girl; but then, of course, I realized that love plays strange tricks upon a man’s mental processes, so that an affront to a mistress might be magnified to the proportions of a national calamity.
“Well, it is all over now,” I said, “and your sweetheart has been returned to you safe and sound.”
At that he looked horrified. “My sweetheart!” he exclaimed. “In the name of the ancestors of all the *jongs*! Do you mean to tell me that you do not know who Duare is?”

===Chinese===
我觉得他把这事看得太重，把『索翁号』船长之举看得太严重——那毕竟未对姑娘造成实际伤害；可转念我又想，爱情对人的心智向来会耍些古怪把戏，对情人的一桩冒犯在他心里怕是会被放大成国难一般。
「罢了，如今都过去了，」我说，「你的心上人也已毫发无损地回到你身边。」
他闻言一脸惊骇。「我的心上人！」他叫起来，「以历代所有『君』（jongs）的祖先之名！你莫不是要告诉我，你不知杜阿雷是谁？」

===Original===
“I thought of course that she was the girl you loved,” I confessed. “Who is she?”
“Of course I love her,” he explained; “all Vepaja loves her—she is the virgin daughter of a Vepajan *jong*!”
Had he been announcing the presence of a goddess on shipboard, his tone could have been no more reverential and awed. I endeavored to appear more impressed than I was, lest I offend him.
“Had she been the woman of your choice,” I said, “I should have been even more pleased to have had a part in her rescue than had she been the daughter of a dozen *jongs*.”
“That is nice of you,” he replied, “but do not let other Vepajans hear you say such things. You have told me of the divinities of that strange world from which you come; the persons of the *jong* and his children are similarly sacred to us.”

===Chinese===
「我原以为她自然就是你钟情的姑娘，」我承认，「她是谁？」
「我当然爱她，」他解释，「全韦帕贾都爱她——她是一位韦帕贾『君』的处子女儿！」
纵使他是在宣告船上有女神降临，语气也不过如此虔敬。我竭力装出比内心更为动容的样子，免得开罪了他。
「她若是你所选的女子，」我说，「那么能参与搭救她，我比搭救一打『君』的女儿还更觉欣慰。」
「你这话真好，」他答，「可别叫旁的韦帕贾人听见你说这种话。你曾对我说起你那奇异故土的神祇；『君』与他子女之体，于我们亦同样神圣。」

===Original===
“Then, of course, they shall be sacred to me,” I assured him.
“By the way, I have word for you that should please you—a Vepajan would consider it a high honor. Duare desires to see you, that she may thank you personally. It is irregular, of course; but then circumstances have rendered strict adherence to the etiquette and customs of our country impracticable, if not impossible. Several hundred men already have looked upon her, many have spoken to her, and nearly all of them were enemies; so it can do no harm if she sees and speaks with her defenders and her friends.”
I did not understand what he was driving at, but I assented to what he had said and told him that I would pay my respects to the princess before the day was over.

===Chinese===
「那么，他们于我自然也是神圣的。」我向他保证。
「对了，我有一桩该叫你高兴的话要传你——韦帕贾人视之为殊荣。杜阿雷想见你，要亲自向你道谢。这自然不合礼数；可眼下的处境，已令严守我国礼俗变得不切实际，乃至不可能。数百个男人已看过她，许多人还同她说过话，其中几乎尽是敌人；所以她见一见、同她的护卫与朋友说说话，也无甚妨碍。」
我不甚明白他话里所指，却顺了他的话，告诉他在天黑之前我会去拜见公主。

===Original===
I was very busy; and, if the truth must be told, I was not particularly excited about visiting the princess. In fact, I rather dreaded it, for I am not particularly keen about fawning and kotowing to royalty or anything else; but I decided that out of respect for Kamlot’s feelings I must get the thing over as soon as possible, and after he had left to attend to some duty, I made my way to the quarters allotted to Duare on the second deck.
The Amtorians do not knock on a door—they whistle. It is rather an improvement, I think, upon our custom. One has one’s own distinctive whistle. Some of them are quite elaborate airs. One soon learns to recognize the signals of one’s friends. A knock merely informs you that someone wishes to enter; a whistle tells you the same thing and also reveals the identity of your caller.

===Chinese===
我很忙；而且，说句实话，去拜见公主这事我并无多大兴致。事实上我倒宁可躲着它，因我向来不喜逢迎、不喜对王权或任何事物卑躬屈膝；可我决定，出于对卡姆洛特感受的敬重，这事须尽早了结。他离去办事之后，我便朝第二层甲板上分给杜阿雷的住处走去。
阿姆托人敲门不敲——他们吹口哨。我想这比我们的习俗要高明些。每个人有自己独特的口哨。有的口哨是颇为繁复的曲调。一个人很快就学会辨听朋友的口哨。敲门只告诉你有人想进来；吹口哨告诉你同样的事，还一并道出访客的身份。

===Original===
My signal, which is very simple, consists of two short low notes followed by a higher longer note; and as I stood before the door of Duare and sounded this, my mind was not upon the princess within but upon another girl far away in the tree city of Kooaad, in Vepaja. She was often in my mind—the girl whom I had glimpsed but twice, to whom I had spoken but once and that time to avow a love that had enveloped me as completely, spontaneously, and irrevocably as would death upon some future day.
In response to my signal a soft, feminine voice bade me enter. I stepped into the room and faced Duare. At sight of me her eyes went wide and a quick flush mounted her cheeks. “You!” she exclaimed.
I was equally dumbfounded—she was the girl from the garden of the *jong*!

===Chinese===
我的口哨很简单，两个短而低的音，接一个较高较长的音；当我立在杜阿雷门前吹响它时，心却不在屋里那位公主身上，而落在远在韦帕贾库阿德（Kooaad）树城里的另一个姑娘身上。她常在我心头——那个我只惊鸿一瞥见过两次、只说过一次话的姑娘，而那一次，我向她倾诉了一份爱——那份爱将我攫住，正如未来某一日死亡将攫住我一样，完满、不由自主、无可挽回。
回应我的口哨，一个柔和的女声请我进去。我踏入屋内，面对杜阿雷。一见我，她的眼睛睁得大大的，双颊飞起一阵红。「是你！」她叫道。
我同样惊得说不出话——她便是『君』花园里那个姑娘！

## XII “A Ship!” / XII 「一艘船！」

===Original===
What a strange contretemps! Its suddenness left me temporarily speechless; the embarrassment of Duare was only too obvious. Yet it was that unusual paradox, a happy contretemps—for me at least.
I advanced toward her, and there must have been a great deal more in my eyes than I realized, for she shrank back, flushing even more deeply than before.
“Don’t touch me!” she whispered. “Don’t dare!”
“Have I ever harmed you?” I asked.
That question seemed to bring her confidence. She shook her head. “No,” she admitted, “you never have—physically. I sent for you to thank you for the service you have already rendered me; but I did not know it was *you*. I did not know that the Carson they spoke of was the man who—” She stopped there and looked at me appealingly.

===Chinese===
何等离奇的变故！它的突如其来叫我一时语塞；杜阿雷的窘态更是再明显不过。然而这是一个罕见的悖论——一桩叫人欢喜的变故，至少对我是如此。
我朝她走过去，我眼里一定流露了远比自己觉察到的更多的东西，因为她往后缩了缩，脸比方才红得更厉害。
「别碰我！」她低语，「你敢！」
「我可曾伤过你？」我问。
这一问题似乎给了她几分安心。她摇摇头。「不曾，」她承认，「你不曾伤过我——肉体上不曾。我差人请你来，是为谢你已为我做的那些事；可我不知那就是 *你*。我不知他们所说的那个卡森，便是那个在——」她顿住，用求恳的目光望着我。

===Original===
“The man who told you in the garden of the *jong* that he loved you,” I prompted her.
“Don’t!” she cried. “Can it be that you do not realize the offensiveness, the criminality of such a declaration?”
“Is it a crime to love you?” I asked.
“It is a crime to tell me so,” she replied with something of haughtiness.
“Then I am a confirmed criminal,” I replied, “for I cannot help telling you that I love you, whenever I see you.”
“If that is the case, you must not see me again, for you must never again speak those words to me,” she said decisively. “Because of the service you have rendered me, I forgive you your past offenses; but do not repeat them.”

===Chinese===
「那个在『君』的花园里对你说他爱你的男人。」我替她接下去。
「别说！」她叫道，「难道你不晓得这样的话有多冒犯、多罪过？」
「爱你也是罪过么？」我问。
「对我说出来便是。」她答，带几分傲气。
「那我便是个死不悔改的罪人，」我答，「因为我一见你，便忍不住要对你说我爱你。」
「若当真如此，你便不能再见到我了，因为你绝不可再对我说这些话，」她断然道，「念你对我的恩义，我恕你过往的冒犯；但不可再犯。」

===Original===
“What if I can’t help it?” I inquired.
“You must help it,” she stated seriously; “it is a matter of life and death to you.”
Her words puzzled me. “I do not understand what you mean,” I admitted.
“Kamlot, Honan, any of the Vepajans aboard this ship would kill you if they knew,” she replied. “The *jong*, my father, would have you destroyed upon our return to Vepaja—it would all depend upon whom I told first.”
I came a little closer to her and looked straight into her eyes. “You would never tell,” I whispered.
“Why not? What makes you think that?” she demanded, but her voice quavered a little.
“Because you want me to love you,” I challenged her.

===Chinese===
「若我忍不住呢？」我问。
「你必须忍住，」她肃然道，「这于你是生死攸关之事。」
她的话叫我纳闷。「我不懂你的意思。」我承认。
「卡姆洛特、霍南，这船上随便哪个韦帕贾人，若知此事，都会取你性命，」她答，「『君』，我的父亲，待我们回到韦帕贾便会将你处死——一切全看我先告诉的是谁。」
我朝她更近了些，直视她的眼。「你永远不会去告诉的。」我低语。
「为什么不会？你凭什么这样想？」她质问，声音却有些发颤。
「因为你要我爱你。」我向她挑战。

===Original===
She stamped her foot angrily. “You are beyond reason or forbearance or decency!” she exclaimed. “Leave my cabin at once; I do not wish ever to see you again.”
Her bosom was heaving, her beautiful eyes were flashing, she was very close to me, and an impulse seized me to take her in my arms. I wanted to crush her body to mine, I wanted to cover her lips with kisses; but more than all else I wanted her love, and so I restrained myself, for fear that I might go too far and lose the chance to win the love that I felt was hovering just below the threshold of her consciousness. I do not know why I was so sure of that, but I was. I could not have brought myself to force my attentions upon a woman to whom they were repugnant, but from the first moment that I had seen this girl watching me from the garden in Vepaja, I had been impressed by an inner consciousness of her interest in me, her more than simple interest. It was just one of those things that are the children of old Chand Kabi’s training, a training that has made me infinitely more intuitive than a woman.

===Chinese===
她怒得直跺脚。「你简直不可理喻、不知克制、不知廉耻！」她叫道，「即刻离开我的舱房；我再也不愿见你。」
她胸口起伏，那双美目闪光，她离我如此之近，一阵冲动攫住我，要把她拥入怀中。我想把她身子紧紧贴住我，我想以吻覆满她的唇；可胜过这一切的，是我想得到她的爱，于是我克制住自己，唯恐做得太过、失了赢得那一份爱的机会——我感觉得到，那份爱就在她意识的门槛下徘徊。我不知自己为何如此确信，可我确信。我断不至于把殷勤强加于一个厌憎它的女子，可从我第一眼看见这姑娘在韦帕贾的花园里窥视我起，我便有一种内心的觉知，觉出她对我有意——那远不止寻常的关切。这不过是老昌德·卡比（Chand Kabi）所授训练所结的果子之一罢了，那训练使我比一个女子还要敏锐得多。

===Original===
“I am sorry that you are sending me away into virtual exile,” I said. “I do not feel that I deserve that, but of course the standards of your world are not the standards of mine. There, a woman is not dishonored by the love of a man, or by its avowal, unless she is already married to another,” and then of a sudden a thought occurred to me that should have occurred before. “Do you already belong to some man?” I demanded, chilled by the thought.
“Of course not!” she snapped. “I am not yet nineteen.” I wondered that it had never before occurred to me that the girl in the garden of the jong might be already married.
I did not know what that had to do with it, but I was glad to learn that she was not seven hundred years old. I had often wondered about her age, though after all it could have made no difference, since on Venus, if anywhere in the universe, people are really no older than they look—I mean, as far as their attractiveness is concerned.

===Chinese===
「你这是要把我打发到形同流放的地步，我实觉遗憾，」我说，「我不觉得我罪有应得，不过你们世界的规矩自然与我那世界不同。在我那里，一个女子不会因一个男子的爱、或因这爱的表白而蒙羞——除非她已许配旁人。」说着我忽地想起一桩早该想起的事。「你莫非已属于某个男人？」我问，这念头叫我发凉。
「自然不是！」她没好气地说，「我尚不足十九岁。」我竟从未想到，『君』花园里那个姑娘也许早已嫁人。
我不晓得这与年纪有何相干，但得知她并未活到七百岁之高龄，我仍是高兴的。我常揣测她的年纪，虽说终究无关紧要——因为在金星，即便全宇宙也别处再无此景，人的真实年纪实在不会比他们看起来的更老——我是说，就其吸引力而言。

===Original===
“Are you going?” she demanded, “or shall I have to call one of the Vepajans and tell them that you have affronted me?”
“And have me killed?” I asked. “No, you cannot make me believe that you would ever do that.”
“Then *I* shall leave,” she stated, “and remember that you are never to see me or speak to me again.”
With that parting and far from cheering ultimatum she quit the room, going into another of her suite. That appeared to end the interview; I could not very well follow her, and so I turned and made my way disconsolately to the captain’s cabin in the tower.

===Chinese===
「你走不走？」她逼问，「还是要我唤一个韦帕贾人来，告诉他们你冒犯了我？」
「好叫他们杀我？」我问，「不，你骗不了我，你绝不会做这事。」
「那便 *我* 走，」她说，「记住，你永不得再见我，也永不得再与我说话。」
丢下这道全然不叫人快慰的最后通牒，她便出了这间房，进了她套房里的另一间。这便算是会面了结；我也不便追过去，于是转身，怏怏地回到塔楼里船长舱房。

===Original===
As I thought the matter over, it became obvious to me that I not only had not made much progress in my suit, but that there was little likelihood that I ever should. There seemed to be some insuperable barrier between us, though what it was I could not imagine. I could not believe that she was entirely indifferent to me; but perhaps that was just a reflection of my egotism, for I had to admit that she had certainly made it plain enough both by words and acts that she wished to have nothing to do with me. I was unquestionably persona non grata.
Notwithstanding all this, or maybe because of it, I realized that this second and longer interview had but served to raise my passion to still greater heat, leaving me in a fine state of despair. Her near presence on board the *Sofal* was constantly provocative, while her interdiction of any relations between us only tended to make me more anxious to be with her. I was most unhappy, and the monotony of the now uneventful voyage back toward Vepaja offered no means of distraction. I wished that we might sight another vessel, for any ship that we sighted would be an enemy ship. We were outlaws, we of the *Sofal*—pirates, buccaneers, privateers. I rather leaned toward the last and most polite definition of our status. Of course we had not as yet been commissioned by Mintep to raid shipping for Vepaja, but we were striking at Vepaja’s enemies, and so I felt that we had some claim upon the dubious respectability of privateerism. However, either of the other two titles would not have greatly depressed me. Buccaneer has a devil-may-care ring to it that appeals to my fancy; it has a trifle more *haut ton* than pirate.

===Chinese===
我把这事翻来覆去地想，渐渐明白：我不独未在我的求爱上有多大寸进，恐怕日后也无甚指望。我们之间似有某种不可逾越之障，究竟是什么我又想不透。我不信她对我全然无情；可这也许不过是我自负的投射罢了，因我不得不承认，她言与行都已明明白白地表示，她不愿与我沾半点干系。我无疑是不受欢迎之人。
尽管如此——或许正因如此——我发觉这第二次、也更长的一次会面，不过把我的痴情烧得更旺，留我陷于一团上好的绝望之中。她在『索法尔号』上近在咫尺的存在时刻撩拨着我，而她对我二人之间往来的禁令，反倒叫我越发渴盼与她一处。我极不快活，而驶回韦帕贾这趟如今波澜不惊的航程单调乏味，更无半点可供分心之处。我巴望我们撞见另一艘船，因为我们所撞见的任何船都将是敌船。我们是法外之徒，『索法尔号』上的我们——是海盗、是劫掠者、是私掠船长。我倒更偏爱最后这个、也是最文雅的一个称谓。诚然，明特普（Mintep）尚未授予我们为韦帕贾劫掠船只的委任，可我们打击的是韦帕贾的敌人，故而我以为，私掠这份可疑的体面，我们多少有几分资格领受。然而另两个头衔，无论哪个，也不会叫我太泄气。劫掠者这名头带着一股子满不在乎的腔调，颇合我的脾性；它比海盗一名要多几分上流气。

===Original===
There is much in a name. I had liked the name of the *Sofal* from the first. Perhaps it was the psychology of that name that suggested the career upon which I was now launched. It means killer. The verb meaning kill is *fal*. The prefix *so* has the same value as the suffix *er* in English; so sofal means killer. *Vong* is the Amtorian word for defend; therefore, *Sovong*, the name of our first prize, means defender; but the *Sovong* had not lived up to her name.
I was still meditating on names in an effort to forget Duare, when Kamlot joined me, and I decided to take the opportunity to ask him some questions concerning certain Amtorian customs that regulated the social intercourse between men and maids. He opened a way to the subject by asking me if I had seen Duare since she sent for me.

===Chinese===
名号里头大有文章。我打一开始便喜欢『索法尔』这个名字。许是这名字背后的心理，启发了眼下我所投身的这桩营生。它意为「杀手」。「杀」的动词是 *fal*。前缀 *so* 的作用与英语的后缀 er 相当；故 sofal 意为杀手。*Vong* 是阿姆托语里「守卫」之意；因此 *Sovong*——我们头一件战利品的名字——意为「守卫者」；只是『索翁号』并未对得起她这个名字。
我仍在名号上头冥想，想借以忘掉杜阿雷，卡姆洛特来与我同处，我便决意趁此机会，向他打听几桩规约男女交往的阿姆托风俗。他问起我，杜阿雷差人请我之后我可曾见她——便这样引出了话题。

===Original===
“I saw her,” I replied, “but I do not understand her attitude, which suggested that it was almost a crime for me to look at her.”
“It would be under ordinary circumstances,” he told me, “but of course, as I explained to you before, what she and we have passed through has temporarily at least minimized the importance of certain time-honored Vepajan laws and customs.
“Vepajan girls attain their majority at the age of twenty; prior to that they may not form a union with a man. The custom, which has almost the force of a law, places even greater restrictions upon the daughters of a *jong*. They may not even see or speak to any man other than their blood relatives and a few well-chosen retainers until after they have reached their twentieth birthday. Should they transgress, it would mean disgrace for them and death for the man.”

===Chinese===
「见了，」我答，「只是我不懂她的态度，那模样仿佛我看她一眼几乎便是罪过。」
「在寻常情形下，确是罪过，」他告诉我，「可当然，我先前已向你解释过，她与我们所经历的种种，至少暂时地，淡化了某些韦帕贾古法古俗的分量。
「韦帕贾女子二十岁方算成年；在此之前不得与男子结契。这一习俗几与法律同效，对『君』之女的约束更甚。她们未满二十岁生日之前，不得见、也不得与血亲及少数精心拣选的侍从之外任何男子说话。她们若有违犯，便是自身蒙羞、那男子则要送命。」

===Original===
“What a fool law!” I ejaculated, but I realized at last how heinous my transgression must have appeared in the eyes of Duare.
Kamlot shrugged. “It may be a fool law,” he said, “but it is still the law; and in the case of Duare its enforcement means much to Vepaja, for she is the hope of Vepaja.”
I had heard that title conferred upon her before, but it was meaningless to me. “Just what do you mean by saying that she is the hope of Vepaja?” I asked.
“She is Mintep’s only child. He has never had a son, though a hundred women have sought to bear him one. The life of the dynasty ends if Duare bears no son; and if she is to bear a son, then it is essential that the father of that son be one fitted to be the father of a *jong*.”
“Have they selected the father of her children yet?” I asked.
“Of course not,” replied Kamlot. “The matter will not even be broached until after Duare has passed her twentieth birthday.”
“And she is not even nineteen yet,” I remarked with a sigh.
“No,” agreed Kamlot, eyeing me closely, “but you act as though that fact were of importance to you.”
“It is,” I admitted.
“What do you mean?” he demanded.
“I intend to marry Duare!”

===Chinese===
「何等的蠢法！」我脱口而出，可我终于明白，我的逾矩在杜阿雷眼中是何等十恶不赦。
卡姆洛特耸耸肩。「或许是蠢法，」他说，「可它仍是法；而就杜阿雷而言，它的执行于韦帕贾干系甚大，因为她是韦帕贾的指望。」
这头衔我先前已听人加诸于她，可于我毫无意义。「你说她是韦帕贾的指望，究竟是何意？」我问。
「她是明特普独生的孩子。他从未有过儿子，纵有一百个女子想为他生一个。杜阿雷若不生儿子，王祚便绝；而她若要生儿子，那儿子的父亲便必须是一个配作『君』之父的人。」
「他们可曾为她孩子们的爹定下人选？」我问。
「自然不曾，」卡姆洛特答，「这事在杜阿雷过了二十岁生日之前，连提都不会提。」
「而她还不足十九岁呢。」我叹了一声。
「不错，」卡姆洛特应道，定定地打量我，「可你这般模样，倒像是这事与你大有干系。」
「确有干系。」我承认。
「此话怎讲？」他逼问。
「我打算娶杜阿雷！」

===Original===
Kamlot leaped to his feet and whipped out his sword. It was the first time that I had ever seen him show marked excitement. I thought he was going to kill me on the spot.
“Defend yourself!” he cried. “I cannot kill you until you draw.”
“Just why do you wish to kill me at all?” I demanded. “Have you gone crazy?”
The point of Kamlot’s sword dropped slowly toward the floor. “I do not wish to kill you,” he said rather sadly, all the nervous excitement gone from his manner. “You are my friend, you have saved my life—no, I would rather die myself than kill you, but the thing you have just said demands it.”
I shrugged my shoulders; the thing was inexplicable to me. “What did I say that demands death?” I demanded.
“That you intend to marry Duare.”
“In my world,” I told him, “men are killed for saying that they do *not* intend marrying some girl.” I had been sitting at the desk in my cabin at the time that Kamlot had threatened me, and I had not arisen; now I stood up and faced him. “You had better kill me, Kamlot,” I said, “for I spoke the truth.”

===Chinese===
卡姆洛特一跃而起，刷地拔出剑来。这是我头一回见他这般大动情绪。我以为他要当场把我杀了。
「拔剑自卫！」他喝道，「你不拔剑，我便不能杀你。」
「你究竟为何要杀我？」我逼问，「你疯了不成？」
卡姆洛特的剑尖缓缓垂向地板。「我不想杀你，」他颇为黯然地说，方才那一身激昂全然退去，「你是我的朋友，你救过我的命——不，我宁可自己死，也不愿杀你；可你方才所说的话，却非此不可。」
我耸耸肩；这事于我全然费解。「我说了什么非死不可？」我问。
「你说你打算娶杜阿雷。」
「在我那个世界，」我对他说，「男人倒是因说自己 *不* 打算娶某个姑娘而被杀的。」卡姆洛特威胁我时，我正坐在舱房的书桌前，并未起身；此刻我站起，面对他。「你还是杀了我罢，卡姆洛特，」我说，「因我说的是实话。」

===Original===
He hesitated for a moment, standing there looking at me; then he returned his sword to its scabbard. “I cannot,” he said huskily. “May my ancestors forgive me! I cannot kill my friend.
“Perhaps,” he added, seeking some extenuating circumstance, “you should not be held accountable to customs of which you had no knowledge. I often forget that you are of another world than ours. But tell me, now that I have made myself a party to your crime by excusing it, what leads you to believe that you will marry Duare? I can incriminate myself no more by listening to you further.”
“I intend to marry her, because I know that I love her and believe that she already half loves me.”
At this Kamlot appeared shocked and horrified again. “That is impossible,” he cried. “She never saw you before; she cannot dream what is in your heart or your mad brain.”

===Chinese===
他踌躇片刻，立在那里望着我；随后还剑入鞘。「我下不了手，」他哑声道，「愿我历代祖先恕我！我杀不了我的朋友。
「也许，」他寻着开脱之词补道，「你不该为你所不知的习俗担罪。我常忘了你与我们并非同一个世界的人。可你告诉我——我既已为你这桩罪开脱而成了同谋——又是什么让你信你能娶杜阿雷？再听你说下去，我也不能把自己牵连得更深了。」
「我打算娶她，因为我知我爱她，也信她已然有几分爱我了。」
闻此，卡姆洛特又是一脸惊骇。「那不可能，」他叫道，「她从未见过你；她做梦也想不到你心里、你那疯脑子里装的是什么。」

===Original===
“On the contrary, she has seen me before; and she knows quite well what is in my ‘mad brain,’ ” I assured him. “I told her in Kooaad; I told her again today.”
“And she listened?”
“She was shocked,” I admitted, “but she listened; then she upbraided me and ordered me from her presence.”
Kamlot breathed a sigh of relief. “At least *she* has not gone mad. I cannot understand on what you base your belief that she may return your love.”
“Her eyes betrayed her; and, what may be more convincing, she did not expose my perfidy and thus send me to my death.”
He pondered that and shook his head. “It is all madness,” he said; “I can make nothing of it. You say that you talked with her in Kooaad, but that would have been impossible. But if you had ever even seen her before, why did you show so little interest in her fate when you knew that she was a prisoner aboard the *Sovong*? Why did you say that you thought that she was my sweetheart?”

===Chinese===
「恰恰相反，她从前见过我；而且她对我那『疯脑子』里装的什么，清楚得很。」我向他保证，「我在库阿德时对她说过；今日我又说了一遍。」
「而她听了？」
「她震惊，」我承认，「可她听了；随后她申斥我，把我赶出她跟前。」
卡姆洛特松了一口气。「至少 *她* 没有疯。我不懂你凭什么信她可能爱你。」
「她的眼睛出卖了她；还有一桩或许更有说服力——她并未揭发我的不轨、把我送上死路。」
他思忖着，摇摇头。「全是疯话，」他说，「我一句也理不出头绪。你说你在库阿德与她交谈过，可那原是不可能之事。可你若当真曾见过她，那你在知她是『索翁号』上囚徒之时，又为何对她命运那般漠然？你为何说你以为她是我的心上人？」

===Original===
“I did not know until a few minutes ago,” I explained, “that the girl I saw and talked with in the garden at Kooaad was Duare, the daughter of the *jong*.”
A few days later I was again talking with Kamlot in my cabin when we were interrupted by a whistle at the door; and when I bade him do so, one of the Vepajan prisoners that we had rescued from the *Sovong* entered. He was not from Kooaad but from another city of Vepaja, and therefore none of the other Vepajans aboard knew anything concerning him. His name was Vilor, and he appeared to be a decent sort of fellow, though rather inclined to taciturnity. He had manifested considerable interest in the klangans and was with them often, but had explained this idiosyncrasy on the grounds that he was a scholar and wished to study the birdmen, specimens of which he had never before seen.

===Chinese===
「直到几分钟之前，我才知我在库阿德花园里所见、所交谈的姑娘，便是杜阿雷——『君』之女。」我解释。
几日后，我又在舱中与卡姆洛特说话，门口一声口哨打断了我们；我应许之后，一个我们从『索翁号』上救出的韦帕贾囚犯走了进来。他不是库阿德人，而是韦帕贾另一座城池的人，故而船上旁的韦帕贾人都对他一无所知。他叫维洛尔（Vilor），瞧着是个正派之人，只是颇为寡言。他对克兰甘表现出相当的兴趣，常与那帮鸟人在一处，但他解释这癖好时说，自己是个学者，想研究这些鸟人——他从前从未见过此等样本。

===Original===
“I have come,” he explained in response to my inquiry, “to ask you to appoint me an officer. I should like to join your company and share in the work and responsibilities of the expedition.”
“We are well officered now,” I explained, “and have all the men we need. Furthermore,” I added frankly, “I do not know you well enough to be sure of your qualifications. By the time we reach Vepaja, we shall be better acquainted; and if I need you then, I will tell you.”
“Well, I should like to do something,” he insisted. “May I guard the *janjong* until we reach Vepaja?”
He referred to Duare, whose title, compounded of the two words daughter and king, is synonymous to princess. I thought that I noticed just a trace of excitement in his voice as he made the request.
“She is well guarded now,” I explained.

===Chinese===
「我来，」他应我的询问解释道，「是想请你委我一个军官之职。我愿入你这一伙，分担此行的劳作与职责。」
「眼下我们军官已足，」我解释，「人手也已齐备。再者，」我坦率地补道，「我对你还不够熟，无从确信你的能耐。待我们到了韦帕贾，彼此更熟络些；那时我若用得着你，自会相告。」
「可我总想做点什么，」他执拗道，「让我守卫 *janjong*，直到我们抵达韦帕贾，可好？」
他所指是杜阿雷——她的头衔由「女儿」与「王」二字合成，意同公主。我觉得他提此请求时，嗓音里隐隐有一丝兴奋。
「她眼下守卫得很好。」我解释。

===Original===
“But I should like to do it,” he insisted. “It would be a service of love and loyalty for my *jong*. I could stand the night guard; no one likes that detail ordinarily.”
“It will not be necessary,” I said shortly; “the guard is already sufficient.”
“She is in the after cabins of the second deck house, is she not?” he asked.
I told him that she was.
“And she has a special guard?”
“A man is always before her door at night,” I assured him.
“Only one?” he demanded, as though he thought the guard insufficient.
“In addition to the regular watch, we consider one man enough; she has no enemies aboard the *Sofal*.” These people were certainly solicitous of the welfare and safety of their royalty, I thought; and, it seemed to me, unnecessarily so. But finally Vilor gave up and departed, after begging me to give his request further thought.

===Chinese===
「可我愿做这事，」他执意道，「那是我为我『君』尽的一份爱与忠。我可以值夜守；寻常没人爱干这差事。」
「不必，」我简短地说，「守卫已足。」
「她在第二层后舱，是么？」他问。
我说是。
「她有专门的守卫？」
「夜里她门前总有一人。」我保证。
「只有一个？」他追问，似乎觉得守卫不够。
「除例行值更之外，我们以为一人便够；『索法尔号』上她没有仇敌。」这些人对其王室安危的关切，真可谓无微不至——我想；而且关切得在我看来未免多余。但维洛尔终究是放弃了，临去时还请我对其所请再加思量。

===Original===
“He seems even more concerned about the welfare of Duare than you,” I remarked to Kamlot after Vilor had gone.
“Yes, I noticed that,” replied my lieutenant thoughtfully.
“There is no one more concerned about her than I,” I said, “but I cannot see that any further precautions are necessary.”
“Nor I,” agreed Kamlot; “she is quite well protected now.”
We had dropped Vilor from our minds and were discussing other matters, when we heard the voice of the lookout in the crow’s nest shouting, “*Voo notar!*” (“A ship!”) Running to the tower deck, we got the bearings of the stranger as the lookout announced them the second time, and, sure enough, almost directly abeam on the starboard side we discerned the superstructure of a ship on the horizon.

===Chinese===
「他对你杜阿雷安危的关切，似乎比你还甚。」维洛尔去后，我对卡姆洛特说。
「是，我也留意到了。」我的副手若有所思地应。
「没人比我更关切她，」我说，「可我看不出有何再加防备的必要。」
「我也看不出。」卡姆洛特附和，「她眼下守备得十分周到。」
我们已把维洛尔抛诸脑后，正在议别的事，忽听得桅斗上瞭望的喊声：「*voo notar*！」（「一艘船！」）我们奔到塔台甲板，瞭望第二遍报出那陌生船的方位，果不其然，几乎正对我方右舷正横方向，我们辨出地平线上一艘船的上层建筑。

===Original===
For some reason which I do not clearly understand, the visibility on Venus is usually exceptionally good. Low fogs and haze are rare, notwithstanding the humidity of the atmosphere. This condition may be due to the mysterious radiation from that strange element in the planet’s structure which illuminates her moonless nights; I do not know.
At any rate, we could see a ship, and almost immediately all was excitement aboard the *Sofal*. Here was another prize, and the men were eager to be at her. As we changed our course and headed for our victim, a cheer rose from the men on deck. Weapons were issued, the bow gun and the two tower guns were elevated to firing positions. The *Sofal* forged ahead at full speed.
As we approached our quarry, we saw that it was a ship of about the same size as the *Sofal* and bearing the insignia of Thora. Closer inspection revealed it to be an armed merchantman.

===Chinese===
出于某种我不甚明了的缘故，金星上的能见度通常格外地好。低雾与霾颇为罕见，尽管大气湿度甚高。这一情形许是源于这颗星球结构里那奇特元素所发出的神秘辐射——正是它照亮了她无月的夜；我不知道。
无论如何，我们看见了一艘船，几乎立时『索法尔号』上下一片振奋。这又是一件战利品，众人急不可耐地要扑上去。我们改变航向、朝猎物驶去时，甲板上的人爆出一阵欢呼。兵器发了下去，船首炮与两门塔炮都昂起到发射位置。『索法尔号』以全速前行。
我们逼近猎物，看清它是一艘与『索法尔号』大小相仿的船，挂着索拉的徽记。再细看，辨出是一艘武装商船。

===Original===
I now ordered all but the gunners into the lower deck house, as I planned on boarding this vessel as I had the *Sovong* and did not wish her to see our deck filled with armed men before we came alongside. As before, explicit orders were issued; every man knew what was expected of him; all were cautioned against needless killing. If I were to be a pirate, I was going to be as humane a pirate as possible. I would not spill blood needlessly.
I had questioned Kiron, Gamfor, and many another Thoran in my company relative to the customs and practices of Thoran ships of war until I felt reasonably familiar with them. I knew for instance that a warship might search a merchantman. It was upon this that I based my hope of getting our grappling hooks over the side of our victim before he could suspect our true design.
When we were within hailing distance of the ship, I directed Kiron to order her to shut down her engines, as we wished to board and search her; and right then we ran into our first obstacle. It came in the form of a pennant suddenly hoisted at the bow of our intended victim. It meant nothing to me, but it did to Kiron and the other Thorans aboard the *Sofal*.

===Chinese===
我随即下令除炮手外所有人都进下层舱房，因我打算像夺『索翁号』一样登这艘船，不愿我船靠上前时被对方看见甲板上满布武装之人。一如既往，明令发下；每人皆知自己该做什么；众人都受了告诫，不得滥杀。我若要做海盗，便要做一个尽可能人道一些的海盗。我不愿无谓地流血。
我已就索拉战船的习俗与惯例，问过基隆、甘福尔与我手下许多索拉人，直至自认对之颇为熟稔。譬如我知，军舰可以登船搜查商船。我便据此指望，能在猎物起疑之前把抓钩搭上他的船舷。
待我们到了可与那船喊话的距离，我命基伦命对方停机，因我们要登船搜查；便在此时，我们撞上了头一道障碍。那障碍以一面三角旗的形式出现，忽然升在我们意中的猎物船首。它于我无甚意义，于基隆与船上其余索拉人却不然。

===Original===
“We’ll not board her so easily after all,” said Kiron. “She has an *ongyan* on board, and that exempts her from search. It probably also indicates that she carries a larger complement of soldiers than a merchantman ordinarily does.”
“Whose friend?” I asked, “Yours?” for *ongyan* means great friend, in the sense of eminent or exalted.
Kiron smiled. “It is a title. There are a hundred *klongyan* in the oligarchy; one of them is aboard that ship. They are great friends unquestionably, great friends of themselves; they rule Thora more tyrannically than any *jong* and for themselves alone.”
“How will the men feel about attacking a ship bearing so exalted a personage?” I inquired.
“They will fight among themselves to be the first aboard and to run a sword through him.”

===Chinese===
「我们到底不能这样轻易登船了。」基隆说，「船上有位 *ongyan*，这便使他免受搜查。这多半也意味着，船上的兵丁比寻常商船要多。」
「谁的朋友？」我问，「你的？」——因 *ongyan* 意为「大友」，有显贵、尊崇之意。
基隆笑起来。「是个头衔。寡头集团里有一百个 *klongyan*；其中一位便在那条船上。他们无疑是『大友』——是他们自己的大友；他们治理索拉，比任何一个『君』都更为暴虐，且只为自己而治。」
「攻一条载着如此显贵之人的船，众人会作何想？」我问。
「他们会争相抢着头一个登船，好一剑捅穿他。」

===Original===
“They must not kill him,” I replied. “I have a better plan.”
“They will be hard to control once they are in the thick of a fight,” Kiron assured me; “I have yet to see the officer who can do it. In the old days, in the days of the *jongs*, there were order and discipline; but not now.”
“There will be aboard the *Sofal*,” I averred. “Come with me; I am going to speak to the men.”
Together we entered the lower deck house where the majority of the ship’s company was massed, waiting for the command to attack. There were nearly a hundred rough and burly fighting men, nearly all of whom were ignorant and brutal. We had been together as commander and crew for too short a time for me to gauge their sentiments toward me; but I realized that there must be no question in any mind as to who was captain of the ship, no matter what they thought of me.

===Chinese===
「不可杀他，」我答，「我有个更好的盘算。」
「他们一旦杀红了眼，便难管束，」基隆向我保证，「能管束他们的军官，我还没见过。在旧日——『君』的时代——还有秩序与纪律；如今却没有了。」
「『索法尔号』上会有。」我断言，「随我来；我要去对众人讲话。」
我们一同进了下层舱房，船上大半人马都聚在那里，等候进攻的号令。近百个粗豪魁梧的战汉，几乎个个无知而粗野。我们做主帅与做兵众同处为时太短，我还拿不准他们对我的心意；可我明白，不论他们如何看我，谁也不可对谁是这船的船长存半点疑念。

===Original===
Kiron had called them to attention as we entered, and now every eye was on me as I started to speak. “We are about to take another ship,” I began, “on board which is one whom Kiron tells me you will want to kill. He is an *ongyan*. I have come here to tell you that he must not be killed.” Growls of disapproval greeted this statement, but, ignoring them, I continued, “I have come here to tell you something else, because I have been informed that no officer can control you after you enter battle. There are reasons why it will be better for us to hold this man prisoner than to kill him, but these have nothing to do with the question; what you must understand is that my orders and the orders of your other officers must be obeyed.
“We are embarked upon an enterprise that can succeed only if discipline be enforced. I expect the enterprise to succeed. I will enforce discipline. Insubordination or disobedience will be punishable by death. That is all.”

===Chinese===
我们一进去，基隆便令众人肃立，此刻我开腔，百双眼睛齐刷刷落在我身上。「我们即将夺取另一艘船，」我开口道，「船上有一个人，基隆告诉我你们会想杀他。他是个 *ongyan*。我来此便是要告诉你们：他不可杀。」此言一出，迎来一阵不满的咕哝，我不予理会，续道，「我来此还要告诉你们另一桩事，因为我被告知你们一旦投入战斗，便无军官能管束你们。留这人性命比杀他更有利的理由是有，可这与眼下之事无关；你们必须明白的是，我的号令、你们其他军官的号令，必须被遵从。
「我们所投身的事业，唯有靠纪律才能成事。我期盼它成事。我会执行纪律。违令或不从者，处死。完了。」

===Original===
As I left the room, I left behind me nearly a hundred silent men. There was nothing to indicate what their reaction had been. Purposely, I took Kiron out with me; I wanted the men to have an opportunity to discuss the matter among themselves without interference by an officer. I knew that I had no real authority over them, and that eventually they must decide for themselves whether they would obey me; the sooner that decision was reached the better for all of us.
Amtorian ships employ only the most primitive means of intercommunication. There is a crude and cumbersome hand signalling system in which flags are employed; then there is a standardized system of trumpet calls which covers a fairly wide range of conventional messages, but the most satisfactory medium and the one most used is the human voice.
Since our quarry had displayed the pennant of the *ongyan*, we had held a course parallel to hers and a little distance astern. On her main deck a company of armed men was congregated. She mounted four guns, which had been elevated into firing position. She was ready, but I think that as yet she suspected nothing wrong in our intentions.

===Chinese===
我离开那间房时，身后留下近百个沉默的人。他们的反应如何，毫无迹象可循。我有意把基隆也一同带了出去；我要让众人有机会在没有军官在场时自行商议。我知我对他们并无真正权威，他们也终须自己决断是否听命于我；这一决断下得越早，对我们大家都越好。
阿姆托的船只用最原始的通讯手段。有一套粗陋笨重的旗语手信号系统；另有一套标准化的号角声系统，涵盖颇广的惯例讯息；然而最称手、也最常用的媒介，还是人声。
自我们的猎物升起 *ongyan* 的三角旗之后，我们便保持与之平行的航向、稍稍落于其后。它主甲板上聚着一队武装之人。它装有四门炮，已昂起到发射位置。它严阵以待，可我想它尚无所疑——对我们的本意未起疑心。

===Original===
Now I gave orders that caused the *Sofal* to close in upon the other ship, and as the distance between them lessened I saw indications of increasing excitement on the decks of our intended victim.
“What are you about?” shouted an officer from her tower deck. “Stand off there! There is an *ongyan* aboard us.”
As no reply was made him, and as the *Sofal* continued to draw nearer, his excitement waxed. He gesticulated rapidly as he conversed with a fat man standing at his side; then he screamed, “Stand off! or someone will suffer for this”; but the *Sofal* only moved steadily closer. “Stand off, or I’ll fire!” shouted the captain.
For answer I caused all our starboard guns to be elevated into firing position. I knew he would not dare fire now, for a single broadside from the *Sofal* would have sunk him in less than a minute, a contingency which I wished to avoid as much as he.
“What do you want of us?” he demanded.
“We want to board you,” I replied, “without bloodshed if possible.”
“This is revolution! This is treason!” shouted the fat man at the captain’s side. “I order you to stand off and leave us alone. I am the *ongyan*, Moosko,” and then to the soldiers on the main deck he screamed, “Repel them! Kill any man who sets foot upon that deck!”

===Chinese===
此刻我下令使『索法尔号』向那船逼近，二者间距缩短之时，我看见猎物甲板上兴奋的迹象愈演愈烈。
「你们要做什么？」那船塔台上一名军官喝道，「让开！船上有 *ongyan*。」
无人答他，而『索法尔号』继续逼近，他的兴奋便更甚。他与身边一个胖子急促地比着手势说话；随后尖叫道：「让开！否则有人要为此吃苦头」；可『索法尔号』只稳稳地更挨近。「让开，否则我开火了！」船长喝道。
作为回答，我令我们右舷所有火炮昂起到发射位置。我知他此刻断不敢开火，因『索法尔号』一轮齐射不到一分钟便能将他击沉——这一结局我与他同样地想避免。
「你们要我们怎样？」他逼问。
「我们要登船，」我答，「如可能，不流血。」
「这是造反！这是叛逆！」船长身畔那胖子吼道，「我命你让开、莫来搅扰。我是 *ongyan*，穆斯科（Moosko）。」接着他朝主甲板上的兵丁尖叫：「击退他们！谁踏上那甲板一步，便杀！」

## XIII Catastrophe / XIII 大祸

===Original===
At the same moment that the ongyan, Moosko, ordered his soldiers to repel any attempt to board his ship, her captain ordered full speed ahead and threw her helm to starboard. She veered away from us and leaped ahead in an effort to escape. Of course I could have sunk her, but her loot would have been of no value to me at the bottom of the sea; instead I directed the trumpeter at my side to sound full speed ahead to the officer in the tower, and the chase was on.
The *Yan*, whose name was now discernible across her stern, was much faster than Kiron had led me to believe; but the *Sofal* was exceptionally speedy, and it soon became obvious to all that the other ship could not escape her. Slowly we regained the distance that we had lost in the first, unexpected spurt of the *Yan*; slowly but surely we were closing up on her. Then the captain of the *Yan* did just what I should have done had I been in his place; he kept the *Sofal* always directly astern of him and opened fire on us with his after tower gun and with a gun similarly placed in the stern on the lower deck. The maneuver was tactically faultless, since it greatly reduced the number of guns that we could bring into play without changing our course, and was the only one that might offer him any hope of escape.

===Chinese===
翁扬穆斯科下令兵丁击退任何登船之举的同一刻，那船船长下令全速前进、舵打右舷。船身一偏，冲了出去要逃。我当然可以将它击沉，可它沉在海底对我毫无用处；于是我吩咐身边的号手向塔上的军官吹响全速前进的号令，追击便开始了。
那艘『扬号』（Yan）——此刻隔着它的船尾已能辨出它的名字——比基隆让我以为的要快得多；可『索法尔号』也是出奇地迅捷，众人很快便看清对方逃不掉。我们慢慢追回了『扬号』起初那一下出其不意的猛冲所拉开的距离；缓慢而稳妥地，我们正逼近它。随后『扬号』船长所做的，正是我若处在他的位置也会做的：他让『索法尔号』始终正对自己船尾，用其后塔炮与下层甲板船尾一尊同样的炮朝我们开火。这一招在战术上无懈可击，因为它大大减少了我方在不改航向的前提下能投入的火炮数量，也是唯一一招或许能给他一线逃生之望的。

===Original===
There was something eery in the sound of that first heavy Amtorian gun that I had heard. I saw nothing, neither smoke nor flame; there was only a loud staccato roar more reminiscent of machine gun fire than of any other sound. At first there was no other effect; then I saw a piece of our starboard rail go and two of my men fall to the deck.
By this time our bow gun was in action. We were in the swell of the *Yan*’s wake, which made accurate firing difficult. The two ships were racing ahead at full speed; the prow of the *Sofal* was throwing white water and spume far to either side; the sea in the wake of the *Yan* was boiling, and a heavy swell that we were quartering kept the ships rolling. The thrill of the chase and of battle was in our blood, and above all was the venomous rattle of the big guns.
I ran to the bow to direct the fire of the gun there, and a moment later we had the satisfaction of seeing the crew of one of the *Yan*’s guns crumple to the deck man by man, as our gunner got his sights on them and mowed them down.

===Chinese===
我所听到的头一记阿姆托重型火炮的声响，颇有几分诡异。我什么也看不见，既无烟也无焰；只有一声响亮的短促轰鸣，比起别的声音，更像机枪的连发。起初并无别的后果；随后我见我船右舷栏杆被打飞一段，两个手下栽倒在甲板上。
此时我船的船首炮也已开火。我们正处在『扬号』尾流的涌浪中，难以瞄准。两船以全速竞驶；『索法尔号』的船首向两侧远远溅起白浪与水沫；『扬号』尾流处海水沸腾，一股我们斜迎着的重涌使两船不住颠簸。追击与厮杀的激荡在众人血里燃烧，盖过一切的，是那大炮毒蛇般的嘎嘎声。
我奔到船首去指挥那里的炮位，片刻之后，我们便得了那满意的一幕——我方炮手瞄准了『扬号』一尊炮的炮手，把他们一个接一个地扫倒在甲板上。

===Original===
The *Sofal* was gaining rapidly upon the *Yan*, and our guns were concentrating on the tower gun and the tower of the enemy. The ongyan had long since disappeared from the upper deck, having doubtlessly sought safety in a less exposed part of the ship, and in fact there were only two men left alive upon the tower deck where he had stood beside the captain; these were two of the crew of the gun that was giving us most trouble.
I did not understand at the time why the guns of neither ship were more effective. I knew that the T-ray was supposedly highly destructive, and so I could not understand why neither ship had been demolished or sunk; but that was because I had not yet learned that all the vital parts of the ships were protected by a thin armor of the same metal of which the large guns were composed, the only substance at all impervious to the T-ray. Had this not been true, our fire would have long since put the *Yan* out of commission, as our T-rays, directed upon her after tower gun, would have passed on through the tower, killing the men at the controls and destroying the controls themselves. Eventually this would have happened, but it would have been necessary first to have destroyed the protective armor of the tower.

===Chinese===
『索法尔号』正迅速追近『扬号』，我们的炮火集中于敌方的塔炮与塔楼。那翁扬早从上层甲板消失了，想必是到船上不那么暴露之处求安全去了；事实上，他曾立于船长身侧的那座塔台上，活人只剩下两个——他们是给我们添麻烦最大的那门炮的炮手，仅余的两个。
当时我不明白，为何两船的炮火都不见多大成效。我知 T 射线（T-ray）按理该是极具破坏力的，故而不明白为何两船都未被击毁或击沉；可那是因为我尚不知，两船所有要害之处都覆有一层薄甲，由与大炮相同的那种金属制成——那是唯一一种 T 射线无法穿透的物质。若非如此，我方的炮火早已令『扬号』瘫痪，因为我们射向其后塔炮的 T 射线，会一路穿过塔楼，杀死操纵器旁的人、毁掉操纵器本身。这事终究会发生，但须得先毁掉塔楼那层防护甲才行。

===Original===
At last we succeeded in silencing the remaining gun, but if we were to draw up alongside the *Yan* we must expose ourselves to the fire of other guns located on her main deck and the forward end of the tower. We had already suffered some losses, and I knew that we must certainly expect a great many more if we put ourselves in range of those other guns; but there seemed no other alternative than to abandon the chase entirely, and that I had no mind to do.
Giving orders to draw up along her port side, I directed the fire of the bow gun along her rail where it would rake her port guns one by one as we moved up on her, and gave orders that each of our starboard guns in succession should open fire similarly as they came within range of the *Yan*’s guns. Thus we kept a steady and continuous fire streaming upon the unhappy craft as we drew alongside her and closed up the distance between us.
We had suffered a number of casualties, but our losses were nothing compared to those of the *Yan*, whose decks were now strewn with dead and dying men. Her plight was hopeless, and her commander must at last have realized it, for now he gave the signal of surrender and stopped his engines. A few minutes later we were alongside and our boarding party had clambered over her rail.

===Chinese===
我们终于压制了剩下那门炮，可若要驶到与『扬号』并肩之处，便须将自己暴露于它主甲板及塔楼前端的其余炮火之下。我方已有损失，我知一旦进入那些炮的射程，必还要折损许多；可除了彻底放弃追击，似无别的选择，而这我无心去做。
我下令沿它左舷靠近，指挥船首炮沿着它的栏杆射击——我们靠近时，便可逐一扫过它左舷的各炮；又下令我方右舷各炮依次在进入『扬号』炮火射程时照样开火。于是，我们一边靠拢、缩短两船距离，一边对这条倒霉的船倾泻着稳定而连绵的炮火。
我们已折损了一些人，但与『扬号』相比简直不值一提——它甲板上此刻横七竖八尽是死的与将死之人。它处境无望，其指挥官到底也必明白了这一点，因为此刻他发出了投降的信号，停了机。几分钟后我们便靠到它身侧，登船队翻过了它的栏杆。

===Original===
As Kamlot and I stood watching these men who were being led by Kiron to take possession of the prize and bring certain prisoners aboard the *Sofal*, I could not but speculate upon what their answer was to be to my challenge for leadership. I knew that their freedom from the constant menace of their tyrannical masters was so new to them that they might well be expected to commit excesses, and I dreaded the result, for I had determined to make an example of any men who disobeyed me, though I fell in the attempt. I saw the majority of them spread over the deck under the command of the great Zog, while Kiron led a smaller detachment to the upper decks in search of the captain and the ongyan.
Fully five minutes must have elapsed before I saw my lieutenant emerge from the tower of the *Yan* with his two prisoners. He conducted them down the companionway and across the main deck toward the *Sofal*, while a hundred members of my pirate band watched them in silence. Not a hand was raised against them as they passed.
Kamlot breathed a sigh of relief as the two men clambered over the rail of the *Sofal* and approached us. “I think that our lives hung in the balance then, quite as much as theirs,” he said, and I agreed with him, for if my men had started killing aboard the *Yan* in defiance of my orders, they would have had to kill me and those loyal to me to protect their own lives.

===Chinese===
卡姆洛特和我站着看这些人——由基隆领着，去接收战利品、并把若干俘虏带上『索法尔号』——我不禁揣度，他们对我夺首领之位的挑战将作何应答。我知他们摆脱暴虐主子的持续威胁才不久，难保不会做出过分之事，而我对后果忧心忡忡，因我已铁了心：谁违我令，便拿谁儆百，纵我因此殒命亦在所不惜。我见他们大半在魁伟的佐格率领下散布甲板各处，基隆则领一小队往上层去寻船长与那翁扬。
足有五分钟，我才看见我的副手从『扬号』塔楼里押着两个俘虏出来。他沿升降口下、穿过主甲板朝『索法尔号』走来，我那一百号海盗默默地望着他们。他们经过时，无一只手朝他们抬起。
那两人翻过『索法尔号』栏杆朝我们走来，卡姆洛特松了一口气。「我想方才我们性命悬于一线，与他们也并无二致。」他说，我赞同他——因我的人若违我之命在『扬号』上动手杀人，便须连我、连同忠于我的人一并杀了，才能保住他们自己的性命。

===Original===
The *ongyan* was still blustering when they were halted in front of me, but the captain was awed. There was something about the whole incident that mystified him, and when he got close enough to me to see the color of my hair and eyes, I could see that he was dumbfounded.
“This is an outrage,” shouted Moosko, the *ongyan*. “I will see that every last man of you is destroyed for this.” He was trembling, and purple with rage.
“See that he does not speak again unless he is spoken to,” I instructed Kiron, and then I turned to the captain. “As soon as we have taken what we wish from your ship,” I told him, “you will be free to continue your voyage. I am sorry that you did not see fit to obey me when I ordered you to stop for boarding; it would have saved many lives. The next time you are ordered to lay to by the *Sofal*, do so; and when you return to your country, advise other shipmasters that the *Sofal* is abroad and that she is to be obeyed.”

===Chinese===
他们被带到我跟前停下时，那翁扬还在咋呼，船长却已被慑住。整桩事有某种令他迷惑之处，待他挨得我够近、看清我头发与眼睛的颜色，我看得出他惊得说不出话。
「这是暴行！」那翁扬穆斯科吼道，「我定要叫你们最后一个一个地为此偿命。」他浑身发抖，气得脸色发紫。
「看着，不问他便不许他再开口。」我吩咐基隆，随即转向船长。「待我们从你船上取走所需之物，」我对他说，「你便可自由继续你的航程。可惜你先前不肯听我停船受检之令；那本可救许多性命。下次『索法尔号』命你停船，照办便是；你回国之后，也转告其他船主：『索法尔号』出海了，她的号令须得遵从。」

===Original===
“Do you mind telling me,” he asked, “who you are and under what flag you sail?”
“For the moment I am a Vepajan,” I replied, “but we sail under our own flag. No country is responsible for what we do, nor are we responsible to any country.”
Pressing the crew of the *Yan* into service, Kamlot, Kiron, Gamfor, and Zog had all her weapons, such of her provisions as we wished, and the most valuable and least bulky portion of her cargo transferred to the *Sofal* before dark. We then threw her guns overboard and let her proceed upon her way.
Moosko I retained as a hostage in the event that we should ever need one; he was being held under guard on the main deck until I could determine just what to do with him.
The Vepajan women captives we had rescued from the *Sovong*, together with our own officers who were also quartered on the second deck, left me no vacant cabin in which to put Moosko, and I did not wish to confine him below deck in the hole reserved for common prisoners.

===Chinese===
「可否见告，」他问，「你是何人，挂的何旗？」
「眼下我是韦帕贾人，」我答，「但我们挂的是自家的旗。我们所为，无一国担其责；我们也对任何一国不负其责。」
征用『扬号』船员为劳力，卡姆洛特、基隆、甘福尔与佐格在天黑前把它所有兵器、我们要的口粮、以及货物中最值钱又不甚笨重的部分，尽数搬到了『索法尔号』上。我们随即将它的火炮抛进海里，放它自去。
穆斯科我留作人质，以备日后或有所需；他暂被看守在主甲板上，待我定夺如何处置。
我们从『索翁号』救回的韦帕贾女俘，连同我方军官——他们也住在第二层甲板——使我再无一间空舱可安置穆斯科，而我又不愿把他关到底舱那专留寻常囚犯的黑洞里去。

===Original===
I chanced to mention the matter to Kamlot in the presence of Vilor, when the latter immediately suggested that he would share his own small cabin with Moosko and be responsible for him. As this seemed an easy solution of the problem, I ordered Moosko turned over to Vilor, who took him at once to his cabin.
The pursuit of the *Yan* had taken us off our course, and now, as we headed once more toward Vepaja, a dark land mass was dimly visible to starboard. I could not but wonder what mysteries lay beyond that shadowy coast line, what strange beasts and men inhabited that terra incognita that stretched away into Strabol and the unexplored equatorial regions of Venus. To partially satisfy my curiosity, I went to the chart room, and after determining our position as accurately as I could by dead reckoning, I discovered that we were off the shore of Noobol. I remembered having heard Danus mention this country, but I could not recall what he had told me about it.

===Chinese===
我恰在维洛尔面前对卡姆洛特提起这事，维洛尔立时自请与他那间小舱分给穆斯科同住，并负责看管他。这看来是个轻易的解决办法，我便命把穆斯科交给维洛尔，维洛尔即刻把他带回自己舱中。
追击『扬号』使我们偏离了航向，如今我们重又驶向韦帕贾，右舷方向隐约可见一团黝黑陆地。我不禁纳罕，那片朦胧海岸之外藏着怎样的奥秘，那片一直延伸进斯特拉博尔、延伸进金星未探赤道地带的未知之地，又栖居着何等怪异的兽与人。为稍餍我的好奇，我进了海图室，凭航迹推算尽可能准确地定了我们的位置，发觉我们正驶在努博尔（Noobol）的海岸外。我记得听达努斯提过这个国度，却记不起他说了些什么。

===Original===
Lured by imaginings, I went out onto the tower deck and stood alone, looking out across the faintly illuminated nocturnal waters of Amtor toward mysterious Noobol. The wind had risen to almost the proportions of a gale, the first that I had encountered since my coming to the Shepherd’s Star; heavy seas were commencing to run, but I had every confidence in the ship and in the ability of my officers to navigate her under any circumstances; so I was not perturbed by the increasing violence of the storm. It occurred to me though that the women aboard might be frightened, and my thoughts, which were seldom absent from her for long, returned to Duare. Perhaps she was frightened!
Even no excuse is a good excuse to the man who wishes to see the object of his infatuation; but now I prided myself that I had a real reason for seeing her and one that she herself must appreciate, since it was prompted by solicitude for her welfare. And so I went down the companionway to the second deck with the intention of whistling before the door of Duare; but as I had to pass directly by Vilor’s cabin, I thought that I would take the opportunity to look in on my prisoner.

===Chinese===
为想象所诱，我出到塔台甲板上，独自而立，越过阿姆托夜色中微光闪烁的海面，朝神秘的努博尔望去。风已涨到近乎狂风，这是我来牧夫星（Shepherd's Star）以来头一回遇见；海上开始涌起巨浪，可我对这船、对我手下的军官在任何情况下驾驭它的本事都满怀信心，故而风暴愈烈我也不以为意。我倒是想，船上的女子们也许受了惊，而我的心事——它难得离开她太久——又回到了杜阿雷身上。也许她受了惊！
对一个想见心上痴念之物的人而言，连「没有借口」也是好借口；可此刻我自矜的是，我有个真切的理由去见她，且这理由她自己也必领情——因为它出于对她安危的关切。于是我沿升降口下到第二层甲板，打算在杜阿雷门前吹口哨；可我正好要打维洛尔舱门口经过，便想趁此探一探我的囚徒。

===Original===
There was a moment’s silence following my signal, and then Vilor bade me enter. As I stepped into the cabin, I was surprised to see an *angan* sitting there with Moosko and Vilor. Vilor’s embarrassment was obvious; Moosko appeared ill at ease and the birdman frightened. That they were disconcerted did not surprise me, for it is not customary for members of the superior race to fraternize with *klangan* socially. But if they were embarrassed, I was not. I was more inclined to be angry. The position of the Vepajans aboard the *Sofal* was a delicate one. We were few in numbers, and our ascendency depended wholly upon the respect we engendered and maintained in the minds of the Thorans, who constituted the majority of our company, and who looked up to the Vepajans as their superiors despite the efforts of their leaders to convince them of the equality of all men.
“Your quarters are forward,” I said to the *angan*; “you do not belong here.”

===Chinese===
我的口哨之后是一阵短暂的静默，随后维洛尔请我进去。我一踏进舱房，便惊见一个安甘（angan）与穆斯科、维洛尔同坐。维洛尔的窘态是明摆着的；穆斯科显得局促，那鸟人则一脸惊惶。他们的失措并不叫我意外，因优等种族之成员与克兰甘（klangan）社交往来，本非习俗所容。可他们若窘，我却不窘，我更倾向于一腔怒火。韦帕贾人在『索法尔号』上的地位微妙。我们人数寥寥，我们的尊位全仗在索拉人心中唤起并维系那份敬意——他们是我们这一伙的多数，又视韦帕贾人为上等人，尽管他们的头领极力向他们灌输人人平等之说。
「你的住处在前面，」我对那安甘说，「你不属于这里。」

===Original===
“It is not his fault,” said Vilor, as the birdman rose to leave the cabin. “Moosko, strange as it may seem, had never seen an *angan*; and I fetched this fellow here merely to satisfy his curiosity. I am sorry if I did wrong.”
“Of course,” I said, “that puts a slightly different aspect on the matter, but I think it will be better if the prisoner inspects the *klangan* on deck where they belong. He has my permission to do so tomorrow.”
The *angan* departed, I exchanged a few more words with Vilor, and then I left him with his prisoner and turned toward the after cabin where Duare was quartered, the episode that had just occurred fading from my mind almost immediately, to be replaced by far more pleasant thoughts.
There was a light in Duare’s cabin as I whistled before her door, wondering if she would invite me in or ignore my presence. For a time there was no response to my signal, and I had about determined that she would not see me, when I heard her soft, low voice inviting me to enter.

===Chinese===
「这不能怪他。」那鸟人起身离舱时，维洛尔说，「穆斯科说来奇怪，竟从未见过安甘；我把这家伙领来，无非为满足他的好奇。若我做错了，我致歉。」
「自然，」我说，「这话便把事情换了个稍稍不同的面目，不过依我看，囚徒还是到他该去的甲板上去看克兰甘为好。明日我准他去看。」
那安甘离去，我又与维洛尔说了几句，便把他与他的囚徒留在那里，转身朝杜阿雷所住的后舱走去——方才那段插曲几乎立时从我脑中褪去，为远更愉快的念头所代。
我立在杜阿雷门前吹口哨时，她舱里有灯，我心下忐忑，不知她会邀我进去，还是会无视我的存在。我的口哨有一阵子没有回音，我已大致认定她不会见我了，忽听得她柔和低回的声音请我进去。

===Original===
“You are persistent,” she said, but there was less anger in her voice than when last she had spoken to me.
“I came to ask if the storm has frightened you and to assure you that there is no danger.”
“I am not afraid,” she replied. “Was that all that you wished to say?”
It sounded very much like a dismissal. “No,” I assured her, “nor did I come solely for the purpose of saying it.”
She raised her eyebrows. “What else could you have to say to me—that you have not already said?”
“Perhaps I wished to repeat,” I suggested.
“You must not!” she cried.
I came closer to her. “Look at me, Duare; look me in the eyes and tell me that you do not like to hear me tell you that I love you!”
Her eyes fell. “I must not listen!” she whispered and rose as though to leave the room.

===Chinese===
「你倒是执拗。」她说，可语气里那股怒意，已比上回对我说话时少了许多。
「我来是问风暴可有吓着你，也向你保证绝无危险。」
「我不怕，」她答，「你要说的就这些？」
这听来颇像一道逐客令。「不，」我向她保证，「我也并非单为说这话而来。」
她扬起眉。「你还能对我说什么——是你尚未说过的？」
「许是我要把说过的话再说一遍。」我提议。
「你不可！」她叫道。
我朝她更近了些。「看着我，杜阿雷；看着我的眼睛，告诉我你不喜欢听我说我爱你！」
她垂下眼。「我不可听！」她低语，站起身像要离开房间。

===Original===
I was mad with love for her; her near presence sent the hot blood boiling through my veins; I seized her in my arms and drew her to me; before she could prevent it, I covered her lips with mine. Then she partially tore away from me, and I saw a dagger gleaming in her hand.
“You are right,” I said. “Strike! I have done an unforgivable thing. My only excuse is my great love for you; it swept away reason and honor.”
Her dagger hand dropped to her side. “I cannot,” she sobbed, and, turning, fled from the room.
I went back to my own cabin, cursing myself for a beast and a cad. I could not understand how it had been possible for me to have committed such an unpardonable act. I reviled myself, and at the same time the memory of that soft body crushed against mine and those perfect lips against my lips suffused me with a warm glow of contentment that seemed far removed from repentance.

===Chinese===
我对她的爱已叫我发了狂；她近在咫尺的存在使我浑身热血沸腾；我把她拥入怀中、拉向我；未等她阻止，我的唇已覆上她的唇。随即她半挣开去，我见她手中一柄匕首寒光一闪。
「你说得对，」我说，「下手罢！我做了不可饶恕之事。我唯一的借口是我对你爱之深切；它冲垮了理智与廉耻。」
她持匕的手垂回身侧。「我下不了手。」她啜泣道，转身奔出了房间。
我回到自己舱中，痛骂自己是畜生、是恶棍。我不懂自己怎会做出这等不可恕之事。我把自己骂了一通，与此同时，那柔软身子紧贴住我、那完美双唇紧贴我唇的回忆，又叫我浑身漫起一股暖融融的满足——这满足与悔恨相去甚远。

===Original===
I lay awake for a long time after I went to bed, thinking of Duare, recalling all that had ever passed between us. I found a hidden meaning in her cry, “I must not listen!” I rejoiced in the facts that once she had refused to consign me to death at the hands of others and that again she had refused to kill me herself. Her “I cannot” rang in my ears almost like an avowal of love. My better judgment told that I was quite mad, but I found joy in hugging my madness to me.
The storm increased to such terrific fury during the night that the screeching of the wind and the wild plunging of the *Sofal* awakened me just before dawn. Arising immediately, I went on deck, where the wind almost carried me away. Great waves lifted the *Sofal* on high, only to plunge her the next moment into watery abysses. The ship was pitching violently; occasionally a huge wave broke across her bow and flooded the main deck; across her starboard quarter loomed a great land mass that seemed perilously close. The situation appeared fraught with danger.

===Chinese===
上床之后我睁着眼躺了许久，想着杜阿雷，回想你我之间曾发生的一切。我在她那句「我不可听！」的喊声里，听出了隐微之意。我为这样的事实而欣喜：她曾一次拒绝把我交付旁人取我性命，又一次拒绝亲手杀我。她那句「我下不了手」在我耳畔回响，几乎像是一份爱的剖白。我的理智告诉我，我已彻底疯了，可我把这疯狂拥在怀里，竟觉其乐无穷。
夜里风暴愈演愈烈，到了骇人的地步，风的尖啸与『索法尔号』的疯狂颠簸在黎明前将我惊醒。我立即起身上了甲板，那风几乎要把我卷走。巨浪把『索法尔号』高高托起，下一刻又把它掼入水的深渊。船剧烈地颠簸着；偶有一道巨浪越过船首、漫过主甲板；右舷后方矗立着一大片陆地，近得令人心惊。这局面看来危机四伏。

===Original===
I entered the control room and found both Honan and Gamfor with the helmsman. They were worried because of our proximity to land. Should either the engines or the steering device fail, we must inevitably be driven ashore. I told them to remain where they were, and then I went down to the second deck house to arouse Kiron, Kamlot, and Zog.
As I turned aft from the foot of the companionway on the second deck, I noticed that the door of Vilor’s cabin was swinging open and closing again with each roll of the vessel; but I gave the matter no particular thought at the time and passed on to awaken my other lieutenants. Having done so, I kept on to Duare’s cabin, fearing that, if awake, she might be frightened by the rolling of the ship and the shrieking of the wind. To my surprise, I found her door swinging on its hinges.
Something, I do not know what, aroused my suspicion that all was not right far more definitely than the rather unimportant fact that the door to her outer cabin was unlatched. Stepping quickly inside, I uncovered the light and glanced quickly about the room. There was nothing amiss except, perhaps, the fact that the door to the inner cabin where she slept was also open and swinging on its hinges. I was sure that no one could be sleeping in there while both those doors were swinging and banging. It was possible, of course, that Duare was too frightened to get up and close them.

===Chinese===
我进了操纵室，见霍南与甘福尔都在舵手身边。他们因离陆地太近而忧心。机器或舵装置只要失灵一件，我们必被冲上岸去无疑。我让他们守在原地，便下到第二层舱房去叫基隆、卡姆洛特和佐格。
我在第二层升降口脚下转身朝后走时，注意到维洛尔舱门随船的每一次摇晃而开合；可我当时未把这事故在心上，便过去唤醒其他副手。唤醒之后，我继续朝杜阿雷的舱房去，怕她若已醒，会被船的颠簸与风的尖啸吓到。出乎我意料，她的门也正在合页上晃荡。
某种东西——我不知是什么——叫我疑心大事不妙，比她外舱门未闩这一无关紧要的事实更为确定地唤起了我的疑心。我快步踏入，点亮灯，飞快环视屋内。一切都无异样，只除开——也许——她睡觉的内间那扇门也开着、也在合页上晃。我确信这两扇门晃着、砰砰作响之时，无人能在里头安睡。当然，杜阿雷也许吓得太厉害，起不来关门。

===Original===
I stepped to the inner doorway and called her name aloud. There was no reply. I called again, louder; again, silence was my only answer. Now I was definitely perturbed. Stepping into the room, I uncovered the light and looked at the bed. It was empty—Duare was not there! But in the far corner of the cabin lay the body of the man who had stood guard outside her door.
Throwing conventions overboard, I hastened to each of the adjoining cabins where the rest of the Vepajan women were quartered. All were there except Duare. They had not seen her; they did not know where she was. Frantic from apprehension, I ran back to Kamlot’s cabin and acquainted him with my tragic discovery. He was stunned.
“She must be on board,” he cried. “Where else can she be?”
“I know she *must* be,” I replied, “but something tells me she is not. We must search the ship at once—from stem to stern.”

===Chinese===
我走到内间门口，大声唤她的名字。无人应。我又唤，更响；依旧，唯有沉默作答。此刻我当真慌了。踏入房内，我点亮灯，看向那张床。床是空的——杜阿雷不在！可舱房远处的角落里，躺着那个原本守在她门外的卫兵的尸首。
我把礼数全抛诸脑后，急忙奔到各间毗连的舱房——其余韦帕贾女子住在那里。除杜阿雷外，人人都在。她们没见过她；不知她在哪里。我忧心如焚，奔回卡姆洛特舱中，把我这悲惨的发现告诉他。他如遭雷击。
「她必在船上，」他叫道，「她还能在哪里？」
「我知道她 *必在*，」我答，「可有什么告诉我她不在。我们得立刻搜船——从船头到船尾。」

===Original===
Zog and Kiron were emerging from their cabins as I came from Kamlot’s. I told them of my discovery and ordered the search commenced; then I hailed a member of the watch and sent him to the crow’s nest to question the lookout. I wanted to know whether he had seen anything unusual transpiring on the ship during his watch, for from his lofty perch he could overlook the entire vessel.
“Muster every man,” I told Kamlot; “account for every human being on board; search every inch of the ship.”
As the men left to obey my instructions, I recalled the coincidence of the two cabin doors swinging wide—Duare’s and Vilor’s. I could not imagine what relation either fact had to the other, but I was investigating everything, whether it was of a suspicious nature or not; so I ran quickly to Vilor’s cabin, and the moment that I uncovered the light I saw that both Vilor and Moosko were missing. But where were they? No man could have left the *Sofal* in that storm and lived, even could he have launched a boat, which would have been impossible of accomplishment, even in fair weather, without detection.

===Chinese===
我从卡姆洛特舱里出来时，佐格与基隆正从自己舱里现身。我把发现告诉他们，下令开始搜寻；随后我唤住一名值更的，派他上桅斗去问瞭望。我要知道瞭望在值更期间可曾见船上发生什么异事——因从他那高处，他能俯瞰整条船。
「把所有人都召集起来，」我对卡姆洛特说，「核点船上每一个人；搜遍船上每一寸。」
众人离去执行命令之时，我想起两扇舱门同时大敞这桩巧合——杜阿雷的与维洛尔的。我想象不出这两件事彼此有何干系，可我什么都查，不论其是否可疑；于是我快步奔到维洛尔舱中，点亮灯的那一刻，我便看见维洛尔与穆斯科都不见了。可他们在哪里？这样的大风暴中，无人能离了『索法尔号』还活得了，即便他能放得下一只小艇——而放小艇这事，即便在好天气里也休想不被人察觉。

===Original===
Coming from Vilor’s cabin, I summoned a sailor and dispatched him to inform Kamlot that Vilor and Moosko were missing from their cabin and direct him to send them to me as soon as he located them; then I returned to the quarters of the Vepajan women for the purpose of questioning them more carefully.
I was puzzled by the disappearance of Moosko and Vilor, which, taken in conjunction with the absence of Duare from her cabin, constituted a mystery of major proportions; and I was trying to discover some link of circumstance that might point a connection between the two occurrences, when I suddenly recalled Vilor’s insistence that he be permitted to guard Duare. Here was the first, faint suggestion of a connecting link. However, it seemed to lead nowhere. These three people had disappeared from their cabins, yet reason assured me that they would be found in a short time, since it was impossible for them to leave the ship, unless—
It was that little word “unless” that terrified me most of all. Since I had discovered that Duare was not in her cabin, a numbing fear had assailed me that, considering herself dishonored by my avowal of love, she had hurled herself overboard. Of what value now the fact that I constantly upbraided myself for my lack of consideration and control? Of what weight my vain regrets?

===Chinese===
从维洛尔舱里出来，我唤来一名水手，派他去知会卡姆洛特：维洛尔与穆斯科已不在舱中，一旦找着便立刻把他们送到我这里；随后我回到韦帕贾女子的住处，打算更仔细地盘问她们。
穆斯科与维洛尔的失踪叫我迷惑，此事与杜阿雷不在舱中一事并看，便构成了一桩天大的谜；我正想找出某种情形上的环节、好把两桩事勾连起来，忽地想起维洛尔曾执意要守卫杜阿雷。这便是头一丝隐隐约约的关联线索。然而它似乎通不到哪里去。这三人都从各自舱中消失，可理智向我保证，他们不久便会寻见——因他们不可能离船，除非——
正是「除非」这个小词最叫我胆寒。自打我发现杜阿雷不在舱中，一种麻酥酥的恐惧便袭上心头：她莫非因我对她表白爱意、自感蒙羞，而投海自尽了？我如今不停地痛骂自己缺乏体谅与克制，又有何用？我那徒然的懊悔，又有何分量？

===Original===
Yet now I saw a tiny ray of hope. If the absence of Vilor and Moosko from their cabin and Duare from hers were more than a coincidence, then it were safe to assume that they were together and ridiculous to believe that all three had leaped overboard.
With these conflicting fears and hopes whirling through my brain, I came to the quarters of the Vepajan women, which I was about to enter when the sailor I had sent to question the lookout in the crow’s nest came running toward me in a state of evident excitement.
“Well,” I demanded, as, breathless, he halted before me, “what did the lookout have to say?”
“Nothing, my captain,” replied the man, his speech retarded by excitement and exertion.
“Nothing! and why not?” I snapped.
“The lookout is dead, my captain,” gasped the sailor.
“Dead!”
“Murdered.”
“How?” I asked.
“A sword had been run through his body—from behind, I think. He lay upon his face.”
“Go at once and inform Kamlot; tell him to replace the lookout and investigate his death, then to report to me.”

===Chinese===
然而此刻我看见了一线微光。维洛尔与穆斯科不在舱中、杜阿雷不在舱中，若这两件事不止单纯是巧合，那便不妨假定他们是在一处的，而相信三人都投了海，则是荒谬的。
心头这般又是惧、又是盼的念头翻涌着，我来到韦帕贾女子住处，正要进去，我所派去桅斗问瞭望的那名水手一脸显然的激动，朝我奔来。
「怎么样，」他气喘吁吁停在我跟前时我追问，「瞭望怎么说？」
「什么也没说，船长。」那人答，激动与奔走使他说话迟滞。
「什么也没说！为什么？」我厉声道。
「瞭望死了，船长。」那水手喘道。
「死了！」
「被谋杀的。」
「怎么死的？」我问。
「一剑穿身——我看是从背后。他面朝下躺着。」
「立刻去告知卡姆洛特；叫他换下瞭望、调查其死，再来向我回话。」

===Original===
Shaken by this ominous news, I entered the quarters of the women. They were huddled together in one cabin, pale and frightened, but outwardly calm.
“Have you found Duare?” one of them asked immediately.
“No,” I replied, “but I have discovered another mystery—the *ongyan*, Moosko, is missing and with him the Vepajan, Vilor.”
“Vepajan!” exclaimed Byea, the woman who had questioned me concerning Duare. “Vilor is no Vepajan.”
“What do you mean?” I demanded. “If he is not a Vepajan, what is he?”
“He is a Thoran spy,” she replied. “He was sent to Vepaja long ago to steal the secret of the longevity serum, and when we were captured the *klangan* took him, also, by mistake. We learned this, little by little, aboard the *Sovong*.”
“But why was I not informed when he was brought aboard?” I demanded.
“We supposed that everyone knew it,” explained Byea, “and thought that Vilor was transferred to the *Sofal* as a prisoner.”
Another link in the chain of accumulating evidence! Yet I was as far as ever from knowing where either end of the chain lay.

===Chinese===
这一不祥之讯叫我一阵震动，我进了女子们的住处。她们挤在一间舱里，脸色苍白、惊魂未定，但外表尚算镇定。
「你可找到杜阿雷了？」其中一位立时问。
「没有，」我答，「可我又发现一桩谜——那翁扬穆斯科不见了，与那个韦帕贾人维洛尔一道不见了。」
「韦帕贾人！」之前问起杜阿雷的那个女子——她叫比娅（Byea）——叫起来，「维洛尔才不是韦帕贾人。」
「你这话怎讲？」我追问，「他若不是韦帕贾人，那是什么？」
「他是索拉的暗探，」她答，「他多年前被派到韦帕贾，想偷长寿血清的秘方；我们被掳时，克兰甘把他也一并抓来了——是抓错了。这些事，我们是在『索翁号』上一点一点打听出来的。」
「可他被带上船时，为何无人告知我？」我追问。
「我们原以为人人都晓得，」比娅解释，「都以为维洛尔是当作囚犯转到『索法尔号』上来的。」
不断累积的证据链上，又多了一环！可我离知道这条链的两端究竟在哪儿，仍和先前一样遥远。

## XIV Storm / XIV 风暴

===Original===
After questioning the women, I went to the main deck, too impatient to await the reports of my lieutenants in the tower where I belonged. I found that they had searched the ship and were just coming to me with their report. None of those previously discovered missing had been found, but the search had revealed another astounding fact—the five *klangan* also were missing!
Searching certain portions of the ship had been rather dangerous work, as she was rolling heavily, and the deck was still occasionally swept by the larger seas; but it had been accomplished without mishap, and the men were now congregated in a large room in the main deck house. Kamlot, Gamfor, Kiron, Zog, and I had also entered this same room, where we were discussing the whole mysterious affair. Honan was in the control room of the tower.
I told them that I had just discovered that Vilor was not Vepajan but a Thoran spy, and had reminded Kamlot of the man’s request that he be allowed to guard the janjong. “I learned something else from Byea while I was questioning the women,” I added. “During their captivity aboard the *Sovong*, Vilor persisted in annoying Duare with his attentions; he was infatuated with her.”

===Chinese===
盘问完那些女子，我去了主甲板，等不及在我该守的塔楼里候副手们的禀报。我发现他们已搜遍全船，正来向我禀报。先前发觉失踪的那些人一个也没寻见，可搜寻又揭出另一桩惊人的事实——五个克兰甘（klangan）也不见了！
搜船时有些地方颇为凶险，因船摇晃得厉害，甲板仍不时被较大的浪扫过；可这事总算无恙地办妥了，众人此刻聚在主甲板舱房的一间大屋里。卡姆洛特、甘福尔、基隆、佐格与我也进了这同一间屋，正在讨论整桩蹊跷事。霍南在塔楼的操纵室里。
我告诉他们，我刚发现维洛尔并非韦帕贾人，而是索拉的暗探，又提醒卡姆洛特此人曾请求守卫君君女（janjong）一事。「我盘问女子们时，从比娅那里还得知一桩，」我补充，「她们在『索翁号』上为囚期间，维洛尔一再以殷勤纠缠杜阿雷；他对她着了迷。」

===Original===
“I think that gives us the last bit of evidence we need to enable us to reconstruct the hitherto seemingly inexplicable happenings of the past night,” said Gamfor. “Vilor wished to possess Duare; Moosko wished to escape from captivity. The former had fraternized with the *klangan* and made friends of them; that was known to everyone aboard the *Sofal*. Moosko was an *ongyan*; during all their lives, doubtless, the *klangan* have looked upon the *klongyan* as the fountain heads of supreme authority. They would believe his promises, and they would obey his commands.
“Doubtless Vilor and Moosko worked out the details of the plot together. They dispatched an *angan* to kill the lookout, lest their movements arouse suspicion and be reported before they could carry their plan to a successful conclusion. The lookout disposed of, the other *klangan* congregated in Vilor’s cabin; then Vilor, probably accompanied by Moosko, went to the cabin of Duare, where they killed the guard and seized her in her sleep, silenced her with a gag, and carried her to the gangway outside, where the *klangan* were waiting.
“A gale was blowing, it is true, but it was blowing toward land which lay but a short distance to starboard; and the *klangan* are powerful fliers.
“There you have what I believe to be a true picture of what happened aboard the *Sofal* while we slept.”

===Chinese===
「我想这给了我们所需的最末一桩证据，足以重建昨夜那些先前看来无法解释的变故。」甘福尔说，「维洛尔欲得杜阿雷；穆斯科欲逃出囚禁。前者早已与克兰甘结交、与之做成朋友；这事船上无人不知。穆斯科是位翁扬（ongyan）；克兰甘一生之中，想必向来视克隆扬（klongyan）为至高权威之源。他们会信他的许诺，会听他的号令。
「想必是维洛尔与穆斯科一同谋划了此事的细节。他们派一个安甘（angan）去杀瞭望，免得他们的举动引起疑心、在他们得手之前被告发。瞭望既除，其余克兰甘聚到维洛尔舱中；随后维洛尔——多半有穆斯科相伴——去到杜阿雷舱中，杀了守卫，趁她熟睡将她掳走，用物堵了她的嘴，把她抬到外面的舷梯口，克兰甘正在那里候着。
「其时确有狂风，可那风是朝陆地吹的，陆地就在右舷不远处；而克兰甘都是强有力的飞行者。
「这便是我所信的、我们酣睡之时『索法尔号』上所发生之事的真貌。」

===Original===
“And you believe that the *klangan* carried these three people to the shores of Noobol?” I asked.
“I think there can be no question but that such is the fact,” replied Gamfor.
“I quite agree with him,” interjected Kamlot.
“Then there is but one thing to do,” I announced. “We must turn back and land a searching party on Noobol.”
“No boat could live in this sea,” objected Kiron.
“The storm will not last forever,” I reminded him. “We shall lie off the shore until it abates. I am going up to the tower; I wish you men would remain here and question the crew; it is possible that there may be someone among them who has overheard something that will cast new light on the subject. The *klangan* are great talkers, and they may have dropped some remark that will suggest the ultimate destination Vilor and Moosko had in mind.”

===Chinese===
「那你以为，是克兰甘把这三个人运到了努博尔的海岸上？」我问。
「我想事实确乎如此，不容置疑。」甘福尔答。
「我与他所见相同。」卡姆洛特插言。
「那就只有一件事可做了，」我宣布，「我们必须折回，在努博尔登陆一队搜寻的人。」
「这样的海，没有小船活得下来。」基隆反对。
「风暴不会永远不停，」我提醒他，「我们停泊在近海等它平息。我要上塔楼去；我希望你们留在这里盘问船员；他们之中也许有人听到了什么，能为这事投下新的光亮。克兰甘是话痨，他们也许漏出过某句话，能点出维洛尔与穆斯科心目中的最终去处。」

===Original===
As I stepped out onto the main deck, the *Sofal* rose upon the crest of a great wave and then plunged nose downward into the watery abyss beyond, tilting the deck forward at an angle of almost forty-five degrees. The wet and slippery boards beneath my feet gave them no hold, and I slid helplessly forward almost fifty feet before I could check my descent. Then the ship buried her nose in a mountainous wave and a great wall of water swept the deck from stem to stern, picking me up and whirling me helplessly upon its crest.
For a moment I was submerged, and then a vagary of the Titan that had seized me brought my head above the water, and I saw the *Sofal* rolling and pitching fifty feet away.
Even in the immensity of interstellar space I had never felt more helpless nor more hopeless than I did at that moment on the storm-lashed sea of an unknown world, surrounded by darkness and chaos and what terrible creatures of this mysterious deep I could not even guess. I was lost! Even if my comrades knew of the disaster that had overwhelmed me, they were helpless to give me aid. No boat could live in that sea, as Kiron had truly reminded us, and no swimmer could breast the terrific onslaught of those racing, wind-driven mountains of water that might no longer be described by so puny a word as wave.

===Chinese===
我踏出到主甲板上的那一刻，『索法尔号』被一架巨浪抬上浪尖，随即船首朝下扎进浪后那水的深渊，甲板向前倾斜到几乎四十五度角。我脚下湿滑的板子毫无着力之处，我无助地向前滑出近五十英尺才煞住下坠。随即船首扎进又一座山一般的浪，一堵水墙从船头扫到船尾，把我卷起，无助地在那浪尖上翻滚。
有一阵子我被没入水中，随后那攫住我的巨人之手一个古怪的劲儿，把我的头托出了水面，我看见『索法尔号』在五十英尺外翻滚颠簸。
即便在星际太空的无垠之中，我也从未像此刻在这未知世界风暴肆虐的海上这般无助、这般无望过——四围是黑暗与混沌，这神秘深海里藏着何等可怖的生灵，我连猜都猜不出。我完了！即便战友们知晓了我所遭的灾，他们也无力相救。这样的海，没有小船活得下来——基隆说得不假——也没有哪个泅水者能迎面顶住那些飞驰的、风催的、山一般的水头之猛攻；那水头已不能再用「浪」这等微不足道的字眼来描摹了。

===Original===
Hopeless! I should not have said that; I am never without hope. If I could not swim against the sea, perhaps I might swim with it; and at no great distance lay land. I am an experienced distance swimmer and a powerful man. If any man could survive in such a sea, I knew that I could; but if I could not, I was determined that I should at least have the satisfaction of dying fighting.
